丑	chǒu	the clown role — Recognizable by the small patch of white makeup 白粉块 báifěn kuài around the nose -- a marking that immediately signals comic status. The 丑 line includes male 男丑 and female 女丑 (also called 彩旦 cǎidàn, a comic older woman type).
臭	chòu	smelly, stinking
臭豆腐	chòu dòu fu	Stinky Tofu; Fermented Tofu
出版	chū bǎn	to publish; to put out an edition — 出 chū (emerge; emit) + 版 bǎn (board; printing plate; edition). To emit a printing plate , to send a text out into the world as a published work. The metaphor is the woodblock or copper plate of traditional printing, the fixed form from which copies emerge.
出版社	chū bǎn shè	publishing house
出产	chū chǎn	to produce; to yield; output — 出 chū (emerge; put out) + 产 chǎn (produce; give birth to; output). To yield products from a source , a region produces goods, a field yields a crop, a factory puts out units. Used for natural resources, agricultural yield, and manufactured goods. As a noun: the output, the produce.
出错	chū cuò	to go wrong; to make an error — 出 chū (to come out) + 错 cuò (error). For an error to surface, often in a process or system: 系统出错了 "the system threw an error," 计算出错 "a miscalculation came up." Common in technical and procedural contexts.
出发	chū fā	to set out; to depart; to start from (a position or principle) — 出 chū (exit; emerge from) + 发 fā (to send out; to dispatch; to start moving). The moment of departure , leaving a point of origin for a destination. Physical: a train, a traveler, a team setting out.
出家	chū jiā	to leave home; enter monastic life
出口	chū kǒu	to export; an exit; to speak — 出 chū (exit; emerge) + 口 kǒu (mouth; opening; port). Three interlocking meanings from the same compound. As trade: to export , goods going out through the port. As architecture: the exit of a building or tunnel. As speech: 出口成章 (words emerge perfectly formed , to speak with eloquence).
出口退税	chū kǒu tuì shuì	export VAT rebate — The refund of input VAT on exported goods, claimed by whoever exports them. A factory with its own export licence (自营出口 zìyíng chūkǒu) claims it directly; one without exports through a trading company (外贸公司 wàimào gōngsī), which adds a layer of margin.
出来	chū lái	to come out (toward speaker)
出路	chū lù	a way out; prospects, future direction — 出 chū (to go out, to exit) + 路 lù (road). Literally "the road out." Used for both physical exit (the way out of a building) and, more often, figurative prospects: career options, an escape from a bad situation, the future of an industry. 没有出路 méiyǒu chūlù = no way forward, no prospects.
出门	chū mén	to go out; to leave home; to set off on a trip — 出 chū (to exit) + 门 mén (door). To step out the door, whether for the day or for a journey: 我出门了 "I'm heading out." Its opposite is 回家 huí jiā or 进门 jìnmén.
出名	chū míng	to become famous; to make a name for oneself — 出 chū (emerge) + 名 míng (name; fame; reputation). To emerge into collective naming , to become the person or thing that others talk about and recognize by name. The 出 marks the transition: previously unknown, now known. 出名 carries no inherent judgment of whether the fame is deserved or welcome.
出去	chū qù	to go out; to head outside — 出 chū (to exit; to emerge) + 去 qù (away from here). To exit and move away from the speaker's reference point. Contrast: 出来 chūlái (to come out , exiting toward where the speaker is). 出去 is used by someone inside sending someone outside, or by someone outside talking about leaving an enclosed space.
出色	chū sè	outstanding; excellent; remarkable — 出 chū (emerge; stand out) + 色 sè (color; appearance; quality). "Emerging in color" , a vivid presence that distinguishes itself against its background. The color metaphor is apt: something outstanding has a quality that registers immediately, the way a saturated color stands out on a pale surface.
出神	chū shén	to enter trance; to be rapt
出生	chū shēng	to be born; birth — 出 chū (emerge) + 生 shēng (life; to be born; raw). Emergence into life: the original threshold crossing. 出生 is used for persons: 他在北京出生 (he was born in Beijing). The date is 出生日期 chūshēng rìqī (date of birth).
出问题	chū wèn tí	a problem occurs; something goes wrong — Here 问题 is unambiguously "problem": 出 chū (to emerge, arise) + 问题 = "a problem crops up, something breaks down." Used for malfunctions, complications, and things going wrong. Contrast 解决问题 (solve a problem) and 发现问题 (spot a problem).
出现	chū xiàn	to appear; to emerge; to come into view — 出 chū (emerge) + 现 xiàn (appear; manifest; current). To emerge into visibility , applicable to everything from a rainbow appearing after rain to a new problem appearing in a system. One of the most common verbs in news language and everyday description.
出于礼貌	chū yú lǐ mào	out of politeness; for the sake of courtesy — 出于 chūyú (proceeding from; out of) + 礼貌. Names the motivation behind a polite-but-not-sincere act: doing something not because you want to but because not doing it would be rude. Useful for marking the gap between social form and personal preference.
出众	chū zhòng	to stand out from the crowd; exceptional — 出 chū (emerge) + 众 zhòng (crowd; the many; multitude). To emerge from the mass of people , to be the one figure that rises above the surrounding level. Often used for talent, appearance, or personal quality that is genuinely rare rather than merely good.
初	chū	beginning; initial
除非	chú fēi	unless; only if — 除 chú (except) + 非 fēi (not). Literally "except if not," hence "unless." Often paired with 否则 fǒuzé or 才 cái: 除非下雨，否则我们就去 "we'll go unless it rains."
除夕	Chú xī	New Year's Eve
厨房	chú fáng	kitchen — 厨 (chú, cooking, the cook) plus 房. The room where food is prepared. One of the most frequent 房 compounds in daily life, and usually among the first room words a learner needs.
楚辞	Chǔ Cí	Songs of Chu
处理	chǔ lǐ	to handle; to deal with; to process — 处 chǔ (to manage; to dispose of) + 理 lǐ (to put in order). To take a matter in hand and see it through: 处理问题 "to deal with a problem," 处理数据 "to process data."
处于	chǔ yú	to be in (a state or situation)
处置	chǔ zhì	to handle; to dispose of; the construction name
触动	chù dòng	to touch; to stir
川菜	chuān cài	Sichuan cuisine — 川 chuān (Sichuan) + 菜 cài (cuisine). The cooking of Sichuan, famous for the numbing-spicy 麻辣 málà of Sichuan peppercorn and chili. 麻婆豆腐 and 回锅肉 are classic 川菜 dishes.
穿过	chuāng uò	to pass through; to cross — 穿 (to pass through) + 过 (guò, across, a directional complement). The verb of moving through and out the other side, a crowd, a forest, a tunnel. 过 marks the full traverse, in one side and out the far side.
穿上	chuān shàng	to put on (and have it on) — 穿 (to thread on) + 上 (shàng, up/onto, here a result complement). The complement 上 marks the garment as now in place. 穿上 stresses the completed act of getting it on, against bare 穿 which can describe the habit of wearing.
穿梭	chuān suō	to shuttle back and forth
穿透	chuān tòu	to penetrate; to pierce through — 穿 (to bore through) + 透 (tòu, thoroughly/through). Two synonyms stacked for force, used for light, sound, water, or radiation breaking all the way through a barrier. The technical word in physics and medicine for penetration.
穿衣	chuān yī	to put on clothes; to dress — 穿 (to thread through) + 衣 (yī, clothing). A verb-object pair, the plainest way to say "get dressed." 衣 is the general word for what covers the upper body, so 穿衣 frames dressing as passing the body into a garment.
穿越	chuān yu è	to pass through; to cut across; (popular fiction) to time-travel — Cutting through a region. Also the standard verb for popular time-travel fiction (穿越剧, 穿越小说) where a modern character is thrown into a past dynasty.
穿着	chuān zhe	wearing; dressed in (current state) — 穿 (to put on clothing) + 着 = the dressed state is in force. This is among the most natural uses of durative 着, because clothing occupies a position continuously. The same pattern applies to 戴着 (wearing a hat/glasses), 拿着 (holding), 带着 (carrying/bringing).
传播	chuán bō	to disseminate; to spread; to broadcast; communication — 传 chuán (to transmit) + 播 bō (to sow, to broadcast). To spread information, ideas, or culture widely: 传播知识 "to disseminate knowledge," 大众传播 "mass communication." Also the academic field name, 传播学 "communication studies."
传承	chuán chéng	to inherit and carry on
传达	chuán dá	to convey; to pass on (a message); to communicate — 传 chuán (to convey) + 达 dá (to reach, to arrive). To deliver a message, an order, or a feeling so that it reaches its target: 传达指示 "to convey instructions," 传达上级的精神 "to pass on the leadership's intent." Stresses that the content arrives intact.
传递	chuán dì	to pass; to relay; to convey; to transmit — 传 chuán (to pass on) + 递 dì (to hand over). To move something from one party to the next in a chain: 传递信息 "to relay information," 传递火炬 "to pass the torch." Stresses the act of handing along, often through several hands or steps.
传记	zhuàn jì	biography; a life story — 传 zhuàn (a handed-down record) + 记 jì (to record). A written account of a person's life: 名人传记 "celebrity biographies," 人物传记 "biographies of notable figures." Note the reading shifts to zhuàn here, never chuán.
传染	chuán rǎn	to infect; to be contagious; to spread (a disease) — 传 chuán (to transmit) + 染 rǎn (to dye, to be tainted). The spread of an illness from one person to another: 传染病 "infectious disease." Figuratively, an emotion or a mood that catches: 他的笑很有传染力 "his laugh is contagious."
传染病	chuán rǎn bìng	infectious disease — 传染 (chuánrǎn, to infect, to pass on) + 病: a disease that spreads from one person to another. It is the headline word during any epidemic and the category label on public-health notices.
传授	chuán shòu	to impart (knowledge or skill)
传说	chuán shuō	legend; folklore; that which is passed down — 传 chuán (to transmit; to pass on) + 说 shuō (speech; to speak; theory). A legend or oral tradition , stories that have been passed down through generations without fixed written form.
传统节日	chuán tǒng jié rì	traditional festival; heritage holiday — 传统 (traditional) + 节日 (festival, holiday). The inherited ritual calendar: 春节, 元宵节, 清明节, 端午节, 七夕节, 中元节, 中秋节, 重阳节. These are 传统节日 as opposed to 法定节假日 (statutory public holidays) like National Day.
传统文化	chuán tǒng wén huà	traditional culture; cultural heritage — 传统 (tradition) + 文化 (culture). The received deposit of Chinese civilization: classical literature, Confucian ethics, folk festivals, traditional arts and crafts, medicine, cuisine, and everything else that is "Chinese" by inheritance rather than by innovation.
传统医学	chuán tǒng yī xu é	traditional medicine; TCM — 传统 (traditional) + 医学 (medicine). The formal name for the system of Chinese medicine (中医 zhōngyī) as a body of inherited knowledge: acupuncture, herbal medicine, moxibustion, massage (推拿 tuīná), and the diagnostic frameworks of qi, yin-yang, and the five phases.
船舶	chuán bó	shipping, vessels (formal)
船票	chuán piào	boat ticket, ferry ticket — 船 (boat) + 票 (ticket): the fare for a passage by water, parallel to 车票 (bus/train ticket) and 机票 (plane ticket).
船员	chuán yuán	crew member, sailor
船长	chuán zhǎng	ship's captain — 船 (boat) + 长 (zhǎng, chief): the head of the ship. The same 长 head-of suffix gives 校长 (principal) and 队长 (team leader).
船只	chuán zhī	vessels, shipping (collective) — 船 (boat) + 只, here a classifier turned collective suffix. The pair names boats as a category rather than one hull, the way 车辆 names vehicles in bulk. Standard in port notices, news, and maritime law.
窗	chuāng	window
闯	chuǎng	to venture boldly into
创新	chuàng xīn	innovation
创业	chuàng yè	to start a business; to found a venture; to pioneer — 创 chuàng (to create; to initiate) + 业 yè (undertaking). To found something from the ground up, whether a company or a larger enterprise: 自主创业 "to start one's own business," 创业公司 "a startup." Carries the flavor of building where nothing stood before.
创作	chuàng zuò	to create (art)
吹牛	chuī niú	to brag, to talk big — 吹 (chuī, to blow) + 牛 (ox). From the full phrase 吹牛皮 (chuī niúpí, blow up an ox-hide), once said of herders inflating a hide skin-raft by lung power: big puff, little substance.
春分	Chūn fēn	Spring Equinox — balance of yin and yang — 春 chūn (spring) + 分 fēn (to divide; to share equally). The Spring Equinox: the day of perfect balance, when day and night are equal. In traditional cosmology, the moment when yin and yang are in exact equilibrium before yang takes the lead toward summer.
春风	chūn fēng	spring breeze; warmth, favor — 春 (spring) + 风 (fēng, wind). The mild wind of spring, and by extension any warm, reviving influence, a kind teacher, good news, a stroke of fortune. The phrase 春风得意 (chūnfēng déyì) describes riding high on success.
春季	chūn jì	spring quarter
春节祭祖	Chūn jié Jì zǔ	New Year Ancestor Offerings — The Lunar New Year (春节 Chūnjié) is the year's most important ritual occasion. Before the family feast on New Year's Eve, offerings of food, incense, and paper money are made to the ancestors (祭祖 jìzǔ). Firecrackers (鞭炮 biānpào) are set off to drive away evil spirits and welcome the new.
春节习俗	Chūn jié xí sú	Lunar New Year customs — Major customs of 春节: 贴春联 paste spring couplets on the door · 放鞭炮 set off firecrackers · 发红包 give red envelopes (现在多用微信红包) · 守岁 stay awake through New Year's Eve · 拜年 pay New Year visits · 吃饺子 eat dumplings (especially in North China, shaped like gold ingots 元宝).
春联	chūn lián	new-year couplets
春秋	Chūn qiū	the Annals; spring and autumn
春秋五霸	chūn qiū wǔ bà	the Five Hegemons
春天	chūn tiān	spring; springtime — 春 (spring) + 天 (tiān, day/season). The everyday word for the season, the one you reach for in conversation. 天 here marks a stretch of time rather than a single day, the same suffix in 夏天 (summer) and 冬天 (winter).
春天了	chūn tiān le	it's spring now; spring has arrived (no verb needed) — One of the most elegant features of sentence-final 了: it can attach directly to a noun phrase, without any verb, to announce that the situation has become that thing. This works because 了 marks change of state , and "it has become spring" is a state change even with no overt verb present.
春意	chūn yì	the feel of spring; signs of spring — 春 (spring) + 意 (yì, sense/intent). The atmosphere of spring in the air, the green budding and softening light that signal the season has arrived. 春意盎然 (chūnyì àngrán) describes a scene brimming with spring.
春游	chūn yóu	spring outing
春雨	chūn yǔ	spring rain
词典	cí diǎn	word dictionary — 词 cí (word; lyric; multi-character unit) + 典 diǎn (canonical reference). A dictionary organized alphabetically by pinyin or by topic that defines words and phrases rather than individual characters.
词句	cí jù	words and phrases; wording; phrasing — 词 cí (word) + 句 jù (phrase). Words and phrases taken together, that is, the wording of a text: 推敲词句 "to weigh one's wording carefully." It frames language at the level of phrasing rather than whole sentences.
词牌	cí pái	Tune pattern , the named musical template to which a 词 is written. Specifies the number of lines, the syllable count of each line, the tonal contour, and the rhyme scheme. Several hundred standard tune patterns exist in the classical repertoire.
词语	cí yǔ	words and phrases; expressions — 词 cí (word) + 语 yǔ (speech; phrase). A cover term for words and set phrases together, broader than 单词. Used when talking about vocabulary as a body of expressions.
祠堂	cí táng	ancestral hall — the lineage's center of gravity — The 祠堂 is architecturally distinct from both domestic and religious buildings. Typically facing south, with a courtyard (天井 tiānjǐng), successive halls of increasing sanctity, and a rear hall housing the ranked spirit tablets, the building's layout enacts a cosmological and social hierarchy simultan…
慈悲为怀	cí bēi wéi huái	to hold compassion in one's heart; compassionate by nature — 慈悲 (compassion) + 为 (to be; as) + 怀 (bosom; to hold in the heart). To carry compassion as one's fundamental orientation. Used to characterize people of consistently gentle, merciful temperament , those who reliably respond to others' suffering with care rather than indifference.
慈母	cí mǔ	loving mother; tender mother — 慈 (loving-kindness; tenderness) + 母 (mother). A classical compound appearing in the famous poem by Mèng Jiāo (孟郊, 751–814 CE): 慈母手中线，游子身上衣 , "In a loving mother's hands, thread; on a wandering son's body, clothing." 慈 here carries the full weight of gentle, attentive care.
慈善	cí shàn	charity; charitable; philanthropic — 慈 (loving-kindness) + 善 (goodness; virtue). The compound that carried 慈 from the religious register into modern social discourse. 慈善事业 (císhàn shìyè, charitable work), 慈善机构 (císhàn jīgòu, charitable organization), 慈善捐款 (císhàn juānkuǎn, charitable donation) are all standard modern compounds.
慈禧太后	Cí xī Tài hòu	Empress Dowager Cixi
辞让之心	cí ràng zhī xīn	the heart of deference and yielding — sprout of 礼 ritual propriety — 辞让 cí ràng: declining (an honor) and yielding (precedence to another). The capacity to defer , to put another's needs or claim before one's own in appropriate situations. The sprout of 礼 lǐ (ritual propriety).
此刻	cǐ kè	this very moment
此外	cǐ wài	apart from this; besides; furthermore — 此 cǐ (this) + 外 wài (outside). A formal written connector, "outside of this," used to add a further point: 此外，还有一点需要注意 "besides, there is one more point to note."
次	cì	time; occurrence
次要	cì yào	secondary; minor
葱	cōng	scallion; green onion; Chinese leek
葱烧海参	cōng shāo hǎi shēn	Scallion-Braised Sea Cucumber
聪明	cōng míng	clever; intelligent; sharp-minded — 聪 (cōng, sharp-eared; quick-witted , 耳 radical) + 明 (bright, clear-sighted , 日 sun + 月 moon). Intelligence as a compound of keen hearing and clear sight. 聪 alone originally meant "sharp of ear" in classical texts; combined with 明 (bright), it became the standard adjective for intelligence.
从此	cóng cǐ	from then on; henceforth — narrative turning point — 从 cóng (from) + 此 cǐ (this; here). 从此 is a narrative marker that signals a permanent change of state after a critical event. Common in stories, histories, and personal narratives: 从此他再也没有回来 "from then on, he never returned." 从此以后 is the fuller form.
从而	cóng ér	thereby; thus; and as a result — 从 cóng (following from) + 而 ér (and so). A written connective that draws a consequence: the second clause follows as a result of the first. Common in formal and academic prose.
从来	cóng lái	always; never (with negation); at all times up to now — 从 cóng (from; since) + 来 lái (coming; up to now). "Coming from the past all the way here," from some starting point up to the present moment. Almost always paired with negation: 从来不 (never; has never) / 从来没有 (never; has never happened).
从前	cóng qián	once upon a time; formerly; in the old days — 从 cóng (from; following) + 前 qián (front; before). "From the front / from the time that was in front": an even more remote and literary sense of "formerly" than 以前.
从容	cóng róng	calm and unhurried; composed
从现在起	cóng xiàn zài qǐ	from now on — A fixed frame meaning "starting from now." 从 (from) plus 现在 plus 起 (to start). It marks a turning point or a resolution: 从现在起，我要早睡 ("from now on, I'll go to bed early"). A close variant is 从现在开始, with 开始 (to begin) in place of 起.
粗鲁	cū lǔ	coarse; rude
村	cūn	village
皴法	cūn fǎ	Texture stroke technique — Texture stroke technique: the set of specialized brushwork methods used to render the surface of rocks and mountains. Different schools developed different 皴 techniques: axe-cut strokes 斧劈皴, hemp-fiber strokes 披麻皴, Mi dots 米点皴.
存在	cún zài	to exist; existence
错过	cuò guò	to miss (a chance, a train, a person) — 错 cuò (crosswise) + 过 guò (to pass). To pass by crosswise rather than meet: two things slip past each other and the moment is lost. Used for chances, trains, and people: 错过机会 "to miss a chance," 错过末班车 "to miss the last bus."
错误	cuò wù	mistake, error; wrong, mistaken — 错 cuò (wrong) + 误 wù (to err, mistake). Both a noun, the mistake itself, and an adjective, mistaken or incorrect: 犯了一个错误 "made a mistake," 错误的决定 "a wrong decision." The standard, slightly formal word for an error of fact or judgment.
错字	cuò zì	a wrongly written character; a typo — 错 cuò (wrong) + 字 zì (character). A character written incorrectly, whether mis-formed by hand or mistyped: 这篇作文有很多错字 "this essay has many wrong characters." A 错别字 cuòbiézì folds in 别字, the right-looking but wrong character.
达	dá	to reach; to convey; to attain
达赖喇嘛	Dá lài Lǎ mā	The Dalai Lama , the title of the head of the Gelug school of Tibetan Buddhism and, historically, the political leader of the Tibetan government. Understood as the successive incarnation of Avalokiteshvara. The current (Fourteenth) Dalai Lama, Tenzin Gyatso, has been in exile in India since 1959.
达摩	Dá mó	Bodhidharma — first patriarch; the "wall-gazing Brahmin" — The semi-legendary Indian founder. His defining image: nine years of wall-gazing at Shaolin, his arms falling off from holding them up in meditation (hence the armless Daruma dolls in Japan).
答	dá	to answer
答案	dá àn	answer; solution
答题	dá tí	to answer questions (on a test) — 答 dá (to answer) + 题 tí (question). To work through the items on an exam or quiz: 认真答题 "answer the questions carefully." Its mirror is 出题 chūtí, to set the questions.
答应	dā ying	to agree; to promise; to answer (a call) — 答 dā (to answer) + 应 yìng (to respond). To answer a request with consent: to agree or promise. Also the literal "to answer" when called: 我叫了半天他都没答应 (I called for ages and he never answered). Note the neutral tone on 应 here.
打电话	dǎ diàn huà	to make a phone call
打江山	dǎ jiāng shān	to conquer the realm; to win power through struggle — 打 dǎ (to strike; to fight; to build through effort) + 江山 (the realm). Always implies hard-won, usually military achievement , you do not inherit 江山, you take it. The character 打 carries physical force: the same verb used for punching, striking, and waging war.
打开	dǎ kāi	to open up (a closed object)
打破传统	dǎ pò chuán tǒng	to break with tradition; to challenge convention — 打破 (to break, to smash) + 传统 (tradition). The act of violating inherited norms deliberately. In modern usage, 打破传统 can be positive (innovative, forward-thinking) or negative (disrespectful, reckless) depending on context.
打扫	dǎ sǎo	to clean; to sweep
打算	dǎ suàn	to plan; to intend
打通	dǎ tōng	to get through (a call); to open up (a passage); to clear a blockage — 打 dǎ (to make; to strike; to do) + 通 (to get through). A resultative verb: to do something until it is through.
打样	dǎ yàng	to make a sample; sampling — The verb for producing samples before an order. 打样费 (dǎyàngfèi) is the sampling fee, often refunded against the first order; 打样周期 is how long sampling takes.
打折	dǎ zhé	to give a discount — 折 counts tenths of the price you pay, not the amount taken off. 八折 means you pay eight tenths, a 20% discount. Learners hear "eight" and think "80% off." In a negotiation 点 (diǎn, a percentage point) is more common than 折 for small moves.
打字	dǎ zì	to type; to type characters — 打 dǎ (to hit; to strike) + 字 zì. You are "striking" the characters into existence: the same 打 used in 打球 (play ball by striking it), 打电话 (make a phone call by striking a connection), 打鼓 (beat a drum).
大般若经	Dà bō rě jīng	the Great Prajñāpāramitā Sutra; the long form — The full Mahāprajñāpāramitā Sūtra, translated by Xuanzang in 600 fascicles, makes the Diamond and Heart Sutras look like fragments. The Heart Sutra is in fact a condensation of its core teaching. The long form unpacks emptiness across hundreds of contexts, dialogues, and lists.
大悲咒	Dà bēi Zhòu	Great Compassion Mantra
大乘	dà chéng	Mahayana — the Great Vehicle
大慈大悲	dà cí dà bēi	great loving-kindness, great compassion — the full bodhisattva quality — 大 (great) + 慈 (loving-kindness) + 大 (great) + 悲 (compassion). The epithet most closely associated with Guanyin: 大慈大悲观世音菩萨. The reduplication of 大 before each component gives it the character of a liturgical formula.
大道	dà dào	main road; grand way; the broad path — 大 dà (big; main; grand) + 道 (way; path). Physically: a wide, main thoroughfare. Philosophically: the grand way of moral order or heaven's intention.
大地	dà dì	the great earth; the land
大都	Dà dū	Dadu (Yuan capital, modern Beijing)
大多数 · 少数	dà duō shù shǎo shù	the majority · the minority — 大多数 (the great majority) and 少数 (the few) partition a set by proportion: 大多数人都同意 (most people agree), with the majority phrase taking 都 like any near-universal. 少数 appears in the fixed civic vocabulary 少数民族 (minority nationalities, China's official term for its 55 recognized non-Han groups).
大方	dà fang	generous; natural; poised; not stingy — 大 dà + 方 fāng (square; proper; generous). Having the big-square manner: poised, natural, generous, not petty. Used for both personality (generous with money) and bearing (natural and composed, not awkward). A high compliment in Chinese social contexts.
大夫	dài fu	doctor; physician (colloquial) — 大 dà + 夫 fū, but in this sense read dàifu with a neutral tone. A common, slightly informal word for a doctor, especially in northern speech: 看大夫 to see a doctor, 张大夫 Dr. Zhang. Read 大夫 dàfū instead and the same characters name a senior official of the old imperial court, a different word entirely.
大概	dà gài	approximately; probably; roughly — 大 dà (large, rough) + 概 gài (general outline; to level off). A rough outline: approximately, probably. One of the most natural hedges in spoken Mandarin: 大概三点 "around three o'clock."
大哥	dà gē	eldest brother; big bro — 大 (big) + 哥. Literally the eldest brother in the family. By extension it addresses the leader of a group, the boss of a gang, or any older man you want to flatter into helping you.
大哥大	dà gē dà	brick phone; top boss — 大哥 (big brother) + 大 again. First a nickname for the 1980s brick mobile phone, the status symbol of bosses; by extension the kingpin or undisputed number one in any field.
大海	dà hǎi	the sea; the open sea — 大 dà (big) + 海 hǎi (sea). The everyday word for the sea as a whole, the vast expanse of salt water. 大海捞针 "to fish a needle from the sea" is the Chinese "needle in a haystack."
大货	dà huò	bulk goods, the production run — Floor slang for the main order as opposed to samples. 大货生产 is bulk production, 大货样 a unit pulled from it. The word appears in nearly every merchandiser message: 大货已经上线了 means your order is on the line.
大家	dà jiā	everyone; everybody (warm, inclusive) — 大 dà (big) + 家 jiā (family; home). Literally "the big family," used for "everyone present" or "all of us." Much warmer and more conversational than 人们. The go-to word for addressing or including a present group: 大家好！(Hello everyone!).
大甲妈祖绕境	Dà jiǎ Mā zǔ Rào jìng	the Dajia Pilgrimage — one of the world's largest religious events — The annual Dajia Mazu Pilgrimage (大甲妈祖绕境进香) is a nine-day, eight-night procession covering approximately 340 kilometers through central and southern Taiwan, beginning from the Zhenglong Temple (镇澜宫 Zhènlán Gōng) in Dajia, Taichung.
大街	dà jiē	main street; avenue — 大 (big) + 街 (street). A broad, busy thoroughfare, the high street of a town. Pairs with 小巷 (xiǎoxiàng, narrow lane) in the set phrase 大街小巷, meaning every street and alley.
大姐	dà jiě	eldest sister; ma'am — 大 (big) plus 姐 marks the eldest of several sisters at home. On the street it doubles as a friendly, respectful way to address a woman somewhat older than the speaker.
大量	dà liàng	a large amount: a great quantity of — 大 dà (big) + 量 liàng (amount). Functions as a quantifier before mass and plural nouns: 大量资金 (a large amount of capital), 大量时间 (a great deal of time), 大量生产 (to mass-produce).
大人	dà rén	adult, grown-up
大声	dà shēng	loud; loudly — Big sound. Used adverbially before the verb: 大声说 (speak up), 大声朗读 (read aloud). Its opposite 小声 keeps the peace: 请小声一点，孩子睡了。Quiet please, the child is asleep.
大师	dà shī	master; grandmaster
大使馆	dà shǐ guǎn	embassy — 大使 dàshǐ (ambassador, lit. great envoy) + 馆. The ambassador's official house abroad. A smaller mission led by a consul is a 领事馆 lǐngshìguǎn, a consulate.
大势至	Dà shì zhì	Mahāsthāmaprāpta bodhisattva
大侠	dà xiá	great knight-errant; heroic wanderer; the wuxia protagonist archetype — 大 dà (great) + 侠 xiá (chivalrous fighter; knight-errant). The archetypal wuxia protagonist: skilled, wandering, honorable, righting wrongs without official sanction. The 大侠 has no fixed home , the entire 江湖 is their domain.
大小	dà xiǎo	size — Big-small: the size scale, the template for the whole antonym-compound family. 这双鞋大小正好 means the shoes fit exactly. Its siblings 长短 (length), 轻重 (weight), and 高矮 (height) work identically, and 轻重 doubles as "relative importance," what a tactless person 不知轻重 fails to weigh.
大笑	dà xiào	to laugh loudly
大兴安岭	Dà Xīng ān lǐng	Greater Khingan Mountains
大学	dà xu é	university; college — and the Confucian Great Learning — 大 dà (great; large) + 学 xué (learning). The modern term for university. But 大学 is also the title of one of the Four Books of Confucianism (四书 Sìshū) , the 大学 (Great Learning), a short text that outlines the program of self-cultivation leading from individual virtue to social order to universal peace…
大学 · 中庸	Dà xu é Zhōng yōng	Great Learning · Doctrine of the Mean — Originally chapters of the 礼记, elevated to independent classic status by Zhu Xi. 大学 Dàxué outlines the nested self-cultivation program (修身齐家治国平天下).
大义	dà yì	the greater cause; moral principle; overriding righteousness — 大 dà (great; large) + 义 yì (righteousness; moral duty). The righteousness that supersedes personal ties, individual interest, or clan loyalty. 大义灭亲 dàyì mièqīn = to put righteousness above family (a set phrase for reporting or condemning a family member for the sake of the greater moral order).
大于	dà yú	to be greater than; larger than — 大 dà (big) + 于 yú (than). The comparison frame "greater than," standard in maths and formal description: 收入大于支出 "income exceeds expenditure." Its mirror is 小于 xiǎoyú (less than).
大篆	dà zhuàn	Greater Seal Script , the broad category encompassing the varied script forms of the Zhou dynasty bronze inscriptions, Stone Drum text, and oracle bone script.
大字报	dà zì bào	big-character poster
大自然	dà zì rán	Nature (the natural world)
代办	dài bàn	to handle on behalf of; to do for another — 代 dài (on behalf of) + 办 bàn (to handle; to manage). To take care of a task or piece of business in someone else's stead: 代办手续 "to handle the formalities for someone," 代办签证 "to arrange a visa on another's behalf." The errand done in your name.
代表	dài biǎo	to represent; a representative; to stand for — 代 dài (on behalf of) + 表 biǎo (to express; outward). As a verb, to represent or stand for: 红色代表喜庆 "red stands for celebration." As a noun, the person who represents others: 班代表 "class representative," 人民代表 "people's deputy."
代工	dài gōng	contract manufacturing, OEM
代购	dài gòu	to buy on behalf of; a purchasing proxy — 代 dài (on behalf of) + 购 gòu (to purchase). To buy something for another person, often goods from abroad: 海外代购 "overseas purchasing proxy." A familiar modern word from the rise of cross-border shopping, where one traveler buys for many at home.
代价	dài jià	price paid, cost (in effort or loss) — 代 (to stand in for, substitute) + 价 (price). The cost something exacts, not in money but in effort, sacrifice, or loss. You pay a 代价 for a mistake, a victory, or a hard choice.
代理	dài lǐ	to act as agent; acting (in a post); agency — 代 dài (on behalf of) + 理 lǐ (to manage). To handle affairs in an official capacity for someone else: 代理人 "an agent," 代理商 "a distributor," 代理校长 "acting principal." As a modifier it marks a temporary or proxy holder of a post.
代替	dài tì	to replace; to substitute for; in place of — 代 dài (to stand in for) + 替 tì (to replace). The general verb for one thing or person taking another's place: 用机器代替人工 "to replace manual labor with machines." Often follows the pattern A 代替 B, "A in place of B."
怠慢	dài màn	to neglect; to slight a guest through inattention — 怠 dài (lazy, inattentive, slack) + 慢 màn (slow). To fail to attend to someone with the care and promptness they deserve. The standard word for hosting someone poorly or neglecting a guest's needs. Often appears in apologies: 怠慢了您, please forgive my poor hospitality.
贷款	dài kuǎn	loan, to grant a loan
戴	dài	to wear (accessories)
丹田	dān tián	the elixir field — the body's energy center — 丹 dān (cinnabar; red; elixir) + 田 tián (field). The "elixir field," the body's primary energy reservoir. Three locations: 下丹田 xià dāntián (lower, 3 inches below the navel, the primary center and focus of most practice) · 中丹田 zhōng dāntián (middle, the chest) · 上丹田 shàng dāntián (upper, the head).
单	dān	single; alone; simple
单词	dān cí	a single word; a vocabulary word — 单 dān (single) + 词 cí (word). One word taken on its own, the unit you memorize when studying a language. Common in 背单词 bèi dāncí, to memorize vocabulary.
单价	dān jià	unit price — The price per piece; 总价 (zǒngjià) is the total, 金额 (jīn'é) the amount on a line. The usual follow-up is what the unit price includes: 包括 (bāokuò) and 不包括 are the words to listen for.
单身汉	dān shēn hàn	a bachelor; a single man — 单身 dānshēn (single, unmarried) + 汉 hàn (man). An unmarried man living on his own. The 汉 here is purely the colloquial "man," with none of the heroic ring of 好汉.
担担面	dān dān miàn	Dan Dan Noodles
担当	dān dāng	to take on; to shoulder (responsibility); accountability — 担 dān (to carry) + 当 dāng (to take up a role). To take responsibility upon oneself; as a noun, the quality of being willing to do so. 有担当 yǒu dāndāng describes a person who steps up rather than dodging.
担心	dān xīn	to worry; to be concerned about — 担 dān (to carry on a shoulder pole; to bear a load) + 心 xīn (heart). Lit. "to carry the weight of the heart." Worry as a physical burden: the heart becomes heavy, something you haul around all day. One of the most common emotional verbs in everyday Mandarin.
但是	dàn shì	but; however
但愿人长久，千里共婵娟	Dàn yuàn rén cháng jiǔ , qiān lǐ gòng chán juān	Su Shi · 水调歌头 · Mid-Autumn 1076 — May people live long; across a thousand li, may we share this moon's beauty together. Written in 1076, when Su Shi had not seen his brother Su Zhe for seven years. 婵娟 (chánjuān) was a classical term for the beautiful full moon.
诞生	dàn shēng	to be born (grand things)
弹	tán	to play (an instrument)
弹性	tán xìng	elasticity; flexibility; springiness — 弹 tán (to spring, to bounce) + 性 xìng (property). The quality of stretching and springing back, literal in materials and figurative in arrangements: 弹性材料 "an elastic material," 弹性工作时间 "flexible working hours." Names a built-in capacity to flex without breaking.
蛋白	dàn bái	egg white; protein — 蛋 (egg) + 白 (bái, white): literally the white of the egg. In science it extends to protein in general, and 蛋白质 (dànbáizhì) is the standard word for dietary protein.
蛋糕	dàng āo	cake — 蛋 (egg) + 糕 (gāo, a steamed or baked flour cake): a cake gets its name from the eggs whipped into the batter. The word covers birthday cakes, sponge cakes, and the slice you buy at a café.
蛋黄	dàn huáng	egg yolk — 蛋 (egg) + 黄 (huáng, yellow): the yolk. It pairs neatly with 蛋白, the two halves of every egg named by their color. Salted yolk, 咸蛋黄 (xián dànhuáng), is a prized flavor in mooncakes and pastries.
蛋壳	dàn ké	eggshell
蛋挞	dàn tà	egg tart
当兵	dāng bīng	to be a soldier
当成	dàng chéng	to treat as
当代	dāng dài	contemporary era
当地	dāng dì	local; of the place in question — 当 dāng (right at) + 地 dì (place). "Of the very place" you are speaking about: 当地人 dāngdì rén (locals), 当地政府 dāngdì zhèngfǔ (the local government). The spatial twin of 当时.
当面	dāng miàn	face to face; to one's face; in person — 当 dāng (right at) + 面 miàn (face). To do something in someone's presence rather than behind their back: 当面说清楚 (sort it out face to face). Carries a note of directness and accountability.
当铺	dàng pù	pawnshop
当然	dāng rán	of course; naturally; certainly — 当 dāng (ought, fitting) + 然 rán (so). "As it should be," and so "of course." Stands alone as a full answer (当然！"of course!") or leads a clause: 当然可以 "certainly you may."
当时	dāng shí	at that time; then; at the time in question — 当 dāng (right at) + 时 shí (time). Points back to a specific past moment and the state of things in it: "at the time." Often paired with a later contrast, as in "back then I did not understand, but now I do."
当下	dāng xià	at this moment; right now; the present — 当 dāng (at; facing; this very) + 下 xià (this point in the downward flow of time). The present moment , the point where time has currently descended to. 当下 has a slightly elevated, philosophical register compared to 现在 (now), and appears frequently in Buddhist and reflective contexts.
当真	dàng zhēn	to take seriously; to take literally — 当 dāng/dàng (to treat as; regard as) + 真. To treat as real. Often used in the negative: 别当真 bié dàngzhēn "don't take it seriously," "I was just kidding."
当众	dāng zhòng	In public; in front of everyone (lit — In public; in front of everyone (lit. "at the mass"). The adverb that transforms many face-related situations. 当众批评 (dāngzhòng pīpíng, to criticize publicly) and 当众表扬 (dāngzhòng biǎoyáng, to praise publicly) are qualitatively different from the same acts done privately.
当作	dàng zuò	to treat as; to regard as; to take for — 当 dàng (to equate) + 作 zuò (to make, to do). To regard X as Y: 把困难当作机会 (treat difficulty as opportunity). A near-synonym is 当成 dàngchéng. Note the falling tone, which marks it apart from level-tone dāng.
刀工	dāo gōng	knife technique; knife skills
刀削面	dāo xiāo miàn	Knife-Shaved Noodles
捣蛋	dǎo dàn	to make trouble, to be naughty
到处	dào chù	everywhere; all over; in every place — 到 dào (arrive at) + 处 chù (place; location). Arriving at every place: ubiquity, pervasiveness. 到处都是 (there are ... everywhere) is one of the most common constructions. 到处找 (to look everywhere) = searching that has spread to every location.
到达	dào dá	to arrive; to reach (a destination) — 到 dào (arrive) + 达 dá (reach; attain). Both components mean roughly the same thing, making 到达 more emphatic and formal than 到 alone. Standard in written Chinese for scheduled arrivals: planes, trains, deliveries, expeditions. Spoken Chinese usually just says 到.
到底	dào dǐ	after all; in the end; (interrogative intensifier) — 到 dào (arrive at) + 底 dǐ (bottom; base). Arriving at the bottom: getting to the root of the matter, reaching the real answer. In interrogative sentences, 到底 intensifies the question and demands a genuine response , it presses the interlocutor to stop evading and give a straight answer.
到来	dào lái	arrival; advent; to arrive (literary) — 到 dào (arrive) + 来 lái (come). A literary and slightly elevated compound used for the arrival of things rather than people in motion: the advent of spring, the arrival of a new era, the coming of opportunity. Found in formal prose and news writing more than casual speech.
到现在	dào xiàn zài	up to now; so far — "Up to the present," used to measure a stretch reaching the present moment, often with 还 (still) or 都 to stress that something has or has not happened by now: 到现在他还没来 ("he still hasn't come, even now"). The fuller form 到现在为止 ("up to now") adds 为止 ("as the limit").
倒不如	dào bù rú	it would actually be better to (colloquial)
道德观	dào dé guān	moral outlook; values framework; one's view of what is right and wrong — 道德 (morality) + 观 guān (view; perspective; lens through which one sees things , the same 观 in 世界观 worldview, 价值观 value system, 人生观 life philosophy).
道德经	dào dé jīng	the Daodejing
道谛	dào dì	the truth of the path — the Noble Eightfold Path — 道 dào = way, path. Sanskrit magga. The prescription: the 八正道 bā zhèng dào (Noble Eightfold Path): right view, right intention, right speech, right action, right livelihood, right effort, right mindfulness, right concentration.
道法自然	dào fǎ zì rán	the Way models nature; the Way follows its own spontaneous pattern — 道 (the Way) + 法 (to model; to follow as a pattern) + 自然 (self-thus; spontaneous; natural). The culminating principle of Daodejing chapter 25. The Way does not follow anything outside itself , its law is its own spontaneous nature.
道观	dào guàn	a Daoist temple; a Daoist monastery — 道 dào (the Way; Daoism) + 观 guàn (temple). The temple of a Daoist community, where 观 takes its fourth-tone reading. Set against the Buddhist 寺 sì (monastery) and the general 庙 miào (shrine).
道家	Dào jiā	Daoism — the way of naturalness and non-action — The Daodejing 道德经 (attributed to Laozi, c. 4th–3rd century BCE) and Zhuangzi 庄子. Central concept: 无为 wú wéi, effortless action aligned with the natural order. The sage ruler governs so lightly that the people believe they govern themselves.
道教	dào jiào	Daoism
道理	dào lǐ	reason; logic; principle; the way things work — 道 dào (way; path) + 理 lǐ (principle; pattern; grain of wood, the inherent structure of things). "The logic of the path." The way things make sense: the reason behind an argument, the principle underlying a situation, the basic logic that should govern behavior.
道路	dào lù	road; path; way — 道 dào (way; path) + 路 lù (road; route). The most standard, neutral word for a road or path. Used freely in both spatial and metaphorical senses. 人生道路 (the road of life) is one of the most common metaphorical applications, at home in political speeches and personal memoirs alike.
道歉	dào qiàn	to apologize
道上的人	dào shàng de rén	underworld figures
道统	dào tǒng	orthodox transmission of the Way
道义	dào yì	moral duty; principle of right conduct; the Way and righteousness — 道 dào (the Way; the correct path; principle) + 义 yì (righteousness; moral duty). The pairing of the Way as principle and righteousness as conduct , what a person owes to the moral order. 道义上的责任 dàoyì shàng de zérèn = moral responsibility (as opposed to legal obligation).
稻草	dào cǎo	rice straw — 稻 dào (rice plant) + 草 cǎo (straw). The dried stalks left after rice is harvested, used for thatch, rope, and scarecrows. 最后一根稻草 "the last straw," the final small thing that breaks a back.
得到	dé dào	to obtain; to get
得气	dé qì	Arrival of qi, the sensation confirming correct needling — Arrival of qi, the sensation confirming correct needling: heaviness, distension, or a spreading warmth at the needled point. Classical and modern acupuncturists both treat déqì as the essential marker of effective treatment.
得罪	dé zuì	to offend, to cross someone — To acquire guilt before someone: the verb of social danger. The folk warning 宁得罪君子，不得罪小人 advises that a crossed gentleman forgives and a crossed petty man files it away, which is the whole word-field in one proverb.
德国	Dé guó	Germany — 德 (phonetic transcription of "Deutsch") + 国 guó (country). 德 is the standard phonetic carrier for "Deutsch" in country and language names: 德国 Germany, 德语 German language, 德文 German script.
德行	dé xíng	virtuous conduct; moral behavior; virtue as it shows in action — 德 dé (virtue; inner moral power) + 行 xíng (conduct; to walk; action). How virtue manifests in behavior , not the inner quality alone but the visible expression of it in how a person moves through the world. 德行 presupposes that genuine virtue is always legible: it shows.
德州扒鸡	Dé zhōu pá jī	Dezhou Braised Chicken
地藏	Dì zàng	Kṣitigarbha — guardian of hell and savior of the dead — Sanskrit Kṣitigarbha ("Earth Womb/Treasury"). The bodhisattva who specifically vowed to save beings in hell realms and not enter nirvāṇa until all hells are empty. Associated with Mount Jiuhua 九华山 in Anhui. His vow: 地狱不空，誓不成佛 (If the hells are not empty, I vow not to become a Buddha).
地点	dì diǎn	place; location; site; venue — 地 dì (ground) + 点 diǎn (point). A specific point on the ground, the precise spot where something happens: a meeting place, an event venue, the scene of an incident. More exact than 地方 dìfang.
地点焦点	dì diǎn jiāo diǎn	place focus — "it was [there] that…" — When the focused element is a place, the construction answers "where?" — again, about a past event both speakers know happened. The place phrase typically includes 在 and appears immediately after 是.
地方	dì fang	place; spot; (also) local, regional — 地 dì (earth; ground) + 方 fang. The all-purpose word for "place," from a physical spot to an abstract point in an argument (这个地方我不同意 "on this point I disagree"). With full tone, 地方 dìfāng means "local" as opposed to "central": 地方政府 local government.
地府	dì fǔ	the underworld; the realm of the dead — 地 (earth; ground) + 府 (government office; official residence). The underground realm of the dead, governed by 阎王 Yánwáng (Yama, the king of the underworld) and his bureaucracy.
地雷	dì léi	landmine — lit. "earth thunder" — 地 dì (earth; ground) + 雷 léi (thunder). Landmine , an explosive buried in the ground that detonates on contact. The compound is viscerally accurate: a mine hidden in the earth that erupts like thunder when triggered.
地名	dì míng	place names
地球	dì qiú	the Earth; the globe — 地 dì (earth) + 球 qiú (ball; sphere). Lit. "earth ball." The modern scientific term for the planet , a coinage that dates to the late 19th century, when Chinese translators had to name the heliocentric model.
地铁	dì tiě	subway
地图	dì tú	map — 地 dì (earth; ground) + 图 tú (diagram; picture; plan). Lit. "earth diagram." A transparent compound , the earth drawn out as a picture. 地图 is the standard word for any kind of map, from a hand-drawn sketch to a satellite navigation app.
地下	dì xià	underground; beneath the earth; covert; subterranean — 地 dì (earth; ground) + 下 xià (below; under). Literally: beneath the earth. Extended to mean "covert" or "underground" in the political sense , 地下组织 (underground organization), 地下工作 (underground/clandestine work). 地铁 dìtiě (subway; underground railway , lit. "underground iron") uses the same 地.
地形	dì xíng	terrain; topography; the shape of the land — 地 dì (ground; land) + 形 xíng. The shape of the land: terrain, topography, the rise and fall of the ground. 地形复杂 = the terrain is complex; 地形图 = a topographic map. The most literal of the situation-words, where the shape being read is the physical landscape itself.
地狱道	dì yù dào	the hell realm; the path of extreme suffering — The lowest of the six realms: extreme suffering as the karmic consequence of severe negative actions , violence, cruelty, deliberate harm.
地支	dì zhī	Earthly Branches
的 · 得 · 地	de	the three de's
灯光	dēng guāng	lamplight, lighting — 灯 (lamp) + 光 (guāng, light, glow): the light a lamp throws, the lighting of a room or stage. Where 灯 is the object, 灯光 is the glow itself.
灯火	dēng huǒ	lights (of a place at night) — 灯 (lamp) + 火 (huǒ, fire): the massed lights of a town or harbor seen at night, often in the literary phrase 灯火通明 (dēnghuǒ tōngmíng), brilliantly lit.
灯笼	dēng long	lantern (paper, festival) — 灯 (lamp) + 笼 (lóng, cage, basket): a flame or bulb held inside a frame of paper or silk. The red 灯笼 hung at the New Year and the Lantern Festival is its most familiar form.
登高	dēng gāo	to climb to high ground; to ascend — 登 dēng (to climb; to ascend; to mount) + 高 gāo (high; tall; elevated). The defining physical act of the festival. As a compound, 登高 appears broadly in Chinese outside the festival context , it can describe any ascent, or metaphorically, advancing in rank or achievement.
登山	dēng shān	mountaineering; to climb
等	děng	to wait
等候	děng hòu	to wait; to await — 等 děng (to wait) + 候 hòu (to await). A more formal, written-leaning word for waiting than plain 等. Common on signs and announcements: 请耐心等候 "please wait patiently."
等会儿	děng huì r	in a while; hold on
等于	děng yú	to equal; to be equivalent to; to amount to — 等 děng (equal) + 于 yú. States equality, literal or figurative: 二加二等于四 "two plus two equals four," 不说话等于同意 "saying nothing amounts to agreeing."
邓小平	Dèng Xiǎo píng	Deng Xiaoping — architect of reform
低矮	dī ǎi	low and short; squat; close to the ground — 低 (low) + 矮 (short, of stature or height). Used for structures rather than people: low-ceilinged rooms, squat walls, single-story buildings hugging the ground. 低矮的平房 (low-slung single-story houses) is a recurring image in descriptions of older hutong neighborhoods or rural architecture.
低沉	dī chén	low and deep (of sound); somber; heavy; low-spirited — 低 (low) + 沉 (to sink, heavy, deep). The compound sits at the intersection of acoustic and emotional: a 低沉的声音 is a deep, resonant voice that seems to come from somewhere down in the chest; a 低沉的心情 is a mood that has sunk below normal , subdued, heavy, not quite depressed but nowhere near cheerful.
低处	dī chù	low place; lower ground; a lower spot — 低 (low) + 处 (place, location). A low-lying spot or lower area. Practical and descriptive in geography, architecture, and flood-risk contexts: 低处积水 (water pooling in low-lying areas) is a standard phrase in weather reports.
低调	dī diào	low-key; understated; keeping a low profile — 低 (low) + 调 (key, register, tone , musical or social). Originally the low musical key; now the social posture of deliberate understatement. 低调做人 (carry yourself with a low profile) and 低调行事 (act without fanfare) are standard phrases in professional and political discourse.
低级	dī jí	low-level; crude; vulgar; base — 低 (low) + 级 (grade, level, rank). At the lower end of a scale , which in practice means either genuinely entry-level (低级职员, junior staff; 低级别, low rank) or, more pejoratively, crude or vulgar in taste and behavior: 低级趣味 (vulgar tastes), 低级错误 (dī jí cuòwù, a basic mistake , the kind you shouldn't mak…
低落	dī luò	low; down; dispirited; fallen in spirit — 低 (low) + 落 (to fall, to drop, to settle down). Mood or morale that has fallen and settled at a low point. 情绪低落 (qíngxù dīluò, spirits low) is the standard phrase for feeling deflated, discouraged, or quietly unhappy without necessarily being in crisis.
低人一等	dī rén yī děng	one rank below others; to consider oneself inferior — 低 (lower) + 人 (person, others) + 一等 (one grade, one rank). To be placed one step below others in status , or to place oneself there through shame or social conditioning.
低声	dī shēng	in a low voice; softly; in a whisper — 低 (low) + 声 (sound, voice). Speaking quietly, in a lowered voice. Appears as an adverb (低声说 , to say in a low voice) or a verb complement.
低头	dī tóu	to lower one's head; to bow; to yield — 低 (low, lower) + 头 (head). The single most loaded compound on this page. Physically, it is simply to look down , at a phone, at work, at the ground while walking.
低音	dī yīn	low pitch; bass; bass register — 低 (low) + 音 (sound, musical tone). The low end of the pitch spectrum , the bass register in music, the bottom frequencies in acoustics. 低音炮 (dīyīn pào, "bass cannon") is the colloquial term for a subwoofer. 低音提琴 (dīyīn tíqín) is the double bass. In vocal music, 低音歌手 is a bass singer.
滴滴	Dī dī	Didi ride-hailing — The main ride app, also usable inside Alipay and WeChat. Industrial zones have confusing addresses, so ask the factory to send a 定位 (dìngwèi, location pin) and set that as the destination. 上车点 (shàngchēdiǎn) is the pickup point.
敌人	dí rén	enemy
弟弟	dì di	younger brother
弟子	dì zǐ	disciple; student of a master — 弟 dì (younger brother) + 子 zǐ. The pupil who studies under a teacher and carries the lineage forward, used of philosophical, martial, and religious schools alike.
帝	dì	emperor (divine)
第二	dì èr	second; number two — 第 dì (ordinal) + 二 èr (two). The ordinal "second." The same pattern extends without limit: 第三 third, 第四 fourth, 第十 tenth. The number after 第 is just the ordinary cardinal, unchanged.
第二人称	dì èr rén chēng	second person
第几	dì jǐ	which (in order); what number in a series — 第 dì (ordinal) + 几 jǐ (how many; which). The question form that asks for a position rather than a quantity. The expected answer is always another 第 form: 第三 (the third), 第七 (the seventh).
第六感	dì liù gǎn	sixth sense
第三人称	dì sān rén chēng	third person
第一	dì yī	first; number one — 第 dì (ordinal) + 一 yī (one). The ordinal "first." Note the live tone-sandhi: 一 yī shifts to second tone before a fourth-tone syllable, so it is read dì-yí in 第一个 but stays dì-yī before a pause or another tone.
第一流	dì yī liú	first-rate; first-class; top-tier — 第一 dì-yī (first) + 流 liú (stream; class). Of the highest grade or quality: 第一流的大学 "a first-rate university." A close synonym of 一流 yīliú, with 第 adding a touch of formality.
第一人称	dì yī rén chēng	first person
颠倒乾坤	diān dǎo qián kūn	to overturn heaven and earth; to turn everything upside down; to invert the natural order — 颠倒 diāndǎo (to invert; to turn upside down; to have things reversed from their proper state). When the natural or moral order is inverted , the corrupt elevated, the worthy suppressed, right made to appear as wrong. Carries strong negative charge: something fundamental has been wrongly reversed.
点菜	diǎn cài	to order dishes (at a restaurant) — 点 diǎn (to choose; to dot) + 菜 cài (dish). To pick dishes off a menu and order them. The host usually does the 点菜 for the table. For the many senses of 点 see the 点 entry.
点火	diǎn huǒ	to ignite; to light a fire
点头	diǎn tóu	to nod (the head) — 点 diǎn (to touch lightly, to dip) + 头 tóu (head). To dip the head: to nod in agreement or greeting. 点头同意 "to nod in assent." Its opposite gesture is 摇头 yáotóu, to shake the head.
点心	diǎn xīn	Dim sum — Dim sum: small Cantonese dishes served with tea in the teahouse tradition. The term literally means "touch the heart." Dim sum culture grew from the Cantonese tea-drinking and commercial meeting tradition of Guangdong and Hong Kong and is now the most internationally recognized format of Chinese com…
点赞	diǎn zàn	To like (a post) — To like (a post): tap-approve. 赞 alone means to praise; 点赞之交 ("a likes-only friendship") names the acquaintance you never see but always like.
电灯	diàn dēng	electric light, lamp — 电 (diàn, electric) + 灯 (lamp): the everyday electric light or bulb. The default ceiling and desk light in a modern home, as opposed to candle or oil flame.
电话	diàn huà	telephone; a phone call — 电 diàn (electric) + 话 huà (speech). Speech carried on electricity, the telephone, and by extension the call itself: 打电话 dǎ diànhuà "to make a phone call," 接电话 "to answer the phone."
电汇	diàn huì	wire transfer (T/T) — "Electric remittance," usually just called T/T. 信用证 (xìnyòngzhèng) is a letter of credit (L/C), slower and more paperwork, with the bank standing between the parties.
电脑	diàn nǎo	Computer — Computer: electric brain. 台式电脑 is a desktop, 笔记本 a laptop. The formal 计算机 persists in degree names and government documents.
电影	diàn yǐng	film; movie — lit. "electric shadow" — 电 diàn (electric) + 影 yǐng (shadow; reflection; image). Film, movie , named for the projected shadows of early cinema. 看电影 = to watch a film. 电影院 = cinema. 电影节 = film festival (Cannes 戛纳电影节, Venice 威尼斯电影节, Berlin 柏林电影节, Hong Kong 香港电影节).
电影票	diàn yǐng piào	movie ticket: a ticket for a film screening — 电影 diànyǐng (film; movie) + 票 piào (ticket). A cinema ticket. The 票 here pulls double duty: it admits you to the hall and assigns you a seat. The takings a film earns at the box office are its 票房 piàofáng, literally the ticket room, the modern measure of a movie's success.
电子	diàn zǐ	electronic; electron — 电 diàn (electric) + 子 zǐ (small particle; child). As a physics term: the electron. As a prefix: electronic, digital. 电子产品 = electronics (products). 电子书 = e-book. 电子商务 = e-commerce (also shortened to 电商 diànshāng). 电子政务 = e-government. 电子竞技 = esports (also 电竞 diànjìng).
店	diàn	shop, store
调查	diào chá	to investigate; to survey; investigation — 调 diào (to direct, to send out) + 查 chá (to check, to examine). To look into a matter systematically, whether a police inquiry or a market survey. 市场调查 (market survey), 调查结果 (the findings of an investigation). Both verb and noun.
调动	diào dòng	to transfer; to mobilize; to arouse — 调 diào (to transfer) + 动 dòng (to move). To move a person to a new post, or to mobilize forces and feelings. 调动工作 (transfer to a new job), 调动积极性 (arouse enthusiasm). The sense is of marshalling people or energy toward a purpose.
调度	diào dù	to dispatch; to schedule; a dispatcher — 调 diào (to move, to transfer) + 度 dù (to measure, to manage). To dispatch and coordinate trains, vehicles, crews, or tasks; also the person who does it. 列车调度 (train dispatching), 调度中心 (control center). The work of directing many moving parts.
调和	tiáo hé	to harmonize; to blend
调节	tiáo jié	to regulate; to adjust; to balance — 调 tiáo (to adjust) + 节 jié (to control, to moderate). To keep a level or rhythm steady: 调节温度 (regulate the temperature), 空调 (air conditioning, literally "air regulator"), 自我调节 (self-regulation). The sense is ongoing balance rather than a single change.
调解	tiáo jiě	to mediate; to reconcile; mediation — 调 tiáo (to harmonize) + 解 jiě (to resolve, to untie). To step between two sides and settle their dispute. 调解纠纷 (mediate a dispute), 调解员 (a mediator). The social form of tiáo: bringing people, not flavors, into accord.
调理	tiáo lǐ	to regulate; to tonify
调料	tiáo liào	seasoning
调试	tiáo shì	to debug; to tune up
调停	tiáo tíng	to mediate a dispute
调味	tiáo wèi	to season; to flavor — 调 tiáo (to blend) + 味 wèi (flavor, taste). To season food, to bring its flavors into balance. The seasoning itself is 调料 tiáoliào or 调味品 tiáowèipǐn. The kitchen sense of tiáo: mixing tastes until they are right.
调整	tiáo zhěng	to adjust; to regulate; adjustment — 调 tiáo (to adjust) + 整 zhěng (to put in order). To set something right by careful change: 调整计划 (adjust the plan), 调整心态 (adjust one's attitude), 价格调整 (a price adjustment). The everyday word for fine-tuning anything.
调子	diào zi	tune; melody; tone (of voice or mood) — 调 diào (melody, pitch) + 子 zi (noun suffix). A tune or melody, and figuratively the pitch or mood of speech or writing. 唱调子 (carry a tune), 定调子 (set the tone, decide the direction). The everyday word for the shape of a sound.
掉	diào	away; off; done with (resultative)
跌落	diē luò	to plummet; to tumble down sharply — 跌 diē (to stumble, to fall sharply) + 落 luò. A more violent or dramatic fall than 落 alone , prices plummeting, a person tumbling. Common in financial and news contexts.
订单	dìng dān	order
订金	dìng jīn	advance payment
定语	dìng yǔ	attributive modifier
丢脸	diū liǎn	to lose moral face; to be shamed; disgraceful — More severe than 丢面子. 丢脸 involves an element of moral failure , betrayal, cheating, acting in ways that contradict one's stated principles. Can affect not just the individual but their family and group. The scope is broader and the damage more lasting than simple social embarrassment.
丢面子	diū miàn zi	to lose face; to be publicly embarrassed — 丢 diū (to lose; to throw away , the losing of something you once had). To lose face is not merely to feel embarrassed privately; it is to have one's standing visibly diminished in the eyes of others. The public, witnessed nature of face-loss is what makes it significant.
东北	dōng běi	Northeast; Manchuria — 东 (east) + 北 (north). In Chinese geography, 东北 almost always means Northeast China , the three provinces of Liaoning, Jilin, and Heilongjiang, corresponding roughly to historical Manchuria (满洲 Mǎnzhōu).
东道主	dōng dào zhǔ	host (of a banquet, event, or country) — 东 (east) + 道 (road; way) + 主 (master, host). From the Zuǒzhuàn (5th century BCE): the state of Zhèng, lying east of the Qin army's road, offered to supply and host the Qin troops. "East-road-master" became the word for host.
东方	dōng fāng	the east; the Orient — 东 dōng (east) + 方 fāng (direction). The eastern direction, and by extension "the East" as a cultural sphere. Its pair is 西方 xīfāng, the West. The four directions all take 方: 南方 the south, 北方 the north.
东林党	Dōng lín Dǎng	Donglin Partisans (reform faction)
东南西北	dōng nán xī běi	east, south, west, north
东西南北	dōng xī nán běi	the four directions
东周	Dōng Zhōu	Eastern Zhou
冬	dōng	winter
冬天	dōng tiān	winter
冬至	dōng zhì	Winter Solstice — the turning of yin into yang — Winter Solstice, the most important of the twenty-four solar terms, celebrated as a major festival throughout Chinese history. Marks the shortest day and the nadir of the yin/yang cycle, from which point yang begins its return. Dumplings are eaten in the north; tangyuan in the south.
懂得	dǒng de	to know how, to appreciate — 懂 (understand) plus the resultative 得 (de). It reaches past facts toward practical wisdom: knowing how to do a thing, or appreciating its value.
懂事	dǒng shì	sensible, mature — 懂 (understand) plus 事 (shì, affairs, the way things work). Said of a child or young person who grasps how to behave, reads a room, and considers others. High praise from a parent.
懂行	dǒng háng	to know the trade — 懂 (understand) plus 行 (háng, a trade or line of work). To be expert in a field, to know its ins and outs as an insider rather than an outsider.
动词	dòng cí	verb — 动 dòng (move; act) + 词 cí (word). The "moving-word," a verb: a word for an action or state. 吃, 走, and 喜欢 are all 动词.
动词 + 过	guo	past experience, "have ever done" — 过 marks that the subject has undergone the experience at least once, at some indefinite point. 我去过中国 (I have been to China) says nothing about when; it registers the experience itself. Contrast 我去了中国, a specific completed trip. Negation keeps 过 but uses 没: 我没去过中国 (I have never been to China).
动词 + 了	le	completed action, the closest thing to a past tense — 了 after the verb marks the action as completed. With a past time word it produces what English renders as simple past: 我昨天买了一本书 (I bought a book yesterday). But 了 marks completion, not pastness: 明天吃了饭再走 (leave tomorrow after finishing dinner) puts 了 in a future clause.
动词 + 一下	dòng cí + yí xià	verb + 一下 — do briefly / casually — Placed right after the verb, 一下 makes the action quick, light, and informal: 看一下 ("have a look"), 想一下 ("think for a sec"), 试一下 ("give it a go"). It softens commands into friendly requests and lowers the stakes of an action.
动词 + 着	zhe	sustained state: the door stands open — 着 marks a state that holds: 门开着 (the door is open), 墙上挂着一幅画 (a painting hangs on the wall), 他穿着一件红衣服 (he's wearing a red shirt). Where 在 tracks an activity unfolding, 着 freezes the result and holds it. The contrast is sharpest with verbs like 穿: 他在穿衣服 (he is putting clothes on) vs.
动功	dòng gōng	moving qigong; forms with coordinated movement and breath — 动 dòng (moving; active) + 功 gōng (practice). Qigong with movement, from slow and gentle forms like 八段锦 Bāduànjǐn (Eight Brocades) and 五禽戏 Wǔqínxì (Five Animals) to more vigorous practices. Movement is synchronized with breath and guided by awareness.
动机	dòng jī	motivation; driving motive
动力	dòng lì	driving force; motive power; motivation — 动 dòng (to move) + 力 lì (force). The force that drives motion. Literal in machines, where it names motive power, and figurative in people, where it names the motivation that keeps one moving toward a goal.
动量词	dòng liàng cí	verbal measure word (次/遍/回)
动气	dòng qì	to get riled up; to let anger stir (colloquial) — 动 dòng (to move; to stir) + 气 qì. For qi to be "moved," to let irritation become active anger. Often used in negative advice: 别动气 "don't get worked up." The qi-as-fluid logic is explicit: something causes the qi to shift and agitate.
动物	dòng wù	animal — 动 dòng (to move) + 物 wù (thing). The "moving things," the animals, set against 植物 the "planted things," the plants. 动物园 dòngwùyuán is the zoo, the "animal garden."
动作	dòng zuò	movement; action; gesture; motion (of the body) — 动 dòng (to move) + 作 zuò (to do; to make). A single physical movement or gesture: 这个动作很难 "this move is hard," 动作快一点 "move faster." Also "action" in film: 动作片 dòngzuòpiàn (action movie).
洞	dòng	hole
都 , 全体	dōu , quán tǐ	都: every member of a group — simultaneous coverage — 都 applies a single predicate to all members of a group at the same time. The group is fixed; 都 says no member is excluded from the statement. Works with pronouns (我们, 他们, 大家), explicit quantifier phrases (三个人, 所有人), and fronted question words (什么, 谁, 哪儿).
都江堰	Dū jiāng yàn	Dujiangyan irrigation — Dujiangyan , the 3rd-century BCE irrigation system on the Minjiang River, still operational. 都 refers to the capital; 江 is river; 堰 (yàn) means weir or low dam. The system controls water through geometry rather than barriers, making it the oldest functioning hydraulic infrastructure in the world.
都市	dū shì	metropolis
都塔尔	dū tǎ ěr	the dutar — Uyghur long-necked lute — The dutar (都塔尔 dūtǎ'ěr, from Persian do-tar meaning "two strings") is the emblematic instrument of Uyghur music: a long-necked lute with two strings, pear-shaped body, and a thin, resonant tonal quality with long-sustained notes.
豆瓣酱	dòu bàn jiàng	broad bean chili paste; Pixian paste — Pixian fermented broad-bean-and-chili paste , the foundational flavoring of Sichuan cooking. Produced in Pixian County outside Chengdu by fermenting broad beans and chilies for months to years. Almost every Sichuan dish begins by frying doubanjiang in oil until the red oil (红油 hóng yóu) releases.
豆腐	dòu fu	tofu — a cross-cuisine staple appearing in nearly every regional school
豆腐干	dòu fu gān	Dried Tofu; Firm Pressed Tofu
豆腐脑	dòu fu nǎo	tofu pudding; soft tofu in brine
豆浆	dòu jiāng	soybean milk
逗	dòu	to tease; to amuse
毒害	dú hài	to poison, harm
毒性	dú xìng	Toxicity, one of the key properties documented for each drug in the Bencao Gangmu. Li distinguished between non-toxic (无毒), slightly toxic (小毒), moderately toxic (有毒), and highly toxic (大毒) substances, with corresponding cautions about dosage, preparation, and contraindications.
独唱	dú chàng	solo (vocal)
独立思考	dú lì sī kǎo	independent thinking; critical thought
独生子女	dú shēng zǐn ǚ	Only child (lit — Only child (lit. "alone-born child-female" , zǐnǚ covers both sexes). The defining identity category for the urban Chinese generation born 1980–2015 under the one-child policy. 独生子 (dúshēng zǐ) is a son; 独生女 (dúshēng nǚ) is a daughter.
读懂	dú dǒng	to read-and-understand; to truly grasp — 读 dú + 懂 dǒng (to understand). A resultative compound: read-to-the-point-of-understanding. Used both literally (read a difficult text and grasp it) and metaphorically (read a person and truly know them).
读书	dú shū	to read; to study; to attend school — 读 dú + 书 shū (book). Three overlapping senses: (1) the literal act of reading, (2) studying for school or self-improvement, (3) attending an educational institution. 他在北京大学读书 "He's a student at Peking University." Closer to "to be in school" than English "to read books" suggests.
读音	dú yīn	pronunciation (of a character or word) — 读 dú + 音 yīn (sound). Literally "the read-sound" , the conventional pronunciation of a written character. The standard reference in dictionaries: 这个字的读音是 zhège zì de dúyīn shì… "the pronunciation of this character is…"
读者	dú zhě	reader (of a publication); readership — 读 dú + 者 zhě (one-who). The standard term for the reader of a book, magazine, or newspaper. 亲爱的读者 "Dear reader" is the conventional opening for a letter from the editor.
堵	dǔ	to block; to stop up; congested
杜甫	Dù Fǔ	Tang dynasty poet
肚子	dù zi	belly, stomach
度量	dù liàng	tolerance; magnanimity
度量衡	dù liàng héng	measures of length, capacity, and weight — Qin issued empire-standard weights and measures, with bronze and iron reference units distributed to local administrators. Surviving Qin weights are stamped with the imperial decree authorizing them. Tax assessment, grain storage, and commercial transactions across the empire used a single system.
度众生	dù zhòng shēng	to liberate sentient beings
渡船	dù chuán	ferry
端午节	duān wǔ jié	Dragon Boat Festival — Dragon Boat Festival , celebrated on the fifth day of the fifth lunar month. Commemorates Qu Yuan's drowning in the Miluo River. Marked by dragon boat races and the eating of 粽子 zòngzi (sticky rice dumplings wrapped in bamboo leaves).
端砚	duān yàn	Duan inkstone, from Duanxi in Guangdong province — Duan inkstone, from Duanxi in Guangdong province. The finest inkstone in the traditional Chinese hierarchy. Prized for its fine grain (described as 嫩 nèn, tender), water retention, and the distinctive circular "eyes" 石眼 that appear in the stone and are incorporated into decorative designs.
端正	duān zhèng	upright; proper
短处	duǎn chu	shortcoming, weak point — 短 (deficient) + 处 (chù, point, place): a point where one falls short, a flaw or weakness. The abstract sense of 短 made concrete as a named fault.
短发	duǎn fà	short hair — 短 (short) + 发 (fà, hair): hair worn short. Contrasts directly with 长发 (chángfà, long hair).
短歌行	Duǎn Gē Xíng	"Short Song" — Cao Cao's most famous poem — A banquet poem on the shortness of life and the hunger to draw great men into one's service. Its opening, 对酒当歌，人生几何 ("facing the wine one should sing; how much is a human life?"), is a stock quotation for the fleetingness of time.
短裤	duǎn kù	shorts — 短 (short) + 裤 (kù, trousers): trousers cut short above the knee. The most literal modern use of the length sense.
短跑	duǎn pǎo	sprint, short-distance run
短片	duǎn piàn	short film, clip
短期	duǎn qī	short term, short period — 短 (short) + 期 (qī, period): a short span of time. Common in finance, planning, and study, opposite of 长期 (chángqī, long term).
短缺	duǎn qu ē	shortage, scarcity — 短 (deficient) + 缺 (quē, lacking): a shortage of supply, when something runs short of what is needed.
短信	duǎn xìn	text message; SMS — 短 duǎn (short; brief) + 信 xìn (word; letter; communication). A short letter. The compound maps the ancient sense of 信 as written communication directly to the mobile-phone era , there was no borrowing from English, no phonetic transcription, just a classical compound structure applied to a new techn…
短暂	duǎn zàn	brief, fleeting, transient — 短 (short) + 暂 (zàn, temporary): short and passing, said of moments, lives, or encounters that do not last.
段落	duàn luò	paragraph
断	duàn	to break; to sever; to cut off
断流	duàn liú	Flow interruption — Flow interruption: the failure of the river to reach the sea due to upstream water extraction. The Yellow River experienced 断流 in 22 of the 28 years between 1972 and 1999, a crisis that triggered national water management reform.
锻炼	duàn liàn	to exercise, to forge
队伍	duì wǔ	ranks, procession, contingent — 队 (formed group) + 伍 (a file of five soldiers, the old squad). The pairing names a body of people standing or moving in order, from a literal military column to a figurative workforce or movement.
队形	duì xíng	formation, marching shape — 队 (formed group) + 形 (shape). The geometric pattern a group holds while moving: a wedge of soldiers, a flying V of geese, a defensive line on the pitch.
队员	duì yuán	team member, squad member — 队 (team) + 员 (member, a person filling a role). Any rank-and-file member of a team: a player, a scout, a rescue worker.
队长	duì zhǎng	captain, team leader — 队 (team) + 长 (chief, the elder/head). The one at the front of the column: a sports captain, a squad leader, the head of a work brigade.
对不对	duì bu duì	right? correct? — The teacher's tag and the explainer's checkpoint, punctuating reasoning the way English "right?" does. The listener's murmured 对对对 in reply is the sound of a Chinese conversation agreeing with itself.
对不起	duì bu qǐ	I'm sorry — lit. "I cannot face you" — 对 duì (to face; toward) + 不起 buqǐ (cannot rise to; cannot face). Literally "I cannot face you," an expression of guilt and apology for having let someone down. The response is 没关系 méi guānxi (it doesn't matter) or 没事 méishì (no problem).
对方	duì fāng	the other party; the opposite side — 对 duì (facing; opposite) + 方 fāng (party). The other side in a conversation, negotiation, contest, or relationship. Its pairs are 双方 shuāngfāng (both parties) and 我方 wǒfāng (our side).
对话	duì huà	dialogue; conversation; to hold talks — 对 duì (facing; mutual) + 话 huà (speech). Speech exchanged face to face. Concrete dialogue in a textbook or film, and figurative dialogue between sides: 和平对话 "peace talks."
对了	duì le	Oh right! — used when remembering something — 对 duì (correct) + 了 le (change of state). "Oh right!", used to introduce something you just remembered or to redirect a conversation. Not about correctness in this use: 对了 at the beginning of a sentence is a discourse marker meaning "Oh! I just remembered…" or "Wait, by the way…".
对立	duì lì	opposition; contradiction
对联	duì lián	Spring Festival couplets
对嘛	duì ma	right, obviously / that's it
对我来说	duì wǒ lái shuō	"For me" or "as far as I'm concerned — "For me" or "as far as I'm concerned." Frames a statement as true from your perspective without claiming it is universally true. Often paired with adjectives of preference or difficulty: 对我来说, 这个太难了 ("for me, this is too hard").
对于	duì yú	regarding; with regard to; as for — 对 duì (toward) + 于 yú (to). Introduces the thing an opinion or attitude is directed at: 对于这个问题 "regarding this question." It heads the topic of a sentence, much like English "as for."
对仗	duì zhàng	Tonal and semantic parallelism , the requirement that paired couplet lines mirror each other grammatically, semantically, and tonally.
兑换	duì huàn	to convert (currency) — 兑 (to cash, redeem) + 换 (swap). The formal term for currency conversion or redeeming points and vouchers. What 换钱 says casually, 兑换 says on the signboard.
敦煌	Dūn huáng	Dunhuang
顿悟	dùn wù	sudden enlightenment
多大	duō dà	how old; how big
多多少少	duō duō shǎo shǎo	more or less; to some extent — The question word reduplicated into a hedge: some amount on the scale, unspecified but nonzero. It softens claims politely, which makes it a favorite of careful speakers: everyone at the meeting 多多少少 had objections, though few voiced them.
多亏	duō kuī	thanks to; fortunately (because of) — 多 (much) + 亏 (kuī, to be indebted to, to owe). "Much-owed-to" , fortunately thanks to X. Expresses relief that something good or fortunate happened because of another person or circumstance: 多亏你帮忙，要不然就麻烦了 (thanks to your help, otherwise it would have been a problem). Colloquial and warm in register.
多么	duō me	how; what a (exclamatory) — 多 (how much) + 么 (question/exclamation suffix). An exclamatory intensifier: 多么美啊 (how beautiful!); 多么幸运 (how lucky). More literary than colloquial 多 alone. Used in song lyrics, poetry, and formal speeches to express admiration or strong feeling.
多少钱	duō shao qián	how much (money); what's the price — The universal way to ask a price. Because cost can be any amount, you use 多少, never 几: 这件衣服多少钱？("how much is this piece of clothing?"). The answer uses money measure words: 块 kuài (yuan, colloquial), 毛 máo (ten cents), 分 fēn (cent). 一共多少钱 ("how much altogether") totals a bill.
多元	duō yuán	diverse; pluralistic; multi-element — 多 (multi-) + 元 (yuan, element, source, unit). Multi-source, diverse in origin. 多元文化 (multiculturalism); 多元社会 (pluralistic society); 多元经济 (diversified economy).
多云	duō yún	cloudy (weather forecast)
鹅	é	goose
恶	è	evil, wicked
饿	è	hungry
饿鬼	è guǐ	hungry ghost — a being trapped in a realm of insatiable craving — 饿 è (hungry; starving) + 鬼 guǐ (ghost; spirit). One of the six realms of Buddhist cosmology (六道 liùdào): hungry ghosts have enormous bellies and throats too narrow to swallow, condemned to perpetual craving without satisfaction: the post-mortem form of greed.
饿鬼道	è guǐ dào	the hungry ghost realm; the path of insatiable craving — 饿鬼 è guǐ (hungry ghosts): beings with enormous appetites and tiny throats , perpetually starving, unable to consume enough to be satisfied. In Buddhist iconography they are depicted as emaciated figures with swollen bellies and needle-thin necks.
鄂伦春族	È lún chūn zú	the Oroqen — northern China's forest hunters — The Oroqen (鄂伦春族 Èlúnchūn zú , from Tungusic words meaning "people who use reindeer" or "people on the mountain ridge") are among the smallest ethnic groups in China, with a population of approximately 9,000, concentrated in Inner Mongolia and Heilongjiang province.
鄂温克族	È wēn kè zú	the Evenki — reindeer herders of the forest steppe — The Evenki (鄂温克族 Èwēnkè zú , "people who live on mountains covered with forests") are the most widely distributed Tungusic people, with populations in China (approximately 30,000, concentrated in Inner Mongolia and Heilongjiang), Russia, and Mongolia.
恩德	ēn dé	kindness received; an unrepayable debt of gratitude — 恩 ēn (favor; kindness; grace) + 德. Kindness so substantial it counts as virtue. Used for a debt of gratitude that runs deeper than ordinary thanks: a teacher who shaped one's life, a friend who saved one in a crisis.
恩怨	ēn yuàn	debts and grievances
儿歌	ér gē	nursery rhyme — 儿 (child) plus 歌 (song). A children's song, the first 歌 most native speakers ever learn.
儿童	ér tóng	children (formal)
儿子	ér zi	son — 儿 ér (child, son) + 子 zi (suffix). The standard word for a son. Its pair is 女儿 nǚ'ér (daughter), which keeps 儿 but not 子.
而后	ér hòu	thereafter; and only then; after that — 而 ér (and) + 后 hòu (after). Orders one event strictly after another in a literary register: do A, 而后 do B. Lives on in the maxim 三思而后行 "think thrice, then act."
而是	ér shì	but rather; but instead — 而 ér (but) + 是 shì (is). The corrective half of the 不是…而是… frame: it rejects a wrong claim and supplies the right one. 不是他，而是我 "it was not him, but rather me."
而已	ér yǐ	that is all; nothing more; merely — 而 ér (and) + 已 yǐ (to stop; only). A sentence-final frame that caps a statement with "and nothing more," often softening or downplaying. Frequently paired with 只是 zhǐshì (merely) earlier in the clause.
耳朵	ěr duo	ear (everyday word) — 耳 (ear) + 朵 (duǒ, flower; measure word for flowers, ears, and clouds). 朵 is the measure word for ear-shaped objects; 耳朵 is the unmarked everyday word for the ear as a physical body part. 一只耳朵 or 一个耳朵 are both acceptable, but 耳朵 alone typically refers to the ear without a number.
耳机	ěr jī	earphones; headphones — 耳 (ear) + 机 (machine; device). The device for the ear. 入耳式耳机 (in-ear earphones), 头戴式耳机 (over-ear headphones). A modern compound that keeps 耳 as its anchor, illustrating how the character's body-part meaning extends naturally to technology.
耳目	ěr mù	ears and eyes; spies; awareness — 耳 (ear) + 目 (eye). The two organs of alert perception together , the paired sentinels. Used figuratively for one's information network, or for spies and informants: 皇帝的耳目 (the emperor's ears and eyes). Also in 耳目一新 ěrmù yīxīn (a completely fresh impression, as if new eyes and ears).
耳闻	ěr wén	to have heard of; auditory knowledge — 耳 (ear) + 闻 (to hear; to be known). To have heard of something, often at a distance or through others.
二哥	èr gē	second eldest brother — 二 (two) + 哥. The second of the brothers, ranked after 大哥. The numbering system is literal: each older brother is addressed by his birth order, never by name within the family.
二胡	èr hú	erhu; two-stringed bowed fiddle — 二 èr (two) + 胡 hú (a category of instruments of non-Han origin, literally "barbarian"). The erhu has exactly two strings, which defines both its name and its voice: the two strings produce the characteristic close-interval resonance that makes the instrument sound like a human throat.
二话	èr huà	a second word; objection; demurral — 二 èr (two; a second) + 话 huà (speech; words). Always appears in the negative: 没有二话 or 不说二话 means to comply without objection or complaint, without a second word.
二十八宿	èr shí bā xiù	The 28 Lunar Mansions — The 28 Lunar Mansions: the primary coordinate system of Chinese astronomy, dividing the ecliptic and celestial equator into 28 unequal stations corresponding to the moon's daily progress around the sky.
二手	èr shǒu	second-hand; used; pre-owned — 二 èr (two; second) + 手 shǒu (hand). Something that has passed through a second hand. Standard term for used goods, pre-owned vehicles, and resale markets.
二维码	èr wéi mǎ	QR code
二元论	èr yuán lùn	dualism; dualist philosophy — 二 èr (two) + 元 yuán (origin; element; primary unit) + 论 lùn (theory; discourse). The philosophical position that reality is composed of two irreducible substances or principles. 二元 èryuán alone means "dual; binary; two-dimensional" and appears across technical and philosophical contexts.
二月	È r yu è	February; the second month — 二 èr (two) + 月 yuè (moon; month). Chinese months are purely positional: the first month is 一月, the second is 二月, through 十二月 (December). There are no separate month names.
发表	fā biǎo	to publish; to issue; to express (publicly) — 发 fā (to issue) + 表 biǎo (to express; surface). To put a piece of writing or an opinion out into the public sphere: 发表文章 "to publish an article," 发表意见 "to voice an opinion."
