发财	fā cái	To get rich, to make money — To get rich, to make money: the aspiration whose first syllable 发 (fā) sounds like 八 (bā, eight) in Cantonese, giving eight its commercial prestige. 恭喜发财 (gōngxǐ fācái, "congratulations and may you prosper") is the standard Spring Festival greeting.
发出	fā chū	to give off; to emit; to issue (a sound, light, order) — 发 fā (to emit) + 出 chū (out). To let something out from a source: a sound, a smell, a light, a command. 发出声音 "to make a sound," 发出命令 "to issue an order."
发慈悲	fā cí bēi	to show mercy; to bring forth compassion (colloquial) — 发 fā (to bring forth; to emit) + 慈悲. The colloquial register for asking someone to be merciful, lenient, or helpful. The religious vocabulary of 慈悲 serves as the vehicle for a thoroughly everyday request. Tone determines whether the phrase is earnest, playful, or ironic.
发电	fā diàn	to generate electricity
发红包	fā hóng bāo	to send a red envelope; to distribute lucky money
发火	fā huǒ	to lose one's temper; to flare up (contrast) — 发 fā (to emit; to break out) + 火 huǒ (fire). Not a 气 compound, but essential for contrast. Where 生气 is quiet simmering anger, 发火 is the eruption: raised voice, visible emotion, the moment qi becomes fire. Colloquial. Very common in domestic and workplace situations.
发酵	fā jiào	oxidation (lit. "fermentation") — the enzymatic browning step
发劲	fā jìn	issuing force — explosive power release
发明	fā míng	to invent
发票	fā piào	official receipt: an invoice; a tax receipt — 发 fā (to issue; to send out) + 票 piào (slip; note). The official, tax-registered receipt a seller issues for a purchase. In China the 发票 is a regulated document used for reimbursement and tax, very different from a plain shop receipt (小票 xiǎopiào).
发热	fā rè	to run a fever; to give off heat — 发 (fā, to emit) plus 热 (heat): to give off heat. Of a body it means to run a fever; of a machine it means to overheat. The clinical register on a thermometer reading or a hospital chart.
发烧	fā shāo	To run a fever — To run a fever: emit burn. 量体温 is to take a temperature; 退烧 is the fever breaking, and 退烧药 the medicine that breaks it.
发生	fā shēng	to happen; to occur; to take place — 发 fā (to send forth) + 生 shēng (life; to arise). For an event to come into being: 发生事故 "an accident happens," 发生变化 "a change occurs." Used of events, not of people deliberately doing things.
发送	fā sòng	to send; to transmit; to dispatch — 发 fā (to send out) + 送 sòng (to deliver). To send a message, signal, or shipment outward to a recipient: 发送邮件 "to send an email." The everyday verb for transmitting in the digital age.
发现	fā xiàn	to discover; to find; to realize — 发 fā (to bring forth) + 现 xiàn (to appear; manifest). To bring something previously hidden into view, whether a fact, an object, or a realization: 发现问题 "to spot a problem," 科学发现 "a scientific discovery."
发音	fā yīn	pronunciation; to pronounce — 发 (emit) + 音 (sound). The compliment every learner works for is 你的发音很标准 ("your pronunciation is very standard"), 标准 being the operative cultural value: the broadcast-Mandarin ideal against which accents are measured.
发愿	fā yuàn	making vows — aspiring to rebirth with sincere intention — 发 fā (to make; to express) + 愿 yuàn (vow; aspiration; wish). The devotional expression of one's sincere desire to be reborn in the Pure Land.
发展	fā zhǎn	to develop; to grow; development — 发 fā (to bring forth) + 展 zhǎn (to unfold; spread). To grow and expand from a smaller or earlier state: 经济发展 "economic development," 发展中国家 "a developing country." Works as both verb and noun.
发展方向	fā zhǎn fāng xiàng	direction of development — Policy and business vocabulary. 行业的发展方向 (the industry's direction of development), 国家的发展方向 (the country's direction of development). Often paired with verbs of pointing or indicating: 明确发展方向 (to clarify the direction).
法官	fǎ guān	judge
法国	Fǎ guó	France
法家	Fǎ jiā	Legalism — governance through law, reward, and punishment — The philosophy that conquered China. Key theorists: 商鞅 Shāng Yāng (architect of Qin's reforms), 申不害 Shēn Bùhài (on administrative technique), 慎到 Shèn Dào (on positional power), and 韩非子 Hán Fēizǐ (synthesizer of all three).
法律	fǎ lǜ	law; statute; legislation — 法 fǎ (law) + 律 lǜ (rule; statute). The body of law as a system, the formal rules a state enacts and enforces. 遵守法律 "to obey the law," 法律问题 "a legal question."
法人	fǎ rén	legal representative
法师	fǎ shī	Dharma master; a learned monk or nun — 法 fǎ (Dharma) + 师 shī (master; teacher). A respectful title for a monk or nun versed in the teaching, qualified to expound it to others. Used before or after a religious name.
法院	fǎ yuàn	court of law; courthouse — 法 fǎ (law) + 院 yuàn (institution; courtyard). The court where law is applied and cases are judged. 人民法院 "the People's Court," 上法院 "to go to court."
帆船	fān chuán	sailboat, sailing ship — 帆 (sail) + 船 (boat): a boat driven by canvas. Covers everything from a small racing dinghy to the old treasure ships, and names the Olympic sport of sailing.
翻	fān	to flip through (a book)
翻脸	fān liǎn	to turn hostile; to fall out with someone — 翻 (to flip, turn over) + 脸 (face). To flip the face from friendly to cold in an instant, breaking off goodwill. The vivid version is 翻脸不认人 (fānliǎn bú rèn rén), to turn cold and pretend you never knew the person.
翻越	fān yu è	to climb over (especially a mountain or wall) — 翻 (fān, to flip; to turn over) + 越. The physical act of climbing up and over an obstacle. Strongly associated with crossing mountain ranges; the verb of choice for descriptions of mountain expeditions, escape across hostile terrain, and the Long March.
凡夫	fán fū	ordinary being (primary audience)
凡间	fán jiān	the mortal realm; the ordinary world (as seen from above) — 凡 fán (mortal; ordinary; not immortal) + 间 (space; realm). The mortal world as defined by its contrast with the celestial.
烦恼即菩提	fán nǎo jí pú tí	the afflictions are themselves awakening
繁体	fán tǐ	traditional (character form)
繁体字	fán tǐ zì	traditional Chinese characters — 繁 fán (complex; numerous) + 体 tǐ (form; body; style) + 字 zì. The original, more structurally complex character forms that preserve more visual information about component etymology. Used in Taiwan, Hong Kong, Macau, and many overseas Chinese communities.
反常	fǎn cháng	abnormal; unusual
反而	fǎn ér	on the contrary; instead; the other way around — 反 fǎn (reverse) + 而 ér (and so). Marks an outcome that runs opposite to expectation: you would predict one thing, but the reverse happens. 不但没…反而… "not only did not... but on the contrary...".
反馈	fǎn kuì	feedback
反应	fǎn yìng	reaction; response; to react — 反 fǎn (back) + 应 yìng (to respond). The response that comes back when something acts on you: a chemical reaction (化学反应), a person's reaction to news, a body's reaction to medicine. Both noun and verb.
反映问题	fǎn yìng wèn tí	to report a problem — The neutral opener. 客诉 (kèsù) is a complaint from your own customers, a useful word because it shifts the pressure to the market rather than to you.
反正	fǎn zhèng	anyway; in any case; regardless — 反 fǎn (reverse) + 正 zhèng (right). Literally "reverse-or-right," whichever way it turns out: the all-purpose "anyway, the outcome is the same." 反正我不去 "anyway, I'm not going."
返工	fǎng ōng	rework — Fixing defective goods. Its harder cousins: 报废 (bàofèi) scrapping, 补货 (bǔhuò) replacing goods, 退货 (tuìhuò) returning them. 整改 (zhěnggǎi) is a corrective action on the process itself, so the defect stops recurring.
返回	fǎn huí	to return; go back
犯困	fàn kùn	to get drowsy; to feel sleepy — 犯 (to come on, to have a bout of) + 困 (sleepy). A spell of drowsiness creeping over you, often at the wrong time. Colloquial and very common: the after-lunch slump, the late-night struggle to stay awake.
犯罪	fàn zuì	crime
泛音	fàn yīn	harmonic tones — the "heavenly" register of the guqin — 泛 fàn (to float; to overflow) + 音 yīn (sound). Produced by lightly touching the string at one of the 13 marked 徽 huī positions without pressing it to the board, then plucking. The result is a clear, bell-like overtone.
饭	fàn	cooked rice; meal
饭馆	fàng uǎn	restaurant, eatery — 饭 fàn (cooked rice, a meal) + 馆. A modest, sit-down place to eat, the neighborhood diner rather than a banquet hall. Often said 小饭馆 for the cheap, friendly kind.
范宽	Fàn Kuān	Fan Kuan — Northern Song master
方便	fāng biàn	convenient; to make things easy for; (euphemism) to use the toilet — 方 fāng + 便 biàn (convenient; suitable). Easy and convenient, of a time, a place, or an arrangement. 方便面 fāngbiànmiàn is instant noodles, literally "convenient noodles." As a polite euphemism it also means to relieve oneself.
方程	fāng chéng	Equation — Equation. The term is two thousand years old, taken from the chapter of the Nine Chapters that solved systems of linear equations with counting rods. 解方程 (jiě fāngchéng) is to solve an equation.
方法	fāng fǎ	method; means; way of doing something — 方 fāng (square; direction) + 法 fǎ (way). A method or procedure considered in the abstract, the systematic way to approach a task: 学习方法 "study methods," 科学方法 "the scientific method."
方面	fāng miàn	aspect; side; area; regard — 方 (direction; side) + 面 (face; surface). A particular "side" or "face" of a topic , one of the aspects of a multi-sided issue. Extremely common in academic, journalistic, and analytical Chinese. 在这方面 (in this regard), 各个方面 (all aspects), and 经济方面 (in terms of economics) are standard expressions.
方式	fāng shì	way; manner; mode; style — 方 fāng (way) + 式 shì (form; pattern). The manner or mode in which something is done, broader and more abstract than 方法: 生活方式 lifestyle, 付款方式 payment method.
方式焦点	fāng shì jiāo diǎn	manner focus — "it was [by X] that…" — When the focused element is a manner, means, or instrument, 是…的 answers "how?" The manner phrase sits after 是 and before the main verb. This is a natural way to specify or contrast the means of travel, communication, or action.
方位	fāng wèi	cardinal position; bearing; orientation — 方 (region) + 位 (position). More technical than 方向: a fixed cardinal bearing rather than a heading. Used in geography, military, navigation, and feng shui contexts. 方位词 (fāngwèicí) is the grammatical category of "position words" , 上 above, 下 below, 前 front, 后 back, 左 left, 右 right.
方位禁忌	fāng wèi jìn jì	Directional Taboos — feng shui and orientation — Spatial orientation is governed by 风水 fēngshuǐ (feng shui, lit. "wind-water"), the art of placing buildings, graves, and furniture to harmonize with the flow of qi 气. The main door of a house should not directly face a staircase, a toilet, or a knife-like corner of another building.
方向感	fāng xiàng gǎn	sense of direction — 方向 + 感 (gǎn, sense; feeling). The personal faculty of knowing where you are in space , and, by extension, where your life or project is heading. Used both literally (in conversations about getting lost) and figuratively (about clarity of purpose).
方向盘	fāng xiàng pán	steering wheel — 方向 + 盘 (pán, plate; disc). The literal "direction-disc" of a vehicle. Used figuratively for whoever holds control: 谁掌握方向盘 ("who holds the wheel") = who is in charge of the direction.
方言	fāng yán	dialect; regional variety — Topolect, regional speech , the Chinese term for what English often calls "dialect." Applied to the major regional speech varieties: Cantonese, Wu, Min, Hakka, Gan, Xiang.
房东	fáng dōng	landlord — 房 (house) plus 东 (dōng, east; by old convention the host stands on the east side). The person who owns the dwelling and rents it out. The 东 element marks the host or owner, the same 东 found in 股东 (gǔdōng, shareholder).
房价	fáng jià	housing price — 房 (house) plus 价 (jià, price). The purchase price of property. A constant topic in contemporary Chinese life, where 房价 in big cities is a daily-news obsession.
房间	fáng jiān	room (inside a building) — 房 fáng (room; building-section) + 间 jiān (space between; a measure word for rooms). The standard neutral word for a room in a house, hotel, or office. 三室两厅 = three bedrooms two living rooms (standard real-estate description).
房客	fáng kè	tenant, lodger
房屋	fáng wū	housing, buildings
房主	fáng zhǔ	homeowner; landlord — 房 fáng (house) + 主 zhǔ (owner). The person who owns a dwelling. The pattern noun + 主 names the owner of a thing: 车主 chēzhǔ (car owner), 业主 yèzhǔ (property owner).
房子	fáng zi	house, building — 房 (building) plus the neutral-tone noun suffix 子 (zi). The default everyday word for a house or any standalone building you live in. Concrete and countable: you buy one, rent one, or build one.
房租	fáng zū	rent — 房 (house) plus 租 (zū, to rent, rent money). The money paid to occupy a dwelling. Pair it with 交 (jiāo, to hand over): 交房租 means to pay the rent.
仿佛	fǎng fú	as if; seemingly; as though — 仿 fǎng (to imitate; resembling) + 佛 fú (phonetic only; here read fú, not fó, with no Buddhist meaning). A high-frequency literary and spoken adverb meaning "as if; seemingly; as though." Used to describe perceptions, memories, or states that have a dreamlike or approximate quality.
访问	fǎng wèn	to visit; to call on; to interview; (computing) to access — 访 fǎng (to call on) + 问 wèn (to ask after). A formal visit made to a person, place, or, by modern extension, a website or file. 国事访问 "a state visit," 访问网站 "to access a website."
放	fàng	to release, to put down
放河灯	fàng hé dēng	floating river lanterns — guiding wandering spirits home — 放 fàng (release; set free) + 河 hé (river) + 灯 dēng (lantern). Paper lanterns, typically lotus-shaped with a candle inside, set onto rivers, ponds, and coastlines on the evening of the 15th.
放慢	fàng màn	to slow down; to ease up the pace — 放 fàng (to release, to let go) + 慢 màn (slow). To deliberately release speed. Used for vehicles, speech, breathing, economic growth. The verb of conscious deceleration.
放松	fàng sōng	to relax; to loosen
放心	fàng xīn	to relax; to stop worrying; to feel at ease — 放 fàng (to put down; to release; to set free) + 心 xīn (heart). Lit. "put the heart down." The direct antonym of 担心. If worry is carrying the heart, 放心 is setting it back on the ground: the relief of knowing that what you feared is safe or resolved. Extremely frequent in reassurances and farewells.
飞白	fēi bái	Flying white — Flying white: a dry-brush effect where the hairs of the brush separate, leaving streaks of paper showing through the ink. A controlled accident, difficult to achieve and highly prized.
飞虫	fēi chóng	flying insect
飞船	fēi chuán	spacecraft, airship
飞机	fēi jī	airplane, aircraft — 飞 (to fly) plus 机 (machine): the flying machine. This is the everyday word for any fixed-wing aircraft, from a jetliner to a small propeller plane.
飞快	fēi kuài	lightning fast; at a flying pace — 飞 fēi (to fly) + 快 kuài (fast). Vivid intensifier: the speed of something that seems to fly. It modifies a verb as an adverb or stands alone as a predicate adjective. Also used for a blade that is razor-sharp , fast at cutting.
飞鸟	fēi niǎo	a bird in flight, flying bird — 飞 (flying) plus 鸟 (bird): a bird on the wing. The word keeps the character close to its pictographic root, naming the very creature the glyph was drawn from.
飞翔	fēi xiáng	to soar, to circle in flight
飞行	fēi xíng	to fly; flight, aviation — 飞 (to fly) plus 行 (to move, to travel): movement through the air. More formal and technical than plain 飞, this is the word for piloting, air travel, and aviation as a field. 飞行员 (fēixíngyuán) is a pilot.
飞行员	fēi xíng yuán	pilot
飞跃	fēi yu è	a leap, a great advance
飞涨	fēi zhǎng	to rise sharply (prices) — 飞 (flying) plus 涨 (to rise, of water or prices): prices climbing as if in flight. The standard word for a steep, fast jump in cost.
非常	fēi cháng	extremely; very; unusual; extraordinary — 非 fēi (not) + 常 cháng (ordinary). As an adverb before an adjective it means "extremely" (非常高兴 "extremely happy"); as an adjective before a noun it keeps the older sense "extraordinary, exceptional" (非常时期 "extraordinary times").
非法	fēi fǎ	illegal; unlawful — 非 fēi (not) + 法 fǎ (law). Outside the law, not lawful: 非法行为 "illegal conduct," 非法移民 "illegal immigration." Its opposite is 合法 héfǎ (lawful).
非攻	fēi gōng	opposition to aggressive war — Mozi distinguished between defensive warfare (permitted) and aggressive conquest (condemned as categorically unjust).
非遗	fēi yí	intangible cultural heritage (abbrev.)
非议	fēi yì	to criticize; to reproach; censure — 非 fēi (to fault) + 议 yì (to discuss; opinion). To talk of something as wrong, to find fault with it: 无可非议 "beyond reproach, nothing to criticize." Here 非 acts as a verb, "to deem wrong."
费	fèi	expense; to spend; to waste
费功夫	fèi gōng fu	to take effort; to be laborious; to be time-consuming — 费 fèi (to spend; to cost; to consume) + 功夫 (effort). Where 下功夫 emphasizes voluntarily investing effort, 费功夫 emphasizes the effort a task demands of you , the cost side of the equation. A task that is 费功夫 is demanding, painstaking, time-intensive.
费用	fèi yòng	expense; cost; fee; expenditure — 费 fèi (to spend; cost; waste; excessive expenditure) + 用 yòng (use). What is spent in order to use something: the cost of deployment. Covers expenses of all kinds , medical fees, travel costs, operational expenditure , across formal and everyday registers.
分担	fēn dān	to share (a cost) — The compromise line. Offering it yourself, once fault is clear, buys goodwill for the next order.
分封	fēn fēng	to enfeoff; to grant fiefs — 分 (fēn, to divide) plus 封 (to confer land): to carve a realm into fiefs and grant them to vassals. The defining act of the early Zhōu political order.
分开	fēn kāi	to separate; to part; to divide up — 分 fēn (divide) + 开 kāi (open; apart). To pull two things apart or keep them distinct: 把垃圾分开 "sort the rubbish," 我们分开走 "let's go separately."
分类	fēn lèi	to classify; to sort into categories — 分 fēn (divide) + 类 lèi (kind; category). To divide things into kinds: 垃圾分类 "waste sorting," 图书分类 "cataloguing books."
分离	fēn lí	to separate, to part — 分 (fēn, to divide) plus 离 (to part). Two reinforcing halves: things that were one are now in pieces. Used for people parting and for substances separated in a process.
分配	fēn pèi	to allot; distribute
分手	fēn shǒu	to part ways; to break up (a couple) — 分 fēn (separate) + 手 shǒu (hand). To let go of one another's hand. In everyday use it most often means a romantic break-up, but it can simply mean two people parting at a crossroads.
分数	fēn shù	score; mark; (math) fraction — 分 fēn (point) + 数 shù (number). A test score, and in mathematics a fraction. Both senses share the idea of a number standing below or against a whole.
分钟	fēn zhōng	minute (of time) — 分 fēn (a divided unit) + 钟 zhōng (clock). A minute as a span of time: 等五分钟 "wait five minutes." To tell the time on the clock face, 分 alone is enough: 两点十五分 "two fifteen."
分子	fēn zǐ	Molecule — Molecule: divided unit. The same word, differently stressed, means "member of a group" in political vocabulary (知识分子, intellectuals), a coincidence of compounding worth knowing before it confuses.
芬芳	fēn fāng	fragrant (literary)
份	fèn	measure word for copies and portions
份子钱	fèn zi qián	share money — the obligatory wedding gift
丰富	fēng fù	abundant, rich, varied; to enrich — 丰 (lush, plentiful) plus 富 (abundant). As an adjective, rich in variety or quantity. As a verb, to enrich or broaden, as in 丰富知识 (to broaden one's knowledge).
丰收	fēng shōu	bumper harvest
风度	fēng dù	grace; bearing; manner
风格	fēng gé	style; manner; character; the distinctive way something is done — 风 fēng (wind; manner) + 格 gé (standard; grid; manner). The characteristic way someone or something presents itself , artistic style, architectural style, speaking style, leadership style.
风景	fēng jǐng	scenery; landscape; a scenic view — 风 fēng (wind; atmosphere) + 景 jǐng (view; scene; spectacle). The atmospheric scene , the view that catches the eye and stirs the spirit. Not just a geographic description but an aesthetic one: 风景 implies the view is worth seeing.
风水宝地	fēng shuǐ bǎo dì	a feng shui treasure land; an auspiciously sited place — 宝 bǎo (treasure; precious) + 地 dì (land; place). A site so well positioned in the landscape , with mountains, water, orientation, and dragon veins all aligning , that it is considered to bring fortune to whoever inhabits it or is buried there.
风水相生	fēng shuǐ xiāng shēng	wind and water mutually generate — the core principle of feng shui energy management — The operative logic behind 风水: when wind is blocked by mountains and water is present to create a boundary, 气 accumulates rather than dispersing. Wind and water are the agents , qi is what they manage.
风水择地	fēng shuǐ zé dì	geomantic grave siting — fortune for the living through the dead — The location and orientation of a grave (阴宅 yīnzhái, "yin dwelling") directly affects the fortune of the descendants, a doctrine that made grave geomancy (阴宅风水 yīnzhái fēngshuǐ) one of the most important applications of feng shui.
风险	fēng xiǎn	risk; danger
风向	fēng xiàng	wind direction; (figurative) which way the wind blows — 风 fēng (wind) + 向 xiàng (direction). The direction the wind comes from. Used figuratively for the drift of opinion or events: 看风向 "to see which way the wind is blowing," to read the mood before acting.
风言风语	fēng yán fēng yǔ	wind-words wind-language — whispers; rumors; idle gossip — Reduplication of 风 (wind) around 言 yán (words; speech) and 语 yǔ (language). Words as insubstantial as wind , whispers and rumors that circulate without clear source or foundation.
封闭	fēng bì	to seal off; closed, sealed — 封 (to seal) plus 闭 (bì, to shut): to close something off completely. Used for sealing a road, a building, a wound, or, figuratively, a closed-off mindset.
封顶	fēng dǐng	to cap; to top out
封建	fēng jiàn	feudal investiture — the Zhou territorial hierarchy — 封 fēng (to enfeoff; to heap earth as a boundary marker) + 建 jiàn (to establish). The Zhou system of distributing territory to vassals with corresponding titles, ritual privileges, and obligations.
封建迷信	fēng jiàn mì xìn	Feudal superstition — Feudal superstition: the official Communist Party category used to delegitimize traditional folk religious practices, ancestor veneration, feng shui, and divination. The phrase was weaponized during the Cultural Revolution.
封口	fēng kǒu	to seal an opening; to keep quiet
封面	fēng miàn	cover (of a book or magazine) — 封 (the closing layer) plus 面 (miàn, face / surface): the outer face that wraps and closes a book. By extension, the front cover of any publication.
封锁	fēng suǒ	to blockade; to seal off — 封 (to seal) plus 锁 (suǒ, to lock): to seal and lock down, blocking passage or flow. Used for military blockades, sealed-off areas, and information blackouts.
封样	fēng yàng	sealed sample; golden sample — "Seal the sample": approved units are labelled, signed by both sides, and kept by each as the binding quality standard for bulk. Also called 标准样 (biāozhǔnyàng) or 金样 (jīnyàng, golden sample). Keep at least one yourself.
烽火台	fēng huǒ tái	beacon tower; signal-fire platform
凤	fèng	phoenix
凤凰	fèng huáng	phoenix (paired with dragon)
凤凰涅槃	fèng huáng niè pán	the phoenix's nirvana; transformative renewal
奉天承运	fèng tiān chéng yùn	receiving heaven's mandate and carrying forward its fortune — the opening formula of imperial edicts — 奉 fèng (to receive; to hold reverently) + 天 tiān (heaven) + 承 chéng (to carry on; to inherit) + 运 yùn (fortune; cycle; destiny). The four characters that opened every imperial edict from the early Ming dynasty onward.
否定	fǒu dìng	to negate; negative
否认	fǒu rèn	to deny, to refuse to acknowledge — 否 fǒu (negation; to deny) + 认 rèn. The structural opposite of 承认. To formally refuse the recognition: to deny an accusation, deny an affair, deny a relationship.
否则	fǒu zé	otherwise; if not
夫妇	fū fù	a married couple; husband and wife (formal) — 夫 fū (husband) + 妇 fù (married woman). A married couple, close in meaning to 夫妻 but more formal and bookish. Often attached to a surname: 张氏夫妇 the Zhang couple, 新婚夫妇 newlyweds. Where 夫妻 feels everyday and warm, 夫妇 reads as written or ceremonious.
夫妻	fū qī	husband and wife — the household foundation — The marital relationship orders the household 家 jiā, which is the structural unit between the individual and the state. The husband is obligated to provide and to lead with moral example; the wife is obligated to manage the inner household and to support the family's moral life.
夫妻肺片	fū qī fèi piàn	Husband and Wife Beef Slices
夫人	fū rén	madam; lady; another man's wife (polite) — 夫 fū (man) + 人 rén (person). A respectful term for a married woman, especially the wife of a person of standing: 总统夫人 the first lady, 王夫人 Madam Wang. It honors the woman through her husband's status, so it is formal rather than intimate; you would not call your own wife 夫人 in casual speech.
敷衍	fū yǎn	to be perfunctory
敷衍了事	fū yǎn liǎo shì	to dispose of perfunctorily
佛法	fó fǎ	the Dharma; Buddhist teaching; the law of the Buddha — 佛 + 法 fǎ (law; method; dharma). The teachings of the Buddha as a whole, understood as both doctrine and practice. Also the specific rendering of Sanskrit dharma, which encompasses cosmic law, the teachings themselves, individual phenomena, and moral truth.
佛教	fó jiào	Buddhism (the religion) — 佛 (Buddha) + 教 jiào (teaching; religion; to teach). The standard modern term for Buddhism as a religion or institutional tradition. Entered China officially during the Han dynasty (around the 1st century CE), though oral transmission and merchant routes likely brought it earlier.
佛教节日	fó jiào jié rì	Buddhist festivals — integrated into the Chinese calendar — 浴佛节 Yùfó Jié (Buddha's Birthday, 4th lunar month, 8th day): ritual bathing of the infant Buddha statue with scented water, commemorating his birth.
佛经	fó jīng	Buddhist scripture; sutra — 佛 fó (Buddha) + 经 jīng (scripture). The sutras, the recorded discourses of the Buddha. Reciting and copying 佛经 is a long-standing devotional practice, as with the much-loved 心经 xīnjīng (Heart Sutra).
佛说阿弥陀经	Fó shuō Ā mí tuó jīng	the Smaller Sukhāvatīvyūha · ~1,800 characters · Kumārajīva's 402 CE translation — The short Amitabha Sutra. The daily-recitation text of Pure Land practice. The Buddha describes the Pure Land's adornments to Śāriputra, names the practice (single-minded recollection of Amitābha), and is supported by the buddhas of the six directions affirming the teaching.
佛系	fó xì	Buddhist-style; detached; low-key; unbothered (modern slang) — 佛 + 系 xì (style; type; lineage, as in 日系 Japanese-style). Emerged in Chinese internet culture around 2017 to describe a mentality of deliberate non-striving: going with the flow, not competing, indifferent to outcomes.
佛性	fó xìng	Buddha nature
服务	fú wù	service (contrasted with product)
符合	fú hé	to conform to; to accord with; to match — 符 fú (a tally; to tally) + 合 hé (to fit). To match a standard or expectation exactly, the way two halves of an ancient tally fit: 符合规定 "to conform to the rules," 符合条件 "to meet the conditions."
符箓	fú lù	talismans and registers — written instruments of Daoist power — 符 fú (talisman, a written or drawn symbol charged with spiritual power) + 箓 lù (registers, lists of divine names that give priests authority over corresponding spirits).
福气	fú qi	blessed fortune; a life touched by luck — 福 fú + 气 qì (atmosphere; quality; energy). The quality of being blessed: having fortune as part of one's life-energy. 有福气 yǒu fúqi = to be blessed / to have good fortune. A compliment that goes beyond luck to a general quality of being favored by life.
府第	fǔ dì	a mansion; an official's grand residence — 府 fǔ (official residence; manor) + 第 dì (ranked dwelling). The stately home of a high official or noble family. Literary and historical; you meet it in novels and on heritage plaques rather than in conversation.
斧	fǔ	axe
俯首	fǔ shǒu	to bow the head; to submit — 俯 fǔ (to bend down, to lower) + 首 shǒu (head). To lower the head, and by extension to yield or submit. 俯首听命 "to bow the head and take orders" describes obedient submission. The direct opposite of 昂首.
腐皮	fǔ pí	tofu skin; bean curd skin; yuba
腐乳	fǔ rǔ	Fermented Bean Curd; Tofu Cheese
父辈	fù bèi	the fathers' generation — 父 fù (father) + 辈 bèi (generation, cohort). The fathers' generation taken as a class, everyone of the parents' age and above. Set against 子辈 zǐbèi, the children's generation, when speaking of how a family changes across the line.
父母	fù mǔ	parents (father and mother) — 父 fù (father) + 母 mǔ (mother). The standard word for parents as a pair. Father comes first by convention, the same ordering that runs through the whole kinship vocabulary.
父亲	fù qīn	father (formal) — 父 fù (father) + 亲 qīn (kin, close relative). The formal, written word for father, the one used on documents, in introductions, and in respectful speech. In daily conversation people say 爸爸 bàba instead; 父亲 carries weight and distance.
父子	fù zǐ	parent and child — the root relationship; filial piety — The foundational relationship , described by Confucians as the root from which all other social virtues grow. The child's obligation is 孝 xiào (filial piety): obedience, care in old age, ritual mourning at death, and continuation of the family line.
负责	fù zé	to be responsible for — Names the owner of an action. Ask it about every open item before the meeting ends. 对……负责 also means "to be accountable for" a result, which makes it the word of quality disputes.
附近	fù jìn	nearby; in the vicinity; around here — 附 fù (to attach to; to be adjacent to) + 近 (near). The area attached nearby , the general term for the vicinity of a place. 附近 functions as both noun (学校附近, in the vicinity of the school) and adverb-like modifier.
附身	fù shēn	possession; spirit entering body
附体	fù tǐ	spirit possession — the spirit descending into the shaman — 附体 (spirit possession , lit. "spirit attaches to body") is one of two modes of shamanic trance in Tungusic tradition. In the other mode (魂游 húnyóu , soul-flight), the shaman's own soul leaves the body and travels to the spirit world while the body remains passive.
复古	fù gǔ	to revive the old; retro
复活	fù huó	to revive; to come back to life; resurrection — 复 fù (again, to return) + 活 huó (to live). To live again: the dead returning to life, or by extension a thing once finished springing back. 复活节 fùhuójié is Easter, the festival of resurrection.
复旧	fù jiù	to restore the old order; to revert to the past — 复 (fù, to return to; to restore; to repeat) + 旧 (old). To restore a previous state , often used in political contexts for attempted counter-revolution or reactionary restoration. 复旧势力 (forces seeking to restore the old order) was a standard phrase in Cultural Revolution rhetoric.
复习	fù xí	to review, revise — 复 (again) + 习 (practice, whose traditional form 習 shows wings over the sun: a fledgling's repeated flights). The exam-season verb, echoing the Analects' opening line about the pleasure of timely review, which every Chinese student can quote and few believe during finals.
复杂	fù zá	complex, complicated
副词	fù cí	adverb
富含	fù hán	to be rich in; to contain abundantly — 富 (abundant) plus 含 (to contain). To hold something in great quantity, common in writing about food and nutrition.
富强	fù qiáng	prosperous and strong (of a nation) — 富 (rich) plus 强 (strong). A wealthy and powerful state, a phrase reaching back to the warring-states ideal of 富国强兵, a rich country and a strong army.
富人	fù rén	rich person; the wealthy — 富 (rich) plus 人 (person). A wealthy individual, and collectively the rich as a class, set against 穷人 (qióngrén), the poor.
富翁	fù wēng	rich man, wealthy magnate
富有	fù yǒu	wealthy; rich in — 富 (rich) plus 有 (to have). Literally "rich and possessing." Describes a person with great wealth, and figuratively something abundantly endowed with a quality, as in 富有经验 (rich in experience).
富裕	fù yù	prosperous, well-off, affluent — 富 (rich) plus 裕 (abundant, ample). Describes a comfortable, well-off condition, often of a household, village, or society rather than a single tycoon.
赋	fù	Direct narration — Direct narration: the mode of straightforward statement and description, naming the subject and describing it without indirection. Also the name of a later Han dynasty poetic genre notable for its elaborate descriptive technique.
该	gāi	should; ought to
改变	gǎi biàn	to change (something); to alter; to modify — 改 gǎi (to correct; to revise; to alter, itself containing 己 self + 攴 striking hand) + 变 biàn. The key difference from standalone 变: 改变 is transitive and agentive. Someone 改变s something. 变 alone can describe change that happens to a subject; 改变 requires an agent making a deliberate alteration.
改变命运	gǎi biàn mìng yùn	to change one's fate
改成	gǎi chéng	to change into, to turn into — 改 (alter) + 成 (become). A result complement: to alter A so that it becomes B. Names the new state explicitly after the verb.
改革	gǎi gé	reform; to reform — 改 gǎi (to correct; to revise) + 革 gé (to reform; to strip away, the hide-tanning radical). The standard term for political and institutional reform. Where 变革 describes change as a phenomenon, 改革 is more often a policy objective: you implement 改革, you carry out 改革.
改革开放	gǎi gé kāi fàng	reform and opening up — the policy itself — The defining phrase of post-Mao China: the paired program of internal structural reform (改革) and outward engagement (开放) launched in 1978.
改进	gǎi jìn	to improve, to refine — 改 (alter) + 进 (advance). To better a method, product, or process by moving it forward. The everyday word in engineering and management for incremental improvement.
改名	gǎi míng	to rename
改模	gǎi mú	to modify a mold — Changing the tool after a trial. The crucial question is who pays: changes caused by the factory's error are on them, changes caused by a new request from you are on you. Agree which is which in writing before work starts. 修模 (xiūmú) is repair or maintenance.
改善	gǎi shàn	to improve, to better — 改 (alter) + 善 (good). To change toward the good: to better conditions, relations, or quality. Used for situations and systems more than for a person's skill.
改天	gǎi tiān	another day, some other time — 改 (change) + 天 (day). To shift a plan to a different day. A soft, friendly way to postpone or to propose meeting later, very common in spoken Mandarin.
改天再聊	gǎi tiān zài liáo	"Let's chat another time — "Let's chat another time." Proposes future contact without committing to a specific time. Warm and noncommittal, like English "let's catch up soon." Common closer among friends and acquaintances.
改造	gǎi zào	to transform, to remold, to renovate — 改 (alter) + 造 (build, make). To rebuild something into a new form: renovating a building, retrofitting a machine, or, in older political language, reforming a person's thinking.
改正	gǎi zhèng	to correct; to put right; to amend — 改 gǎi (to change) + 正 zhèng (straight). To change something crooked back to straight: 改正错误 "to correct a mistake." The act of bringing a thing back onto the line.
盖	gài	to cover; to cap
盖头	gài tóu	The red veil worn by the bride during the wedding procession and ceremony, lifted by the groom in the bridal chamber. The lifting of the veil (揭盖头 jiē gàitóu) was the culminating moment of the traditional wedding ceremony.
盖碗	gài wǎn	lidded bowl — the standard gōngfu brewing vessel
盖章	gài zhāng	to stamp a seal
甘	gān	sweet, willing
赶	gǎn	to rush, to catch
赶紧	gǎn jǐn	hurry up; quickly; right away — 赶 (gǎn, to rush, to drive forward) + 紧 (tight). To move with no slack, to act before the moment slips. A spoken, everyday urging to do something now.
赶快	gǎn kuài	hurry up; do it right now — 赶 gǎn (to catch up; to rush) + 快 kuài (fast). Used when something needs to happen without delay. Always an adverb modifying a following verb; it conveys urgency rather than habitual speed, with an implicit time pressure in the context.
敢	gǎn	dare to
感到	gǎn dào	to feel; to register a state — 感 gǎn (feel) + 到 dào (arrive at · result marker). The 到 is a resultative complement signaling that the feeling has landed, been registered as a completed state. The most neutral, all-purpose feeling verb. Almost always takes an adjectival or clausal complement.
感动	gǎn dòng	to be moved; to touch someone's heart — 感 gǎn + 动 dòng (to move). The heart set in motion by something beautiful, kind, or touching. One of the most emotionally central verbs in Chinese, high frequency in literature, film, and daily speech.
感恩	gǎn ēn	to honor all grace received; gratitude as virtue — 感 gǎn + 恩 ēn (grace; benevolence: the kindness of parents, teachers, heaven). A Confucian virtue, the act of recognizing and honoring all goodness received throughout one's life. The deepest gratitude word.
感化	gǎn huà	to reform through moral influence; to transform with virtue — 感 gǎn + 化 huà (to transform; to convert). To touch someone's heart so deeply they are morally transformed. A key Confucian concept: the ideal ruler governs by exemplary virtue; his presence alone 感化s those around him.
感激	gǎn jī	deeply grateful; moved by another's kindness — 感 gǎn + 激 jī (to stir; to agitate; rapids, turbulent water). The heart stirred up by a significant kindness. Implies emotional debt that moves you. Stronger than 感谢; use for meaningful help, not everyday thanks.
感觉	gǎn ju é	feeling; impression; to feel (V &amp; N) — 感 gǎn + 觉 jué (to awaken; become aware; originally: waking from sleep, the first moment of consciousness). A dual-category word that functions as both verb and noun. The most flexible word in the 感 family. Covers gut feeling, subjective impression, intuition.
感觉动词 + 起来	gǎn ju é dòng cí + qǐ lái	"when it comes to [sense]: [adjective]" — When a perception verb (看 kàn, 听 tīng, 吃 chī, 闻 wén, 用 yòng, 做 zuò) is followed by 起来 and then an adjective, the meaning is: "judging by [sense] / when you [sense] it, [it is adj]." This is one of the most common patterns in spoken Mandarin for expressing impressions and evaluations.
感慨	gǎn kǎi	bittersweet swell of emotion; to sigh with feeling — 感 gǎn + 慨 kǎi (to sigh; to lament; a person exhaling with grief). The bittersweet wave felt when contemplating time, change, and transience: the specific ache of things passing. Quintessentially literary. Common in memoirs, essays, and poetry.
感冒	gǎn mào	cold; flu; to catch a cold — 感 gǎn + 冒 mào (to emit; to brave recklessly). In traditional Chinese medicine, illness = pathogenic qì 气 entering the body. 感冒 = receiving and being overcome by wind-cold 风寒.
感情	gǎn qíng	feelings; affection; emotional bond — 感 gǎn (to feel) + 情 qíng (feeling). The broad word for emotional feeling, and especially the affection that builds up between people: 他们感情很好 "they have a close bond." Also "to develop feelings": 日久生情 "feelings grow with time."
感染	gǎn rǎn	to infect; to inspire; to spread into — 感 gǎn + 染 rǎn (to dye; to stain; to spread, as dye spreads through cloth). The same word covers pathogen transmission and emotional contagion, both understood as resonance spreading from one body to another, rooted in the classical cosmology of qì 气.
感人	gǎn rén	touching; moving (adj)
感伤	gǎn shāng	melancholy; tender sadness — 感 gǎn + 伤 shāng (wound; grief). The heart wounded by what it feels. Tender, autumnal. The feeling of parting, of beauty that cannot last. A key register in classical Chinese poetry and the ci 词 lyric tradition.
感受	gǎn shòu	lived experience; to experience deeply — 感 gǎn + 受 shòu (to receive; to undergo; to be a recipient). Something comes to you and is taken in. Emphasizes the experiential process rather than the end-state, richer and more embodied than 感觉. Common in art criticism, travel writing, and introspective prose.
感叹	gǎn tàn	to exclaim with feeling; to marvel — 感 gǎn + 叹 tàn (to sigh; to exclaim). When emotion erupts as an exclamation. Memorable fact: 感叹号 gǎntànhào = the exclamation mark (!). The character literally encodes the grammar of feeling.
感悟	gǎn wù	felt insight; wisdom from lived experience — 感 gǎn + 悟 wù (to awaken; enlightenment; the word for Buddhist satori). Insight that comes from experience touching the heart. Deeper than knowledge.
感谢	gǎn xiè	to thank sincerely; gratitude (formal) — 感 gǎn + 谢 xiè (to thank; originally: water overflowing, pouring out one's feelings). Warmer and more sincere than 谢谢. Standard in writing, speeches, and formal situations. Also functions as a noun in 表示感谢.
感性	gǎn xìng	emotional; sensory; intuitive
感应	gǎn yìng	resonance; sympathetic response; induction — 感 gǎn + 应 yìng (to respond; to echo). Used in both physics (电磁感应 diàncí gǎnyìng, electromagnetic induction) and classical cosmology. The doctrine of 天人感应 tiān rén gǎnyìng, Heaven and humanity in constant resonant dialogue, is a pillar of Han Confucian thought.
感知	gǎn zhī	perceptual cognition; to perceive — 感 gǎn + 知 zhī (to know; cognition). Perception as an act of knowing: the full cycle from sensation to understanding. The most formal and academic of the four.
干	gān	to do; to work (colloquial)
干杯	gān bēi	Bottoms up , "dry cup — Bottoms up , "dry cup." The standard toast call, implying the glass is to be emptied. Contrasted with 随意 (suíyì, "as you like"), which allows partial drinking. 干杯 is the stronger and more committed version. Refusing to 干杯 with someone who has singled you out for a toast is a social rebuff.
干部	gàn bù	cadre; official
干活	gàn huó	to work; to do manual labor — 干 gàn (to do, to labor) + 活 huó (work, a task). The everyday spoken phrase for doing physical or hands-on work. Colloquial and slightly blue-collar in flavor, the verb you reach for about chores, jobs on a site, or work around the house.
干支	gān zhī	Stems and Branches — Stems and Branches: the shorthand for the combined system of Ten Heavenly Stems and Twelve Earthly Branches used to name years, months, days, and two-hour periods.
刚 · 已经 · 以前	gāng yǐ jīng yǐ qián	just now · already · formerly, the past-pointing adverbs — 刚 gāng (just now) places an action in the immediate past: 他刚走 (he just left). 已经 yǐjīng (already) pairs with 了 to mark a completed state: 我已经吃了 (I've already eaten). 以前 yǐqián (before, formerly) frames a whole clause in the past: 我以前住在上海 (I used to live in Shanghai).
刚才	gāng cái	just now; a moment ago — 刚 gāng (just; barely) + 才 cái. A time word for the very recent past, the moment that has only just passed: 刚才他还在这儿 "he was still here just now." Here 才 keeps its sense of a threshold only just crossed.
钢笔	gāng bǐ	fountain pen — 钢 gāng (steel) + 笔 bǐ. The fountain pen, named for its steel nib. Its cousins fill out the modern desk: 圆珠笔 yuánzhūbǐ (ballpoint), 毛笔 máobǐ (brush), 铅笔 qiānbǐ (pencil).
高低	gāo dī	height difference; high and low; relative level; social hierarchy — 高 (high) + 低 (low). The paired antonym compound that covers any vertical differential: the height gap between two objects, the difference in quality between two options, or the gap in social standing between two people.
高调	gāo diào	high-key; ostentatious; attention-seeking
高考	Gāo kǎo	college entrance exam
高楼	gāo lóu	tall building, tower
高硼硅玻璃	gāo péng guī bō li	borosilicate glass — "High boron-silicon glass," resistant to thermal shock. 耐热 (nàirè) is heat-resistant, 温差 (wēnchā) the temperature difference it can survive, 爆裂 (bàoliè) bursting or shattering.
高山	gāo shān	high mountain; alpine — 高 (high) + 山 (mountain). A tall mountain , the compound appears in geography, poetry, and cultural discourse.
高手	gāo shǒu	expert; master; highly skilled person — 高 (high, superior) + 手 (hand; skill; person with a skill). A superior hand , someone with excellent technique. 象棋高手 (chess master); 编程高手 (programming expert); 武林高手 (martial arts master, from wuxia fiction). Colloquial and approving. The opposite is 新手 xīnshǒu (newcomer, novice).
高铁	gāo tiě	high-speed rail — Trains are booked on the official 12306 app or site (it has an English version) or through travel apps, and your passport is the ticket: it is checked at the station entrance and at the gate. 动车 (dòngchē) are slightly slower bullet trains. Arrive early; large stations have security checks.
高兴得跳起来	gāo xìng de tiào qǐ lai	so happy (she) jumped up — adj + 得 + result — 得 can also follow an adjective (not just a verb) when the adjective itself takes a result clause. The structure is Adj + 得 + [result] , "so [adj] that [result]." This is the correlative "so…that" pattern.
高原	gāo yuán	plateau; highlands
搞	gǎo	to do, to manage
搞笑	gǎo xiào	funny; hilarious
告别	gào bié	to bid farewell
告诉	gào su	to tell
哥儿俩	gēr liǎ	the two brothers; the pair of bros
哥哥	gē ge	elder brother — The base word: 哥 doubled, the second syllable toned down to neutral. Standard everyday term for one's own older brother, and a child can also use it for an unrelated older boy.
哥们儿	gē men r	buddy, bro, mate — 哥 + 们 (plural) + the Beijing 儿 ending. Close male friends, sworn-brother style. Singular or plural, it marks loyalty between guys; northern and casual, common in Beijing speech.
歌唱	gē chàng	to sing (in praise); singing as an art — 歌 gē (song) + 唱 chàng (to sing). More formal and elevated than 唱歌. Used for singing as a discipline and for singing in celebration: 歌唱家 gēchàngjiā is a professional vocalist, and 歌唱祖国 means to sing the praises of one's homeland.
歌词	gē cí	song lyrics; the words of a song — 歌 gē (song) + 词 cí (wording). The words set to music, as opposed to 曲 qǔ, the melody. A songwriter is often credited separately for 作词 zuòcí (writing the lyrics) and 作曲 zuòqǔ (composing the tune).
歌剧	gē jù	opera — 歌 (song) plus 剧 (drama). A sung drama; the term used for Western-style opera, as distinct from 京剧 (jīngjù) Peking opera.
歌曲	gē qǔ	song (as a work) — 歌 (song) plus 曲 (tune, melody). 歌 leans on the lyrics, 曲 on the music; together they name a song as a finished piece. Slightly more formal than a bare 歌.
歌手	gē shǒu	singer — a skilled vocalist — 歌 gē (song) + 手 shǒu (skilled person). The standard word for a professional singer. This -手 suffix pattern for skilled persons: 歌手 singer, 选手 athlete/contestant, 鼓手 drummer, 射手 archer/shooter, 旗手 flag-bearer.
歌星	gē xīng	singing star
革	gé	to reform; leather; to strip away
革命	gé mìng	revolution (literally "to strip away the mandate") — 革 gé (leather; to strip; to change) plus 命. Originally classical: to remove the Mandate of Heaven (天命) from a dynasty that had lost it, the pre-modern theory of legitimate dynastic overthrow.
格	gé	grid; cell; square; standard
格物	gé wù	investigation of things; rectification of affairs — A term from the Great Learning 大学, interpreted radically differently by Zhu Xi and Wang Yangming. For Zhu Xi: the patient investigation of external things to grasp their 理.
个别	gè bié	individual; a small number of; exceptional — 个 gè (single) + 别 bié (separate; other). Either "one by one, individually" (个别谈话 "to talk to people one at a time") or "only a few, exceptional" (个别情况 "a few isolated cases").
个人	gèr én	individual; personal; in one's own view — 个 gè (single) + 人 rén (person). The single person set against the group, and by extension "personal, private": 个人观点 "a personal opinion," 个人信息 "personal information." Its foil is 集体 jítǐ (the collective).
个体	gè tǐ	an individual (entity); a single member — 个 gè (single) + 体 tǐ (body; entity). A single unit within a larger whole, used in biology and economics alike: 个体差异 "individual differences," 个体户 "a self-employed business owner."
个性	gè xìng	individuality; individual character; distinctiveness — 个 gè (individual) + 性 xìng (nature). The traits that distinguish one person from another, the stamp of the individual: 有个性 "to have a strong personality, to be distinctive," 个性化 "personalized, customized." Often praised as a mark of a person who is not interchangeable with the crowd.
个子	gè zi	height; stature; build (of a person) — 个 gè + 子 zi (noun-suffix). A person's height or physical build: 高个子 "tall build," 矮个子 "short build," 个子很高 "very tall." Used of the whole frame of a person, not a measured number.
各	gè	each; every (distributive)
各得其所	gè dé qí suǒ	everyone gets what they need; each to their proper place
各其	gè qí	each its own; respectively — 各 gè (each; every) + 其 qí (its). Used in classical and formal modern Chinese to indicate that each member of a set has its own distinct version of something. Common in philosophical and legal prose where precision about individuals within a group matters.
各自	gè zì	each (on their own); respectively — 各 gè (each) + 自 zì (self). Each person doing a thing for their own self, separately: 各自回家 "each went home on their own." A near relative of 己, built on the same idea of the individual self set apart from the group.
给面子	gěi miàn zi	to give face; to show respect; to honor someone's standing — 给 (to give) + 面子. To actively enhance or protect someone else's face. Can mean: attending their event (your presence honors them), publicly praising them, deferring to them in front of others, or not challenging them when they are wrong , to spare their embarrassment.
根	gēn	roots; origin
根本	gēn běn	fundamental; basic; (with a negative) at all — 根 gēn (root) + 本 běn (root). The two words for "root" doubled. As an adjective, fundamental or essential: 根本问题 "the fundamental issue." As an adverb with a negative, it means "not at all": 我根本不知道 "I had no idea whatsoever."
更多	gèng duō	even more (quantity) — 更 gèng (even more) + 多 duō (many; much). The most frequent collocation for comparative quantity. Appears in both spoken and written registers without restriction.
更改	gēng gǎi	to alter; to change (formal) — 更 gēng (to change) + 改 gǎi (to alter; to revise). A formal compound for changing plans, decisions, or documents. More official in tone than 改变 gǎibiàn and preferred in administrative contexts.
更好	gèng hǎo	even better — 更 gèng + 好 hǎo (good). The quintessential comparison pair. Appears in everyday speech, advertising, and formal writing with equal frequency.
更何况	gèng hé kuàng	let alone; much less; to say nothing of — 更 gèng + 何况 hékuàng (how much more; not to mention). A rhetorical connective that escalates from a difficult or surprising premise to an even more extreme conclusion. Introduces the harder case: if X is already the case, 更何况 Y is the clincher.
更换	gēng huàn	to replace; to switch out — 更 gēng (to change) + 换 huàn (to swap; to exchange). To replace one thing with another of the same kind: a part, a component, a person in a role. Emphasizes substitution more than 更改, which emphasizes revision.
更加	gèng jiā	even more; still more (emphatic) — 更 gèng + 加 jiā (to add; to increase). A compound adverb that amplifies the degree of 更 further. Preferred in formal writing, speeches, and academic prose. In speech, bare 更 is more natural; 更加 in conversation can sound slightly elevated.
更新	gēng xīn	to update; to renew; to replace with something new — 更 gēng (to change; to replace) + 新 (new). To make new again: to update software (更新系统), to renew a contract (更新合同), to replace aging infrastructure (更新设备). A standard verb in technology, administration, and policy. 持续更新 (continuous updating) is a phrase familiar to every software user.
更衣	gēng yī	to change clothes; dressing room — 更 gēng (to change) + 衣 yī (clothes; garment). To change one's clothing. Formal and classical in register; in modern spoken Chinese 换衣服 huàn yīfu is more natural. Survives today primarily in 更衣室 gēngyīshì (changing room; locker room).
工厂	gōng chǎng	factory
工程	gōng chéng	engineering
工程师	gōng chéng shī	engineer — 工程 gōngchéng (engineering; project , lit. "work + process") + 师 shī (master; teacher; professional). Engineer , one of the most respected professional titles in China, associated with the nation's modernization drive. 工程 alone means a large project or undertaking: 三峡工程 = Three Gorges Project.
工夫	gōng fu	time and effort; trouble spent on a task — 工 gōng (work, labor) + 夫 fū (effort). The time and care put into doing something: 花工夫 to spend effort, 没工夫 to have no time. It measures the investment a task demands rather than the skill it yields, the cost side of mastery.
工具	gōng jù	tool; instrument
工人	gōng rén	worker; laborer
工业	gōng yè	industry; the industrial sector — 工 gōng (labor; work) + 业 yè (sector). Manufacturing industry as a branch of the economy: 重工业 "heavy industry," 工业化 "industrialization." Paired with 农业 (agriculture) in the standard rhetoric of national development.
工业区	gōng yè qū	industrial zone
工艺	gōng yì	craft; technique; craftsmanship — 工 gōng (work; skill) + 艺 yì (art; skill; technique). Craft as the intersection of technical skill and artistry. 工艺品 gōngyìpǐn = handicraft item; artisan product. 传统工艺 = traditional crafts. 工艺美术 = arts and crafts (as a discipline).
工资	gōng zī	salary; wages
公案	gōng àn	kōan — a paradoxical case used as a meditation object — 公 gōng (public) + 案 àn (case/record). Originally a legal term: a judicial precedent. In Chan, a 公案 is a recorded exchange between a master and student, or a question posed by the master, used as a meditation object.
公差	gōng chā	tolerance — The allowed deviation from a dimension. Tight tolerances cost money; for parts that thread or seal against each other they decide whether the product works. 超差 (chāochā) is out of tolerance.
公道杯	gōng dào bēi	fairness pitcher / sharing pitcher
公德	gōng dé	civic virtue; public ethics — 公 gōng (public; common) + 德. The virtue owed to strangers and the shared world: not littering, not jumping queues, not playing music loudly on the train. 没有公德心 "to lack civic-mindedness" is a routine complaint; 社会公德 "social public ethics" is one of the standard categories in civic discourse.
公共	gōng gòng	public; communal; shared by all — 公 gōng (public) + 共 gòng (common; together). Belonging to or used by the community at large: 公共汽车 "public bus," 公共场所 "public place." The plain modifier for anything held in common.
公鸡	gōng jī	rooster, cock — 公 (male) + 鸡 (chicken). The crowing bird of the farmyard, the one Shuōwén praised for knowing the hour.
公斤	gōng jīn	kilogram — 公 gōng (metric) + 斤 jīn (a traditional unit of weight, about half a kilo). The standard kilogram. The 公 again marks the international unit against the older 斤.
公开	gōng kāi	open; public; to make public — 公 gōng (public) + 开 kāi (to open). Out in the open rather than hidden: as an adjective, 公开信 "an open letter"; as a verb, 公开秘密 "to make a secret public." The opposite of 秘密 mìmì (secret).
公筷	gōng kuài	Serving chopsticks , a second pair of chopsticks placed at the table for taking from shared dishes, kept separate from the pair used for eating. The practice is hygienic and has become more widespread post-2020. At formal banquets, a serving spoon (公勺 gōng sháo) may accompany each dish.
公里	gōng lǐ	kilometer — 公 (gōng, public, metric/standardized) + 里 (Chinese li, 500 m). The standard modern unit of distance in China: 公里 = 1 kilometer = 2 市里 (shìlǐ, "market li," the standard li of 500 m).
公历	gōng lì	the common (Gregorian) calendar
公路	gōng lù	highway, public road
公民	gōng mín	citizen (a legal member of a state) — 公 gōng (public, common) + 民. The legal-political term for a citizen: a person who holds the nationality of a state and bears its rights and obligations. The standard word in constitutional and legal contexts, paired with 权利 quánlì (rights) and 义务 yìwù (duties).
公牛	gōng niú	bull
公平	gōng píng	fair; just; impartial — 公 gōng (public; impartial) + 平 píng (level). Even-handed dealing that favors no side: 公平竞争 "fair competition," 这不公平 "that's not fair." The everyday word for fairness.
公认	gōng rèn	universally acknowledged; generally recognized — 公 gōng (public; common) + 认 rèn. Publicly recognized. The word for consensus recognition: a writer is 公认 the best of her generation; a fact is 公认 to be true.
公式	gōng shì	Formula — Formula: "public pattern," a form that holds generally. 套公式 (tào gōngshì, "slot into the formula") is the study-hall phrase for plug-and-chug problem solving.
公司	gōng sī	company; firm; corporation — 公 gōng (public; common) + 司 sī (to manage; an office). A jointly run enterprise, a body managed in common: 一家公司 "a company." Note the contrast with the homophone 私 sī (private).
公园	gōng yuán	park; public garden — 公 gōng (public) + 园 yuán (garden). A garden held in common, open to all, as opposed to a private 花园 huāyuán. One of the first 公 words a beginner meets.
公章	gōng zhāng	company seal — A Chinese company binds itself with its red seal more than with a signature. 合同专用章 (hétong zhuānyòngzhāng) is a seal used only for contracts and is equally valid.
公正	gōng zhèng	just; impartial; fair-minded — 公 gōng (impartial) + 正 zhèng (upright). The public standard joined to the straight line: justice as judgment held to the same mark for all. Common of judges, verdicts, and arbiters: 公正的判决 "a fair verdict."
公众	gōng zhòng	the public; general public
功德	gōng dé	merit; accumulated virtue from good deeds; karmic credit — 功 gōng (meritorious achievement; effort; skill) + 德 dé (virtue; moral force). The accumulated virtue that comes from righteous acts , most alive in Buddhist and popular religious usage, where performing good deeds (捐款, helping the poor, building a temple) generates 功德 that benefits the giver in this…
功夫茶	gōng fu chá	gongfu tea ceremony
功课	gōng kè	homework; schoolwork; preparation; research done before a task — 功 gōng (work; effort) + 课 kè (lesson; course). Originally: the work (功夫) one does for one's lessons (课).
功劳	gōng láo	merit: credit for meritorious service — 功 gōng (merit) + 劳 láo (toil; labor). The credit owed to someone for hard work that produced real results. Used to acknowledge contribution: 这是大家的功劳 = this is everyone's accomplishment. The modest counterpart is 苦劳 kǔláo, credit merely for effort even without success.
功利	gōng lì	utility; benefit — the Mohist evaluative standard — 功 gōng (merit; effect) + 利 lì (benefit; profit). The criterion Mozi applied to every institution, practice, and policy: does it enrich the state, increase the population, and benefit the people? If not, it should be eliminated regardless of tradition or aesthetic value.
功率	gōng lǜ	power (physics)
功能	gōng néng	function; capability; feature — 功 gōng (work; merit) + 能 néng (ability; can). The work that something is able to do , its function. Appears everywhere: product features (软件功能 , software features), biology (心脏的功能 , the function of the heart), linguistics (句子的功能 , the function of a sentence).
功效	gōng xiào	efficacy: the effect produced — 功 gōng (effect) + 效 xiào (effect; result). The efficacy of a medicine, treatment, or method, the actual beneficial result it achieves. Especially common in medicine and traditional Chinese health: 这种药的功效很好 = this medicine is very effective.
攻读	gōng dú	to pursue (a degree, a field of study); to read-for — 攻 gōng (to attack; to apply oneself to) + 读. The verb for actively pursuing advanced study , a master's, a doctorate, a specialty. The 攻 conveys the sense of going at it, attacking the subject rather than passively absorbing.
供应	gōng yìng	to supply; to provide; supply — 供 gōng (to provide) + 应 yìng (to meet a need). To supply goods or services to meet a demand: 供应商 (supplier), 供应链 (supply chain), 供不应求 (supply falls short of demand). The yìng here is the answering of a need with provision.
供应链	gōng yìng liàn	supply chain
供应商	gōng yìng shāng	supplier
宫保鸡丁	gōng bǎo jī dīng	Kung Pao Chicken
恭敬	gōng jìng	respectful; deferential
恭喜	gōng xǐ	to congratulate; congratulations — 恭 gōng (respectful) + 喜 xǐ (joy). To respectfully share in someone's happiness. Doubled as 恭喜恭喜 it is the warm spoken "congratulations," and 恭喜发财 gōngxǐ fācái is the standard New Year greeting wishing wealth.
恭喜发财	gōng xǐ fā cái	The classic New Year greeting — The classic New Year greeting: "congratulations and may you get rich." Especially used in Southern China and the diaspora. The Cantonese pronunciation (gung1 hei2 faat3 choi4) is equally well-known internationally.
共	gòng	together; shared
共产党	Gòng chǎn dǎng	Communist Party, founded 1921
共和	gòng hé	republic; shared harmony
共鸣	gòng míng	resonance; to resonate
共同	gòng tóng	common; shared; jointly; together — 共 gòng (together; shared) + 同 tóng (same). Held in common by all parties: 共同的目标 "a shared goal," 共同努力 "to strive together." Stronger and more formal than 一起.
沟通	gōu tōng	to communicate; to bridge; to reach an understanding — 沟 gōu (a ditch; a channel; a conduit) + 通 (to connect; to make pass through). Originally to link two waterways by a channel; now overwhelmingly used for communication between people: to open a channel of understanding so meaning can flow back and forth.
狗叫	gǒu jiào	a dog barks — 狗 + 叫 (jiào, to call out, cry). The plain way to say a dog is barking. 汪汪 (wāngwāng) is the onomatopoeia for the sound itself.
狗粮	gǒu liáng	dog food
狗窝	gǒu wō	kennel, doghouse
狗熊	gǒu xióng	black bear; coward — 狗 + 熊 (xióng, bear). Literally a dog-bear, the colloquial name for the Asian black bear. Figuratively 狗熊 brands a coward, the opposite of a 英雄 (yīngxióng, hero), a pun that turns on the rhyme.
枸杞	gǒu qǐ	wolfberry / goji berry — the ubiquitous health supplement
孤魂野鬼	gū hún yě guǐ	lone soul, wild ghost — a wandering spirit with no family or anchor — 孤 gū (lone; orphaned; isolated) + 魂 hún (soul) + 野 yě (wild; untamed; in the open) + 鬼 guǐ (ghost). The classical and still-current phrase for a spirit with no one to care for it , no family performing rites, no ancestral hall, no spirit tablet.
古板	gǔ bǎn	old-fashioned and rigid; inflexible; stuffy — 古 gǔ (old-fashioned) + 板 bǎn (stiff, a board). Describes someone set in old ways and unwilling to bend: 思想古板 "old-fashioned in outlook," 古板的老师 "a stuffy, rigid teacher." Less about strangeness than about humourless inflexibility.
古城	gǔ chéng	ancient city; old town — 古 gǔ (ancient; old) + 城 chéng (city). A city or walled district that preserves its historic fabric. The phrase covers both whole heritage towns, such as the 丽江古城 (Lijiang Old Town) and 平遥古城 (Pingyao Old Town), and the old quarter inside a modern city.
古代	gǔ dài	ancient times: antiquity — 古 gǔ (ancient) + 代 dài (era). The distant past as a historical age, the time of the old dynasties, sages, and classics: 古代文学 "classical literature," 古代史 "ancient history." Stands opposite 现代 (modern) and 近代 (the recent, early-modern past).
古典	gǔ diǎn	classical (of the canonical tradition) — 古 gǔ (ancient) + 典 diǎn (canon, standard work). Pertaining to the classical heritage and its refined standards: 古典文学 "classical literature," 古典音乐 "classical music," 古典美 "classical beauty." Marks something as belonging to a venerable, canonical tradition rather than the modern or popular.
古董	gǔ dǒng	an antique; a curio; an old fogey (figurative) — 古 gǔ (ancient) + 董 dǒng (to collect, to keep). A prized old object valued for its age and craft: 古董家具 "antique furniture," 收藏古董 "to collect antiques." Used figuratively of a person hopelessly behind the times, an "old relic."
古尔邦节	Gǔ ěr bāng Jié	Eid al-Adha (Sacrifice Festival)
古怪	gǔ guài	eccentric; odd; strange and unconventional — 古 gǔ (old, out of step) + 怪 guài (strange). Describes a person, temper, or thing that is peculiar and hard to predict: 性格古怪 "an eccentric character," 古怪的脾气 "an odd temper." Carries a sense of not fitting ordinary norms, often a little hard to get along with.
古迹	gǔ jì	historic site
古今	gǔ jīn	past and present
古兰经	Gǔ lán jīng	the Quran
古老	gǔ lǎo	ancient; age-old; long-established — 古 gǔ (ancient) + 老 lǎo (old). Describes something of great age that has endured: an 古老的传统 age-old tradition, an 古老的文明 ancient civilisation, an 古老的传说 old legend. It praises longevity and depth of roots rather than fixing a date.
古琴	gǔ qín	scholar's seven-string zither
古树茶	gǔ shù chá	old-tree tea — material from trees over 100 years old
古文	gǔ wén	classical prose
古筝	gǔ zhēng	guzheng zither
骨	gǔ	bone
骨力	gǔ lì	bone-force; structural strength in a stroke
固定	gù dìng	fixed; to fix in place; regular — 固 gù (solid; firm) + 定 dìng (fixed). Held firmly in one place or pattern, not movable or variable: 固定收入 "a fixed income," 把架子固定在墙上 "to fix the shelf to the wall."
故宫	Gù gōng	the Forbidden City; the Old Palace — 故 (old, former) + 宫 (gōng, palace). Literally "the Old Palace," the formal Chinese name for the former imperial palace complex in Beijing, known in English as the Forbidden City. It was the residence of emperors of the Ming and Qing dynasties and is now the Palace Museum (故宫博物院, Gùgōng Bówùyuàn).
故旧	gù jiù	old friends; old acquaintances; former associates — 故 (gù, old; former; intentional; reason , a hand holding a stick, the old way of doing things) + 旧 (old). Old friends, specifically those known from a previous period of life. A literary and somewhat formal term; 老朋友 (lǎo péngyou) is the colloquial equivalent.
故人	gù rén	old friend; an acquaintance from former days — 故 (old, former) + 人 (rén, person). An old friend, specifically one known from an earlier period of life, now perhaps far away or long unseen. 故人 belongs to the literary and poetic register, common in classical verse about parting and reunion; the everyday equivalent is 老朋友 (lǎo péngyou).
故事书	gù shì shū	storybook; picture book with a narrative — 故事 gùshì (story) + 书 shū (book). A storybook , the most common type of book for children learning to read. 讲故事 jiǎng gùshì = "to tell a story" (the standard phrase). 听故事 tīng gùshì = "to listen to a story." 故事情节 gùshì qíngjié = "plot, storyline."
故土	gù tǔ	homeland; native soil
故乡	gù xiāng	hometown; native place; homeland — 故 (old, former) + 乡 (xiāng, countryside, native village). The place one is from, charged with the weight of belonging and longing. 故乡 is the literary and emotional register; the plainer 家乡 (jiāxiāng) is more neutral and everyday.
故意	gù yì	deliberately; on purpose; intentionally — 故 (deliberate) + 意 (yì, intention, will, mind). To do something on purpose, with conscious intent. Used adverbially: 他故意迟到 (he was late on purpose), 不是故意的 (didn't mean to, it wasn't intentional).
故障	gù zhàng	malfunction; breakdown; fault — 故 (cause; here, an underlying fault) + 障 (zhàng, obstruction, barrier). A malfunction in a machine or system: something with a cause behind it that obstructs normal operation.
顾面子	gù miàn zi	to consider face; to save face for someone; to protect dignity — 顾 gù (to consider; to look after; to take into account). 顾面子 means to take face into consideration when making decisions or acting , whether one's own face or someone else's. More deliberate and conscious than simply 给面子. Suggests a thoughtful navigation of face dynamics.
顾问	gù wèn	adviser; consultant — 顾 gù (to look after; to consider) + 问 wèn (to ask). The person one turns to and asks for counsel: 法律顾问 "legal adviser," 技术顾问 "technical consultant." Literally the one whom you regard and question.
卦	guà	hexagram; trigram; the unit of the system — 卦 guà is the technical term for both the six-line hexagrams and the three-line trigrams (distinguished as 六爻卦 liù yáo guà and 三爻卦 sān yáo guà when precision is needed). The character depicts a hand 卜 over a ground 土 , divination.
挂号	guà hào	To register at a hospital — To register at a hospital: hang a number. 挂个专家号 is booking the senior specialist; 挂不上号 is the national complaint about famous hospitals.
挂席救船	guà xí jiù chuán	hanging her mat to calm the storm — A convoy of ships was caught in a typhoon. Crew members and merchants, terrified, invoked Mazu. Her red skirt (or in some versions a red lantern) was seen at the masthead: she was present. The storm subsided.
拐	guǎi	to turn (a corner)
怪不得	guài bu de	"That explains it!" Synonym for 难怪 with the same function — "That explains it!" Synonym for 难怪 with the same function. Slightly more drawn out and emphatic. 怪不得他不来 ("that's why he didn't come").
关闭	guān bì	to close (formal)
关灯	guān dēng	to turn off the light
关帝	Guān Dì	Emperor Guan — deified general, god of war and righteousness — Guan Yu 关羽 (d. 220 CE), the historical Three Kingdoms general celebrated for his loyalty and righteousness 义 yì, was progressively deified over the Tang, Song, and Ming dynasties, eventually receiving the title 关圣帝君 (Holy Emperor Guan).
关汉卿	Guān Hàn qīng	greatest Yuan playwright
关怀	guān huái	to show concern and care for (warm, often institutional)
关机	guān jī	to power off (a device); to switch off a phone
关键	guān jiàn	key, crucial, the decisive hinge
关键在于	guān jiàn zài yú	"The key is" or "what matters is — "The key is" or "what matters is." Marks the core of an argument. Useful when you want to highlight the deciding factor among several you have just laid out: 关键在于时间 ("the key is timing").
关节	guān jié	joint (of the body); juncture — 关 guān (to join; pass) + 节 jié (joint). An anatomical joint, the hinge where bones meet: 膝关节 "knee joint." By extension, a critical juncture in a matter, the place where things turn.
关口	guān kǒu	strategic pass; checkpoint
关联	guān lián	correlation; connection; to be associated with — 关 (connection) + 联 (to join; to link). More formal and analytical than 关系 , used for logical correlations, data relationships, and systematic connections. Common in academic, research, and analytical writing. 相互关联 xiānghù guānlián = mutually correlated; interconnected.
关门	guān mén	to close the door; (of a shop) to close for the day or for good
关起来	guān qǐ lái	to lock up; to shut in; to imprison
关系到	guān xi dào	to affect; to have a bearing on; to be relevant to — 关系到 (lit. "the relationship reaches to") means that something has a bearing on, affects, or is relevant to something else. Used for high-stakes matters where the connection is important to note explicitly. Common in official, journalistic, and persuasive writing.
关系网	guān xi wǎng	network of connections; relationship network — 网 wǎng = net; network. 关系网 is the spider's web of personal connections , who you know, who they know, and the overlapping obligations and trust that run through the web.
关系重大	guān xi zhòng dà	of great importance; high-stakes; consequential — A common fixed expression used to characterize something as having major consequences or importance. 重大 zhòngdà = major, significant. Used in formal registers to signal that a decision, situation, or event carries real weight and should be treated with corresponding seriousness.
关心	guān xīn	to care about; concern; care — 关 (to concern; to be connected to) + 心 (heart). The heart connected to something , caring. One of the most important relational verbs in Chinese. Both the action of caring and the expression of concern. High frequency in family, friendship, and professional contexts.
关于	guān yú	about; concerning; with respect to — 关 guān (to connect) + 于 yú (to). Marks the subject matter of a book, talk, or document: 关于历史的书 "a book about history." Where 对于 frames an attitude, 关于 frames a topic.
关羽	guān yǔ	Guan Yu — the archetype of loyalty and honour — The red-faced sworn brother of Liu Bei, towering in loyalty and martial valour, who refuses every bribe and honour Cao Cao offers to keep him.
关注	guān zhù	to pay attention to; to follow closely; attention; concern — 关 (to concern; to connect) + 注 (to pour; to focus , water pouring into a single point). Focused, sustained attention toward something important. In digital contexts, 关注 means to follow someone's account (social media).
观沧海	Guān Cāng hǎi	"Gazing at the Vast Sea" — an early landscape poem — Written after Cao Cao's northern campaign of 207 CE, often cited as the first fully realized landscape poem in the tradition. The poet gazes from a cliff and the sea, swallowing sun and moon, becomes the mirror of his own ambition.
观察	guān chá	to observe; to watch closely; observation — 观 guān (to look) + 察 chá (to examine). To watch with attention in order to learn something: a scientist, a doctor, or a detective 观察 their subject. Stronger and more deliberate than simply watching.
观点	guān diǎn	viewpoint; point of view; standpoint — 观 guān (view) + 点 diǎn (point). The particular angle from which you regard an issue: 我同意你的观点 "I agree with your view." The opinion you argue from, lighter and more debatable than a fixed 观念.
观看	guān kàn	to watch; to view (an event or show) — 观 guān (to view) + 看 kàn (to look). To watch a performance, game, or spectacle. A slightly formal partner to the everyday 看 kàn, fitting for events that draw an audience.
观念	guān niàn	concept; notion; ingrained idea — 观 guān (view) + 念 niàn (thought). A deeper, more settled idea than a 观点: the concepts and assumptions one lives by, often slow to change. 传统观念 "traditional notions," 时间观念 "a sense of time / punctuality."
观世音	Guān shì yīn	Guanyin — bodhisattva of compassion, the most beloved in China — Sanskrit Avalokiteśvara ("the one who perceives the sounds of the world"). One of the most significant transformations in Chinese Buddhist history: in Indian and Central Asian Buddhism, Avalokiteśvara is male.
观无量寿经	Guān Wú liàng shòu Jīng	Contemplation Sutra — sixteen visualization practices — Also called the 观经 Guān Jīng. A narrative framed by Queen Vaidehi's suffering (her son has imprisoned her husband, King Bimbisāra): she asks Śākyamuni to teach her how to reach a realm free from suffering.
观想	guān xiǎng	visualization — contemplating the Pure Land and Amitābha in detail — 观 guān (contemplate; observe) + 想 xiǎng (imagine; think). The more advanced and demanding practice outlined in the 观无量寿经 (Contemplation Sutra): systematic visualization of the Western Pure Land (its jeweled trees, lotus pools, the thrones, the light) and ultimately of Amitābha himself in full detail…
观音	Guān yīn	the bodhisattva 观自在 by another name
观众	guān zhòng	audience; spectators; viewers — 观 guān (to watch) + 众 zhòng (the many, a crowd). The watching crowd: the audience at a show, the spectators at a game, the viewers of a broadcast. Its cousins are 听众 tīngzhòng (listeners) and 读者 dúzhě (readers).
官	guān	official, office
官方	guān fāng	official; governmental
棺材	guān cai	coffin — the most important object in traditional death culture — The coffin (棺材 guāncai) was traditionally a major family investment: solid wood, red lacquered, with auspicious carvings. The quality of the coffin reflected both the family's means and its filial devotion.
管理	guǎn lǐ	to manage; to administer; management — 管 guǎn (to govern; to be in charge of) + 理 lǐ (to put in order). To run and oversee an organization, system, or resource: 管理公司 "to manage a company," 时间管理 "time management."
贯穿	guàn chuān	to run through, to permeate
光	guāng	light, ray
光杆动词	guāng gǎn dòng cí	the bare verb: habits, facts, general truths — The unmarked verb is the default for habitual actions and standing facts, usually anchored by a frequency word: 我每天喝咖啡 (I drink coffee every day), 他住在北京 (he lives in Beijing), 地球绕太阳转 (the earth orbits the sun). No particle needed; adding 了 here would wrongly signal a completed event.
光明	guāng míng	light; radiance; a bright future — 光 guāng (light; beam) + 明 míng (bright). More elevated and abstract than 明亮. Used in spiritual and political registers: 光明大道 "the road of light" (a righteous path), 走向光明 "moving toward the light." The opposite is 黑暗 hēi'àn darkness.
光速	guāng sù	The speed of light — The speed of light. Colloquially any blinding speed: 光速下班 is leaving work the second the clock strikes, a usage every office knows.
光阴	guāng yīn	time; the passing days (literally "light and shadow") — 光 guāng (light) + 阴 yīn (shadow). The most poetic word for time in Chinese, naming the days by the very thing that measures them: the alternation of sunlight and shade, day and night, sweeping across the ground like the shadow on a sundial.
广东	Guǎng dōng	Guangdong Province — 广 (guǎng, broad, expansive) + 东 (east). "The broad eastern region": the province that was the easternmost of the early southern territories brought under Tang-Song dynasty control.
广寒宫	Guǎng hán Gōng	the Palace of Vast Cold — the Moon Palace
逛街	guàng jiē	to stroll around shopping — 逛 (to stroll, wander) + 街 (street). A verb-object pair: to roam the streets with no fixed errand, window-shopping as much as buying. The default leisure activity in any Chinese city.
归宿	guī sù	final home; where one belongs
规定	guī dìng	to stipulate; a rule; a regulation — 规 guī (rule; compass) + 定 dìng (to fix). To lay down a rule in advance, and the rule so laid down. 按照规定 ànzhào guīdìng "according to the regulations." The formal, institutional cousin of 决定.
规矩	guī ju	unwritten rules; etiquette
硅胶圈	guī jiāo quān	silicone seal ring — 硅胶 (guījiāo) is silicone; 密封圈 (mìfēngquān) is any sealing ring or gasket. Food-grade silicone should not smell; 异味 (yìwèi, an odd smell) and 发黄 (fāhuáng, yellowing) are the common complaints. 漏水 (lòushuǐ) is a leak.
鬼鬼祟祟	guǐ guǐ suì suì	furtive; sneaky; behaving suspiciously — AABB reduplication of 鬼祟 (guǐsuì, surreptitious; stealthy). The reduplication intensifies and gives the phrase a rhythmic, memorable quality. 祟 suì originally means the malign influence of spirits.
鬼话	guǐ huà	ghost talk — nonsense; lies — 鬼 guǐ + 话 huà (speech; talk). Literally "ghost speech" , nonsense, lies, deceptive talk. The logic: ghosts mislead and confuse; speech that misleads and confuses is ghost-speech. 说鬼话 (to speak ghost talk) = to talk rubbish, to lie.
鬼魂	guǐ hún	ghost; spirit — the disembodied soul — 鬼 guǐ (ghost) + 魂 hún (soul; spirit). The ghost or spirit of a deceased person , the closest equivalent to the English "ghost" in its neutral, descriptive sense. Less colloquially charged than 鬼 alone. 闹鬼 (nàoguǐ, spirits making trouble) describes a haunting; 闹鬼的地方 is a haunted place.
鬼月	guǐ yu è	Ghost Month — Ghost Month: the seventh month of the lunar calendar, during which the gates of the underworld are held to open and the spirits of the dead roam freely. Considered an inauspicious period for weddings, starting new businesses, moving house, or making major decisions.
贵	guì	noble, honored; expensive
贵公司	guì gōng sī	your company (honorific) — The polite way to refer to the other side's company; 贵厂 (guì chǎng) for a factory. Your own side is plain 我们公司 or 我们. 贵 is the same character as "expensive," here meaning "honoured."
桂林	Guì lín	Guilin (canonical 山水 site)
滚蛋	gǔn dàn	get lost, scram — 滚 (gǔn, to roll) + 蛋: a blunt order to get out. The image is of telling someone to roll away like a dropped egg. It is rude and forceful, used only in anger.
国风	guó fēng	Airs of the States , the 160 regional folk songs that form the first and largest section of the 诗经. The word 风 carries the sense of "wind" or "air" , something that moves through a region, picking up its local character.
国父	guó fù	father of the nation
国歌	guó gē	national anthem — 国 (nation) plus 歌 (song). The official song of a country, sung standing at ceremonies and medal podiums.
国画	guó huà	traditional Chinese painting — 国 guó (nation) + 画 huà (painting). The native tradition of brush-and-ink painting on silk or paper, kin to calligraphy. Short for 中国画 Zhōngguóhuà, and set against 油画 yóuhuà, the imported oil-painting tradition.
国际	guó jì	international — 国 guó + 际 jì (boundary; border; between). "Between countries." The standard word for anything international: 国际机场 international airport · 国际关系 international relations · 国际化 internationalization.
国民党	Guó mín dǎng	Kuomintang / Nationalist Party (KMT)
国情	guó qíng	national conditions; the state of the nation — 国 (country) + 情 (conditions, circumstances, feelings). The specific circumstances of a given country , its history, population, resources, culture, and current state.
国土	guó tǔ	national territory
国外	guó wài	abroad
国王	guó wáng	king (of a country)
国语	Guó yǔ	Taiwan term for Mandarin
果	guǒ	fruit; result
果仁	guǒ rén	fruit kernel; nut meat (general term) — 果 guǒ (fruit) + 仁. The general category term: any kernel or "nutmeat." Wang Yangming and other Neo-Confucians explicitly used this image as the metaphor for moral 仁: a 果仁 is what gives the fruit its capacity to generate the next tree, just as 仁 is what gives a person the capacity to relate fully to…
果肉	guǒ ròu	fruit pulp, flesh of fruit — 果 (guǒ, fruit) + 肉 (flesh). The soft edible part between the skin and the pit, the meat of the fruit.
果实	guǒ shí	fruit; the fruits (of labor) — 果 guǒ (fruit) + 实 shí (the ripened, full thing). The fruit a plant bears, and by extension the rewards of effort: 劳动的果实 "the fruits of labor." This is the seed-and-fruit sense of 实 made explicit.
过错	guò cuò	fault, wrongdoing
过分	guò fèn	excessive; going too far
过关	guò guān	to pass the pass; to clear a hurdle, exam, or test
过河	guò hé	to cross a river — 过 (guò, to cross) + 河 (river). A verb-object pair, to get to the far bank. It anchors several proverbs, since crossing water is the classic image of a one-way commitment.
