教师	jiào shī	teacher (formal); educator — 教 jiào (to teach) + 师 shī (master; expert). The formal word for a teacher as a profession, common in writing and titles. In direct address the warmer 老师 lǎoshī is used instead.
教室	jiào shì	classroom — 教 jiào (to teach) + 室 shì (room). The room where teaching happens. Watch the tone of 室 (shì), and do not confuse it with 教师 jiàoshī (teacher); the two words differ only in the second syllable.
教授	jiào shòu	professor
教书	jiāo shū	to teach (as a profession); to be a teacher — 教 jiāo (to teach) + 书 shū (books). Literally "to teach books," the standard way to say one teaches for a living. Note the first-tone jiāo here, the colloquial verb reading.
教堂	jiào táng	church (the building) — 教 jiào (religion) + 堂 táng (hall). The hall of worship, a church building. The community of believers, by contrast, is 教会 jiàohuì.
教训	jiào xun	a lesson (learned the hard way); to reprimand — 教 jiào (to teach) + 训 xùn (to admonish). As a noun, the lesson drawn from a mistake or setback: 吸取教训 "to learn the lesson." As a verb, to scold or take someone to task.
教育	jiào yù	education; to educate — 教 jiào (to teach) + 育 yù (to raise; to nurture). The whole enterprise of teaching and bringing up, as both a noun and a verb: 高等教育 "higher education," 教育孩子 "to bring up a child." This is the formal jiào reading.
窖	jiào	The fermentation pit — The fermentation pit: the earthen or stone pit in which the fermented grain mash (酒醅 jiǔpēi) undergoes anaerobic fermentation. Old pits with established microbial ecosystems are considered the most valuable assets of a distillery.
阶梯价	jiē tī jià	tiered pricing — "Staircase price": the unit price falls as quantity rises. Asking for a tier table is the cleanest way to learn the factory's real cost curve, and it moves the conversation from "lower the price" to "at what quantity."
接班	jiē bān	to take over a shift
接触	jiē chù	to come into contact, to engage with — 接 (to touch) + 触 (chù, to make contact). Two surfaces meet, or two people start dealing with each other. Used for physical contact and for first encounters with ideas, fields, or strangers.
接电话	jiē diàn huà	to answer the phone — 接 (receive) + 电话 (diànhuà, phone call). To take up a ringing call and connect the line. The hand that closes the gap now closes a circuit.
接近	jiē jìn	to approach; to be close to; to approximate — 接 jiē (to connect; to receive; to meet) + 近 (near). To connect by closing the gap , a more formal and abstract verb than 靠近.
接连	jiē lián	one after another; in succession; repeatedly — 接 jiē (to join; to connect) + 连 lián (in a series). An adverb meaning that events occur in close, repeated succession, each new one arriving before the last has finished registering. Often reinforced as 接连不断 (continuously, without a break).
接纳	jiē nà	to accept (into a group)
接人	jiē rén	to pick someone up, to meet someone — 接 (to meet) + 人 (rén, person). To go to a station, airport, or door and collect a person who is arriving. The everyday counterpart to ceremonial 迎接.
接受	jiē shòu	to accept, to receive — 接 (receive) + 受 (shòu, to be given). The hand takes what is offered and keeps it. Used for offers, opinions, criticism, conditions, anything you take in and agree to.
接受不了	jiē shòu bù liǎo	cannot accept — A clear, polite refusal. Stronger than 有点难, softer than 不行. Pair it with the reason and what you could accept.
接着	jiē zhe	next, then, to follow on — 接 (join) + 着 (zhe, continuing aspect). One action takes the baton from the one before it. Use it to mean "and then, right after that," or "go on (doing something)."
揭谛揭谛，波罗揭谛	jiē dì jiē dì , bō luó jiē dì	gone, gone, gone beyond — The opening of the closing mantra. Even taken out of liturgical context, these lines carry a strong recognizable rhythm in Chinese Buddhist culture; readers and listeners hear the rest of the mantra by implication when these first phrases sound.
街道	jiē dào	street; (urban) subdistrict — 街 (street) + 道 (road, way). The everyday word for a city street. In administrative use it also names the smallest urban division, the 街道办事处 (jiēdào bànshìchù), a neighborhood subdistrict office.
街灯	jiē dēng	street lamp
街坊	jiē fang	neighbor; the neighborhood — 街 (street) + 坊 (a city ward). The people who share your street, your neighbors in the old close-knit sense. 街坊邻居 (jiēfang línjū) is the warm collective phrase for the whole neighborhood.
街角	jiē jiǎo	street corner — 街 jiē (street) + 角 jiǎo (corner). The physical corner where two streets meet. In narrative, the street corner is a classic site of waiting, meeting, and chance encounter.
街区	jiē qū	city block; neighborhood — 街 (street) + 区 (district, zone). A block or built-up quarter bounded by streets. Common in urban planning and real-estate talk: 商业街区 (shāngyè jiēqū) a commercial district.
街头	jiē tóu	street corner; on the streets — 街 (street) + 头 (head, end). The mouth of a street or the streetside in general. It frames public, open-air activity: 街头艺人 (jiētóu yìrén) a street performer, 街头篮球 (jiētóu lánqiú) streetball.
节骨眼	jié gǔ yǎn	critical juncture; the pivot point of a situation — 节 jié (joint) + 骨 gǔ (bone) + 眼. The eye at the joint of the bone , the precise small spot at which a thing turns. Colloquial; almost always paired with 上 in 节骨眼上 "at the critical juncture."
节令	jié lìng	seasonal timing
节目	jié mù	program; show; performance item — 节 (jié, section; festival; segment) + 目 (item). A listed performance item or broadcast segment. 电视节目 (TV program); 春节联欢晚会节目 (Spring Festival Gala items). The word traveled from variety show programs into streaming, where 综艺节目 means a variety program and 节目单 is a program schedule.
节日	jié rì	holiday; festival; special day — 节 jié (node; joint; festival) + 日 rì (day). The standard word for a festival or public holiday. 传统节日 chuántǒng jiérì = traditional holiday (Spring Festival, Mid-Autumn, Dragon Boat). 法定节假日 fǎdìng jiàjià rì = statutory public holiday. 情人节 Qíngrén Jié = Valentine's Day (lit. "lover's day").
节日祝福	jié rì zhù fú	holiday greetings — A short greeting at Chinese New Year and the Mid-Autumn Festival maintains the relationship between orders. 新年快乐 or 中秋快乐 with a line of thanks is enough.
节省	jié shěng	to save; to cut down on; to use sparingly — 节 jié (restrain) + 省 shěng (to economize; to spare). To save money, time, or effort by using less of it: 节省时间 "save time," 节省开支 "cut expenses." The hands-on, daily-budget cousin of 节约.
节用	jié yòng	frugality; moderate use of resources — Mozi condemned extravagant funerals, elaborate music, and luxury consumption on utilitarian grounds: if a practice does not benefit the people, enrich the state, and increase the population, it is harmful. His critique of Confucian ritual was direct and sustained.
节约	jié yu ē	to economize; to conserve; thrift — 节 jié (restrain) + 约 yuē (to bind; to limit). To hold consumption within bounds, especially of shared or large-scale resources: 节约用水 "conserve water," 节约能源 "conserve energy." The word of public campaigns and stated principle.
节葬	jié zàng	simple burial; against elaborate funerary practice — Three inches of wood for the coffin; three garments for the body; no grave mound. 节葬 is the direct application of 节用 to death. It was a pointed attack on the bronze-vessel burial culture of Mozi's era , the practice of entombing rulers with vast quantities of ritual bronzes, servants, and goods.
节制	jié zhì	to restrain; to be moderate; temperance — 节 jié (restrain) + 制 zhì (to control). To hold one's appetites or conduct within limits: 饮食节制 "moderation in eating and drinking." The inward, moral side of 节, keeping a thing from running past its proper bound.
节奏	jié zòu	rhythm, pace (of life)
结果	jié guǒ	result; outcome; as a result — 结 jié (to conclude) + 果 guǒ (fruit; result). The outcome a process ties off into. Also a connective: "as it turned out." Read this way it is jiéguǒ; the verb "to bear fruit" reads jiē guǒ instead.
结合	jié hé	to combine; to integrate; to unite — 结 jié (to tie) + 合 hé (to join). To bring two things together so they work as one: 理论与实践结合 "to combine theory with practice." Abstract and constructive, used of ideas, methods, and elements, not of physical knots.
结婚	jié hūn	to marry; to get married — 结 jié (to tie) + 婚 hūn (marriage). To tie the marriage bond, the standard word for getting married. A separable verb: 结过婚 "have been married," 结了婚 "got married." Its opposite is 离婚 líhūn (to divorce).
结论	jié lùn	conclusion (of an argument or inquiry) — 结 jié (to tie, to knot, to conclude) + 论. The conclusion that an argument or investigation arrives at: the point where reasoning is tied off. 下结论 xià jiélùn "to draw a conclusion," 得出结论 déchū jiélùn "to reach a conclusion."
结清	jié qīng	to settle in full: to pay off
结实	jiē shi	sturdy; durable; (of a body) strong and solid — 结 jiē (to bear fruit; to form) + 实 shi (solid). Built to last, not flimsy: 这张桌子很结实 "this table is very sturdy." Of a person it means strongly built: 身体结实 "physically robust." Note the toneless reading jiēshi here, distinct from 结 jié elsewhere.
结束	jié shù	to end; to finish; to conclude — 结 jié (to tie off) + 束 shù (to bind; a bundle). To bring an event or period to its close: 会议结束了 "the meeting is over." Its opposite is 开始 kāishǐ (to begin).
结缘	jié yuán	to form a karmic bond; to connect; to begin a meaningful relationship — 结 jié (to tie; to form; to knot) + 缘. To tie a karmic thread , to form a connection that carries the weight of 缘分.
姐夫	jiě fu	elder sister's husband; brother-in-law — 姐 (elder sister) plus 夫 (husband) names the man married to one's older sister. Chinese kinship marks the relation precisely, distinct from 妹夫 (mèifu), a younger sister's husband.
姐姐	jiě jie	elder sister — The doubled character is the everyday word for one's older sister. The second 姐 takes a neutral tone, giving the warm, soft cadence Chinese kinship terms favor.
姐妹	jiě mèi	sisters; female friends — 姐 (elder sister) plus 妹 (younger sister) yokes the two into a single word for "sisters" as a pair or group, and by extension a tight circle of close female friends.
解除	jiě chú	to remove; to lift; to terminate (a state or obligation) — 解 jiě + 除 chú (to remove; to do away with). To untie and clear away. Used for lifting a binding state or formal tie: 解除警报 = lift an alert; 解除合同 = terminate a contract; 解除武装 = disarm; 解除疲劳 = relieve fatigue. More formal than 解开, it dissolves an arrangement or condition rather than a physical knot.
解答	jiě dá	to answer; to solve and explain; a solution — 解 jiě + 答 dá (to answer; to reply). To unravel and answer. Used for working out and explaining the answer to a question or problem, especially in study: 解答疑问 = answer a question; 标准解答 = the standard solution.
解读	jiě dú	to interpret; to construe; to make sense of — 解 jiě (to untie; to dissolve; to explain) + 读. The standard verb for interpretive reading , of a text, a result, a signal, a person's behaviour. Where 阅读 is comprehending what is plainly there, 解读 is going after what is implicit.
解放	jiě fàng	to liberate; to free; liberation — 解 jiě + 放 fàng (to release; to set free). To untie and let go. The standard word for liberating people from bondage, oppression, or restraint: 解放思想 = free up one's thinking; 解放双手 = free one's hands (from drudgery).
解封	jiě fēng	to unseal; to lift a lockdown
解雇	jiě gù	to dismiss; to fire (an employee) — 解 jiě + 雇 gù (to hire; to employ). To undo the hiring. The formal word for dismissing someone from a job, the legal counterpart to the colloquial 炒鱿鱼 chǎo yóuyú (lit. stir-fry the squid). 解雇 separates the worker from the employment tie, the same "undoing a bind" logic applied to a contract of work.
解决	jiě ju é	to solve; to resolve; to settle — 解 jiě + 决 jué (to decide; to settle). To undo and settle. The all-purpose verb for solving a problem or settling a matter: 解决问题 = solve a problem; 解决困难 = overcome a difficulty; 想办法解决 = find a way to settle it.
解开	jiě kāi	to untie; to undo; to unfasten — 解 jiě + 开 kāi (to open). The most literal 解-word: to untie a knot, undo a button, unfasten a rope. By extension it covers loosening any bind, even a metaphorical one: 解开心结 = untie the knot in one's heart, resolve an inner tension. The picture of separating the bound thing is right on the surface.
解渴	jiě kě	to quench thirst — 解 jiě + 渴 kě (thirst; thirsty). To undo thirst. The everyday verb-object phrase for slaking thirst: 喝水解渴 = drink water to quench thirst; 西瓜很解渴 = watermelon is very thirst-quenching.
解释	jiě shì	to explain; to interpret; an explanation — 解 jiě + 释 shì (to release; to elucidate). To open a thing up and let its sense out. The everyday word for explaining: 解释原因 = explain the reason; 做出解释 = give an explanation. It also covers defending oneself: 解释误会 = clear up a misunderstanding. Where 解决 settles a problem, 解释 makes a meaning plain.
解脱	jiě tuō	liberation; release; to be freed from bondage or constraint — 解 jiě (to untie; to release; to dissolve) + 脱 tuō (to escape; to shed; to free oneself from). In Buddhist context: liberation from 轮回, the shedding of karmic bondage and conditioned existence.
界面	jiè miàn	interface; the surface between two systems — 界 (boundary; boundary marker) + 面 (surface; face). The surface at the boundary between two things. In technology and computing: the user interface (用户界面 yònghù jièmiàn), screen interface. In science: the interface between two phases or systems.
借给	jiè gěi	to lend (to someone) — 借 (lend) + 给 (give to). Pinning 给 after 借 fixes the direction outward: the subject is the one handing the object over.
借鉴	jiè jiàn	to learn from, draw on as a model — 借 (to borrow) + 鉴 (mirror, to reflect on). To borrow another's experience as a mirror for your own, taking what is useful from a model or precedent.
借口	jiè kǒu	excuse, pretext — 借 (to borrow) + 口 (mouth). A "borrowed mouth," words you reach for that are not the true reason. As a noun it is the excuse itself; as a verb, "to use as a pretext."
借钱	jiè qián	to borrow / lend money — 借 (borrow/lend) + 钱 (money). A verb-object pair whose direction depends on the prepositions around it: 向他借钱 is borrowing from him, 借钱给他 is lending to him.
借条	jiè tiáo	IOU, written loan note
借用	jiè yòng	to borrow for use
借助	jiè zhù	to draw on, with the help of — 借 (to make use of) + 助 (aid). To accomplish something by leaning on an external tool, force, or person. Formal register, common in writing.
今	jīn	now; the present
今世	jīn shì	this life; the present life; this age — 今 jīn (present; now) + 世 shì (life; age). The life one is living now, set against 前世 (a former life) and 来世 (the next): 今生今世 "this very life." Can also mean the present age in a more general sense.
今天	jīn tiān	today — 今 jīn (now; the present moment) + 天 tiān (day). The word for "today" encodes "the now-day." Chinese time words are nouns that typically occupy the topic position at the start of a sentence, before the verb; they work differently from English adverbs like "today."
今天主要想谈	jīn tiān zhǔ yào xiǎng tán	today I mainly want to discuss — Sets the agenda. Saying it early, with numbered points, is the strongest move a visitor has: it decides what gets talked about and in what order. Follow with 第一 (dì-yī), 第二, 第三, and close each topic with 这个先到这里 ("let's leave this one here for now").
斤	jīn	The market half-kilo — The market half-kilo. 一斤多少钱 ("how much per jin?") is the essential market question. Originally a pictograph of an axe, which is still its job as a radical.
斤两	jīn liǎng	weight; one's true worth or standing — 斤 jīn (catty) + 两 liǎng (tael), the two traditional units of weight, hence "weight" in general, and figuratively a person's substance or worth. 掂量掂量自己的斤两 (to weigh up one's own worth) means to take an honest measure of what one can really do.
金刚经	Jīng āng jīng	the Diamond Sutra; the foundational text of Chan Buddhism — The full title is 金刚般若波罗蜜经 (Jīngāng bōrě bōluómì jīng), the Diamond Prajñāpāramitā Sutra. The diamond image is the cutting power of 般若 that severs every clinging.
金花	jīn huā	golden flowers — the prized mold colonies in Fu Brick
金黄	jīn huáng	golden yellow
金钱	jīn qián	money (formal/literary) — 金 jīn (gold) + 钱 qián (coin; money). A slightly elevated or literary term for money. Used in formal writing and in philosophical contexts: 金钱不是万能的 "Money is not omnipotent." Compare: 钱 (everyday "money"), 金钱 (money as an abstraction or moral question), 财富 cáifù (wealth/riches).
金石学	jīn shí xu é	Epigraphy — Epigraphy: the study of inscriptions on bronze and stone. The scholarly discipline that underlies serious seal carving: to carve seal script well, one must have studied the ancient bronzes and stone inscriptions from which the script derives.
金芽	jīn yá	golden bud — the premium single-bud grade
仅仅	jǐn jǐn	merely, only (quantity minimizer)
尽管	jǐng uǎn	even though, despite
尽力	jìn lì	to do one's utmost; to try one's best — 尽 jìn (to exhaust; to the limit) + 力 lì (effort). To use up all the effort one has, to spare nothing: 我会尽力 "I will do my best," 尽力而为 "do all one can."
尽人事听天命	jìn rén shì tīng tiān mìng	do what you can as a human, then accept Heaven's decree
尽头	jìn tóu	the end; the very end; the limit — 尽 jìn (to exhaust, to use up) + 头 tóu (end). The point where a thing runs out: the far end of a road, the limit of a stretch. 路的尽头 "the end of the road."
紧跟	jǐng ēn	follow closely; keep up with — 紧 (close, tight) + 跟 (gēn, to follow). To follow with no gap. Physical (stay right behind) or figurative (紧跟时代, keep pace with the times).
紧急	jǐn jí	urgent; emergency — 紧 (pressing) + 急 (jí, hurried, anxious). Two words for urgency stacked together, intensifying each other. The register of sirens, evacuations, and red-banner notices: 紧急出口 (emergency exit), 紧急情况 (emergency situation).
紧紧	jǐn jǐn	tightly; closely; firmly — The doubled form intensifies the grip. 紧紧 describes an action done with full, unrelenting tightness, often a hold or an embrace that won't let go.
紧张	jǐn zhāng	nervous; tense; in short supply — 紧 (taut) + 张 (zhāng, to stretch open). A surface pulled tight from both ends. Used for nerves before an exam, the tension in a thriller, and resources stretched thin (时间紧张, time is short).
近代	jìn dài	early modern era (1840–1949)
近景	jìn jǐng	foreground; near scene
近来	jìn lái	recently; lately; in recent times — 近 jìn (near; close; recent) + 来 lái (coming; approaching the present). "What has come near," the recent past approaching the present. A slightly more formal or literary synonym of 最近 zuìjìn. Common in formal speech, writing, and news contexts.
近年来	jìn nián lái	in recent years; over recent years — 近 (recent) + 年 (year) + 来 (coming from , a directional particle marking elapsed duration). Time that has been coming toward the present moment across recent years. A standard phrase in news, academic writing, and formal speech: 近年来，中国的城市化速度加快 (in recent years, China's urbanization has accelerated).
近期	jìn qī	in the near future; recently; in the near term — 近 (near) + 期 qī (a period of time; a term; a scheduled point). A time period that is near , used for both the recent past and the near future depending on context. 近期内 (within the near term); 近期计划 (near-term plans). The period implied is typically weeks to a few months, not years.
近视	jìn shì	nearsighted; myopic — 近 (near) + 视 shì (to see; vision). Seeing only what is near , the clinical and colloquial term for myopia. 近视眼镜 (myopia glasses , corrective lenses); 高度近视 (high myopia, severe nearsightedness).
进步	jìn bù	progress; to improve; progressive — 进 jìn (to advance) + 步 bù (a step). To move forward step by step, hence to improve. Works as a verb (你进步了 "you've improved") and as a noun (很大的进步 "great progress").
进城	jìn chéng	to go into town; to go to the city — 进 jìn (to enter) + 城 chéng (city; town). A verb-object phrase: to go into the city. The expression preserves the old sense of the city as a bounded place one literally enters, crossing from outside to inside.
进度	jìn dù	progress — 跟进 (gēnjìn) is to follow up, 催 (cuī) to chase. Ask for photos as well as words; a picture of goods on the line settles more than 快了 (kuài le, "nearly there").
进化	jìn huà	Evolution — Evolution: advance-transformation. 进化论 is Darwin's theory; gamers use 进化 for level-ups, which Darwin might have minded. The neutral scholarly variant is 演化.
进口	jìn kǒu	to import; imports; entrance (of a place) — 进 jìn (to take in) + 口 kǒu (mouth; opening). To bring goods in across a border, the opposite of 出口 chūkǒu (to export). The same word also names a physical "entrance," the opening you go in through.
进来	jìn lái	to come in (toward the speaker) — 进 jìn (to enter) + 来 lái (to come). To enter moving toward where the speaker is. 请进来 "please come in" is what you say from inside a room to someone at the door.
进去	jìn qù	to go in (away from the speaker) — 进 jìn (to enter) + 去 qù (to go). To enter moving away from where the speaker stands. From outside a building you tell someone 你先进去 "you go in first."
进入	jìn rù	to enter; to go into (a place or stage) — 进 jìn (to enter) + 入 rù (to go in). A more formal "to enter," used of physical spaces and of abstract stages alike: 进入房间 "enter the room," 进入新阶段 "enter a new phase."
进士	jìn shì	palace graduate — the highest examination degree — The degree conferred on all who passed the palace examination (殿试). Literally "advanced to scholar" or "presented scholar." Receiving the 进士 degree placed a candidate in the imperial bureaucracy; specific posting and rank depended on one's place in the final ranking.
进香	jìn xiāng	to make a pilgrimage offering incense
进行	jìn xíng	to carry out; to be in progress; to conduct — 进 jìn (to advance; to enter) + 行 xíng. The all-purpose formal verb for "to carry out" , used before two-syllable activity nouns: 进行讨论 "carry out a discussion," 进行调查 "conduct an investigation," 进行比赛 "hold a competition." Functions like a grammaticalized "do" for activities that are too dignified for…
进行中	jìn xíng zhōng	in progress (formal)
进展	jìn zhǎn	to make progress; headway; development — 进 jìn (to advance) + 展 zhǎn (to unfold). The headway a task, plan, or situation makes over time: 工作有了进展 "the work has made progress." More about an undertaking than a person.
禁止	jìn zhǐ	forbidden, prohibited
京	jīng	capital
经常	jīng cháng	often; frequently; regularly — 经 jīng (to pass through; constant) + 常 cháng (constant). The standard adverb for an action that recurs as a regular habit: 我经常去那家店 "I go to that shop often." Stresses regularity, not just repetition.
经典	jīng diǎn	classic; classical; a classic work — 经 jīng (canon) + 典 diǎn (standard text; canon). A proven, enduring work, or the quality of being a touchstone: 经典电影 "a classic film," 文学经典 "literary classics."
经过	jīng guò	to pass through; to go by; (after) the course of — 经 jīng (pass through) + 过 guò (cross over). To travel through a place, or to go through a process. As a noun it names the whole course of events: 事情的经过 "how the matter unfolded."
经济	jīng jì	economy; economic; economical — 经 jīng (manage; order) + 济 jì (aid; relieve). A shortening of 经世济民, "to order the age and aid the people." Now the standard word for the economy, and as an adjective "economical, thrifty": 经济实惠 "good value for money."
经济特区	jīng jì tè qū	Special Economic Zone — the instrument of the opening — 经济 (economy, economic) + 特区 (special zone, from 特 special/exceptional + 区 zone/district). The bounded territories where market rules applied before they were extended nationally: 改革开放's experimental method made spatial.
经理	jīng lǐ	manager — 经 jīng (manage) + 理 lǐ (to put in order). The one who runs the day-to-day affairs of a business: 总经理 zǒngjīnglǐ "general manager," 部门经理 "department manager."
经历	jīng lì	to experience; experience; what one has been through — 经 jīng (to pass through; warp threads; to undergo) + 历 lì (to go through). The most common everyday word for living through something and for the sum of what one has lived through. As a verb it takes events as objects: you 经历 a war, a hardship, a change.
经络	jīng luò	meridians; the qi channel system — 经 jīng (warp thread; main channel) + 络 luò (network thread; connecting channel). Together they describe the body's energy network: 12 primary 经 channels + 8 extraordinary 奇经八脉 vessels + an extensive network of 络 branch channels. The basis of acupuncture, acupressure, and qigong.
经堂教育	jīng táng jiào yù	mosque school education — traditional Islamic learning — 经堂教育 (mosque school education, lit. "sutra hall education," using the Buddhist term 经堂 for a building devoted to sacred texts) is the traditional system by which Hui Muslim boys study Arabic, Persian, and Islamic jurisprudence under an imam (阿訇 āhōng, from Persian ākhund).
经验	jīng yàn	experience (accumulated know-how) — 经 jīng (pass through) + 验 yàn (to test; verify). The practical know-how gathered by having passed through and tested much: 工作经验 "work experience." Distinct from 经历, which is the events themselves rather than the lessons drawn from them.
经营	jīng yíng	to run a business
惊喜	jīng xǐ	a pleasant surprise; to be pleasantly surprised — 惊 jīng (startled) + 喜 xǐ (joy). The happy jolt of an unexpected good thing: 一个惊喜 "a surprise (gift or event)," 又惊又喜 "both startled and delighted."
精	jīng	essence; vitality; refined
精简	jīng jiǎn	to cut down, trim, pare — 精 (refined, essential) + 简 (pared down). To trim away the excess and keep only what is needed, used of staff, budgets, text, and organizations. Sharper and more deliberate than plain 简化.
精神	jīng shén	spirit; vitality; morale; mental state
精神胜利法	jīng shén shèng lì fǎ	The method of spiritual victory , Ah Q psychological mechanism of reframing defeats as victories. Lu Xun intended this as a portrait of a national psychological defense mechanism: the substitution of internal narrative for genuine change.
精神文明	jīng shén wén míng	spiritual civilization; cultural-moral development as a policy domain — A signature term of post-1978 reform-era political vocabulary, paired with 物质文明 (wùzhì wénmíng, material civilization).
精通	jīng tōng	to be proficient in; to master; to have a thorough command of — 精 jīng (refined; essence; finely worked) + 通 (to be all the way through). To have passed thoroughly through a field down to its fine grain: full mastery, not mere acquaintance. 精通 is reserved for genuine expertise: 精通三门外语 (fluent in three foreign languages), 精通法律 (thoroughly versed in law).
净	jìng	painted-face role
净土	jìng tǔ	Pure Land
净土宗	Jìng tǔ Zōng	Pure Land Buddhism — the most widely practiced school in China — Pure Land centers on faith in Amitābha Buddha 阿弥陀佛 Āmítuófó and the aspiration to be reborn in his Western Pure Land 西方极乐世界, from which enlightenment is easy to attain. The primary practice is 念佛 niànfó (recitation of Amitābha's name).
径	jìng	path; trail; shortcut
竟然	jìng rán	surprisingly, to one's surprise
敬	jìng	to toast respectfully — Return the host's toast once the host has toasted you, standing and holding the glass with both hands. A short toast in Chinese is worth more than a long one in English.
敬酒	jìng jiǔ	To toast , to offer a drink as a gesture of respect — To toast , to offer a drink as a gesture of respect. 敬 means "to respect" or "to offer respectfully"; 酒 means "alcoholic drink." The act of toasting at a Chinese meal is a structured social protocol, not a casual gesture.
靖难之役	Jīng nán zhī Yì	Jingnan Campaign (Yongle's usurpation)
静	jìng	still; quiet
静功	jìng gōng	still-form qigong; seated or standing meditation — 静 jìng (still; quiet) + 功 gōng (practice). Qigong performed without external movement: seated meditation, standing post (站桩 zhàn zhuāng), or lying-down practices. The body is still while breath and attention cultivate interior movement of qi.
镜子	jìng zi	mirror
究	jiū	to investigate; to probe
鸠摩罗什	Jiū mó luó shí	Kumārajīva, the earlier translator
九层之台	jiǔ céng zhī tái	a nine-story platform
九歌	jiǔ gē	Nine Songs — Nine Songs: the series of eleven shamanic hymns attributed to Qu Yuan, addressing the gods of the Chu pantheon. Among the most directly religious texts in the 楚辞, they preserve the ritual language of Chu shamanism within a literary frame.
九九	jiǔ jiǔ	double nine — colloquial shorthand; homophonic longevity wish — 九 jiǔ (nine) + 九 jiǔ (nine). The colloquial compressed name for the festival, and a sound-based wish: 九九 is homophonous with 久久 (jiǔ jiǔ, for a long time; lasting), turning the calendar date into an expression of the wish for long life.
九九表	jiǔ jiǔ biǎo	The multiplication table — The multiplication table: memorized in China as a chant of 45 compressed phrases from 一一得一 (1×1=1) to 九九八十一 (9×9=81). The chant begins with the smaller number, which is why it runs from 1×1 up rather than 9×9 down as in some other traditions.
九龙	Jiǔ lóng	Kowloon, "Nine Dragons," the peninsula ceded to Britain in 1860. The name comes from a legend that eight mountains in the area represented eight dragons; a Qing emperor passing through added himself as the ninth.
九五之尊	jiǔ wǔ zhī zūn	the supreme position; the imperial throne — 九五 (the Nine-Five, the fifth yang line of the Qian hexagram, the "flying dragon in the heavens") + 之尊 (the honored, the exalted). The throne itself, the supreme position of the emperor. To 登上九五之尊 is to ascend the throne.
九霄	jiǔ xiāo	the highest heavens; the ninth sky — 九 jiǔ + 霄 xiāo (the upper sky; the clouds). The ninefold heaven, the very top of the sky, as high as the cosmos reaches.
九月	jiǔ yu è	September; the ninth month — 九 jiǔ + 月 yuè (month). September in the solar calendar. The ninth day of the ninth lunar month is the Double Ninth Festival (重阳节 Chóngyáng Jié), when the doubled nine (the highest yang) is celebrated by climbing heights and honoring the elderly, the day's name 重阳 meaning "double yang." So 九月初九 (the…
九州	jiǔ zhōu	the Nine Provinces; all of China — 九 jiǔ + 州 zhōu (province; region). The nine ancient provinces into which the legendary sage-king Yu was said to have divided the land after taming the floods. 九州 became a poetic name for the whole of China, the entire civilized realm: 九州大地 (the great land of the nine provinces).
九转大肠	jiǔ zhuǎn dà cháng	Nine-Turn Intestine
久	jiǔ	long-lasting; for a long time
久远	jiǔ yuǎn	long ago; from the distant past; far back in time — 久 jiǔ (long; for a long time) + 远 yuǎn (far). Temporal distance mapped onto the same word as spatial distance. 久远 specifically emphasizes the antiquity of something , a tradition, a custom, a wound, a memory. Literary and slightly formal.
酒店	jiǔ diàn	hotel
酒鬼	jiǔ guǐ	drunkard — lit. "alcohol ghost" — 酒 jiǔ (alcohol; wine; liquor) + 鬼 guǐ (ghost). A drunkard , someone possessed by alcohol to the point of losing themselves. The pattern is productive: 赌鬼 (dǔguǐ) is a compulsive gambler, 烟鬼 (yānguǐ) is a heavy smoker, 色鬼 (sèguǐ) is a lecher.
酒量	jiǔ liàng	drinking capacity: how much alcohol one can hold — 酒 jiǔ (alcohol) + 量 liàng (capacity). A person's tolerance for drink, the amount they can take before they are affected. A culturally loaded word at the banquet table, where 酒量好 (a good capacity) is treated as a social asset: 他酒量很大 (he can really hold his drink).
酒桌	jiǔ zhuō	The drinking table , refers not just to the physical table but to the entire social ritual of the banquet. 酒桌文化 (jiǔzhuō wénhuà) is "drinking table culture," often discussed critically for its excesses.
旧历	jiù lì	the old calendar; the lunar calendar — 旧 (old) + 历 (lì, calendar; to pass through). The traditional lunisolar calendar, officially called 农历 (nónglì, agricultural calendar) or 阴历 (yīnlì, lunar calendar).
旧时代	jiù shí dài	the old era; the old order — 旧 (old) + 时代 (shídài, era, age , time + generation). A period that has ended and been superseded. The phrase carries strong evaluative loading: in Communist-era writing, 旧时代 nearly always means the pre-1949 Republican or imperial period, and the connotation is negative.
旧址	jiù zhǐ	former site; former address — 旧 (former) + 址 (zhǐ, site, address , earth 土 + stop 止, the place where one stops/settles). The location of something that has moved or no longer exists. Appears on historical markers, museum labels, and administrative documents.
救护车	jiù hù chē	ambulance — 救 (rescue) + 护 (hù, protect) + 车 (chē, vehicle). The "rescue-and-protect vehicle." Hear a siren in a Chinese city and this is the word.
救活	jiù huó	to save someone's life; to revive (the dying) — 救 jiù (to rescue) + 活 huó (alive) as a resultative ending. To rescue with the result of being alive: 活 here marks the outcome, that the rescue succeeded and the person lives. The same pattern builds 养活 yǎnghuó (to support, to keep alive by feeding).
救火	jiù huǒ	to fight a fire — 救 (rescue) + 火 (huǒ, fire). Counterintuitively this means "to put out a fire," not to save the fire; here 救 keeps its Shuōwén sense of 止, halting the disaster. The same logic gives 救灾 (jiùzāi, disaster relief).
救济	jiù jì	to relieve, give relief — 救 (rescue) + 济 (jì, to aid, to cross over). Material relief for the poor or disaster-struck: food, money, shelter. This is 救 in its social-welfare register.
救命	jiù mìng	help! / to save a life — 救 (rescue) + 命 (mìng, life). Literally "save a life." As a shout it is the standard Chinese cry of distress, the equivalent of "Help!" As a verb-object phrase it describes the act of saving someone from death.
救人	jiù rén	to save / rescue someone — 救 (rescue) + 人 (rén, person). The general act of pulling a person out of danger, whether from water, fire, illness, or ruin.
救援	jiù yuán	to rescue; rescue operation — 救 (rescue) + 援 (yuán, to aid, to reach a hand). A coordinated, often large-scale rescue effort: teams, equipment, logistics. The default word for earthquake and mountain rescue.
救灾	jiù zāi	disaster relief
就	jiù	then; as soon as
就近	jiù jìn	nearby; at a convenient location close by — 就 jiù (then; right there; to conform to; to make use of what is present) + 近 (near). Making use of what is nearby , going to the closest available option rather than traveling farther.
就是	jiù shì	precisely; exactly; even if; it's just that — 就 jiù (then; precisely; at once) + 是. Multiple functions make 就是 one of the most versatile particles in spoken Mandarin.
就是说	jiù shì shuō	that is to say / in other words
就要	jiù yào	about to; soon (imminent)
就业	jiù yè	to find employment
就这么简单	jiù zhè me jiǎn dān	it's just that simple; merely this easy — 就 in this usage minimizes or dismisses: it is merely, just, only this , simpler or smaller than one might have expected. 就这样 "just like this, that's all" and 就这么 "it's simply this" are extremely common in casual speech for dismissing complexity or closing a matter.
居民	jū mín	resident; inhabitant (of a place) — 居 jū (to dwell, to reside) + 民. A person defined by where they live rather than by their nationality: the residents of a building, a neighbourhood, a city, a region. The everyday administrative word, as in 居民区 jūmínqū (a residential district) and 城市居民 (urban residents).
居然	jū rán	surprisingly, unexpectedly
居室	jū shì	living quarters (formal)
局部	jú bù	a local part; partial
菊花	jú huā	chrysanthemum — the flower of the ninth month — 菊 jú (chrysanthemum) + 花 huā (flower; blossom). The chrysanthemum blooms in the ninth and tenth lunar months, when most flowering plants have finished. This timing made it a literary symbol of resilience, late-life achievement, and the refusal to conform to expected seasonal rhythms.
巨大	jù dà	enormous; gigantic
巨子	jù zǐ	Grand Master — the head of the Mohist organization — 巨 jù (great; chief) + 子 zǐ (master). The elected or designated leader of the Mohist order, holding authority over all members. The 巨子 could direct members to travel, take on defense contracts for states, and enforce the school's internal law.
句号	jù hào	full stop; period — 句 jù (sentence) + 号 hào (mark; sign). The punctuation mark that closes a sentence, written in Chinese as a small hollow circle 。 rather than a dot. Idiomatically 画上句号 "to draw the full stop" means to bring something to a close.
句子	jù zi	sentence — 句 jù (sentence) + 子 zi (noun-suffix). The everyday word for a complete sentence as a grammatical unit: 一个句子 "one sentence," 简单的句子 "a simple sentence." The building block every grammar lesson works with.
具体	jù tǐ	concrete; specific; particular — 具 jù (to possess, to furnish) + 体 tǐ (body, form). Having a definite body, hence concrete and specific as against vague or abstract. Its opposite is 抽象 chōuxiàng (abstract): 具体的例子 "a concrete example."
具有	jù yǒu	to possess (a quality, characteristic, or abstract attribute) — 具 jù (to possess; equipped with; concrete tool) + 有. Formal written register. Used specifically to assert that a subject possesses an abstract quality, characteristic, feature, or capacity , not physical objects. 具有能力 (to possess ability), 具有价值 (to have value), 具有代表性 (to be representative).
据我所知	jù wǒ suǒ zhī	"As far as I know — "As far as I know." A hedge that acknowledges your information may be incomplete. Useful when stating something you believe to be true but do not want to assert with full confidence. Carries a slightly bookish, considered tone.
距离	jù lí	distance; to be distant from — 距 (jù, to be apart) plus 离 (to be apart). A noun for the measured space between two points, and also a verb for being that far apart. Used for physical and emotional distance alike.
决	ju é	to decide; to settle
决定	ju é dìng	to decide; a decision — 决 jué (to resolve; to break through) + 定 dìng (to settle). To bring a choice to rest, and also the decision itself. 做决定 zuò juédìng "to make a decision." The most common everyday word for deciding.
绝句	ju é jù	Quatrain , four lines of five or seven characters — Quatrain , four lines of five or seven characters. The most concentrated Tang form.
军队	jūn duì	army, the armed forces — 军 (military) + 队 (formed unit). The standing army as an institution, the original martial home of 队 made explicit.
军阀	jūn fá	warlord
军事	jūn shì	military affairs
君	jūn	ruler, gentleman
君臣	jūn chén	ruler and subject — hierarchical, mutually obligated — The political relationship. The ruler (君 jūn) is obligated to govern benevolently and justly; the subject (臣 chén) is obligated to serve loyally and to remonstrate when the ruler errs. 忠 zhōng (loyalty) is the core virtue for the subject; 仁政 rénzhèng (benevolent governance) for the ruler.
君臣佐使	jūn chén zuǒ shǐ	Ruler, minister, assistant, envoy — Ruler, minister, assistant, envoy: the four functional roles within a compound herbal formula.
君君，臣臣，父父，子子	jūn jūn , chén chén , fù fù , zǐ zǐ	let the ruler rule, minister minister, father father, son son — The Analects 12.11 formula for 正名 in practice, given when Duke Jing of Qi asked Confucius about governance. Each binome repeats the same character twice: the first instance is the title, the second is the standard the title entails.
君主	jūn zhǔ	monarch; sovereign
君子协议	jūn zǐ xié yì	gentleman's agreement — A binding agreement resting on personal honor and the parties' characters, without legal documentation. The term assumes both sides are junzi , people whose word is their commitment. Widely used in modern business and political contexts where formal contracts are either inappropriate or impossible.
郡县制	jùn xiàn zhì	commandery-county system — China's foundational administrative structure — 36 commanderies (郡) at unification (later expanded to over 40), subdivided into counties (县). Officials were appointed from the center, paid salaries, and could be dismissed, creating administrative accountability upward rather than hereditary independence downward.
咖啡馆	kā fēi guǎn	café — 咖啡 kāfēi (coffee) + 馆. The modern counterpart to the 茶馆, a place to sit with a laptop or a friend. The 馆 suffix quietly marks it as a public establishment.
喀什	Kā shí	Kashgar , the westernmost major city in China, situated at the edge of the Tarim Basin near the Kyrgyz and Tajik borders. Historically the greatest bazaar city on the Silk Road, where the northern and southern routes reconverged. The old city (老城 lǎochéng) retains much of its traditional character.
开车	kāi chē	to drive (a car)
开灯	kāi dēng	to turn on a light — 开 (kāi, to open, to switch on) + 灯 (lamp): the verb-object phrase for switching a light on. Its opposite is 关灯 (guān dēng), to turn the light off.
开放	kāi fàng	to open up; open (in attitude or policy)
开工	kāi gōng	to start work; to begin construction
开关	kāi guān	a switch
开花	kāi huā	to bloom; to blossom; to flower — 开 kāi (to open) + 花 huā (flower). To come into bloom, said of a plant opening its flowers. Figuratively, of an effort that bears fruit: 遍地开花 "blooming everywhere," spreading and flourishing.
开会	kāi huì	to hold a meeting; to attend a meeting
开机	kāi jī	to power on (a device); to start filming
开口	kāi kǒu	to open one's mouth — to speak (especially after a silence, or to ask for something)
开门	kāi mén	to open the door; (of a shop) to open for business — 开 kāi (to open) + 门 mén (door). To open a door, and idiomatically for a shop to open: 商店几点开门？ "What time does the shop open?" Its pair is 关门 guānmén, to close.
开模	kāi mú	to make a mold; to commission tooling — The step that commits real money. 开新模 is new tooling; 共模 (gòngmú) or 公模 (gōngmú) is a shared or public mold the factory already owns, cheaper but not exclusive to you. 私模 (sīmú) is a private mold for one customer.
开始	kāi shǐ	to begin; the beginning
开头	kāi tóu	the beginning; the start; at first — 开 kāi (to open) + 头 tóu (head, end). The opening end of something: the start of a story, a year, a task. 一开头 "right at the start." Both a noun (the beginning) and a verb (to begin).
开心	kāi xīn	happy; glad; to be happy — 开 kāi (open) + 心 xīn (heart). Lit. "open heart." The heart is opened, unblocked, free. One of the most common everyday happiness words in Mandarin, lighter and more colloquial than 高兴 gāoxìng (pleased, glad) and more immediate than 幸福 xìngfú (lasting happiness; bliss).
开学	kāi xu é	school starts; the start of term
开斋节	Kāi zhāi Jié	Eid al-Fitr
楷书	kǎi shū	regular script — the foundation script — 楷 kǎi (model; standard) + 书 shū (writing). The standard script , the foundation that every calligrapher must master before attempting other styles. Print fonts are based on regular script. 不会楷书，不学草书 "Don't attempt cursive without mastering regular script" , the traditional maxim.
堪舆	kān yú	geomancy; classical term
坎儿井	kǎn er jǐng	Karez — Karez: the ancient underground irrigation system that made Turpan's agricultural oasis possible. A network of vertical shafts connected by horizontal tunnels channels glacial meltwater from the Tianshan foothills to fields in the desert basin.
砍价	kǎn jià	to haggle
看病	kàn bìng	to see a doctor; to seek medical treatment — 看 (to look at, to examine) + 病 (illness). The verb covers both sides of the clinical encounter: the patient "looks at" the illness by going to be treated, and the doctor "looks at" the illness to diagnose it. Context distinguishes who is doing the looking. 我去看病 (I'm going to see a doctor).
看不见	kàn bù jiàn	cannot see; invisible
看不起	kàn bu qǐ	to look down on; to disdain; to have contempt for — 看 (to look) + 不起 (cannot rise up to). The potential complement 不起 indicates inability to raise the gaze high enough , to look up to. Contempt as a failure of elevation. Its opposite is 看得起 kàndéqǐ (to respect, to think well of, to regard highly). 他看不起任何人 , He has contempt for everyone.
看穿	kàn chuān	to see through; to figure out — 看 (kàn, to look) + 穿 (to pierce, as a result complement). The gaze bores all the way through the surface, you see through a lie, a disguise, a scheme. 穿 here is the original drilling sense turned to the mind: the look penetrates and reaches the truth behind.
看到	kàn dào	to see; to catch sight of — 看 kàn (to look) + 到 dào (arrive). Looking that arrived at its object: perception confirmed. 看到 asserts that the visual information reached awareness. Without 到, 看 is merely the act of looking, which may or may not result in seeing.
看电视	kàn diàn shì	to watch television — 看 (to watch) + 电视 (television). 看 is the default verb for watching screens: 看电影 (to watch a film), 看视频 (to watch a video), 看直播 (to watch a live stream), 看比赛 (to watch a match). The verb doesn't change regardless of the medium , 看 covers any directed visual consumption of moving images.
看懂	kàn dǒng	to understand by seeing — 看 (kàn, to look) plus 懂. The visual counterpart to 听懂: to read a text, watch a film, or view a chart and grasp it.
看法	kàn fǎ	viewpoint; opinion; way of seeing — 看 (to look) + 法 (method, way). A held view as a particular way of looking at something. 你对这件事有什么看法？ (What's your view on this matter?). More formal and substantive than 意见 yìjiàn (opinion, suggestion). 世界观 shìjièguān (worldview) extends the same logic: how the world appears from your vantage.
看好	kàn hǎo	to favor; to back; to be bullish on — 看 (to see, to judge) + 好 (as good/positive). To evaluate something as likely to succeed or worth supporting. Widely used in investment contexts (看好这只股票 , bullish on this stock), sports prediction (我看好他们赢 , I favor them to win), and interpersonal support (我很看好你 , I have high hopes for you).
看见	kàn jiàn	to see, to spot
看见 · 看完 · 看懂	kàn jiàn kàn wán kàn dǒng	Resultative complements with 看 — 看 combines with resultative complements to specify the outcome of the looking. 看见 kànjiàn (to catch sight of , result: visual contact made). 看完 kànwán (to finish reading or watching , result: completion). 看懂 kàndǒng (to read and understand , result: comprehension).
看来是	kàn lái shì	it seems to be
看了两遍	kàn le liǎng biàn	watched twice; read it through twice — 了 with a frequency complement (遍 biàn = complete passes through, 次 cì = times, 回 huí = times in colloquial speech). Marks the action as having been performed a counted number of complete cycles. The quantification of repetition implies completion of each cycle.
看门狗	kān méng ǒu	watchdog, guard dog — 看门 (kānmén, to watch the gate) + 狗. A dog that guards the door. Figuratively it names anything that stands watch, including the software process that restarts a crashed system.
看朋友	kàn péng you	to visit a friend; to go see someone — 看 extends naturally from visual attention to social attention: going to see someone implies caring, checking in, tending to the relationship. 看朋友 (visit a friend), 看望 kànwàng (to visit and care for, especially an elder or patient), 探望 tànwàng (to pay a visit, more formal).
看起来	kàn qǐ lái	looks like; visually appears
看书	kàn shū	to read (a book); to study — 看 (to look at) + 书 (book). The standard way to say "to read" in Mandarin , specifically reading for comprehension or leisure. 读书 dúshū overlaps but emphasizes reading aloud or formal study; 看书 is the unmarked everyday verb for sitting with a book.
康熙帝	Kāng xī Dì	Kangxi Emperor (r. 1661–1722)
康熙字典	Kāng xī Zì diǎn	the Kangxi Dictionary — Commissioned by the Kangxi Emperor 康熙帝, completed 1716. 47,035 characters organized under 214 radicals. The definitive classical reference work, still consulted by scholars of classical Chinese today.
考	kǎo	to examine; to investigate
考古	kǎo gǔ	archaeology; to study antiquity through remains — 考 kǎo (to examine, to investigate) + 古 gǔ (the ancient). To investigate the past through its physical remains: 考古学 "archaeology (the discipline)," 考古发现 "an archaeological discovery." The verb of unearthing and reading 古迹, the traces the past left in the ground.
靠近	kào jìn	to approach; to draw near; to get close to — 靠 kào (to lean against; to approach; to rely on) + 近 (near). To lean close , the verb of physical approach. 靠近 carries the movement more explicitly than 接近: it often describes the final stage of approach, closing the last stretch of distance.
科技	kē jì	science and technology
科学	kē xu é	science; scientific — 科 kē (branch; category; department) + 学 xué (learning; -ology). Lit. "categorized learning" , the systematic division of knowledge into disciplines. Coined as a translation for Western "science" in the late 19th century.
科学家	kē xu é jiā	scientist — 科学 kēxué (science, lit. "the study of branches/categories") + 家 jiā. One who has made science their home.
瞌睡	kē shuì	to nod off, doze
磕头	kē tóu	prostration — kowtow — the physical language of reverence — 磕头 (kowtow, lit. "knock head") is the full prostration: kneeling and touching the forehead to the floor.
可爱	kě ài	cute; adorable; lovable — 可 kě (can; worthy of) + 爱 ài (love). Worthy of being loved: cute, adorable. One of the most-used adjectives in everyday Mandarin. Used for children, animals, cartoon characters, and endearing people.
可不是嘛	kě bú shì ma	isn't that the truth / exactly
可见	kě jiàn	evidently; it can be seen that; clearly — 可 (can; worth) + 见 (to see; to understand). "Can be seen" , used to introduce a logical inference or conclusion drawn from evidence. A formal connector common in essays and arguments.
可靠	kě kào	reliable; trustworthy; dependable — 可 (worth) + 靠 (to lean on; to rely on). Worth leaning on. Used for people, sources of information, systems, and promises.
可能会	kěn éng huì	might; may well (possibility + likelihood) — A very common stack: 可能 ("possibly") plus the modal 会 ("will / be likely to"), giving "might, may well." 明天可能会变冷 ("it might turn cold tomorrow"). The 会 adds the sense of a future tendency, so 可能会 feels a touch more about what is likely to happen, while bare 可能 just flags that something is possible.
可能性	kěn éng xìng	possibility; likelihood — 可能 kěnéng (possible) + 性 xìng (-ness). The abstract noun "possibility," built by attaching the -ity suffix to the adjective 可能: 有可能性 "there is a possibility," 很大的可能性 "a strong likelihood." A model case of how 性 turns a quality into a countable, discussable thing.
可怕	kě pà	frightening; terrible; dreadful — 可 (worth) + 怕 (to fear). Worth fearing. Used for things that inspire genuine dread , horror films, dangerous situations, extreme weather, powerful people.
可是	kě shì	but; however (spoken)
可惜	kě xī	unfortunately; what a pity; it is a shame that — 可 (worth) + 惜 (to pity; to regret the loss of something). Worth regretting. Functions as an adjective (这很可惜 , this is a pity) and as a sentential adverb opening a clause (可惜他没来 , unfortunately he did not come).
可笑	kě xiào	laughable; ridiculous; ludicrous — 可 kě (worthy of; -able) + 笑 xiào (laugh). Worthy of being laughed at: ridiculous, absurd. Built on the same 可 + verb pattern as 可爱 (worthy of love → cute) and 可怕 (worthy of fear → frightening). Often carries a sting.
可以	kě yǐ	can; may; it is permissible to; acceptable; not bad — The most versatile 可-compound. 可 (permitted/possible) + 以 (by means of; using). As a modal verb it covers both capability and permission. As a colloquial adjective it means "acceptable" or "not bad." It is the go-to answer when someone asks if something is okay.
可以进来吗？	kě yǐ jìn lái ma ?	May I come in? — requesting permission — 可以 is the canonical permission modal. It asks or grants social authorization , whether rules, norms, context, or a person in authority allows an action. The asker knows they have the physical ability; the question is whether they have the social right.
渴	kě	thirsty
渴望	kě wàng	to crave; to yearn for
克己	kè jǐ	to restrain oneself; self-disciplined; self-denying — 克 kè (to overcome) + 己 jǐ (self). To conquer one's own impulses, the Confucian ideal of self-mastery. The root of 克己复礼, "subdue the self and return to ritual," and of 克己奉公, "curb oneself and serve the public good."
刻	kè	to carve; to engrave
刻苦	kè kǔ	hardworking, painstaking, assiduous — 刻 (kè), to carve or engrave, joins 苦 (kǔ) to describe effort that bites in: study or training pursued through self-denial. A 刻苦 student drills past comfort, the way a blade is sharpened against stone.
客船	kè chuán	passenger ship
客户	kè hù	customer — Factories like to name famous customers. The useful questions are which markets they ship to, and how much of the output the largest customer takes (占比 zhànbǐ, share), since a dominant customer gets the line first in peak season.
客家人	Kè jiā rén	The Hakka people, literally "guest family people," so named because they arrived in the regions where they settled as migrants, after earlier inhabitants.
客人	kèr én	guest
客厅	kè tīng	living room
客星	kè xīng	Guest star — Guest star: a star that appears briefly and then fades, i.e., a nova or supernova. Chinese court astronomers maintained careful records of guest stars for astrological-political interpretation: a guest star in a particular mansion was a portent for specific ministers, regions, or dynasties.
课	kè	class; lesson
课本	kè běn	textbook — 课 kè (lesson) + 本 běn (volume). The book for a course of study. A close synonym is 教科书 jiàokēshū, slightly more formal.
课堂笔记	kè táng bǐ jì	class notes; lecture notes — 课堂 kètáng (classroom) + 笔记 bǐjì. Notes taken during class. 借笔记 jiè bǐjì = to borrow someone's notes. 整理笔记 zhěnglǐ bǐjì = to organize/clean up one's notes.
课文	kè wén	text (in a textbook)
肯定	kěn dìng	certainly; to affirm; positive; sure — 肯 kěn (to be willing; to assent) + 定 dìng (to settle). To settle a thing as yes: as an adverb, "certainly," and as a verb, "to affirm, to confirm." Its opposite is 否定 fǒudìng (to negate; negative).
空白	kòng bái	blank space; gap
空话	kōng huà	empty talk
空间	kōng jiān	space; room (abstract); scope — 空 kōng (empty) + 间 jiān (space). Empty space, both physical and figurative: 没有空间 "no room," 发展空间 "room to grow." Paired with 时间, the two name space and time together.
空姐	kōng jiě	flight attendant (female) — 空 (sky, air) plus 姐 is the colloquial word for a female flight attendant, literally a "sky sister." It is a clipped, affectionate everyday term beside the formal 空中小姐 (kōngzhōng xiǎojiě).
空气	kōng qì	air; atmosphere (physical) — 空 kōng (empty; void; sky) + 气 qì. The physical substance of the atmosphere, what you breathe. Also used metaphorically for social atmosphere: 房间里的空气很紧张 "the air in the room was tense." The same word covers oxygen and social mood.
空闲	kòng xián	leisure; idle, free
空想	kōng xiǎng	empty thought, fantasy
孔子	Kǒng zǐ	Confucius
恐惧	kǒng jù	fear, dread
口才	kǒu cái	eloquence; a gift for speaking — 口 kǒu (mouth) + 才 cái (talent). Literally "mouth-talent," the ability to speak well and persuasively: 他很有口才 "he is very eloquent." One of a family of 才 compounds naming a specific gift, alongside 文才 wéncái (literary talent).
口渴	kǒu kě	thirsty (mouth dry)
口气	kǒu qì	manner of speaking; breath smell; implied attitude — 口 kǒu (mouth) + 气 qì. Operates on two levels: (1) the qi from the mouth, literally breath smell (口气重 "bad breath"); (2) the implied attitude and tone in what someone says, what their words reveal about how they feel. 你这个口气 "That tone of yours…" carries reproach.
口头禅	kǒu tóu chán	pet phrase; catchphrase — Mouth-Chan: enlightenment talked rather than lived, generalized into anyone's verbal habit. 他的口头禅是"没问题" means "no problem" is his catchphrase. One of the most useful conversation words about conversation.
口头承诺	kǒu tóu chéng nuò	verbal promise
口味	kǒu wèi	flavor; taste preference; palate — 口 kǒu (mouth) + 味 wèi (flavor; taste). Both the objective flavor of food and the subjective preference of a taster. 这不合我的口味 "This doesn't suit my taste." 口味重 kǒuwèi zhòng = "strong-flavored preferences" , liking bold, heavy flavors.
口吻	kǒu wěn	manner of speech; implied standpoint (literary) — 口 kǒu (mouth) + 吻 wěn (lips; to kiss, the form of the mouth). The shape of what comes from the mouth, the distinctive way a person speaks, implying their perspective and attitude. More literary than 口气, used in written analysis, narration, and formal commentary.
口音	kǒu yīn	accent; regional way of speaking — 口 kǒu (mouth) + 音 yīn (sound). The sound a particular mouth gives a language, marking where the speaker is from: 南方口音 "a southern accent," 带口音 "to have an accent."
口语	kǒu yǔ	spoken language
叩手礼	kòu shǒu lǐ	knocking hand ritual — tapping thanks
扣款	kòu kuǎn	deduction from payment — Often the most practical settlement, since you hold the next payment. Agree the amount in writing before deducting.
哭	kū	to cry; to weep
苦谛	kǔ dì	the truth of suffering — dukkha — 苦 kǔ = suffering, bitterness, difficulty. Sanskrit dukkha: a wheel whose axle is off-center, generating grinding rather than smooth motion. The Buddha's first noble truth: life as ordinarily lived is characterized by dukkha.
苦瓜	kǔ guā	bitter melon
苦劳	kǔ láo	credit for hard work
苦力	kǔ lì	hard manual labor; a coolie — 苦 (bitter, hard) plus 力 (lì, strength) names grueling physical labor and, historically, the laborer himself. The English word "coolie" carries this same syllable. In modern speech 干苦力 means to do back-breaking work.
苦练	kǔ liàn	to train hard, to drill relentlessly — Here 苦 works as an adverb on 练 (liàn, to train): to practice bitterly, to grind. The word frames mastery as something earned through painful repetition rather than talent.
苦难	kǔn àn	suffering, tribulation, misery — 苦 (bitter) joins 难 (nàn, calamity, disaster) to name suffering on a large, often historical scale: famine, war, oppression. Weightier than everyday hardship, 苦难 belongs to the language of nations and generations.
苦恼	kǔn ǎo	vexed, distressed
苦心	kǔ xīn	painstaking effort; careful pains — 苦 (bitter) plus 心 (xīn, heart, mind) names the trouble one's heart goes through for someone else's sake. 一片苦心 (yí piàn kǔxīn) is a heartful of well-meant pains, often unrecognized.
苦衷	kǔ zhōng	hidden hardship; unspoken difficulty — 苦 (bitter) plus 衷 (zhōng, inner feelings) names the private difficulty behind a person's puzzling choice, the bitterness they cannot say aloud. To have a 苦衷 is to act badly for a reason you keep hidden.
库存	kù cún	stock, inventory — Containment covers goods not yet shipped. Ask the factory to check 库存 and the work in progress (在制品 zàizhìpǐn) before anything else leaves.
裤子	kù zi	trousers
夸	kuā	to boast; to praise (phonetic of 跨)
跨步	kuà bù	to take a stride; a step — 跨 + 步 (bù, step). The base physical sense , a single deliberate stride, often a long one. Used in athletics, dance, military drill, and figuratively for any decisive move forward.
跨度	kuà dù	span (of a bridge, an era, a project) — 跨 + 度 (dù, degree; measure). The measurable distance from one support to another , originally an architectural and engineering term (the 跨度 of an arch or bridge), now extended to time spans, salary ranges, age gaps, and the scope of a project or career.
跨国	kuà guó	transnational; multinational — 跨 + 国 (guó, country). 跨国公司 (kuàguó gōngsī, multinational corporation) is the canonical compound. Also 跨国婚姻 (cross-national marriage), 跨国合作 (cross-national cooperation), 跨国犯罪 (transnational crime).
跨过	kuà guò	to step over; to get past — 跨 + 过 (guò, to pass). The everyday verb of stepping over a low obstacle: a threshold, a puddle, a fallen branch. By extension, getting past a difficulty in a single move. Common with 门槛 ménkǎn (threshold) , both literal and metaphorical.
跨界	kuà jiè	crossover (between fields, industries, genres) — 跨 + 界 (jiè, field; domain). A buzzword in entertainment, design, and business since the 2000s: an actor turning singer, a fashion brand collaborating with a tech company, a novelist directing a film. 跨界合作 (kuàjiè hézuò) "crossover collaboration" appears constantly in marketing copy.
跨境	kuà jìng	cross-border — 跨 + 境 (jìng, border; territory). The standard term in trade and law. 跨境电商 (kuàjìng diànshāng, cross-border e-commerce) is one of the most-used compounds in contemporary business writing. 跨境 carries a more administrative tone than 跨国; 国 invokes the country, 境 the legal frontier.
跨年	kuàn ián	to cross over into the new year — 跨 + 年 (nián, year). The standard verb for what one does on New Year's Eve , celebrating through midnight from the old year into the new. 跨年晚会 (kuànián wǎnhuì) "New Year's Eve gala" is the genre name for the live-broadcast countdown shows.
跨上	kuà shàng	to mount; to step up onto (astride) — 跨 + 上 (shàng, up; onto). To swing one leg over and mount: a horse, a bicycle, a motorcycle, a stage. The verb preserves the original image of legs spread on either side. The poetic phrase 跨上战马 kuà shàng zhànmǎ "to mount the war-horse" recurs throughout classical narrative and modern wuxia.
跨文化	kuà wén huà	cross-cultural; intercultural — 跨 kuà (to straddle, to cross over) + 文化 (culture). Cross-cultural: spanning or bridging different cultural contexts. 跨文化交际 kuà wénhuà jiāojì (intercultural communication) is an academic field. 跨文化婚姻 (cross-cultural marriage) appears in demographic and social discussions.
跨学科	kuà xu é kē	interdisciplinary — 跨 + 学科 (xuékē, academic discipline). The standard rendering of "interdisciplinary" in Chinese academic writing. 跨学科研究 (kuà xuékē yánjiū) "interdisciplinary research" is now a frequent funding category.
跨越式	kuà yu è shì	leap-forward style; "leap-across" type (modifier) — The modifier form, suffixing 式 (shì, type/style) to make an adjective. Most common in 跨越式发展 (kuàyuè shì fāzhǎn, "leap-forward development"), the standard term in Chinese policy and journalism for skipping intermediate stages of economic or technological growth.
块 · 毛 · 分	kuài máo fēn	units of currency, spoken — Spoken prices use 块 for the main unit, 毛 for tenths, 分 for hundredths; written prices use 元, 角, 分. The trailing unit is often dropped: 四块一 is 4.10, 三块八五 is 3.85. Factories apply the same words to dollars, so add 美金 (měijīn) or 人民币 (rénmínbì) when it could be either.
快餐	kuài cān	fast food
快车	kuài chē	express train or bus
快递	kuài dì	express delivery; courier
快点	kuài diǎn	be quick; a bit faster; hurry — 快 kuài (fast) + 点 diǎn (a little; somewhat , the softening particle). The most colloquial way to tell someone to speed up. Tonal in its delivery: said gently it is a nudge, said sharply it is impatient.
快活	kuài huó	carefree; cheerful; lively and at ease — 快 kuài (unobstructed) + 活 huó (alive; living; moving freely). The image is life flowing without constraint. Carries a slightly old-fashioned or literary flavor in modern Mandarin, but remains warm and vivid in fiction and colloquial northern speech. Describes people who seem naturally unburdened.
快乐	kuài lè	happy; joyful; happiness (as a state) — 快 kuài (released; unobstructed) + 乐 lè (music; joy; ease). The standard word for happiness in modern Mandarin: it is an adjective predicate, a pre-noun modifier, and a noun. Appears in greetings and fixed phrases far more than the simple 快 alone.
快速	kuài sù	fast; rapid; at high speed — 快 kuài (fast) + 速 sù (speed; velocity). The compound reinforces both components, producing the most neutral, all-purpose word for fast in formal or written Chinese. Works as an adjective before nouns or as an adverb before verbs.
筷子	kuài zi	chopsticks
筷子架	kuài zi jià	Chopstick rest — Chopstick rest: a small ceramic or metal rest placed beside the bowl to lay chopsticks on between bites. Used in formal settings; absent in casual ones.
宽容	kuān róng	lenient; tolerant; magnanimous — 宽 kuān (wide; broad) + 容 róng (to hold). To hold broadly: a generous, forgiving disposition that leaves wide room for others' faults. Where 容忍 is a grudging act, 宽容 is an admired character trait. The 宽 (wide) intensifies the holding image directly: a broad vessel spills less.
狂欢	kuáng huān	to revel; carnival; wild celebration — 狂 kuáng (wild; unrestrained) + 欢 huān (joy). Joy turned up to its loudest: unrestrained revelry. 狂欢节 kuánghuānjié is the standard word for Carnival, and 狂欢 names any wild, all-out celebration.
况且	kuàng qiě	moreover; besides; what's more
矿	kuàng	mineral, ore
坤 ䷁	kūn	earth; pure yin; the receptive; hexagram 2 of the Yijing — Six broken yin lines: ䷁. The second hexagram represents pure receptive capacity, responsiveness, sustaining, nurturing, the mother principle, earth, the minister. 坤 does not passively endure; it actively receives, nurtures, and brings to fruition what 乾 initiates.
昆曲	kūn qǔ	Kun Opera
困顿	kùn dùn	destitute and weary
困惑	kùn huò	puzzled, confused
困境	kùn jìng	predicament; tight spot — 困 (trapped) + 境 (situation, circumstance). A predicament you are stuck inside, with no obvious exit. Stronger and more structural than 困难: a 困境 is the whole walled situation, not a single problem.
困局	kùn jú	deadlocked situation
困倦	kùn juàn	weary and drowsy — 困 (sleepy) + 倦 (weary, jaded). The settled, heavy tiredness that pulls a person toward sleep. More literary than 犯困, fitting written description of a long day's fatigue.
困苦	kùn kǔ	privation, hardship
困难	kùn nan	difficulty; hardship — 困 (hemmed in) + 难 (hard). The everyday word for a problem that resists you, or for the strained circumstances a person lives through. Works as both a noun (a difficulty) and an adjective (difficult, hard-up).
困扰	kùn rǎo	to perplex, beset
困意	kùn yì	drowsiness; the urge to sleep — 困 (sleepy) + 意 (feeling, inclination). The sensation of sleepiness itself, the rising pull you can either fight off or give in to.
蜡黄	là huáng	sallow, waxy yellow
蜡烛	là zhú	candle
来不及	lái bu jí	there's not enough time; too late to do something — 来 lái (to come; to reach) + 不 bù (not) + 及 jí (to reach; to catch up to). The time has not come far enough to reach this action, so there is not enough time. Opposite: 来得及 lái de jí (there is still time; still possible).
来了	lái le	(he/she/it) has come; has arrived; here it is — 来 lái (to come) + 了 le (completion/change-of-state particle). The arrival has been completed: the thing or person is now here. One of the most basic and high-frequency phrases in Mandarin.
来去	lái qù	directional complements
来世	lái shì	the next life; the afterlife; the life to come — 来 lái (coming) + 世 shì (life). The life that follows this one in the Buddhist cycle of rebirth: 来世再相见 "to meet again in the next life." Paired with 前世 (a former life) and 今世 (this life) to span the chain of rebirths.
来源	lái yuán	source; origin; where something comes from — 来 lái (to come) + 源 yuán (source; origin; spring, where water comes from). "The place from which it comes." Used for the source of funding (资金来源), information (信息来源), authority (权力来源), and anything whose origin needs to be named.
来自	lái zì	to come from; to originate from; hailing from — 来 lái (to come; to arrive) + 自 zì (from; self; origin). The standard formal expression for geographic or conceptual origin. Used for people (来自北京), ideas (这个想法来自实验), data (数据来自2023年), and institutions (来自联合国的报告).
兰亭序	Lán tíng Xù	Preface to the Orchid Pavilion
兰州拉面	Lán zhōu lā miàn	Lanzhou Hand-Pulled Noodles
蓝	lán	blue
懒	lǎn	lazy
烂	làn	rotten, mushy
朗读	lǎng dú	to read aloud (clearly, with expression) — 朗 lǎng (clear; bright; resonant) + 读. The school-room and literary register for reading aloud , the kind of reading where pronunciation, rhythm, and feeling matter. Standard exercise in Chinese language classes.
浪费	làng fèi	to waste
劳动	láo dòng	labor; to work
牢	láo	enclosure; pen; firm; secure
老白茶	lǎo bái chá	aged white tea — white tea stored for 3+ years
老百姓	lǎo bǎi xìng	the common people; ordinary citizens — 老 (old/established) + 百 (hundred) + 姓 (surname; clan). Lit. "the hundred old clans": the established common families as opposed to the aristocracy. The standard term for ordinary people, used across the political spectrum.
老板	lǎo bǎn	boss; business owner — 老 lǎo + 板 bǎn (plank; board, extended to the person in charge). The everyday term for boss, owner, or the person in charge of a business. In restaurants, calling out 老板！is the standard way to get the owner's attention.
老大哥	lǎo dà gē	big brother (figure or nation)
老豆腐	lǎo dòu fu	Firm Tofu; Northern Tofu
老公	lǎo gōng	husband (colloquial) — 老 lǎo (old; familiar prefix) + 公 gōng (elder male). The warm, everyday word a woman uses for her husband, paired with 老婆 lǎopó (wife). More casual than 丈夫 zhàngfu.
老江湖	lǎo jiāng hú	a seasoned worldly person; a streetwise veteran; an old hand — 老 lǎo (old; experienced) + 江湖 (the wanderers' world). A person who has been around , who has seen the tricks, navigated the codes, survived the dangers, and accumulated knowledge with scars.
老练	lǎo liàn	seasoned, experienced — 老 (old) + 练 (tempered). Weathered by long practice into steadiness: a veteran's unflappable competence. Praises judgment and poise, not just technical speed.
老朋友	lǎo péng you	old friend; longtime friend — A friend of long standing. 老 lǎo (old) marks the length of the friendship, not the person's age. A warm word, often used on reunion: 老朋友了 ("we go way back"). The opposite, a new friend, is 新朋友 xīn péngyou.
老人节	lǎo rén jié	Elders Day; Senior Citizens Day — 老人 lǎorén (elderly person; old person) + 节 jié (festival; designated day). The official civic designation established in 1989, co-locating a state-recognized day honoring elderly citizens with the existing Double Ninth Festival.
老生	lǎo shēng	bearded male role
老师	lǎo shī	teacher — the most common honorific in Chinese education — 老 lǎo (venerable) + 师 shī (master; teacher; expert). The standard term for teacher at all levels, from elementary school through university. Used as a title of address (王老师 "Teacher Wang") and as a term of respect for anyone with expertise to transmit.
老实	lǎo shi	honest; well-behaved; simple and straightforward — 老 lǎo (established; true) + 实 shí (solid; real). Meaning cluster: (1) honest and straightforward (他很老实，不会骗人 "he's very honest, won't deceive anyone"); (2) well-behaved, obedient (孩子今天很老实 "the child is well-behaved today"); (3) naive, easy to take advantage of (别太老实了 "don't be too naive").
老实说	lǎo shí shuō	"Frankly" or "to be straight with you — "Frankly" or "to be straight with you." Close cousin to 说实话, with the same function. 老实 literally means "honest, straightforward." Slightly warmer in tone, often used among friends before offering an opinion the listener might not want to hear.
老天 · 老天爷	lǎo tiān lǎo tiān yé	dear heaven; good God (colloquial exclamation) — 老 lǎo (old; venerable, a term of respectful familiarity) + 天 tiān + 爷 yé (grandfather; lord). The folk personification of heaven as a kindly, or sometimes indifferent, old grandfather.
老子	lǎo zǐ	Laozi, the attributed author
乐队	yu è duì	band, orchestra
乐观	lè guān	optimistic; hopeful — 乐 lè (happy) + 观 guān (to view). To view things on the bright side. Its mirror is 悲观 bēiguān (pessimistic), 悲 bēi being sorrow: the two share 观 and differ only in the mood that colors the seeing eye.
乐器	yu è qì	musical instrument — 乐 (music, yuè) + 器 (implement). Each instrument takes its own playing verb: 弹 (pluck) for piano and guitar, 拉 (draw) for bowed strings, 吹 (blow) for winds, 打 (strike) for percussion. Asking 你会什么乐器? is standard small talk among students.
乐曲	yu è qǔ	a musical composition; a piece of music — 乐 yuè (music) + 曲 qǔ (tune; melody). A composed piece of music: 一首乐曲 "a piece of music." The 曲 names the melody, the 乐 the musical art it belongs to.
乐趣	lè qù	delight; pleasure; the fun of a thing — 乐 lè (joy) + 趣 qù (interest; fun). The pleasure or enjoyment found in an activity: 读书的乐趣 "the pleasure of reading."
乐山乐水	yào shān yào shuǐ	to take joy in mountains and water; to be a person of refined nature — A condensed echo of the Analects line. Note the alternative reading: in this fixed phrase 乐 is sometimes read yào in the classical pronunciation (the same character as 乐 lè / yuè, with multiple historical readings , yào here marks affective preference).
乐意	lè yì	to be glad to; willing — 乐 lè (glad) + 意 yì (intention; will). To be happy to do something, with the nuance of genuine willingness: 我很乐意帮忙 "I'd be glad to help."
了不起	liǎo bu qǐ	remarkable; extraordinary; amazing — 了 liǎo (to conclude; to surpass) + 不 + 起 (unable to rise above; potential complement marker). Literally "cannot be surpassed or concluded" , what cannot be surpassed is extraordinary. A high-praise adjective in everyday speech.
了结	liǎo jié	to conclude; to settle; to bring to a definitive end — 了 liǎo (to conclude) + 结 jié (knot; tied up; settled). To tie off and close a matter definitively. Used for settling disputes, concluding legal cases, or resolving something that has dragged on without resolution. Has a formal or literary flavor , matters of weight are 了结d.
了解	liǎo jiě	to understand thoroughly; to have rounded knowledge of — 了 liǎo (clear-sighted; complete) + 解 jiě (to unravel; to untie). The full unraveling of something until it is entirely clear , understanding that goes all the way through, not just surface knowledge. More thorough than 知道 zhīdao (to know a fact). Common across casual speech and formal registers.
雷达	léi dá	radar — phonetic loanword — Phonetic transliteration of the English "radar" (Radio Detection And Ranging). 雷 léi (thunder) + 达 dá (to reach; to arrive). A felicitous loanword: the chosen characters evoke the technology's function , a signal (like thunder, striking out from a source) that reaches and returns.
雷公	Léi gōng	Duke of Thunder — the thunder deity — 雷 léi (thunder) + 公 gōng (duke; honorific for male deities and elder men). The personified god of thunder, depicted with a bird beak, wings, and ring of drums. Works alongside 电母 (Diàn Mǔ, Mother of Lightning).
雷声	léi shēng	the sound of thunder; a thunderclap — 雷 léi (thunder) + 声 shēng (sound). The rumble or crack of thunder. Often used in the phrase 雷声大，雨点小 (léishēng dà, yǔdiǎn xiǎo) , "thunder loud, raindrops small" , meaning all bluster and announcement with little actual follow-through.
雷雨	léi yǔ	thunderstorm — 雷 léi (thunder) + 雨 yǔ (rain). A thunderstorm , rain accompanied by thunder and lightning. Also the title of one of the most important plays in modern Chinese literature: 雷雨 (Léiyǔ, 1934) by Cao Yu (曹禺), a family tragedy of suppressed desire and social constraint.
累	lèi	tired, weary
累死了	lèi sǐ le	exhausted; dead tired
冷	lěng	Cold — Cold. 天冷了，多穿点 ("it's gotten cold, wear more") is the all-purpose expression of care from mothers, landladies, and group chats.
冷门	lěng mén	unpopular, obscure
离 + 时间点 + 还有	lí + ti me + hái yǒu	until (a point in time) — 离 measures distance in time as well as space. Naming a future point, then 还有 ("there still is") plus a span, counts down to it: 离新年还有两个星期 ("there are still two weeks until New Year"), 离上课还有五分钟 ("five minutes until class"). It is the natural way to say "how long until" something.
离岸价	lí àn jià	FOB price — "Leave-shore price." Most export quotes are FOB, always with a port: FOB宁波, FOB深圳. In speech people simply say the letters, F-O-B. The port matters, since inland trucking to a distant port is part of the factory's cost.
离别	lí bié	to part, to take leave (of people) — 离 (to part) plus 别 (bié, to part, to bid farewell). A literary word for the parting of people, weighted with feeling. It carries the ache of long goodbyes.
离婚	lí hūn	to divorce — 离 (to part) plus 婚 (hūn, marriage). A separable verb: the two halves can split apart, mirroring the meaning. 离了婚 (líle hūn) means got divorced.
离开	lí kāi	to leave, to depart from — 离 (to part) plus 开 (kāi, to open, to move away). The most common verb for leaving a place or a person. It takes a direct object: you leave something.
离去	lí qù	to leave; to depart (with finality) — 离 lí (to separate from; to leave; distance) + 去 qù (to go away). To separate and go , departure with a sense of severance. More emphatic and often more final than 走 alone. Used for leaving a job, a relationship, a city, or a life. 她悄悄地离去了 (She quietly departed) , the 去 carries the irreversibility.
离职	lí zhí	to resign, to leave a post — 离 (to leave) plus 职 (zhí, post, office). The formal verb for quitting a job or stepping down from a position.
黎明	lí míng	dawn; daybreak
礼拜	lǐ bài	prayer (Islamic); also "week" in Chinese
礼记 · 易经 · 春秋	Lǐ jì Yì jīng Chūn qiū	Record of Rites · I Ching · Spring and Autumn Annals — The three remaining classics. 礼记 Lǐjì , encyclopedic compilation of ritual texts. 易经 Yìjīng (I Ching, Book of Changes) , 64 hexagrams with commentaries, used for divination and cosmological reasoning; Confucius reportedly said he would need 50 more years to study it fully.
礼教吃人	lǐ jiào chī rén	Ritual morality eats people — Ritual morality eats people: the compressed formula, derived from "A Madman's Diary," for Lu Xun central argument that the Confucian system of ritual and ethical teaching functions as a mechanism of social domination and destruction rather than genuine moral cultivation.
礼节	lǐ jié	formal etiquette; protocol
礼乐	lǐ yu è	ritual and music — the twin pillars of Zhou social order — 礼 lǐ (ritual; propriety; ceremony) + 乐 yuè (music; joy). The cultural program systematized by the Duke of Zhou: ritual prescribed the correct form for all significant social acts; music calibrated the relationship between human society and the cosmic order.
礼貌用语	lǐ mào yòng yǔ	polite expressions; courtesy phrases — 礼貌 + 用语 yòngyǔ (set expressions; phrases used in a particular register). Names the inventory of fixed polite phrases: 您好 nín hǎo, 谢谢 xièxiè, 不客气 bú kèqi, 对不起 duìbuqǐ, 请 qǐng, 麻烦您 máfan nín, 辛苦了 xīnkǔ le. Mastering 礼貌用语 is the visible threshold of being 有礼貌.
礼物	lǐ wù	gift; present — 礼 lǐ (ritual offering) + 物 wù (thing; object; matter). Lit. "a ritual-offering made material." A gift in Chinese culture is always, at some level, a ritual object that carries social meaning exceeding its material value.
礼仪	lǐ yí	etiquette; protocol; ceremony — 礼 lǐ + 仪 yí (appearance; manner; instrument of measurement). The formal standards of conduct for official occasions. 礼仪小姐 = ceremonial hostess. 外交礼仪 diplomatic protocol. More formal and institutional than 礼貌.
李白	Lǐ Bái	Li Bai — Tang dynasty poet
李逵	lǐ kuí	Li Kui — the Black Whirlwind — The wild, axe-wielding "Black Whirlwind," fiercely loyal to Song Jiang and utterly without restraint, killing with a berserk joy that the novel both admires and shudders at.
李斯	Lǐ Sī	Li Si — prime minister of Qin, architect of the unified empire, student of Xunzi — 李斯 (280–208 BCE): Xunzi's student who rose to become prime minister of the Qin state and then the unified Qin empire. He implemented many of the administrative reforms , standardization of weights, measures, script, and roads , that defined the Qin empire.
李自成	Lǐ Zì chéng	rebel leader who took Beijing in 1644
里程	lǐ chéng	mileage, distance covered
里面	lǐ miàn	inside; interior; within — 里 (inside) + 面 (face, surface, side). The most direct spatial interior term in Mandarin. Used for concrete physical interiors (inside a box, inside a building) and abstract interiors (inside a system, inside a relationship).
里外	lǐ wài	inside-outside
里子	lǐ zi	The lining; the private reality beneath the public presentation. The counterpart to 面子. 里子不足，面子撑着 ("the lining is insufficient, face holds it up") describes a situation where the private reality does not support the public claim.
理发	lǐ fà	to get a haircut; to have one's hair done — 理 lǐ (to tidy; put in order) + 发 fà (hair). To have the hair cut or styled: 理发店 "barbershop, hair salon." A separable verb, so 理了发 "got a haircut."
理解	lǐ jiě	to understand; to comprehend; understanding — 理 lǐ (principle; reason) + 解 jiě. To untie a thing down to its reason. To understand the logic or sense of something, and by extension to be understanding toward a person: 理解万岁 = "long live mutual understanding"; 请你理解 = please understand (my position).
理论	lǐ lùn	theory; a body of principles — 理 lǐ (principle, pattern, reason) + 论. A theory: an organised set of principles, as opposed to practice (实践 shíjiàn). 理论上 lǐlùn shàng "in theory" pairs with 实际上 shíjì shàng "in practice" in the standard contrast.
理想	lǐ xiǎng	ideal; aspiration; ideal (adjective) — 理 lǐ (principle; pattern; the inherent structure of things) + 想 xiǎng (thought; thinking). Principled thinking: the vision of how things ought to be, arrived at by sustained reflection on value and order. Functions as a noun (an ideal, a dream) and as an adjective (ideal conditions, ideal outcome).
理性	lǐ xìng	rational (contrast with 感性)
理学	lǐ xu é	School of Principle; Neo-Confucianism in the Zhu Xi tradition — The dominant Neo-Confucian tradition associated with Zhu Xi and the Song dynasty. Holds that 理 (moral principle) is immanent in external things and texts, and must be reached through gradual investigation and learning.
理由	lǐ yóu	a reason; grounds; justification — 理 lǐ (principle) + 由 yóu (cause; from). The specific reason or grounds given for an action or claim: 没有理由 "there is no reason," 充分的理由 "ample justification."
力量	lì liàng	strength: force, power, capability — 力 lì (force, strength) + 量 liàng (measure, amount). The measurable quantity of force, whether physical, social, or moral: 体力的力量 (bodily strength), 团结的力量 (the power of unity), 知识就是力量 (knowledge is power).
