力气	lì qi	physical strength; bodily effort — 力 lì (force) + 气 qì (breath; vital energy). The bodily strength you can feel in your muscles, the kind used to lift, carry, or push: 没力气 "have no strength," 花力气 "to spend effort." Concrete and physical, never abstract.
历代	lì dài	successive dynasties; through the generations — 历 lì (successive; passing through) + 代 dài (generation; dynasty; era). Refers to the whole sequence of past dynasties or generations taken together: "every dynasty in turn," "successive ages." Common in historical and cultural writing to describe traditions, emperors, scholars, or customs that recur…
历法	lì fǎ	lunisolar calendar
历经	lì jīng	to go through; to undergo (a long or arduous process) — 历 lì (to pass through) + 经 jīng (to undergo). The same two characters as 经历 in reverse order, but the register and feel differ: 历经 is more literary and emphasizes a long, often difficult passage through many stages.
历来	lì lái	always; throughout history; from of old up to now — 历 lì (passing through time) + 来 lái (coming up to the present). An adverb meaning "all along throughout the past until now," used to assert that something has consistently been the case across the whole span of history.
历史故事	lì shǐ gù shì	historical story; story from history — 历史 lìshǐ (history) + 故事 gùshì (story). A historical narrative , story with identifiable historical figures and events. Distinguished from 神话 (myth) and 传说 (legend) by its claimed factual basis.
历史观	lì shǐ guān	historical worldview; philosophy of history — 历史 + 观 (view; perspective; outlook , the same 观 as in 世界观 shìjièguān, worldview, and 人生观 rénshēngguān, outlook on life). 历史观 is the framework through which one interprets history: its patterns, its forces, its meaning.
历史决议	lì shǐ ju é yì	Resolution on Party History (1981 official verdict)
历史性	lì shǐ xìng	historic; of historical significance — 历史 + 性 (the suffix forming abstract quality nouns: 重要性 importance, 可能性 possibility). 历史性 modifies nouns to mean "of historic significance" , a moment or event that will be recorded as consequential. 历史性会议 (a historic meeting), 历史性协议 (a historic agreement).
历史学	lì shǐ xu é	historiography; the academic discipline of history — 历史 + 学 (the suffix marking a field of systematic study: 哲学 philosophy, 科学 science, 心理学 psychology). 历史学 is history as an academic discipline , the methods, debates, and institutions of scholarly historical inquiry, as distinct from history as the accumulated record of events.
历史学家	lì shǐ xu é jiā	historian — 历史学 + 家 (the suffix for a person who is expert in or practises something: 科学家 scientist, 艺术家 artist, 音乐家 musician). The formal word for a professional historian. The classical equivalent, 史官 (shǐguān, court historian), referred specifically to the official record-keeper of a dynasty.
厉害	lì hai	fierce, formidable; impressive — 厉 (severe, sharp) + 害 (harm), here neutral-toned. Literally cutting and harmful, the word swung into admiration: someone or something so strong it could hurt you. Praise and warning at once.
立	lì	to stand; to establish
立春	lì chūn	Start of Spring (solar term) — 立 (lì, to stand/establish) + 春 (spring). The first of the twenty-four solar terms (节气 jiéqì), falling around February 4, when the year's farming cycle is reckoned to begin. 立 marks a season being set up, the same verb in 立夏, 立秋, 立冬.
立功	lì gōng	to render meritorious service: to perform a deed — 立 lì (to establish; to set up) + 功 gōng (merit). To establish merit through a notable deed, originally military, now also civic and professional. A verb-object compound, so it splits: 立了大功 = rendered great service. Often paired with reward: 立功受奖 = to perform a deed and receive a commendation.
立刻	lì kè	immediately; at once
利	lì	profit; self-interest
利己	lì jǐ	to benefit oneself; self-interested — 利 lì (benefit) + 己 jǐ (self). Acting for one's own advantage. Often paired in contrast: 利己 vs. 利人 (to benefit others), and condemned in the phrase 损人利己, "harm others to profit oneself."
利人	lì rén	to benefit others
利润	lì rùn	profit
利益	lì yì	benefit, interest
利用	lì yòng	to make use of; to leverage; to exploit — 利 lì (benefit; advantage; profit) + 用 yòng (to use). To use something for advantage, to derive profit from a resource, opportunity, or person. Context determines valence: 利用时间 (make good use of time) is positive; 利用别人 (exploit others) is negative.
例如	lì rú	for example; for instance; such as — 例 lì (example; case) + 如 rú (like). The standard phrase for introducing an instance: 例如这本书 "for example, this book." Its close cousin is 比如 bǐrú, which is more colloquial.
例外	lì wài	exception
隶书	lì shū	clerical script
连词	lián cí	conjunction; linking word
连接	lián jiē	to connect, to link — 连 (lián, to be joined in a chain) + 接 (to splice). To make two separate things into one continuous thing. Used for bridges, cables, networks, anything that bridges a gap.
连接词	lián jiē cí	connector word
连老师都不会	lián lǎo shī dōu bù huì	even the teacher can't — authority at the top of the scale fails — 连…都 also works in "positive" extremes , where the person chosen is the most likely to be able to do something, the highest authority on the scale. When even they fail, the implication is that certainly no one else can.
连忙	lián máng	hurriedly; promptly; at once — 连 lián (right after; immediately) + 忙 máng (busy; to hurry). An adverb describing a reaction that follows hard on the heels of its cause, "at once, without delay." It typically reports a response to a situation that has just arisen, so it appears mostly in narration of past events rather than in com…
连睡觉的时间都没有	lián shuì jiào de shí jiān dōu méi yǒu	not even time to sleep — the most basic need is unmet — The item after 连 can be an entire noun phrase, including a relative clause. 连睡觉的时间 = "even the time for sleeping" , 睡觉 is the most basic physical requirement; not having time even for that represents the absolute extreme of busyness or exhaustion.
连他都不知道	lián tā dōu bù zhī dào	even he doesn't know — the informed one is stumped — The simplest and most frequent form: 连 + person/thing + 都 + (negated) verb. The person chosen is the one who would be most expected to know or be able to do the thing , making their failure maximal evidence for the claim.
连同	lián tóng	together with; along with — 连 lián (to join) + 同 tóng (same; together). A prepositional connector meaning "together with," used to add an item to a group as part of the same action. It bundles the second thing into the first, treating them as one joined set: send the report along with the attachments.
连续	lián xù	continuous; consecutive; in a row — 连 lián (in a series) + 续 xù (to continue; to extend). Without interruption, one period or event following directly on the last. Frequently paired with a counter: 连续三天 (three days running), 连续剧 (a TV serial, lit. "continuous drama"). The emphasis is on an unbroken stretch.
连一分钱都没有	lián yī fēn qián dōu méi yǒu	doesn't have even a single cent — the minimum quantity = nothing — When the extreme case is a minimum quantity (one cent, one word, one minute), the construction means "not even the smallest amount." This is a highly emphatic way of expressing absolute absence. The minimum unit becomes the threshold , and even that threshold is not crossed.
连长	lián zhǎng	company commander (military)
莲花	lián huā	lotus flower
联合	lián hé	to unite; to join together; joint; united — 联 lián (to link; to connect) + 合 hé (to join). To link separate parties into a single front: 联合国 "the United Nations," 联合声明 "a joint statement." The uniting is formal and often institutional.
联系	lián xì	to contact; to get in touch; to link; connection — 联 lián (to join; to unite) + 系 xì (tie). Both the act of making contact and the link itself. As a verb: 联系我 "get in touch with me." As a noun: 保持联系 "keep in contact."
廉价	lián jià	cheap, low-priced
脸蛋	liǎn dàn	cheeks; (affectionate) little face — 脸 (face) + 蛋 (egg, oval). The egg-smooth oval of the cheeks, a warm word usually aimed at children or a pretty face. Often softened further to 脸蛋儿 (liǎndànr) with the northern -r ending.
脸红	liǎn hóng	to blush
脸面	liǎn miàn	face (combining both concepts); dignity; decency — A compound that fuses both face concepts. 脸面 covers the full spectrum , both the social prestige dimension of 面子 and the moral character dimension of 脸. Often used in contexts where both are at stake: 要顾脸面 (to consider one's dignity) means both social standing and personal integrity.
脸盆	liǎn pén	washbasin — 脸 (face) + 盆 (basin). The basin you fill to wash your face, a fixture of older Chinese households before plumbing reached every sink. A small window onto domestic life packed into two characters.
脸皮	liǎn pí	facial skin; (figurative) sense of shame; nerve — 脸 (face) + 皮 (skin). The skin of the face, and by extension one's capacity for shame. 脸皮厚 (liǎnpí hòu), thick-skinned, means shameless or unembarrassable; 脸皮薄 (liǎnpí báo), thin-skinned, means easily mortified.
脸谱	liǎn pǔ	painted faces
脸色	liǎn sè	complexion; facial expression; mood read off the face — 脸 (face) + 色 (color, look). Literally the color of the face, used both for physical complexion and for the mood it betrays. To 看脸色 (kàn liǎnsè) is to read someone's expression and act accordingly, a core social skill.
练兵	liàn bīng	to drill troops
练功	liàng ōng	to train one's craft, drill skills
练习	liàn xí	to practice; an exercise — 练 (drill) + 习 (to study, to be habituated). The two near-synonyms stack into the all-purpose word for practicing, and as a noun for a practice exercise or drill set.
练字	liàn zì	to practice writing characters; to practice calligraphy — 练 liàn (to practice; to train; to drill; originally, to boil silk to soften it) + 字 zì. Implies regularity, discipline, and sustained improvement. 练字 is deliberate, structured practice, not casual doodling.
炼	liàn	to refine, to smelt
良朋	liáng péng	a good friend: a worthy companion — 良 liáng (good, fine) + 朋 péng (friend). A good and worthy friend, the term carrying the older weight of 朋. Literary in tone; often paired as 良朋益友 "good friends who do you good." You would write it, not say it over coffee.
良心话	liáng xīn huà	honest words; words from the conscience; what one genuinely means — Speech that comes directly from the conscience, stripped of social performance, flattery, or evasion. The preface 说句良心话 signals: what follows is my genuine view, even if it is unwelcome.
良知	liáng zhī	innate moral knowledge; knowing good and evil without deliberation — The philosophical term from Mencius, developed by Wang Yangming into the center of his system. More formal and theoretical than 良心; primarily found in philosophical and intellectual contexts.
凉	liáng	cool
凉茶	liáng chá	cooling tea — Guangdong herbal brews for clearing heat
两岸	liǎng àn	Both shores — Both shores: the standard PRC and Taiwan term for the cross-strait relationship: literally "both banks" (of the Taiwan Strait). 两岸关系 (cross-strait relations) is the neutral term used by both governments.
两边	liǎng biān	both sides; the two sides — 两 liǎng + 边 biān (side). Both sides, the two sides of something, drawing directly on 两's sense of a balanced pair: 马路两边 (both sides of the road), 两边都同意 (both sides agree). Related are 两手 (both hands), 两旁 (on either side), and 两端 (the two ends).
两点	liǎng diǎn	two o'clock; two points — 两 liǎng + 点 diǎn (o'clock; point). Two o'clock: 下午两点 (two in the afternoon). Because 点 functions as a measure word here, the two must be 两, not 二. The same logic gives 两点意见 (two points of opinion).
两个	liǎng gè	two (of something); the two — 两 liǎng + 个 gè (the general measure word). The basic, high-frequency way to count two of almost anything: 两个人 (two people), 两个问题 (two questions), 两个小时 (two hours). The use of 两 rather than 二 here is obligatory, since a measure word follows.
两口子	liǎng kǒu zi	a married couple; husband and wife — 两 liǎng + 口 kǒu (mouth, here a person) + 子 (suffix). A colloquial, warm word for a married couple, the two of them as a household pair: 他们两口子 (the two of them, that couple). 小两口 (xiǎo liǎngkǒu) is an affectionate term for a young couple.
亮	liàng	bright; light up
量体温	liáng tǐ wēn	to take a temperature: to measure body heat — 量 liáng (to measure) + 体温 tǐwēn (body temperature). A high-frequency everyday verb-object phrase: to take someone's temperature with a thermometer. The same 量 measures other body data in parallel phrases, 量血压 (liáng xuèyā, to take blood pressure), 量身高 (liáng shēngāo, to measure height).
量子	liàng zi	Quantum — Quantum. 量子力学 is quantum mechanics; 量子纠缠 (entanglement, "quantum entwining") supplies metaphors for codependent relationships in internet slang.
聊天	liáo tiān	to chat
潦草	liáo cǎo	sloppy (writing)
列传	liè zhuàn	Biographies — individual lives — Seventy chapters, the largest and most celebrated section: biographies of statesmen, generals, philosophers, and also of assassins, jesters, merchants, physicians, knights-errant, and recluses.
猎狗	liè gǒu	hunting dog, hound — 猎 (liè, to hunt) + 狗. A dog bred or trained to hunt: pointer, retriever, hound. The character 猎 itself carries the beast radical 犭, so the compound stacks two animal-marked graphs.
邻居	lín jū	neighbor
林	lín	grove
林彪	Lín Biāo	Lin Biao, Mao's designated successor (d. 1971)
林则徐	Lín Zé xú	Commissioner who destroyed the opium
临济	Lín jì	Linji school (Rinzai in Japan)
临摹	lín mó	to copy a master's work in order to learn — 临 lín (to face; to approach, as in approaching the model directly) + 摹 mó (to trace; to copy). The foundational method of calligraphic learning: copying master works character by character to internalize their structure and spirit.
临写	lín xiě	to copy a calligraphic model
临终助念	lín zhōng zhùn iàn	deathbed recitation assistance — accompanying the dying — 临终 línzhōng (at the moment of death) + 助念 zhùniàn (assistance-recitation). A distinctively Chinese Pure Land practice: when a practitioner is dying, fellow practitioners gather and recite 南无阿弥陀佛 aloud continuously, for hours or even days.
灵	líng	spirit; efficacious
灵魂	líng hún	soul
灵活	líng huó	flexible; nimble; agile; adaptable — 灵 líng (quick, clever, responsive) + 活 huó (lively, free). Able to move or change freely: nimble fingers, a flexible schedule, a supple mind. The opposite is 死板 sǐbǎn (rigid, literally dead-stiff), where the water has stopped flowing.
灵梦救父	líng mèng jiù fù	the trance-rescue — spirit-sailing to save her father — The foundational legend: Lin Mo, while weaving at home, fell into a trance. In her spirit-state, she crossed the sea, seized her father's boat with one hand and her brother's boat with the other, guiding them through a terrible storm.
灵位	líng wèi	spirit seat; where soul dwells
绫罗绸缎	líng luó chóu duàn	The four major silk fabric types (gauze, thin silk, thick silk, satin) often used together as a set phrase meaning luxury silk goods in general. An elegant way to say "silk" in classical and literary contexts.
聆听	líng tīng	to listen attentively; to give careful ear to — 聆 (to listen carefully , 耳 radical) + 听 (to hear). A formal compound for respectful, focused listening. Used for listening to elders, teachers, and music: 聆听老师的教诲 (listen attentively to the teacher's instruction); 聆听音乐 (to truly listen to music, not just hear it).
零件	líng jiàn	spare part; component part — 零 líng (fragmentary; odd; loose) + 件 jiàn (a piece). The small individual parts that make up a machine, especially replaceable ones. 零 carries the sense of scattered, loose pieces, so 零件 is the loose hardware: screws, gears, the bits kept in a parts drawer.
零落	líng luò	to wither and fall; scattered; declined and broken up — 零 líng (to fall in drops) + 落 luò (to fall; to drop). Closest to the character's root: things falling and scattering. Of flowers and leaves, to wither and drop; of people, families, or fortunes, to scatter and decline (家道零落, the household fallen on hard times).
零钱	líng qián	small change; loose coins — 零 líng (zero; scattered; odd bits) + 钱 qián. Change, small bills and coins. Increasingly rare in mainland China where mobile payment (微信支付/支付宝) dominates. 找钱 zhǎo qián = to give change. 整钱 zhěng qián = a whole bill (no change needed).
零食	líng shí	snacks; between-meal food — 零 líng (small, piecemeal) + 食 shí (food). The bits eaten between whole meals: snacks, nibbles, treats. 吃零食 is to snack. The word reflects 零's sense of the scattered and fractional, food taken in small odd amounts rather than as a proper sit-down meal (整餐).
零下	líng xià	below zero; sub-zero (temperature) — 零 líng (zero) + 下 xià (below). Below zero degrees, the standard way to state freezing temperatures: 零下十度 (minus ten degrees). Its counterpart 零上 (above zero) is less often said, since positive temperatures are usually given as a bare number.
领会	lǐng huì	to grasp the import of
领事馆	lǐng shì guǎn	consulate
领土	lǐng tǔ	territory
领悟	lǐng wù	to grasp deeply
另外	lìng wài	"Besides" or "in addition — "Besides" or "in addition." Marks a related but separate point. Useful when you want to add information that is not strictly part of the same argument: 另外, 我想问你一件事 ("also, I want to ask you something").
刘半农	Liú Bàn nóng	Liu Bannong, the inventor of 她 (1891–1934)
刘备	liú bèi	Liu Bei — the archetype of the benevolent ruler — The distant Han relative who founds Shu, in the novel the moral hero: humane, humble, and devoted to righteousness, winning loyalty not through force but through virtue and human feeling.
流	liú	to flow
流传	liú chuán	to circulate; to spread and survive; to be handed down — 流 liú (to flow) + 传 chuán (to pass on). For a story, saying, or work to circulate widely and endure over time: 这个传说一直流传至今 "this legend has been handed down to the present day." Combines the flowing of 流 with the passing-on of 传.
流利	liú lì	fluent
流行	liú xíng	popular; trending; to spread (of fashion, illness, songs) — 流 liú (to flow) + 行 xíng. Flowing-and-traveling. The verb for something that spreads through a population , fashion, slang, popular songs, viruses. 流行音乐 = pop music; 流行病 = an epidemic; 这种说法很流行 = "this expression is very widespread."
留白	liú bái	留白 — leaving white; the empty space in composition — 留 liú (to leave; to keep) + 白 bái (white; blank). The deliberate empty space left unpainted in Chinese composition: 留白 is an active compositional element, not a failure to fill space. Empty space creates breathing room, implies what is unseen, and activates the imagination.
六波罗蜜	liù bō luó mì	the six paramitas; the six perfections of the bodhisattva path — 波罗蜜 bōluómì from Sanskrit pāramitā: "crossing to the other shore" , from suffering to liberation, from delusion to insight.
六道	liù dào	the six realms of rebirth — 六 (six) + 道 (path; way; realm). The six realms through which beings cycle in Buddhist cosmology: gods, asuras, humans, animals, hungry ghosts, and hell beings. The human realm (人道 rén dào or 人间道 rénjiān dào) occupies the middle tier. Beings move between realms through karma generated in each life.
六道轮回	liù dào lún huí	the six-realm cycle of rebirth; the full Buddhist cosmological cycle — The full phrase naming the complete cycle: 六道 (six realms) + 轮回 (the turning cycle). Used when the complete cosmological picture is being invoked rather than just the concept of rebirth in general.
六度	liù dù	the six perfections; the sixth is 般若
六法	liù fǎ	Six Principles
六合	liù hé	the six directions; the whole universe — 六 liù + 合 hé (to unite; union). The four compass directions plus up and down, hence the three-dimensional totality of space, the entire cosmos.
六六大顺	liù liù dà shùn	"six-six, all goes smoothly" — a wish for things to go well — 六六 (six-six) + 大顺 (great smoothness). A set blessing built on the homophony of 六 liù with 流 liú (to flow) and the auspicious 顺 shùn (smooth, unobstructed). Said at weddings, openings, and new beginnings to wish that everything proceed without obstacle.
六器	liù qì	six ritual jades
六亲	liù qīn	the six relations; close kin — 六 liù + 亲 qīn (relative; kin). The six categories of close relatives (variously listed: father, mother, elder and younger brothers, wife, children, in some reckonings). In practice 六亲 means one's close family as a whole.
六十甲子	liù shí jiǎ zǐ	The sixty-year cycle — The sixty-year cycle: the combination of twelve animals with ten Heavenly Stems, the basic unit of traditional Chinese time reckoning.
六艺	liù yì	the six arts of the Confucian gentleman — 六 liù + 艺 yì (art; skill). Rites (礼), music (乐), archery (射), charioteering (御), writing (书), and mathematics (数). The complete curriculum of the Zhou-dynasty gentleman, balancing civil and martial accomplishments.
六月	liù yu è	June; the sixth month — 六 liù + 月 yuè (month). June in the Gregorian calendar. Like all Chinese month names, it is simply the number plus 月, with no separate words for the months.
六祖	Liù zǔ	the Sixth Patriarch (Huineng)
六祖坛经	Liù zǔ tán jīng	the Platform Sutra of the Sixth Patriarch · ~20,000 characters · Tang dynasty — The only Chinese-authored Buddhist text designated a sutra. The recorded teachings of Huineng (惠能, 638-713 CE), the sixth patriarch of Chan and the founder of the Southern School.
龙场悟道	Lóng chǎng wù dào	the Longchang Enlightenment — The awakening Wang Yangming experienced in 1508 while exiled to Longchang in Guizhou province. In the middle of the night, he grasped that the principles he had been seeking in external objects were already present in the mind itself , the insight that became the foundation of 心学.
龙的传人	lóng de chuán rén	descendants of the dragon; heirs of the dragon civilization — 龙 lóng + 的 de (possessive marker) + 传人 chuánrén (one to whom something is transmitted; inheritor). The phrase reached mass consciousness through the 1978 song 龙的传人 by Taiwanese songwriter Hou Dejian 侯德健, recorded in 1980 and covered countless times since.
龙凤	lóng fèng	dragon and phoenix; the imperial pair; auspicious union of opposites — 龙 lóng + 凤 fèng (phoenix; 凤凰 fènghuáng). In classical Chinese cosmology, the dragon and phoenix are complementary opposites: 龙 is yang, male, the emperor, authority, and the power of water; 凤 is yin, female, the empress, grace, and the power of fire.
龙井	Lóng jǐng	Longjing — Dragon Well green tea — From 龙坞 Dragon Hollow near Hangzhou, Zhejiang. China's most famous green tea , flat-pressed leaves, clean chestnut aroma, and a 1,200-year history. Imperial tribute tea. 西湖龙井 Xī Hú Lóngjǐng (West Lake Dragon Well) is the protected-origin premium grade.
龙脉	lóng mài	dragon vein; underground energy channel — 龙 lóng + 脉 mài (vein; pulse; channel). In feng shui 风水 theory, mountains are the visible backs of buried dragons; the ridgelines and peaks are the spine of a living creature moving through the earth. The 龙脉 are the underground channels through which this vital energy flows.
龙门	Lóng mén	Dragon Gate — the grottoes; the legend of transformation — 龙 lóng + 门 mén (gate; door). Two distinct referents share this name. The historical Longmen Grottoes 龙门石窟 near Luoyang (Henan province) are a UNESCO World Heritage site: over 100,000 Buddhist statues carved into cliff faces along the Yi River, primarily during the Northern Wei and Tang dynasties.
龙年	lóng nián	Year of the Dragon — 龙 lóng + 年 nián (year). The Dragon is the fifth sign of the twelve-year zodiac cycle 生肖 shēngxiào and the only mythic creature among the twelve.
龙袍	lóng páo	dragon robe; the emperor's ceremonial garment — 龙 lóng + 袍 páo (long robe). The emperor's official ceremonial dress, embroidered with nine five-clawed dragons on a yellow ground (yellow being the imperial color, reserved exclusively for the Son of Heaven).
龙头	lóng tóu	dragon head; leader; industry frontrunner; water faucet — 龙 lóng + 头 tóu (head). Three active meanings in modern Chinese: (1) the dragon's literal head, (2) the leader or dominant player in an industry or organization (龙头企业 is the standard term for a market-leading company), and (3) a water faucet or tap, because classical bronze spigots were cast as a dra…
龙椅	lóng yǐ	the Dragon Throne; the imperial seat — 龙 lóng + 椅 yǐ (chair; seat). The emperor's throne, elaborately carved with five-clawed dragons, in the Hall of Supreme Harmony 太和殿 of the Forbidden City in Beijing.
龙舟	lóng zhōu	dragon boat — 龙 (dragon) + 舟 (boat, the classical word for it; 船 chuán is more common in modern speech). The ornate long boat with a dragon head at the prow. 赛龙舟 (to race dragon boats) is the verb phrase. 龙舟竞渡 (lóngzhōu jìngdù) is the literary term for the race.
聋	lóng	deaf
楼	lóu	multi-story building; floor
楼房	lóu fáng	multi-storey building — 楼 (lóu, a building of multiple floors) plus 房. A building with stories stacked above ground, contrasted with the single-level 平房 (píngfáng). The everyday word for the apartment blocks of a modern Chinese city.
楼上	lóu shàng	upstairs; the floor above — 楼 lóu (building; storied structure; floor) + 上 shàng (above; up). The floor above; upstairs. 楼下 is the natural pair. These two form one of the most common directional pairs in everyday speech, used for apartments, offices, restaurants, and any multi-story space.
鲁班	lǔ bān	Lu Ban, master carpenter
鲁菜	Lǔ cài	Shandong Cuisine
鲁国	Lǔ Guó	the state of Lu — Confucius's home state
鲁迅	lǔ xùn	Lu Xun, who praised it
鲁智深	lǔ zhì shēn	Lu Zhishen — the tattooed monk — A hot-blooded army officer who kills a bullying butcher to save a wronged girl, then becomes a monk to escape the law, the "Flowery Monk" covered in tattoos.
录音	lù yīn	to record; a recording
禄	lù	official salary; prosperity (三星福禄寿)
路程	lù chéng	journey distance, road length
路灯	lù dēng	street lamp — 路 (lù, road) + 灯 (lamp): the tall lights lining a street. They come on at dusk and mark the shape of the road after dark.
路口	lù kǒu	intersection, road junction — 路 lù (road) + 口 kǒu (mouth, opening). The "mouth of the road," where roads meet and a choice opens. Used both literally (the intersection up ahead) and figuratively for any decision point: 人生的路口 rénshēng de lùkǒu (a crossroads in life).
露	lù	dew; to expose
驴	lǘ	donkey
旅行	lǚ xíng	to travel; travel, a trip — 旅 lǚ (to journey; a traveler) + 行 xíng. The standard word for travel as recreation or as an extended trip. Distinguished from 出差 chūchāi (business travel) and 旅游 lǚyóu (tourism, sightseeing). 一次愉快的旅行 "a pleasant trip."
旅行社	lǚ xíng shè	travel agency — 旅行 lǚxíng (to travel) + 社 shè (agency). The business that arranges trips, tours, and tickets: 跟旅行社报名 "to sign up through a travel agency," 旅行社的导游 "the agency's tour guide." A textbook example of the agency sense of 社 attached to a whole two-syllable field word.
律诗	lǜ shī	regulated verse — the Tang's dominant poetic form — The eight-line regulated poem: fixed tonal pattern, mandatory parallelism in the middle two couplets (对仗 duìzhàng), and strict rhyme scheme. An eight-line poem in five-character lines (五言律诗 wǔyán lǜshī) or seven-character lines (七言律诗 qīyán lǜshī).
虑	lǜ	to deliberate; to consider; worry
绿	lǜ	green
绿茶	lǜ chá	green tea — the unoxidized counterpart white tea is most often contrasted with
绿灯	lǜ dēng	green light
绿叶红边	lǜ yè hóng biān	green leaf red edge — the visual sign of oolong oxidation
卵	luǎn	egg (literary/biological)
乱八糟	bā	eight as "every kind / all over" — In a large family of four-character expressions, 八 pairs with another number (most often 七) to mean "of all sorts, every which way," not a literal count: 七嘴八舌 (everyone talking at once), 四面八方 (from all directions), 七上八下 (agitated).
轮船	lún chuán	steamship, large powered ship — 轮 (wheel) + 船 (boat): the wheel is the paddle wheel of the first steamers, so the word came to mean any large engine-driven ship. Today it is the everyday term for a big passenger or cargo liner.
论文	lùn wén	a paper; a thesis; a dissertation — 论 + 文 wén (writing, text). A formal academic argument in writing: a journal paper, a term paper, a graduate thesis. 毕业论文 bìyè lùnwén "graduation thesis," 发表论文 fābiǎo lùnwén "to publish a paper."
罗汉	luó hàn	arhat — Theravada ideal of personal liberation
罗盘	luó pán	Geomantic compass — Geomantic compass: a multi-ring instrument centered on a magnetic needle, encoding the eight trigrams, Heavenly Stems and Earthly Branches, 24 solar term directions, 28 lunar mansions, and multiple other cosmological correspondence systems in concentric rings.
锣鼓	luó gǔ	Gongs and drums — Gongs and drums: the percussion ensemble that provides the rhythmic foundation of Peking Opera and cues entrances, emotional intensification, and combat sequences.
落成	luò chéng	to be completed (of a building); inauguration — 落 luò (to settle) + 成 chéng (to complete, to become). Used specifically for the completion of a building or major construction project. 落成典礼 = inauguration ceremony.
落第	luò dì	to fail the examination; to be rejected from the list — Literally "to fall from the notice board": 第 refers to the ranked list of successful candidates posted publicly after each examination. To fail was to have your name absent from that list.
落后	luò hòu	to fall behind; backward; underdeveloped — 落 luò + 后 hòu (behind). To lag behind others in a race, in development, or in progress. Used in sports, economics, and social commentary. Carries a normative weight , something that has fallen behind should catch up.
落脚	luò jiǎo	to settle in (a place); to find lodging; to come to rest — 落 luò (to fall; to land) + 脚. To set the foot down after travel , to find a place to stay, to settle in temporarily or permanently. 在北京落脚 "to settle in Beijing" (especially as a newcomer); 落脚点 luòjiǎodiǎn "a place to settle, a foothold."
落实	luò shí	to implement; to pin down; to make concrete — 落 luò (to land) + 实 shí (solid, real, concrete). To take something from the abstract and make it real , to land on a decision, confirm a plan, carry out a policy. One of the most important governance and management words in Chinese.
落下	luò xià / là xia	to fall; to drop behind; to be left behind — Two distinct pronunciations with different meanings. luò xià = to fall downward (rain falling, price falling). là·xia (neutral tone) = to fall behind, to leave something behind by accident: 把钥匙落下了 "left the keys behind."
妈妈	mā ma	mother
妈祖	Mā zǔ	Mazu — goddess of the sea, protector of sailors — A historical woman named Lin Mo (林默, 960–987 CE) from Fujian Province who died young and was venerated as a protector of fishermen. Over centuries, the imperial court awarded her progressively higher titles, and she was absorbed into the Daoist pantheon.
麻烦	má fan	trouble; hassle
麻辣	má là	numbing-spicy — Numbing-spicy , the characteristic flavor combination of Sichuan cuisine, combining the numbing sensation (麻 má) of Sichuan pepper (花椒 huājiāo) with the heat (辣 là) of chili pepper. The geographic signature of the Sichuan basin's isolated, humid interior.
麻辣锅底	má là guō dǐ	mala broth base; numbing-hot pot
麻婆豆腐	má pó dòu fu	Mapo Tofu
马车	mǎ chē	horse-drawn carriage, cart — 马 (horse) + 车 (cart, vehicle). The literal horse-and-cart, the carriage that ran on the 马路 long before engines.
马虎	mǎ hu	careless, sloppy — 马 (horse) + 虎 (tiger). A folk tale tells of a painter who drew a creature half horse, half tiger and could not say which it was; the slapdash beast gave its name to careless, half-attentive work.
马可·波罗	Mǎ kě Bō luó	Marco Polo
马拉松	mǎ lā sōng	marathon
马力	mǎ lì	horsepower — 马 (horse) + 力 (power, force). A loan-translation of "horsepower," the unit that measures an engine in horses, keeping the animal alive inside the machine that replaced it.
马路	mǎ lù	street, paved road, main thoroughfare — 马 mǎ (horse) + 路 lù (road). Originally "horse road," wide and packed enough to take horse traffic, in contrast with footpaths. In modern usage 马路 is the standard word for a paved urban street, especially one with vehicle traffic. Crossing it: 过马路 guò mǎlù.
马年	mǎ nián	Year of the Horse
马上	mǎ shàng	immediately; right away; at once — 马 mǎ (horse) + 上 shàng (on top of; mounted on). Literally "on horseback," from the military image of an order being carried out while still mounted, without dismounting. One of the most common adverbs in spoken Mandarin.
马戏	mǎ xì	circus
码头	mǎ tou	pier; also: turf (underworld)
买	mǎi	to buy; to purchase
买不起	mǎi bu qǐ	cannot afford — Buy + not + shoulder. Distinct from 买不到 (can't get one: sold out) , the two negatives diagnose different tragedies, wallet versus supply chain.
买了	mǎi le	bought; purchased (transaction complete) — Transactional and decisional verbs with 了 are extremely common in everyday conversation. The completion is commercially and practically significant , the item has been acquired, the deal is done, the decision has been executed.
麦西热甫	mài xī rè fǔ	meshrep — the traditional Uyghur community gathering — 麦西热甫 màixīrèfǔ (meshrep, from Persian/Turkic mäshrip meaning "gathering place") is the traditional Uyghur social institution: a community assembly that brings people together for music, dance, storytelling, comedy, and the resolution of social disputes through a combination of festivity and collecti…
馒头	mán tou	steamed bun
满	mǎn	full, brimming
满意	mǎn yì	satisfied; pleased; content — 满 mǎn (full) + 意 yì (heart; wish). The heart filled to its wish, hence satisfied: 对结果很满意 "very pleased with the result." Its opposite is 不满 bùmǎn, dissatisfied.
满月	mǎn yu è	the one-month celebration for a newborn — 满 mǎn (full; complete) + 月 yuè (month). When a baby has survived its first full month , historically the most dangerous period , the family celebrates with red eggs (红鸡蛋 hóng jīdàn), gifting of gold jewelry, and a feast. The custom signals that the child is likely to thrive.
满足	mǎn zú	satisfied; to satisfy; contentment — 满 (mǎn, full; filled to capacity) + 足 (sufficient). Completely sufficient: fulfillment, contentment, the state of having enough and knowing it. Both a verb (满足需求, to satisfy a need) and an adjective (感到很满足, to feel deeply satisfied).
满族	Mǎn zú	Manchu people
慢慢	màn màn	slowly; gradually; little by little — Reduplication of 慢 to form an adverb of manner. 慢慢 implies an unhurried pace that unfolds naturally over time, without pressure. Warmer in register than 慢慢地 (the version with the adverbial particle 地), though both are standard.
慢慢地走	màn màn de zǒu	walks slowly — reduplicated adverb + 地 + verb — 地 links a manner adverb or adverbial phrase to the verb it modifies. It always sits between the adverb and the verb. The adverb answers "how?" the action is performed. Reduplication of adjectives to form adverbs + 地 is extremely common and productive: 慢慢地, 快快地, 轻轻地, 安静地.
慢性	màn xìng	chronic; slow-developing (medical) — 慢 màn (slow) + 性 xìng (nature, character, disposition). "Of a slow nature." The medical term for chronic conditions, contrasted with 急性 jíxìng (acute, sudden-onset). 慢性病 mànxìngbìng = chronic illness. 慢性子 mànxíngzi = a person with a slow, unhurried temperament (slightly pejorative; see note).
慢走	màn zǒu	walk slowly — polite farewell to a departing guest — 慢 màn (slow) + 走 zǒu (to walk; to leave). Literally "walk slowly" , said by a host as a guest departs, equivalent to "take care" or "safe travels." The Chinese farewell phrase, so common it has become almost automatic. The literal meaning (don't rush away) has calcified into pure social courtesy.
漫画	màn huà	comics; cartoon; manga — 漫 màn (free; unrestrained) + 画 huà (drawing). A free, sketchy drawing: the word for comics and cartoons. The same two characters name Japanese manga, where they are read manga.
忙	máng	busy
盲	máng	blind
盲目	máng mù	blindly; without judgment; reckless — 盲 (blind) + 目 (eye). An intensified compound where both characters carry the same meaning: acting as though blind, without seeing the situation clearly. 盲目乐观 mángmù lèguān means "blindly optimistic"; 盲目跟风 means to follow trends without thinking.
猫	māo	cat
毛	máo	body hair; fur
毛笔	máo bǐ	writing brush; ink brush — 毛 máo (hair; fur) + 笔 bǐ. The classic soft-tipped brush of calligraphy and ink painting, named for the animal hair of its tuft. The tool that gives Chinese writing its living, varying line.
毛病	máo bìng	defect, flaw, bad habit; malfunction — 毛 (máo) means fine hair or fur, here the trivial detail; 毛病 is a small fault that keeps recurring. It stretches across a machine that breaks down, a person's annoying habit, and a minor recurring ailment, all the everyday senses of "something is wrong with it."
毛茶	máo chá	rough tea / mao cha — the uncompressed base material
毛肚	máo dǔ	tripe; honeycomb tripe
毛泽东	Máo Zé dōng	Mao Zedong — predecessor, contrast
矛盾论	máo dùn lùn	On Contradiction — On Contradiction: Mao Zedong's 1937 philosophical essay applying materialist dialectics to political analysis. One of the two foundational texts of Mao Zedong Thought (alongside 实践论 Shíjiàn Lùn, "On Practice").
茅台	máo tái	Moutai — Moutai: the prestige baijiu produced in Maotai Town, Guizhou province. Made from sorghum using the sauce-aroma (酱香 jiàngxiāng) method, it is the most famous Chinese spirit and functions as a currency of respect in formal Chinese business culture.
茅屋	máo wū	thatched hut; humble dwelling — 茅 máo (thatch grass) + 屋 wū (dwelling). A simple hut roofed with grass. The 茅屋 is a literary image of humble, virtuous poverty , the dwelling of the reclusive scholar or hermit.
冒出	mào chū	to pop up; to suddenly emerge
贸易	mào yì	trade, commerce
贸易公司	mào yì gōng sī	trading company
没	méi	not; have not
没吃	méi chī	didn't eat; have not eaten — 没(有) is the standard negator for completed actions. It replaces 了 entirely , you never say 没吃了, because 没 already carries the information that no completion occurred. The 了 is superfluous and wrong. This is a near-universal rule: 没 and post-verb 了 are mutually exclusive.
没错	méi cuò	that's right; exactly; no mistake — 没 méi (not have) + 错 cuò (error). "No error here," used to agree firmly: 没错，我就是这个意思 "exactly, that's just what I mean." Often softened to 没错儿 in northern speech.
没道理	méi dào lǐ	unreasonable; makes no sense; unjust — 没 méi (lack; without) + 道理 dàolǐ (reason; logic). "Without reason": lacking the logic or justice to be acceptable. Used when something is not just inconvenient but genuinely wrong in its reasoning or fairness.
没关系	méi guān xi	it doesn't matter; never mind; that's okay — One of the most indispensable phrases in Mandarin. The standard response to 对不起 (sorry). Literally "no relationship" , meaning "it has no effect on us; it carries no weight." Learn this before your first week of speaking Chinese.
没礼貌	méi lǐ mào	rude; ill-mannered; lacking in manners — 没 méi (lacks; does not have) + 礼貌. The standard rebuke. Heavier than English "rude" because it implies a failure of formation, not just a momentary lapse.
没面子	méi miàn zi	to lose face; to be embarrassed; without prestige — To be without face , whether because one has lost it through embarrassing behavior or because one's status is insufficient to command it. 没面子 is the social embarrassment register, distinct from the moral shame of 丢脸.
没什么	méi shén me	nothing / it's nothing / no big deal — 没 + 什么 (what; anything). 没什么 = "nothing; it's nothing." As a response to thanks or apologies: 没什么！= "No worries! / It's nothing!" Also as a statement: 冰箱里没什么吃的 "There's nothing much to eat in the fridge."
没事	méi shì	it's nothing; no problem; never mind; to be free (not busy) — 没 méi (not have) + 事 shì. "No matter." The everyday reassurance: it's fine, don't worry, no harm done. Also literally "to have nothing on," to be free.
没问题	méi wèn tí	no problem; sure; that's fine — One of the most useful everyday phrases, used to agree to a request, reassure, or confirm that something will be fine. Equivalent to "no problem / sure / consider it done." A warm, willing reply.
没意思	méi yì si	boring; pointless; not interesting — The negation of 有意思 , lacking appeal, engagement, or point. Can describe activities, conversations, people, or situations. Often used for mild complaint rather than outright criticism. 说没意思的话 (to say pointless things) is a common mild rebuke.
眉	méi	eyebrow
梅兰芳	Méi Lán fāng	greatest Peking Opera performer
梅雨	méi yǔ	plum rains
湄洲祖庙	Méi zhōu Zǔ miào	the ancestral temple — source of all Mazu worship — The Meizhou Ancestral Temple (湄洲祖庙) on Meizhou Island, Putian, Fujian, is the origin point of all Mazu temples worldwide. The first small temple was reportedly built at her grave site not long after her death in 987 CE. It has been expanded, burned, rebuilt, and expanded again across ten centuries.
每	měi	every, each one of an identical series — 每 walks through a set member by member: 每个人 (every person), 每本书 (every book). It requires a measure word before the noun, with a short list of exceptions that are themselves measure-like: 每天, 每年, 每次 (day, year, time).
每天 · 天天	měi tiān tiān tiān	every day · day after day (emphatic) — 每天 měitiān: 每 měi (every; each) + 天 tiān (day). The neutral distributive "every day." 每 attaches to any time unit: 每年 (every year), 每月 (every month), 每周 (every week).
美德	měi dé	a virtue; an admirable quality — 美 měi (beautiful; admirable) + 德. A beautiful virtue , a particular admirable quality, usable as a count noun. 谦虚是一种美德 "humility is a virtue." 中华民族的传统美德 "the traditional virtues of the Chinese nation" is one of the standard formulas in civic discourse.
美感	měi gǎn	aesthetic sense; sense of beauty — 美 měi + 感 gǎn (feeling; sense). The capacity to perceive and appreciate beauty. Used in art criticism, design, and discussions of taste. 有美感 "has a good aesthetic sense" · 缺乏美感 "lacks aesthetic awareness."
美国	Měi guó	USA (lit. "beautiful country") — 美 měi (beautiful) + 国 guó. From the phonetic transcription of "America" (亚美利加 Yà-Měi-lì-jiā), with 美 selected as both sound-match and flattering character. A lucky phonetic fit.
美好	měi hǎo	beautiful; wonderful
美化	měi huà	to beautify; to prettify; to whitewash — 美 měi (beautiful) + 化 huà (the -ize suffix). Literally "to make beautiful." 美化 covers both the literal beautifying of a place (planting flowers, cleaning streets) and the figurative dressing-up of something unattractive, that is, putting a flattering gloss on a person, a record, or a history.
美丽	měi lì	beautiful (poetic, elevated) — 美 měi + 丽 lì (splendid; brilliant , originally: a deer with fine antlers). Used for landscapes, natural scenes, and elevated emotional beauty. More literary than 漂亮. 美丽的风景 "beautiful scenery" · 美丽的心灵 "a beautiful soul."
美女	měi nǚ	miss (lit. beauty); address for a young woman
美食	měi shí	delicious food; gourmet cuisine; gastronomy — 美 měi (beautiful; excellent) + 食 shí (food; to eat). Fine food; delicacies. Used everywhere food culture is celebrated , travel blogs, TV shows, restaurant descriptions. 美食家 měishíjiā = gourmet; food connoisseur.
妹妹	mèi mei	younger sister
昧良心	mèi liáng xīn	to go against one's conscience; to act in bad faith — 昧 mèi (to obscure; to violate knowingly). 昧良心 is the standard phrase for suppressing what one's conscience perceives and doing wrong anyway , the act Wang Yangming calls covering the sun with clouds.
门槛	mén kǎn	threshold (canonical object of 跨过)
门口	mén kǒu	doorway; entrance — 门 mén (door) + 口 kǒu (mouth; opening). The mouth of the door, the spot just at the entrance: 在门口等 "to wait at the door," 学校门口 "the school gate."
门派	mén pài	martial sect; faction
门票	mén piào	entrance ticket: an admission ticket; the entry fee — 门 mén (door; gate; entrance) + 票 piào (slip; ticket). The ticket that admits you through the gate: into a park, a museum, a temple, a concert. By extension it also names the price of admission, what you pay at the door.
门神	mén shen	door god; guardian deity of the threshold
闷黄	mèn yuáng	smothering / yellowing — the defining yellow tea step
盟会	méng huì	covenant assembly — the ritual mechanism of Spring and Autumn interstate order — The 盟会 was the formal interstate assembly convened by the hegemon lord, at which vassal states renewed oaths of loyalty and the Zhou order was publicly reaffirmed through sacrifice and blood covenant (歃血为盟 shà xuè wéi méng , smearing sacrificial blood on the lips as oath).
蒙古人	Měng gǔ rén	Mongols — first class — Rulers and military commanders. Held supreme authority. Exempt from many taxes. Administered the empire primarily in Mongolian. Kublai maintained a deliberately Mongolian court culture even as he adopted many Chinese administrative forms.
孟姜女	Mèng Jiāng nǚ	Meng Jiangnu (the wall-weeping legend)
孟子	Mèng zǐ	Mencius — the second great Confucian philosopher — Mengzi (372–289 BCE) developed Confucian thought in two crucial directions: the argument for the innate goodness of human nature (性善论 xìngshàn lùn , humans are naturally oriented toward virtue), and a robust theory of political legitimacy based on the welfare of the people.
梦	mèng	dream
梦见	mèng jiàn	to see in a dream, to dream of
梦想	mèng xiǎng	dream; aspiration; to dream of — 梦 mèng (dream; to dream) + 想 xiǎng (thought; aspiration). Dream-thought: a vision held in the heart that exceeds the present reality. Warmer and more personal than 理想 (which has a principled, philosophical register); closer to the English "dream" in the sense of a cherished personal ambition.
弥勒	Mí lè	Maitreya — the future Buddha; the Laughing Buddha of popular China — Sanskrit Maitreya: the Buddha of the future age, who will appear in the world when the teachings of Śākyamuni have been entirely forgotten, to re-teach the Dharma.
迷幻状态	mí huàn zhuàng tài	trance state
迷路	mí lù	to lose one's way, to get lost — 迷 mí (confused, lost) + 路 lù (road). Literally "to be confused about the road." Used for getting lost in a city, in mountains, or on the way to somewhere. The figurative sense (losing one's moral or career direction) usually prefers 迷失方向 míshī fāngxiàng or 走错路 zǒu cuò lù.
迷失方向	mí shī fāng xiàng	to lose one's bearings; to be lost (literally or figuratively) — 迷 (mí, to be confused; to lose oneself) + 失 (shī, to lose) + 方向. A heavier and more poetic version of 失去方向. Used for someone literally lost in a forest or city, but more often for someone in a moral, professional, or personal crisis. Common in advice writing, sermons, and the chorus of pop songs.
迷信	mì xìn	superstition
米	mǐ	Meter, borrowed onto the rice character purely for sound — Meter, borrowed onto the rice character purely for sound. 一米八 is the shorthand for an enviable height; 百米 is the hundred-meter dash.
密封	mì fēng	to seal hermetically; airtight — 密 (mì, tight / dense) plus 封 (to seal): to seal tightly so nothing leaks in or out. The word for airtight packaging, vacuum jars, and sealed envelopes.
密宗	Mì Zōng	Esoteric / Tantric Buddhism — mantra, mudra, mandala — The esoteric or Tantric school introduced to China in the 8th century CE by Indian masters Śubhakarasiṃha, Vajrabodhi, and Amoghavajra. Also called 真言宗 Zhēnyán Zōng (Mantra School).
面包	miàn bāo	bread — 面 miàn (flour) + 包 bāo (wrapped, bun). Literally "flour-bun." The general word for bread of the Western loaf type, as distinct from steamed 馒头 mántou.
面对	miàn duì	to face; to confront; to deal with — 面 (face) + 对 (to face; to be opposite to). Lit. "face-to-face with." To confront a reality, problem, or situation squarely rather than avoiding it. Widely used in motivational, psychological, and journalistic writing.
面前	miàn qián	in front of; before (someone); in one's presence — 面 miàn (face) + 前 qián (front). The space directly before one's face. Often used figuratively: 在困难面前 "in the face of difficulty," 在事实面前 "before the facts."
面容	miàn róng	countenance; the face
面条	miàn tiáo	noodles — The same 面 character. In northern China, 面 = wheat-flour dough → noodles. The flat, surface-like sheet of worked dough becomes strips (条 tiáo = strip). A vivid reminder that 面 means "surface/face" in the most general sense , the flat plane.
面子工程	miàn zi gōng chéng	Face engineering — Face engineering: infrastructure or projects built primarily for prestige rather than practical need. Empty airports in small cities, oversized government buildings, unnecessary highways: these are classic 面子工程.
描	miáo	to trace; to describe
描写	miáo xiě	to describe; to depict; to portray
秒	miǎo	second (time)
渺小	miǎo xiǎo	insignificant; tiny before the vast
庙会	miào huì	temple fair — festival of community and gods — 庙会 (temple fair, lit. "temple gathering") are among the oldest continuing community events in Chinese civilization. Major ones, like the Mazu pilgrimage in Dajia, Taiwan, or the Tianjin Tianhou Palace fair, attract tens or hundreds of thousands of participants.
灭谛	miè dì	the truth of the cessation of suffering — nirvāṇa — 灭 miè = extinction, cessation. Sanskrit nirodha. The good news: the cause can be eliminated. When craving ceases, suffering ceases. This is 涅槃 niépán (nirvāṇa), literally the "blowing out" of the fires of craving, hatred, and delusion. The Chinese 灭 captures the extinction aspect.
灭火	miè huǒ	to extinguish fire
民胞物与	mín bāo wù yǔ	all people are kin; all things are companions — universal fellowship — 民 (people) + 胞 (womb-siblings; kin) + 物 (things) + 与 (companions; associates). Zhang Zai's ethical derivation from 天人合一: if Heaven and Earth are the parents of all, then all human beings are kin born of the same parents, and all other things are our companions in this cosmic family.
民歌	míng ē	folk song — 民 + 歌 gē (song). A song that arises and circulates among the people rather than being composed for the concert hall: the traditional songs of a region or an ethnic group, often anonymous and passed down orally.
民间	mín jiān	among the people; folk; non-governmental — 民 + 间 jiān (among, within). The realm of ordinary life as opposed to the official, the courtly, or the state: that which exists among the people.
民间信仰	mín jiān xìn yǎng	folk religion
民权主义	Mín quán Zhǔ yì	Democracy — the rights and sovereignty of the people — Sun's vision of Chinese democracy combined Western constitutionalism with Chinese institutional traditions. The "Five-Power Constitution" added examination and control functions to the legislative-executive-judicial trifecta.
民生	mín shēng	the people's livelihood
民生主义	Mín shēng Zhǔ yì	People's Livelihood — economic justice and social welfare — The social-economic principle, influenced by land-value taxation theory and early socialism. Sun feared that China would develop capitalism's inequalities along with its productive power.
民心	mín xīn	the heart of the people
民众	mín zhòng	the masses; the general public — 民 + 众 zhòng (multitude, crowd). The ordinary public viewed as a large body, common in news writing and formal description: how the public reacts, what the public expects. More descriptive and less ideologically charged than 人民, and broader and more neutral than the warmer, address-style 大家.
民主	mín zhǔ	democracy; democratic — 民 mín (the people) + 主 zhǔ (master). The people as master, the modern term for democracy. Also used adjectivally: 民主国家 "a democratic country," 民主选举 "a democratic election."
民族	mín zú	ethnic group; nationality; a people — 民 + 族 zú (clan, lineage, group). A people considered as a cultural and ethnic stock: an ethnic group, a nationality, a nation in the cultural sense. China officially recognises 56 民族, the 汉族 Hànzú (Han) and 55 少数民族 shǎoshù mínzú (ethnic minorities).
民族主义	Mín zú Zhǔ yì	Nationalism — national identity and anti-imperialism — The first principle, evolving from anti-Manchu sentiment to pan-Chinese national identity to resistance against foreign imperialism. The evolution of this principle tracks the broader evolution of Chinese nationalism: from ethnic to civic, from anti-dynastic to anti-colonial.
闽南语	Mǐn nán yǔ	Southern Min , the Hokkien topolect spoken in southern Fujian, Taiwan (as 台語 Táiyǔ or 台灣話 Táiwānhuà), and across Southeast Asian Chinese communities in Malaysia, Singapore, Indonesia, and the Philippines.
敏感	mǐng ǎn	sensitive; highly responsive — 敏 mǐn (quick; alert; agile) + 感 gǎn (feel). Quick to feel. Used across registers: personality traits, skin types, and politically sensitive topics.
名辩	míng biàn	Mohist name-disputation
名词	míng cí	noun — 名 míng (name) + 词 cí (word). The "name-word," a noun: a word for a person, place, thing, or idea. 桌子, 北京, and 自由 are all 名词.
名次	míng cì	ranking; place
名分	míng fèn	name and duty — the obligation that comes with a title — 名 míng (name) + 分 fèn (portion, allotted duty). One's station and the obligations that station carries.
名家	Míng jiā	School of Names
名句	míng jù	a famous line; a well-known quotation — 名 míng (famous) + 句 jù (line). A celebrated line of verse or prose, so well known that it stands on its own apart from the work it came from. The pairing 千古名句 "a famous line for the ages" praises a line that has lasted.
名片	míng piàn	business card — 名 míng (name) + 片 piàn (flat slice; card). A "name slice" , the physical object that carries your name into another person's hand. Business card exchange in China carries ritual weight: present and receive with both hands, read the card before putting it away, never write on it.
名人	míng rén	famous person; celebrity; notable — 名 míng (name; fame) + 人 rén (person). A person whose name is widely known. Covers historical greats, contemporary celebrities, and anyone of notable standing. Context determines whether the register is elevated (historical figures) or casual (entertainers).
名声	míng shēng	reputation; standing (can be good or bad) — 名 míng (name) + 声 shēng (sound; voice; what is heard). What others say and hear about you , your name as it sounds in the world. Crucially neutral: 好名声 (good reputation) vs 坏名声 (bad reputation). The modifier does the moral work; 名声 itself simply denotes the fact of being talked about.
名实	míng shí	name and reality — the alignment that 正名 seeks — 名 míng (name) + 实 shí (reality, actuality). The pair names the problem: when 名 and 实 diverge, something false is being presented to the world. 名实相符 (name and reality correspond) is the condition 正名 aims to restore.
名望	míng wàng	fame; good standing; reputation — 名 míng (name) + 望 wàng (esteem). The fame and good repute a person enjoys: 很有名望 "to be of high standing." It blends being widely known with being respected, the regard attached to a name.
名誉	míng yù	reputation
名字	míng zi	name (personal name, general term) — 名 míng (name) + 字 zì (character; in classical usage, the courtesy name given at adulthood). In modern Mandarin, 名字 is the everyday word for a person's name , first name, full name, or nickname depending on context. Neutral register, universally usable.
明朝	míng cháo	Ming dynasty
明了	míng liǎo	to understand clearly
明亮	míng liàng	bright; luminous; well-lit — 明 míng + 亮 liàng (bright; shining). The compound for physical and figurative luminosity. Used for rooms, eyes, futures, and voices (a 明亮的嗓音 is a clear, bright singing voice).
明年	míng nián	next year — 明 míng + 年 nián (year). The same "bright/coming" sense extended to years. Pattern: 明天 tomorrow · 明年 next year · 明月 the bright moon (classical). The character 明 reaches across all timescales.
明前茶	míng qián chá	pre-Qingming tea — the highest harvest grade
明确	míng qu è	clear and definite; explicit; to specify — 明 míng + 确 què (definite; certain; firm). Used for rules, policies, goals, and instructions that leave no room for ambiguity. More formal than 明白. 请明确你的要求 = "Please specify your requirements."
明堂	míng táng	Bright hall — Bright hall: the open space in front of a geomantically ideal site, where qi gathers before the building. The concept appears in both palace architecture and burial site theory.
明天	míng tiān	tomorrow — 明 míng (bright; dawn; the next) + 天 tiān (day). The "bright day," the day the sun will rise again. 明 also means "next" in 明年 míngnián (next year) and 明月 míngyuè (the bright moon, a poetic image for longing). Note: in classical Chinese, 明日 míngrì is the literary form; 明天 is modern spoken Mandarin.
明天会下雨	míng tiān huì xià yǔ	it will rain tomorrow — 会 as future/likelihood marker — 会 has a second major function entirely distinct from learned ability: expressing expectation, likelihood, or prediction about a future event. This 会 does not mean "has learned how to" , rain cannot "learn" to rain. Instead, it signals the speaker's confident expectation that something will occur.
明天就到了	míng tiān jiù dào le	arrives as soon as tomorrow; it'll be here by tomorrow already — 就 can also point toward an imminent future event, expressing that something will happen sooner than might be expected. The speaker's attitude: "it's coming up fast, it'll be here before you know it." This future-pointing 就 often co-occurs with 了 at the sentence end.
明显	míng xiǎn	obvious; evident; marked — 明 míng + 显 xiǎn (to show; visible; eminent). What is bright is also visible to all. Used when something is undeniably apparent: improvement, change, a lie. 明显进步 "marked improvement."
明星	míng xīng	star (celebrity) — 明 (bright) + 星: the bright star, classical Chinese for Venus before it became the word for screen and stage fame. 追星 ("chasing stars") is fandom, and a 粉丝 (fěnsī, fan, a sound-loan that also means glass noodles) does the chasing.
明智	míng zhì	wise; sensible; showing good judgment — 明 míng (clear, bright) + 智 zhì (wisdom; intelligence). A bright mind, one that sees clearly. The compliment given to good decisions, prudent actions. 这是个明智的选择 "That is a wise choice."
冥币	míng bì	Ghost money — Ghost money: paper currency printed for burning at funerals and during ghost festivals. The term literally means "underworld currency." The notes often imitate real currency designs or depict the Bank of Hades. A broader category, 纸钱 (zhǐqián), covers all paper goods burned for the dead.
命苦	mìng kǔ	hard fate; to have a bitter lot in life
命令	mìng lìng	to order; an order, a directive — 命 plus 令 lìng (order). A synonym-compound: both characters mean "command," doubled for clarity. Used for formal, top-down orders in military, governmental, and parental contexts. Cooler in tone than 让 ràng (to let, to make) and harder than 要求 yāoqiú (to request).
模仿	mó fǎng	to imitate, to mimic
模具费	mú jù fèi	tooling fee — The one-off charge for the mold. Common arrangements: paid in full up front, split 50/50 with the balance at sample approval, or refunded (返还 fǎnhuán) once order volume passes an agreed number.
模具归属	mú jù guī shǔ	tooling ownership — The clause that states who owns the mold, that it may only be used for your orders, and that the factory must release it on request. 归 is "belongs to," 所有权 (suǒyǒuquán) ownership.
模样	mú yàng	looks; appearance
末	mò	secondary older male roles — The 末 was originally a distinct category for secondary older male characters -- supporting figures who are not the central hero (that is 老生) but are male, older, and positive or neutral in moral valence. In modern Peking Opera practice, 末 has merged almost entirely into 老生.
陌生	mò shēng	unfamiliar; strange; unknown — 陌 mò (a stranger; a path between rice fields, where you encounter people you don't know) + 生 shēng (unprocessed; unfamiliar). For unfamiliar places, strangers, and new experiences. 陌生人 = stranger. 对我来说很陌生 = "It's unfamiliar to me."
陌生人	mò shēng rén	stranger
墨	mò	ink
墨家	Mò jiā	Mohism — universal love, opposition to aggressive war — Founded by 墨子 Mòzǐ (c. 470–391 BCE). Distinguished by: 兼爱 jiān'ài (impartial concern for all, not just one's own family and state), 非攻 fēi gōng (opposition to offensive war), and a utilitarian standard of moral judgment (actions are good if they benefit the people materially).
墨经	Mò Jīng	the Mohist Canon — logic and proto-science — The 墨经 consists of four chapters within the 墨子 (经上, 经下, 经说上, 经说下) and represents the most systematic engagement with formal reasoning, optics, mechanics, and geometric principles in pre-Han Chinese thought. The Canon defines terms, categorizes knowledge claims, and argues from evidence.
墨子	Mò zǐ	Master Mo; the person and the text — The name refers both to the historical philosopher (c. 470–391 BCE) and to the text compiled by his followers. The 墨子 consists of 71 chapters (of which 53 survive), covering ethics, political philosophy, logic, and the 墨经 (the scientific and logical canons).
默读	mò dú	to read silently — 默 mò (silent) + 读. The marked term for silent reading , marked precisely because the unmarked sense of 读 is voiced. A reading classroom may instruct students 先默读，再朗读 "first read silently, then aloud."
默认	mò rèn	to tacitly acknowledge; (computing) default — 默 mò (silent) + 认 rèn. Silent acknowledgment , agreeing by not objecting, acquiescing without explicit confirmation. In computing and modern Chinese tech vocabulary, the same word means "default": 默认设置 mòrèn shèzhì = "default settings."
母	mǔ	mother
母鸡	mǔ jī	hen — 母 (female) + 鸡 (chicken). The egg-laying hen; a 母鸡下蛋 (the hen lays eggs) is the first farmyard fact every child learns.
母亲河	mǔ qīn hé	Mother River , the affectionate designation for the Yellow River, emphasizing its role as the origin of Chinese agricultural civilization. The Yangtze also claims this title in southern China, but the Yellow River carries the deeper historical weight.
母语	mǔ yǔ	mother tongue; first language — 母 mǔ (mother) + 语 yǔ (language). Mother tongue , the language absorbed in childhood, tied to family and home. 母语为汉语的人 = native Chinese speakers. 母语习得 = first language acquisition. 目标语 mùbiāoyǔ = target language (the language being learned). 外语 wàiyǔ = foreign language.
亩	mǔ	The farmland unit, about 667 square meters — The farmland unit, about 667 square meters. 一亩三分地 is everyone's metaphorical private turf, from an emperor's ceremonial plot.
木耳	mù ěr	wood ear fungus; cloud ear mushroom — 木 (wood, tree) + 耳 (ear-handle). The edible black fungus Auricularia auricula-judae , named for its resemblance to a wooden vessel's ear-handle. A staple of Chinese cooking, added to stir-fries and soups for its chewy texture.
木卡姆	Mù kǎ mǔ	The Uyghur Muqam , twelve suites of music incorporating song, instrumental performance, and dance, each built on a distinct modal system. A living art form and UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage, the Muqam is considered the highest expression of Uyghur musical culture.
木马	mù mǎ	wooden horse; vaulting horse; trojan — 木 (wood) + 马 (horse). A horse made of wood: the rocking toy, the gymnastics vaulting horse, and the computer 木马 (a trojan), all named for the most famous wooden horse of all, the one at Troy (特洛伊木马).
木头	mù tou	wood; timber; (of a person) wooden, dull — 木 mù (wood, tree) + 头 tou (suffix). Wood as a material. Used figuratively of a person who is slow or unresponsive: 木头人 "a blockhead," literally a wooden person.
目标	mù biāo	goal; target
目标价	mù biāo jià	target price — Naming your target moves the discussion from "lower" to "can you reach this." Give it with a reason, such as a retail price point or a competing quote (另一家的报价 lìng yì jiā de bàojià), without naming the other factory.
目光	mù guāng	gaze; line of sight; vision (figurative) — 目 (eye) + 光 (light). The light from the eye , the gaze as something projected. Used both literally (眼中的目光, the look in someone's eyes) and figuratively (目光短浅 mùguāng duǎnqiǎn, short-sighted vision; 目光长远 mùguāng chángyuǎn, far-sighted).
目连	Mù lián	Mulian (Maudgalyāyana), the filial monk
目录	mù lù	table of contents; catalogue; directory — 目 (eye; item) + 录 (record, to record). The document your eye scans to locate content. 书的目录 (book's table of contents); 图书目录 (library catalogue); 产品目录 (product catalogue).
目前	mù qián	at present; currently
墓地	mù dì	cemetery; grave site
穆斯林	mù sī lín	Muslim (transliteration)
拿	ná	to hold; to take
拿出来	ná chū lái	to take out; to produce; to bring out — 拿 ná (take; hold; grasp) + 出来 chūlái (bring out). To produce something from a container, pocket, bag, or interior , making it visible to the speaker. Also used abstractly: 拿出证据 (produce evidence), 拿出方案 (put forward a plan). The 出来 marks the transition from concealed to revealed.
哪	nǎ	which
哪儿	nǎ r	where (fronted + 都 = everywhere)
哪儿都	nǎ r dōu	everywhere; anywhere — 哪儿 (where, which place) fronted before 都 covers all locations. 哪里都 is the less colloquial variant; 哪儿都 is the Beijing/northern colloquial form. The construction makes a place-universal claim: no location is excluded.
哪里	nǎ lǐ	where; (modest deflection) — 哪 (nǎ, which) + 里 = 哪里 (which interior zone, where). Beyond literal location questions, 哪里 doubles as a stock modest deflection: when someone praises you, responding 哪里哪里 (nǎlǐ nǎlǐ, literally "where, where?") means "oh, not at all, you're too kind." The deflection works because 哪里 implies the prais…
哪里哪里	nǎ lǐ nǎ lǐ	"where, where?" — the classic deflection of a compliment; "you flatter me" — The quintessential 谦虚 response to a compliment , a rhetorical question that denies the existence of the praised quality. Literally "where is this quality you're seeing?" The doubling intensifies the deflection.
哪怕	nǎ pà	even if; no matter how (spoken)
那个	nà ge	that one — "Uh…" or "that…" The single most common verbal filler in Mandarin, used to stall for half a second while the next word arrives. Stretched out as 那个… 那个… it functions almost identically to English "um." Overusing it sounds hesitant; sprinkling it in sounds native.
那就这样吧	nà jiù zhè yàng ba	"Okay, let's leave it at that — "Okay, let's leave it at that." The standard wrap-up marker. Signals that the conversation has reached a natural stopping point and you are about to close. The 吧 invites the other party to agree to wind down.
那里	nà lǐ	there
那时候	nà shí hou	at that time
那样	nà yàng	that way
乃至	nǎi zhì	and even; to the extent of
奶牛	nǎi niú	dairy cow
耐心	nài xīn	patience
男	nán	man; male
男孩	nán hái	boy — 男 (male) plus 孩 (child). Often expanded to 男孩子 (nánháizi) with no change in meaning. Used for boys and, loosely, for young men.
男朋友	nán péng you	boyfriend: a male romantic partner — 男 nán (male) + 朋友 péngyou (friend). The standard word for a boyfriend; 女朋友 nǚ péngyou is the matching girlfriend. The 朋友 base shows how the modern romantic term grew out of the plain word for companion.
男性	nán xìng	male; men (as a category) — 男 nán (man) + 性 xìng (sex). The male sex, men considered as a category: 成年男性 "adult males," 男性朋友 "a male friend." The formal counterpart to 女性, used wherever one speaks of men as a group rather than addressing a particular man.
男子汉	nán zǐ hàn	a real man; a manly man — 男子 nánzǐ (male, man) + 汉 hàn (fellow). A man in the sense of courage and backbone, the one who keeps his word and bears hardship. 男子汉大丈夫 "a true man and great fellow" is the full heroic flourish.
南北	nán běi	north-south
南方	nán fāng	the South; southern China — 南 (south) + 方 (direction; region). In Chinese cultural geography, 南方 (the South) and 北方 (the North) mark one of the most significant cultural divides in the country: the Yangtze River (扬子江) and the Qinling-Huai River line are the approximate cultural boundary.
南京	Nán jīng	Nanjing (city) — 南 (south) + 京 (capital). The Southern Capital , the counterpart to 北京 Běijīng (Northern Capital). Nanjing served as the Ming dynasty capital from 1368 to 1421, before Yongle moved the court north.
南人	Nán rén	Southerners — fourth class — Former Southern Song subjects, the last conquered and most numerous. Faced highest taxes, most restricted access to office, forbidden from owning weapons. Approximately 85% of the Yuan's total population.
南无阿弥陀佛	Ná mó Ē mí tuó fó	I take refuge in Amitābha Buddha
南无观音菩萨	Nā mó Guān yīn Pú sà	the invocation — "homage to Guanyin Bodhisattva" — 南无 nāmó is the Chinese rendering of Sanskrit namas (homage, refuge). The full invocation 南无观音菩萨 is spoken at times of fear, suffering, gratitude, or supplication. It requires no ritual setting, no clergy, no formal initiation.
难	nán	difficult, hard
难怪	náng uài	"No wonder!" The realization that a previously confusing situation now makes sense given new information. 难怪你那么累 ("no wonder you're so tired") after learning the other person worked all night. Compact and very natural.
难过	náng uò	sad
难题	nán tí	hard problem; knotty issue; difficult question — 难 nán (difficult) + 题 tí (problem). A problem that resists easy solution, whether on paper or in life: 出了一道难题 "posed a tough problem." A close cousin is 习题 xítí, the practice exercises a student works through.
馕	nāng	Naan bread — Naan bread: the staple flatbread of Uyghur cuisine, baked in a clay tandoor oven (馕坑 nāngkēng). Sold fresh throughout southern Xinjiang and available across China at Xinjiang restaurants. A cultural marker and the center of the daily Uyghur table.
脑	nǎo	brain
脑力	nǎo lì	mental effort
闹洞房	nào dòng fáng	The wedding-night hazing — The wedding-night hazing: guests entering the bridal chamber to subject the couple to games and pranks. The custom is ancient, widely practiced, and occasionally criticized for going too far. The word 闹 (nào) means to make noise, to create commotion.
内	nèi	inner, internal
内部	nèi bù	internal; the interior; inside (of an organization) — 内 nèi (inside) + 部 bù (section). The inner part of a body, machine, or group, often the confidential inside of an organization: 内部消息 "inside information," 内部会议 "an internal meeting." Its pair is 外部 wàibù (external).
内丹	nèi dān	inner alchemy — meditation, breathwork, and cultivation of vital energies — 内 nèi (inner) + 丹 dān (cinnabar; elixir). Inner alchemy uses the body itself as the crucible: through breathing exercises (吐纳 tǔnà), meditation (静坐 jìngzuò), and visualization, the practitioner refines the body's three treasures (精 jīng essence/semen, 气 qì vital breath, 神 shén spirit) and circulates…
内功	nèi gōng	internal martial cultivation
内家拳	nèi jiā quán	internal martial arts — 内 nèi (internal) + 家 jiā (school; tradition) + 拳 quán (fist; boxing). The internal arts (Taijiquan 太极拳, Baguazhang 八卦掌, Xingyiquan 形意拳) emphasize cultivation of internal energy (气 qì), sensitivity, and relaxed power over brute muscular force.
内容	nèi róng	content; what is contained
内心	nèi xīn	inner heart; deepest feelings; the inner self — 内 nèi (inside; inner) + 心 xīn (heart). The deepest, most private layer of a person's interior life, what they feel before they decide how to present it to the world. Common in introspective prose, therapeutic contexts, and literary characterization. 内心世界 = the inner world.
内行	nèi háng	expert; insider; one who knows the trade — 内 nèi (inside) + 行 háng. Inside-the-row. Someone who is inside the trade , knows its tools, its standards, its tells. The opposite is 外行 wàiháng "outside-the-row," an outsider or amateur. 这件事我是外行 = "I'm a layperson on this." 一看就是内行 = "you can tell at a glance he's an expert."
嫩豆腐	nèn dòu fu	Silken Tofu
能	néng	can; be able to
能工巧匠	néng gōng qiǎo jiàng	skilled craftsmen
能力	néng lì	ability; capability; capacity — 能 néng (be able) + 力 lì (strength). The general word for ability, what a person is capable of doing: 工作能力 "work ability," 有能力 "to be capable."
能量	néng liàng	Energy — Energy. Physics term and self-help term alike: 正能量 (positive energy) is the government-endorsed phrase for uplifting content, a usage physicists did not authorize.
尼姑	ní gū	Buddhist nun
你吃了吗	nǐ chī le ma	have you eaten? (intimate register)
你好	nǐ hǎo	hello (formal register)
你好吗	nǐ hǎo ma	how are you? (borrowed register)
你好意思	nǐ hǎo yì si	you have the nerve / how shameless of you
你们	nǐ men	you (plural)
你认真的吗	nǐ rèn zhēn de ma	are you serious? are you for real?
你什么意思？	nǐ shén me yì si	What do you mean? / What are you implying? — Can be a neutral request for clarification or a challenging, slightly confrontational demand , "what exactly are you getting at?" Tone of voice and context determine everything.
你说嘛	nǐ shuō ma	just say it / tell me then (urging)
你在做什么？	nǐ zài zuò shén me ?	What are you doing? — The standard colloquial question for asking what someone is currently doing. 在 alone (not 正在) is the norm in this question. 你正在做什么？is grammatical but sounds slightly unnatural or formal in casual conversation. The question naturally invites a 在/正在 answer.
你知道吗	nǐ zhī dào ma	"You know?" Functions like English "you know?" both as a sincere check-in and as discourse filler. Useful for keeping the listener engaged mid-sentence. Overused in casual conversation by younger speakers, where it can become as habitual as English "like."
年代	nián dài	decade; period
年龄	nián líng	age; years of life — 年 nián (year) + 龄 líng (age; years of life , used specifically for age of living things). The formal word for a person's age. 年龄段 niánlíng duàn = age group. 适龄 shìlíng = of appropriate age (e.g., 适龄儿童 school-age children).
年轻	nián qīng	young — 年 nián (year; age) + 轻 qīng (light; young; not heavy). The standard adjective for "young" , the opposite of 老 lǎo. 年轻人 niánqīngrén = young people. 年轻化 niánqīnghuà = to become younger/more youthful. 越活越年轻 "getting younger as I live" , a compliment to someone who looks youthful.
年夜饭	nián yè fàn	New Year's Eve reunion dinner — the most important meal of the year — 年 nián (year) + 夜 yè (night/eve) + 饭 fàn (meal). The reunion dinner on New Year's Eve, the sacred center of Spring Festival. Every family member is expected to return home for this meal, regardless of distance.
念佛	niàn fó	Buddha-recitation — the primary and most accessible practice — 念 niàn (to recite; to think of; to hold in mind) + 佛 fó (Buddha). The recitation of Amitābha's name, 南无阿弥陀佛 Nāmó Āmítuófó ("homage to Amitābha Buddha"), as a continuous practice.
念书	niàn shū	to read aloud; to study; to attend school
念头	niàn tou	a thought; an idea; an intention (that arises)
念珠	niàn zhū	recitation beads (mala)
鸟	niǎo	bird
涅槃重生	niè pán chóng shēng	to be reborn through the fire; the modern metaphor
您	nín	you (formal, respectful)
您的意思是	nín de yì si shì	what you mean is — Restating what you heard is the most powerful check you have, because it forces a yes or no on your version. 我理解的是……对吗 ("my understanding is ..., right?") does the same job. 也就是说 (yě jiù shì shuō, "in other words") works mid-conversation.
宁可	nìng kě	would rather (at a cost)
宁缺毋滥	nìng qu ē wú làn	better to lack than to have inferior
宁夏	Níng xià	Ningxia — the Hui homeland — 宁夏回族自治区 (Ningxia Hui Autonomous Region) is the only autonomous region in China named for the Hui people. With a population of about 7 million, roughly one-third of whom are Hui, Ningxia has the highest concentration of Hui in any single administrative unit.
宁愿	nìng yuàn	would willingly prefer
牛逼	niú bī	badass, awesome (vulgar)
牛角	niú jiǎo	cow horn
牛奶	niú nǎi	milk (cow's) — 牛 (ox/cow) + 奶 (nǎi, milk/breast). The default word for drinking milk in Mandarin; 奶 alone leans toward breast milk or the act of nursing, so the 牛 makes the source explicit.
牛年	niú nián	Year of the Ox
牛排	niú pái	steak — 牛 (ox) + 排 (pái, a slab/cutlet, from 排骨 spare-rib). A Western-style cut of beef cooked as one piece, ordered by doneness the way English orders rare to well-done.
牛肉	niú ròu	beef — 牛 (ox) + 肉 (ròu, meat). Chinese names meat by its animal, so beef is literally ox-meat, parallel to 猪肉 (zhūròu, pork) and 鸡肉 (jīròu, chicken).
牛仔	niú zǎi	cowboy
牛仔裤	niú zǎi kù	jeans — 牛仔 (niúzǎi, cowboy, literally ox-lad) + 裤 (kù, trousers). The denim of the American cowboy, named in Chinese after the herder himself.
扭转乾坤	niǔ zhuǎn qián kūn	to reverse the entire situation; to turn things completely around — 扭转 niǔ zhuǎn (to twist around; to reverse; to turn back from a course) + 乾坤. A phrase for overturning a decisive losing situation through dramatic intervention.
农场	nóng chǎng	farm — 农 (farming) plus 场 (ground, site). A working farm as an enterprise, larger and more organized than a single household plot.
农村	nóng cūn	countryside, rural area — 农 (farming) plus 村 (village). The rural world as a whole, the standing opposite of 城市 (chéngshì, the city) in any discussion of Chinese society.
农夫	nóng fū	farmer; a man of the land — 农 nóng (agriculture, farming) + 夫 fū (man). A man who works the land, the farmer in a plain, somewhat literary or old-fashioned key. It pictures the individual tiller rather than the peasantry as a social class, and turns up often in fables and proverbs about country life.
农历	nóng lì	the lunar calendar — 农 (farming) plus 历 (calendar). The traditional lunisolar calendar, named for farmers because its solar terms tell you when to plant and harvest. 春节 (chūnjié) falls on 农历 New Year.
农民	nóng mín	farmer, peasant — 农 (farming) plus 民 (the common people). A person whose work and household registration are agricultural. In modern China it also marks a social and administrative category, not only an occupation.
农民起义	nóng mín qǐ yì	peasant uprising
农业	nóng yè	agriculture; farming — 农 nóng (farming) + 业 yè (sector). The agricultural sector: 农业生产 "agricultural production," 农业大国 "a major agricultural country." The oldest of the great 业 in Chinese economic thought, long held as the root on which all else rests.
农作物	nóng zuò wù	crops — 农 (farming) plus 作物 (cultivated plant). The cultivated crops themselves: grain, cotton, vegetables, anything grown on purpose for harvest.
弄错	nòng cuò	to get wrong; mix up
努力	nǔ lì	to make an effort; to strive; hardworking — 努 nǔ (to put forth) + 力 lì (effort). To bend one's strength toward a goal, the everyday word for trying hard: 努力工作 "to work hard," 努力学习 "to study diligently." Works as both verb and noun.
女儿	nǚ ér	daughter — 女 nǚ (female) + 儿 ér (child; son , used neutrally here). Daughter. The contrast with 儿子 érzi (son , lit.
女孩	nǚ hái	girl — 女 (female) plus 孩 (child). Like 男孩, it freely takes the extra 子 as 女孩子. It covers girls and, in everyday speech, young women.
女朋友	nǚ péng you	girlfriend
女王	nǚ wáng	queen (regnant)
女性	nǚ xìng	female; women (as a category) — 女 nǚ (woman) + 性 xìng (sex). The female sex, women considered as a category or class: 职业女性 "professional women," 女性用品 "products for women." More formal and collective than 女人, the way one refers to women in writing, statistics, and public discourse.
暖	nuǎn	warm
哦	o	mild realization particle
偶尔	ǒu ěr	occasionally
排斥	pái chì	to repel; to reject
排队	pái duì	to line up, to queue — 排 (to arrange in a row) + 队 (a formed line). The verb every visitor learns first: to stand one behind another and wait your turn.
排练	pái liàn	to rehearse
排他	pái tā	exclusive; to exclude others
牌位	pái wèi	spirit tablet — the ancestor's dwelling in the home — The spirit tablet (牌位, also 神主牌 shénzhǔpái or 祖先牌位 zǔxiān páiwèi) is a wooden or paper plaque inscribed with the ancestor's name, birth and death dates, and honorific. It is the material dwelling of the ancestor's spirit within the home or ancestral hall.
派出所	pài chū suǒ	local police substation; community police office
盼望	pàn wàng	to look forward to; to long for eagerly — 盼 pàn (to long for, to expect) + 望 wàng (to look toward). To wait eagerly and warmly for something one cares about: 盼望已久 "long looked forward to." More emotional and yearning than 希望, it suggests an active, heartfelt waiting.
旁边	páng biān	beside; next to
旁人	páng rén	bystanders; those on the sidelines (literary) — 旁 páng (side; beside) + 人 rén. Literally "people at the side": observers, onlookers, those not directly involved in a situation. The word carries a slightly theatrical quality: you, the protagonist, and 旁人 who watch. More literary than 别人. Common in classical and modern literary prose.
跑步	pǎo bù	to jog; to run (as exercise); a run — 跑 + 步 (bù, step). The everyday verb-noun for running as exercise. Used both as an activity (我每天跑步, "I jog every day") and as a noun (一次跑步, "one running session"). The word covers everything from a casual neighborhood jog to marathon training.
跑出来	pǎo chū lái	to run out (toward speaker) — 跑 pǎo (run) + 出来 chūlái (come out). The full directional compound: running out of an enclosed space toward the speaker's position. The 来 anchors orientation , the speaker is outside, watching the emergence happen.
跑道	pǎo dào	running track; runway (airport); a course of action — 跑 + 道 (dào, road; way; track). The physical track for running, the airport runway for planes, and , figuratively , a chosen course of professional or personal direction. 选择新的跑道 ("to choose a new track") is a near-fixed metaphor in career writing.
跑了	pǎo le	got away; ran off; (of fish, opportunity) escaped — 跑 + 了 (le, perfective particle). A small but very common construction marking that something or someone has escaped or slipped away. The subject is often something one wanted to keep: a thief, a debtor, a fish, a customer, an opportunity.
跑腿	pǎo tuǐ	to run errands
泡茶	pào chá	to brew tea; to steep tea — 泡 pào (to soak; to steep; to foam) + 茶 chá (tea). The everyday verb for making tea. 你喝什么茶？我去泡 "What tea would you like? I'll go steep it." Also: 泡茶时间 steeping time · 第一泡 the first infusion.
