设备	shè bèi	equipment, machinery — 机器 (jīqì) is a single machine. Ask whether equipment is owned and how old it is; 进口设备 (imported equipment) is a point of pride.
设计	shè jì	To design; a design — To design; a design. 设计师 (shèjìshī) covers designers from fashion to industrial. In engineering documents, 设计院 (design institute) is the state-descended firm that produces the drawings.
社会保障	shè huì bǎo zhàng	social security; social welfare system — 社会 + 保障 (bǎozhàng, guarantee; protection; safeguard). The welfare system: pensions, unemployment insurance, medical insurance, and other state-provided protections against life's major risks. The compound mirrors the English "social security" almost exactly in logic.
社会地位	shè huì dì wèi	social status; social standing; position in society — 社会 + 地位 (dìwèi, position; standing; status). One's rank or standing within the social order. Closely related to 面子 , social status is the structural foundation from which face is generated and maintained. Hierarchically sensitive: 地位 implies a vertical ordering.
社会风气	shè huì fēng qì	social atmosphere; moral climate; the prevailing spirit of society — 社会 + 风气 (fēngqì, prevailing atmosphere; social mores; lit. "wind and air"). The ambient moral climate of a society at a given moment: whether honesty, integrity, and mutual care are in the air, or whether cynicism, materialism, and corruption have taken hold.
社会关系	shè huì guān xi	social relationships; social network; connections — 社会 + 关系 (guānxi, relationship; connection). One's web of social connections. In Chinese social contexts, 社会关系 encompasses what is often simply called 关系 , the practical network of relationships that enables things to get done.
社会稳定	shè huì wěn dìng	social stability — 社会 + 稳定 (wěndìng, stable; stability). A central governing priority. 维护社会稳定 (to maintain social stability) appears throughout official documents, legal texts, and policy announcements.
社会信用体系	shè huì xìn yòng tǐ xì	social credit system — 社会 (society) + 信用 (xìnyòng, credit; trustworthiness) + 体系 (tǐxì, system; framework). A multifaceted platform for tracking trustworthiness and compliance across financial, legal, and behavioral domains.
社会学	shè huì xu é	sociology — 社会 + 学 (xué, the study of). The academic discipline. The word makes the Meiji translation story visible: 社会学 was coined as the direct Chinese equivalent of sociology (German Soziologie, French sociologie) through the same process that produced 社会. The discipline and its name arrived together.
社会责任	shè huì zér èn	social responsibility — 社会 + 责任 (zérèn, responsibility; obligation; duty). The obligation one has to the broader social world. Used both in the CSR (Corporate Social Responsibility) sense , 企业社会责任 (qǐyè shèhuì zérèn) , and in the broader sense of individual duty to society.
社会主义	shè huì zhǔ yì	socialism — 社会 + 主义 (zhǔyì, -ism; doctrine; principle). The dominant political compound built on 社会 in the PRC context. 中国特色社会主义 (Zhōngguó tèsè shèhuì zhǔyì, Socialism with Chinese Characteristics) is the formal name for the governing ideology.
社稷	shè jì	the altars of soil and grain; the state, the realm — 社 shè (the soil-god) + 稷 jì (the grain-god). Originally the two altars a ruler maintained, to the powers of the earth and of the harvest. Because land and food are the foundation of any polity, the word came to stand for the state itself: 江山社稷 "the rivers, mountains, and altars," the whole realm.
社交	shè jiāo	social interaction; socializing — 社 shè (social) + 交 jiāo (to associate, to exchange). The act of mixing with others, the give-and-take of social life: 社交活动 "social activities," 社交能力 "social skills," 社交媒体 "social media." It names the personal practice of meeting and getting along with people.
社区	shè qū	community; residential neighbourhood — 社 shè (community) + 区 qū (district, zone). A bounded local community: the people of a single neighbourhood, plus the services and administration that serve them. 社区服务 "community services," 社区医院 "a community clinic," 社区活动 "neighbourhood activities." Smaller and more concrete than 社会.
社团	shè tuán	club; society; an organized group — 社 shè (association) + 团 tuán (a group, a body). A voluntary organized group bound by a shared interest: 学生社团 "student clubs," 民间社团 "civic associations." The everyday word for the clubs and societies people choose to join, especially at school and university.
谁	shéi	who (fronted + 都 = everyone)
谁都	shéi dōu	everyone; anyone (no person excluded) — 谁 (who) fronted before 都 universalizes over all persons. 谁都知道 = "everyone knows," with the implication that no person could be ignorant of this fact. Often carries a mild rhetorical force: the speaker is emphasizing how obvious or widespread something is.
身材	shēn cái	figure, build, physique — 身 (body) + 材 (timber, material): the body as raw material. 身材好 is a compliment on someone's figure; 保持身材 is keeping in shape. Its sibling 身高 (shēngāo) is height, the statistic every basketball conversation begins with.
身份	shēn fèn	identity; status — 身 (self) + 份 (share, allotment): one's allotted position. 身份证 (shēnfènzhèng) is the national ID card, the document requested at every train station, hotel, and SIM-card counter, which makes this compound survival vocabulary rather than abstraction.
深衣	shēn yī	deep robe — the scholar's garment
深远	shēn yuǎn	profound and far-reaching (of influence or impact) — 深 shēn (deep) + 远 yuǎn (far). The compound stacks vertical depth with horizontal reach. Reserved for influence that is both substantial and long-lasting: 深远影响 shēnyuǎn yǐngxiǎng (profound, far-reaching influence) is a fixed collocation that appears in historical analysis and political commentary.
深圳	Shēn zhèn	Shenzhen — the first SEZ
什么	shén me	what (fronted + 都 = everything)
什么东西	shén me dōng xi	what (thing); what on earth; exclamation of disgust — 什么东西 has two registers. As a genuine question, it means "what is this thing?" As an exclamation, it expresses shock, disgust, or contempt , "what the heck is this?" or simply "ugh!" The exclamatory reading is a complete utterance on its own: 什么东西！registers revulsion at something offensive, absurd, o…
什么都	shén me dōu	everything; anything (without exception) — 什么 fronted before 都 universalizes over all things or all options. 什么都行 literally "anything goes" , no restriction exists. Extremely common in everyday speech for expressing total flexibility or total acceptance.
什么时候	shén me shí hou	when? (question word) — The question word "when," literally "what time/moment." It sits where the time answer would go, not at the front as in English: 你什么时候走？("When are you leaving?"). One of the core question patterns alongside 哪儿 (where), 谁 (who), 怎么 (how).
什么意思	shén me yì si	what does it mean — Ask about a word, not a person: "这个词是什么意思" is neutral, while a bare 你什么意思？ means "what are you implying?" and sounds hostile. Keep 这个词 in front.
神诞	shén dàn	deity's birthday
神服	shén fú	the spirit costume — the shaman's armor and identity — The spirit costume (神服 shén fú or 萨满服 sàmǎnfú) is a complex ritual garment encoding the shaman's entire cosmological universe.
神鼓	shén gǔ	the shaman's drum — vehicle of the spirit-journey — The shaman's drum (神鼓 shén gǔ or 萨满鼓 sàmǎn gǔ) is the most essential ritual implement , sometimes called the shaman's horse (萨满的马), for it is the vehicle on which the shaman rides to the spirit world.
神话	shén huà	mythology; myth (lit. "god-talk") — 神 (gods; divine) + 话 huà (speech; story; words). The modern compound for mythology emerged in the late 19th century as a translation equivalent for the Western academic concept.
神轿	shén jiào	deity's palanquin (carried in procession)
神经	shén jīng	nerve; nervous system
神灵	shén líng	spirits; supernatural beings (diffuse, including ghosts and nature spirits) — 神 (deity; numinous) + 灵 (spirit; soul; efficacious). 神灵 is broader than 神明: it covers any spirit with numinous presence, including ghosts (鬼 guǐ), nature spirits, and ancestral spirits that do not quite reach the status of recognized deities. A 神明 is always a 神灵; a 神灵 is not necessarily a 神明.
神秘	shén mì	mysterious; mystical
神明之容	shén míng zhī róng	the bearing of divine clarity; the sage's luminous presence (literary) — A Zhuangzi phrase describing the outward appearance of someone whose inner cultivation has reached a state of unconditioned clarity. 容 róng (bearing; countenance; appearance) suggests that 神明 is visible , it shows in how a person carries themselves.
神明自得	shén míng zì dé	divine clarity comes of itself; luminous intelligence arises spontaneously from cultivation — From Xunzi's 劝学 (Encouraging Learning): "积善成德，而神明自得，圣心备焉" , accumulate virtue until integrity forms, and divine clarity will arise of itself; the sage-mind will be complete.
神奇	shén qí	miraculous; magical; extraordinary (modern secular usage) — 神 (numinous; wondrous) + 奇 qí (rare; strange; remarkable). In classical usage, 神奇 described genuine divine wonder. In modern Chinese it has drifted secular: it commonly means simply "remarkable" or "amazing," with no religious connotation.
神色	shén sè	look; demeanor; expression (literary) — 神 shén (spirit; presence; expression) + 色 sè (appearance). More elevated than 脸色 , captures the whole impression of a person's bearing and expression. 神色慌张 shénsè huāngzhāng "flustered expression" · 神色自若 "composed and calm."
神圣	shén shèng	sacred; holy; inviolable (adjective) — 神 (divine; numinous) + 圣 shèng (sage; sagely; sacred). Where 神明 names a being or a quality of intelligence, 神圣 names the quality of sacredness itself , the inviolability of something set apart.
神态	shén tài	expression; demeanor; bearing
神仙	shén xiān	immortals; transcendents (Daoist; deities who achieved divinity through cultivation) — 神 (divine; deity) + 仙 xiān (immortal; one who has transcended ordinary existence, often depicted living in mountains). 神仙 in the Daoist tradition are achieved immortals: human beings who through alchemical or meditative practice transformed themselves into divine beings. They were not born divine.
神志	shén zhì	consciousness; mental clarity
审美	shěn měi	aesthetics; aesthetic judgment — 审 shěn (to examine; to judge) + 美 měi (beauty). The act of aesthetic discernment , the capacity to evaluate beauty. 审美观 "aesthetic outlook/values" · 审美趣味 "aesthetic taste." Common in design, art, and cultural criticism.
甚至	shèn zhì	even; to the point that
甚至于	shèn zhì yú	going so far as to (emphatic formal)
升	shēng	to rise; to ascend
生病	shēng bìng	to fall ill, get sick — 生 (shēng) means to give rise to or produce, so 生病 is literally "to produce illness," the standard verb for getting sick. It is a separable verb: an object or aspect marker can split the two halves.
生产关系	shēng chǎn guān xi	relations of production — 生产 (production) + 关系 (relations, relationships). The Marxist term for the social and economic relationships people enter into in the process of production , ownership of means of production, the relationship between capital and labor, the organization of work.
生产力	shēng chǎn lì	productive forces; productivity — 生产 (production) + 力 (force, power). In Marxist theory: the combination of labor power, tools, technology, and organization that determines how much and how efficiently a society can produce. In everyday business usage: productivity in the simpler sense , output per unit of input.
生产线	shēng chǎn xiàn	production line; assembly line — 生产 (production) + 线 (line). The physical or process sequence through which materials move to become finished products. 全自动生产线 (fully automated production line), 流水线 (liúshuǐ xiàn, flowing-water line , assembly line).
生产者	shēng chǎn zhě	producer
生抽	shēng chōu	light soy sauce
生词	shēng cí	new word; unfamiliar vocabulary item — 生 shēng (raw; unfamiliar) + 词 cí (word). A word you have not yet learned, the staple of every textbook vocabulary list. Its opposite is 熟词 shúcí, a word already mastered.
生存	shēng cún	to survive; to subsist — 生 shēng + 存 cún (to exist; to preserve). Survival , continuing to exist under difficult conditions. 生存竞争 "survival competition" · 生存危机 "survival crisis." A more pressured, primal register than 生活.
生旦净末丑	shēng dàn jìng mò chǒu	opera role types
生活	shēng huó	life; to live; daily living — 生 shēng (to be born, to grow) + 活 huó (to live). The pairing of two life-words covers both the verb (to live, to make a life) and the noun (life as it is lived day to day). This is the everyday word for "life," distinct from 命 mìng, the life that can be lost.
生活智慧	shēng huó zhì huì	life wisdom; the wisdom of living well — 生活 shēnghuó (life; daily living) + 智慧. The wisdom that shows in how a person navigates ordinary life: relationships, hardship, practical decisions, aging, loss. 生活智慧 is the most common everyday compound with 智慧 , found in book titles, popular philosophy, grandparents' advice.
生机	shēng jī	vitality, life-force
生命	shēng mìng	life (the abstract concept; the fact of being alive) — 生 shēng (to be born; to live) plus 命. The standard modern word for life in the broad sense: biological life, the human life-course, life as a value worth protecting. Distinct from 生活 shēnghuó (daily life, way of living): 生命 is the precious thing you have once, 生活 is what you do with it.
生气	shēng qì	to be angry; to get mad (lit. "produce qi") — 生 shēng (to produce; to arise) + 气 qì. The most common everyday word for anger, what you reach for when someone cuts in line, when plans fall through, when people are unreasonable. Colloquial, direct, very high frequency.
生日	shēng rì	birthday — the day of birth — 生 shēng (birth; life) + 日 rì (day). The universal word for birthday. 生日快乐！Shēngrì kuàilè! = "Happy birthday!" (lit. "birth-day joyful"). 生日蛋糕 = birthday cake. 过生日 guò shēngrì = "to celebrate a birthday" (lit. "to pass through the birthday").
生疏	shēng shū	rusty, out of practice
生死轮回	shēng sǐ lún huí	the birth-and-death cycle; the wheel of life and death — 生死 shēngsǐ (birth and death; life and death) + 轮回. Emphasizes the specific axis of birth and death as the visible face of the cycle: the moments of entry and exit that mark each turn of the wheel.
生物	shēng wù	living thing; organism; biology — 生 shēng (to live) + 物 wù (thing). A living organism, and also the name of the school subject, biology. 微生物 wēishēngwù is a microorganism, a "tiny living thing."
生肖	shēng xiào	zodiac animals; birth sign
生意	shēng yì	doing business
生于	shēng yú	to be born in; to be born at — 生 shēng (to be born) + 于 yú (at, in). The set formal frame for stating birthplace or birth date, standard in biographies and records: 生于1980年 "born in 1980," 生于江南 "born south of the river."
生长	shēng zhǎng	to grow (of plants/children)
声调	shēng diào	tone (in a tonal language) — 声 shēng (sound, voice) + 调 diào (pitch pattern). The linguistic tone: the rise and fall of pitch that distinguishes meaning in Mandarin. 第一声 (first tone), 四个声调 (the four tones). Every syllable on this very page carries a 声调.
声明	shēng míng	to declare; to state publicly; a statement — 声 shēng (to sound out) + 明 míng (to make clear). To state something openly and on the record, or the formal statement itself: 发表声明 "to issue a statement."
声母	shēng mǔ	initial consonant (of a syllable) — 声 shēng (sound) + 母 mǔ (mother; base). The consonant that opens a syllable, such as the m in mā. Paired with the 韵母 yùnmǔ, the final that carries the vowel and tone.
声望	shēng wàng	reputation; public esteem; prestige — 声 shēng (voice, repute) + 望 wàng (esteem). The general public regard a person or institution enjoys: 声望很高 "to be held in high esteem." Close to 名望, it leans toward the public, reputational dimension, what people say and think of someone.
声誉	shēng yù	prestige; reputation; standing — 声 shēng (name; repute) + 誉 yù (praise; honor). A more formal, weightier word than 名声, leaning toward esteem and public standing: 国际声誉 "international prestige."
圣经	Shèng jīng	the Bible; holy scripture — 圣 shèng (holy; sage) + 经 jīng (scripture). Literally "the holy classic," the standard Chinese name for the Bible. The same pattern names other scriptures: 古兰经 Gǔlánjīng (the Quran).
圣人	shèng rén	sage; fully realized moral exemplar
圣战	shèng zhàn	jihad (lit. "holy war")
盛世	shèng shì	a flourishing age; a golden age; a prosperous era — 盛 shèng (flourishing; abundant) + 世 shì (age). An era of peace and prosperity, the high point of a dynasty or a civilization: 太平盛世 "an age of peace and plenty," 康乾盛世 "the High Qing flourishing." The bright opposite of 乱世, an age of chaos.
失败	shī bài	to fail; failure
失败乃成功之母	shī bài nǎi chéng gōng zhī mǔ	failure is the mother of success
失去	shī qù	to lose; to be deprived of — 失 shī (to lose; to miss; to fail) + 去 qù (to go away). Something that was yours has gone away , loss. 失去 covers losing people (失去亲人 , lose a loved one), opportunities (失去机会), abilities (失去记忆 , lose one's memory), and qualities (失去耐心 , lose patience). A highly emotive verb.
失望	shī wàng	to be disappointed; to lose hope — The natural counterpart: 失 (to lose) plus 望 (hope), to have one's hope let down. 我对他很失望 ("I'm very disappointed in him"). Stronger still is 绝望 juéwàng ("to despair," hope cut off entirely). Knowing this pair sharpens the warmth of 希望 itself: to hope is to risk 失望.
失业	shī yè	to be unemployed
师父	shī fu	master; teacher of a craft — 师 shī (teacher) + 父 fù (father). A master who trains an apprentice in a trade or martial art, addressed with the warmth and deference owed a father. The near-homophone 师傅 shīfu is the everyday term for a skilled worker or driver; 师父 keeps the closer master-disciple bond.
师傅	shī fu	Master , the reliable general-purpose address for service workers and skilled tradespeople. Also the formal term for a craft or trade teacher. The safest default when addressing an unfamiliar adult male in a service or public context.
诗	shī	poem; regulated verse
诗经	shī jīng	Book of Songs
诗经 · 书经	Shī jīng Shū jīng	Classic of Poetry · Classic of Documents — Two of the Five Classics. 诗经 Shījīng (Classic of Poetry, c. 11th–6th century BCE) , 305 poems, folk songs and court hymns among them; Confucius allegedly edited the collection and praised it as the foundation of literary education.
诗句	shī jù	a line of verse; lines of poetry — 诗 shī (poem) + 句 jù (line). A line or verse of poetry, the unit a poem is built and quoted in: 优美的诗句 "beautiful lines of verse." Often used in the plural sense, the lines of a poem taken together.
诗人	shī rén	poet
诗圣	shī shèng	Sage Poet — Sage Poet: honorific for Du Fu, whose verse embodied moral gravity and historical witness.
诗仙	shī xiān	Immortal Poet — Immortal Poet: honorific for Li Bai, whose verse seemed to transcend ordinary human craft.
施工	shī gōng	To carry out construction — To carry out construction: "execute the work." The word on every fence and detour sign. 施工中 means "under construction," on streets and, borrowed, on websites.
施事	shī shì	agent (linguistics)
十二	shí èr	twelve — 十 shí (ten) + 二 èr (two). The separator apostrophe in pinyin marks the syllable boundary: shí·èr, not shí·ér. Twelve is culturally loaded: 十二生肖 shí'èr shēngxiào (the twelve zodiac animals) and 十二个月 shí'èr gè yuè (twelve months) both run on this number.
十二木卡姆	Shí èr Mù kǎ mǔ	the Twelve Muqam — Uyghur classical music tradition — The Twelve Muqam (木卡姆 mùkǎmǔ, from Arabic maqam, meaning mode/scale) is the comprehensive musical tradition of the Uyghur people: twelve suites, each a multi-movement composition comprising song, instrumental interlude, and dance, totaling hundreds of pieces.
十二生肖	shí èr shēng xiào	The twelve zodiac animals
十分	shí fēn	very; extremely; completely (lit. "ten parts") — 十 shí (ten; complete) + 分 fēn (part; portion; to divide). Ten out of ten parts: the full measure of something. The most natural intensifier in formal and written Mandarin, sitting between 很 hěn (colloquial "very") and 非常 fēicháng (extremely, emphatic).
十干	shí gān	the Ten Heavenly Stems — 十 shí (ten) + 干 gān (stem; heavenly stem). The cycle of ten: 甲 jiǎ, 乙 yǐ, 丙 bǐng, 丁 dīng, 戊 wù, 己 jǐ, 庚 gēng, 辛 xīn, 壬 rén, 癸 guǐ.
十年	shín ián	a decade; ten years — 十 shí + 年 nián (year). A decade. In Chinese cultural memory, ten years functions as the basic unit of historical reckoning: the Cultural Revolution lasted "ten years" (1966-1976, the 十年浩劫 shínián hàojié , ten-year catastrophe); a generation is roughly one decade; the proverb 十年树木，百年树人 places educati…
十有八九	shí yǒu bā jiǔ	eight or nine times out of ten; most probably — 十 (ten) + 有 (have) + 八九 (eight or nine). Out of every ten, eight or nine, hence almost always, in all likelihood. A common way to state strong probability: 这件事十有八九是真的, "this is most probably true." The form 十之八九 (eight or nine out of ten) is equivalent.
十月	shí yu è	October; the tenth month — 十 shí + 月 yuè (moon; month). The standard Mandarin name for October and for the tenth month of any lunar calendar. Chinese month names are purely numerical: 一月 through 十二月, January through December. The naming system makes 十月 the last single-digit-ten month before 十一月 (November) and 十二月 (December).
十字	shí zì	cross shape
十字路口	shí zì lù kǒu	crossroads; intersection (lit. "ten-character road opening") — 十字 shízì (cross shape) + 路 lù (road) + 口 kǒu (opening; mouth). The point where two roads cross to form the shape of 十: a four-way intersection. Widely used both literally (traffic intersections) and metaphorically (a crossroads in life, a moment of decision between multiple paths).
石头	shí tou	stone; rock — 石 shí (stone) + 头 tou (suffix). The everyday word for a stone or rock. The bare 石 survives mostly in compounds and set terms; 石头 is the word you say out loud.
时辰	shí chen	two-hour period (traditional)
时代精神	shí dài jīng shén	the spirit of the age; the zeitgeist — The defining outlook and values of an era, the calque that matches the German "Zeitgeist." 时代 (the age) plus 精神 (spirit). Used in cultural and political commentary to name what an age believes about itself: 把握时代精神 ("grasp the spirit of the times").
时光	shí guāng	time (poetic)
时机	shí jī	the right moment; timing; an opportune occasion — 时 shí (time) + 机 jī (machine; pivot; crucial moment; opportunity). 时机 is the Confucian classical meaning of 时 made into a compound: the moment that is ripe, when conditions align. It is strategic time, the narrowing window before it closes.
时间词 + 光杆动词	shí jiān cí + ve r b	the plain future: tomorrow I go — A future time word with a bare verb is complete and idiomatic: 我明天去上海 (I'm going to Shanghai tomorrow), 下个月放假 (vacation starts next month). No marker is required at all.
时间焦点	shí jiān jiāo diǎn	time focus — "it was [then] that…" — When the focused element is a time expression, it appears directly after 是. This answers the implicit question "when?" about a past event both parties know occurred. The event itself is not in question , only its timing is being asserted.
时期	shí qī	period; phase
时中	shí zhōng	timely centrality; acting appropriately for the moment
识	shí	to recognize; knowledge
识见	shí jiàn	insight; discernment; perceptive judgment
实话	shí huà	the truth; honest words — 实 shí (true) + 话 huà (words; speech). Words that match the facts, plainly told: 说实话 "to tell the truth," also a set phrase "to be honest." Its opposite is 假话 jiǎhuà (a lie).
实话实说	shí huà shí shuō	to speak frankly — Inviting candour removes the face cost of a no. Say early that you prefer bad news now to surprises later; engineers in particular will take you up on it.
实际	shí jì	actual; real; practical; reality — 实 shí (real) + 际 jì (juncture; situation). The actual state of things as opposed to the theoretical: 实际情况 "the actual situation," 实际上 "actually, in fact." Of a person, 很实际 means practical and realistic.
实际上	shí jì shàng	in actual fact; in reality
实践	shí jiàn	practice; to put into practice — 实 shí (actual) + 践 jiàn (to tread; to carry out). The doing that turns an idea into action and tests it against reality: 理论与实践 "theory and practice," 实践证明 "practice has proven."
实力	shí lì	real strength; ability
实习	shí xí	internship; to do practical training
实现	shí xiàn	to realize; to bring about; to achieve — 实 shí (real) + 现 xiàn (to appear; to manifest). To make something real, to turn a plan, goal, or dream into an accomplished fact: 实现目标 "to achieve a goal," 实现梦想 "to make a dream come true."
实行	shí xíng	to put into practice; to implement (a policy, a system) — 实 shí (real; actual) + 行 xíng. To act on for real. Distinguished from 进行 (to be in the process of) by emphasis on real-world implementation: 实行新政策 "implement a new policy"; 实行改革 "carry out reforms."
实验	shí yàn	Experiment — Experiment: to test against reality. 做实验 is to run an experiment; 实验室 is the laboratory; 实验中学 is the "experimental high school" found in nearly every Chinese city.
实用	shí yòng	practical; utilitarian; pragmatic
实在	shí zài	really; honestly; (of a person) down-to-earth — 实 shí (real) + 在 zài (to be present). As an adverb it intensifies: 实在太累了 "I'm really exhausted." As an adjective it describes someone solid and unpretentious: 这个人很实在 "this person is genuine and reliable."
食物	shí wù	food — 食 shí (to eat; food) + 物 wù (thing). Food as a category of substance, the general written term: 健康食物 "healthy food." For "a meal" you would say 饭 fàn, and for cooked dishes 菜 cài.
史	shǐ	history; the scribe; chronicle
史记	Shǐ jì	Records of the Grand Historian (Sima Qian, c. 94 BCE) — 史 (historical record) + 记 (to record; to note). The foundational work of Chinese historiography, written by Sima Qian (司马迁, c. 145–86 BCE) over decades and completed after his castration by order of Emperor Wu of Han.
史料	shǐ liào	historical materials; primary sources — 史 (historical record) + 料 (materials; raw material , the same 料 as in 材料 cáiliào, material/ingredient). 史料 refers to primary source materials: documents, inscriptions, court records, archaeological finds , the raw evidence from which historical accounts are constructed.
史书	shǐ shū	historical texts; history books (classical register) — 史 (historical record) + 书 (book; written text). The classical compound for historical writings, used in literary and academic contexts. 史书记载 (shǐshū jìzǎi, "according to historical records") is a standard phrase for citing the written tradition.
使	shǐ	cause to (formal causative)
使坏	shǐ huài	to play a dirty trick — 使 (to make, to set going) + 坏 (bad). To deliberately make trouble for someone behind their back, to pull a malicious prank or sabotage on the sly.
使命	shǐ mìng	mission; calling; sense of purpose — 使 shǐ (to send; to cause; an envoy) + 命 mìng (mandate). The mandate one is sent to fulfill, a mission or calling. 使命感 shǐmìnggǎn = sense of mission (the felt conviction that one has a purpose to fulfill).
使用	shǐ yòng	to use; to employ; to make use of — 使 shǐ (to cause; to employ; to dispatch) + 用 yòng (to use). The formal, neutral verb for using a tool, a resource, a method, or a right. Appropriate in written and professional contexts. More deliberate in tone than plain 用: where 用 is the bare act, 使用 implies purposeful, authorized deployment.
始	shǐ	to begin (classical)
始于足下	shǐ yú zú xià	begins beneath the feet
世代	shì dài	generations; for generations; from age to age — 世 shì (generation) + 代 dài (generation, era). The succession of generations taken together: 世代相传 "handed down through the generations," 世代务农 "farming for generations." Often adverbial, meaning across the whole line of descent.
世故	shì gù	worldly-wise
世纪	shì jì	century — 世 shì (age) + 纪 jì (a record; a span of years). A hundred-year stretch of time: 二十一世纪 "the twenty-first century," 半个世纪 "half a century," 跨世纪 "spanning the turn of the century." The standard unit for dividing modern history.
世家	shì jiā	Hereditary Houses — the great families — Thirty chapters on the feudal lords and hereditary houses of the Zhou and after. Notably, Sima Qian placed 孔子 Confucius among the 世家, treating an uncrowned teacher as the equal of princes because of his lasting influence, an early statement that cultural authority can outweigh political rank.
世间	shì jiān	the world of sentient beings; this life; among the world — More literary than 世界, similar in range to 人间, but cooler and more doctrinally precise in Buddhist usage. Appears in classical compounds: 出世间 (transcending this world), 世间法 (the dharmas of this world, as opposed to transcendent dharma), 世间万象 (the ten thousand phenomena of this world).
世界	shì jiè	world; the world — 世 shì (the living age) + 界 jiè (boundary; realm). The world as a whole, both the physical globe and the human sphere within it: 全世界 "the whole world," 世界和平 "world peace," 世界观 "worldview." One of the most common nouns in the language.
世界第一	shì jiè dì yī	first in the world; the world's best — 世界 shìjiè (world) + 第一 dì-yī (first). The top of a global ranking, by extension the finest there is. Used of athletes, products, and achievements that lead the field.
世界观	shì jiè guān	worldview
世俗	shì sú	secular; worldly; common; mundane — 世 shì (the human world) + 俗 sú (custom; common). What belongs to ordinary worldly life rather than the religious or ideal: 世俗社会 "secular society," 世俗的眼光 "a worldly point of view." Can describe the secular as a neutral domain or carry a mild note of the common and unrefined.
世袭	shì xí	hereditary; to inherit by succession — 世 shì (generation) + 袭 xí (to inherit; to succeed to). Passed down by birth from one generation to the next: 世袭制度 "the hereditary system," 世袭爵位 "a hereditary title." Marks a right or rank inherited through bloodline rather than earned or appointed.
市	shì	market; city
市场	shì chǎng	Market, in both senses — Market, in both senses: the physical 菜市场 (vegetable market) and the abstract 市场经济 (market economy). 市场部 is the marketing department.
市场经济	shì chǎng jīng jì	market economy — what China was moving toward — 市场 (market, literally marketplace) + 经济 (economy). The conceptual opposite of 计划经济 jìhuà jīngjì (planned economy, the command economy the PRC had used since 1949).
市长	shì zhǎng	mayor
示	shì	to show; to indicate; to reveal
事故	shì gù	accident; incident; mishap — 事 (shì, matter, affair, event) + 故 (cause, the reason behind it). An accident or untoward incident: literally an event with a cause, usually an unwanted one. 交通事故 (jiāotōng shìgù) is a traffic accident; 安全事故 (ānquán shìgù) is a safety incident.
事后诸葛亮	shì hòu Zhū gě liàng	a Zhuge Liang after the fact; hindsight wisdom
事件	shì jiàn	incident; event
事情	shì qing	matter; affair; thing (to be dealt with) — 事 shì + 情 qíng (situation; circumstance). The everyday noun for a matter or affair, anything happening that you talk about or handle. The most common way to say "thing" when you mean a situation rather than an object.
事上磨炼	shì shàng mó liàn	tempering oneself through practical affairs — Wang Yangming's insistence that moral cultivation is not a retreat into contemplation but a process that occurs within engagement with actual affairs. The battlefield, the courtroom, the family, the market , all are sites where 良知 is extended or blocked.
事实	shì shí	fact; the actual state of affairs — 事 shì + 实 shí (real; solid). The matter as it really is, a fact. The phrase 事实上 shìshí shàng means "in fact, actually," used to correct an impression or introduce the truth of a case.
事实上	shì shí shàng	as a matter of fact
事先	shì xiān	beforehand; in advance; prior to the event — 事 shì + 先 xiān (before; first). Before the matter happens, in advance. Its mirror is 事后 shìhòu (afterward), so the pair frames an event as before-and-after.
事业	shì yè	cause; career; lifework; undertaking — 事 shì (matter; affair) + 业 yè (undertaking). The cause or career one commits to, larger and more aspirational than a job: 事业心 "career ambition," 教育事业 "the cause of education." To 干一番事业 is to accomplish something lasting with one's life.
势	shì	momentum; force; tendency
视	shì	to view; to regard (literary/formal)
视线	shì xiàn	line of sight; gaze
试模	shì mú	mold trial; first shots — Running the new tool for the first time. Rounds are numbered T1, T2, T3, said as letters plus Chinese numbers (T一, T二). Each round produces parts to measure; the goal is to stop modifying.
室	shì	room, chamber
是不是	shì bu shì	is it or isn't it? · right? (A-not-A question / tag) — The A-not-A question form of 是 , a canonical Chinese question-formation strategy. Verb + 不 + Verb creates a yes-no question with slightly more rhetorical directness than a rising-intonation question. 是不是 also functions as a sentence-final tag question: 你也去，是不是？ ("You're going too, right?").
是的	shì de	yes; that's right; indeed (affirming something just said) — 是的 in its collapsed, fully reduced form is simply an affirmation: "yes, that is the case." The 是 confirms the truth of what was said; the 的 creates a sense of definitiveness or "that's just how it is." It is warmer and more emphatic than a bare 对, and more measured than an enthusiastic 当然.
是非	shì fēi	right and wrong; the rights and wrongs; trouble; gossip — 是 shì (right; this; is) + 非 fēi (wrong). The pairing of right and wrong as a single notion: 明辨是非 "to clearly distinguish right from wrong." By a common slide it also means the quarrels and gossip that arise from disputes: 惹是非 "to stir up trouble."
是非之心	shì fēi zhī xīn	the heart of right and wrong — sprout of 智 wisdom — 是非 shì fēi: right and wrong, approval and disapproval. The capacity to distinguish correct from incorrect actions , not just in oneself (羞恶之心) but in the world in general. The sprout of 智 zhì (moral wisdom , knowing how to act rightly in complex situations).
是非之心，智之端也	shì fēi zhī xīn , zhì zhī duān yě	The heart of right and wrong is the beginning of wisdom — Mencius, 孟子·公孙丑上. 是非 shìfēi = right and wrong (是 shì = right, correct; 非 fēi = wrong, incorrect). 之心 zhī xīn = the heart of; the feeling for. 端 duān = beginning; germ; tip of something.
是否	shì fǒu	whether or not; if (formal written) — 是 (yes/right) + 否 fǒu (no; to negate , a classical negative particle). A formal, written-register conjunction meaning "whether or not." The colloquial equivalents are 有没有 or 是不是. 是否 appears in journalism, academic writing, formal correspondence, and polished speech.
是吗	shì ma	"Is that so?" or "really?" Slightly cooler than 真的吗, often signaling polite interest without strong surprise. The neutral acknowledgment that you have heard and registered what was just said.
是谁告诉你的	shì shéi gào su nǐ de	who told you? (FOCUS: which agent, unknown) — The agent-focus question: 是谁 + Verb + 的. Both speakers know that someone told this person something , the unknown is who. 谁 occupies the focus position between 是 and the verb phrase. This is one of the most natural everyday uses of 是…的: interrogating the agent of a known past action.
是这样啊	shì zhè yàng a	"Oh, so it's like that — "Oh, so it's like that." A softer, gentler version of 原来如此. The 啊 particle gives it a reflective, taking-it-in quality. Useful when someone has just shared something personal or explained a situation.
适当	shì dàng	appropriate; suitable; proper — 适 shì (to suit) + 当 dàng (to fit). Describes a measure, amount, or action that is right for the situation: 适当的时候 (at an appropriate time), 适当休息 (rest as appropriate).
适合	shì hé	to suit; to be suited to; to fit — 适 shì (to be apt) + 合 hé (to fit). A verb taking an object: X 适合 Y means X suits Y. 这本书适合孩子 "this book is suited to children."
适应	shì yìng	to adapt; to get used to; to suit — 适 shì (to suit) + 应 yìng (to respond, to meet). To adjust oneself to new circumstances: 适应环境 (adapt to the environment), 适应新工作 (get used to a new job). The response here is the slow one of fitting in over time.
逝者	shì zhě	that which passes; the departed; what flows away — 逝 (to pass; to depart; to flow away) + 者. A thing-nominalization rather than a person-nominalization , 者 here points to an event or process rather than a human agent. This usage shows the full range of 者: it nominalizes any predicate, not just those describing human beings.
释迦牟尼	Shì jiā móu ní	Śākyamuni, the historical Buddha
收	shōu	to receive; gather in
收到	shōu dào	received, noted — The standard acknowledgement. 好的 (hǎo de) is "okay," 明白 (míngbai) "understood," 收到，谢谢 the polite version. Acknowledging promptly is expected; silence reads as a problem.
收千里眼顺风耳	shōu Qiān lǐ yǎn Shùn fēng ěr	subjugating the two divine generals — Two demonic generals, 千里眼 Qiānlǐyǎn (Eyes That See a Thousand Li, a green-faced demon) and 顺风耳 Shùnfēng'ěr (Ears That Hear Downwind, a red-faced demon), terrorized the coastal people. Lin Mo defeated them in combat through her superior spiritual power, converting them to her service.
手表	shǒu biǎo	wristwatch: a watch worn on the wrist (錶 in traditional script) — 手 shǒu (hand; wrist) + 表 biǎo (here the instrument sense, written 錶 in traditional script). A wristwatch. This is the sense that traditional writing marks with the metal radical (錶), since a watch is a metal device whose face displays a reading. Simplified Chinese writes it 表.
手工	shǒu gōng	handmade; handicraft
手机	shǒu jī	mobile phone — lit. "hand machine" — 手 shǒu (hand) + 机 jī (machine). China's most common word for mobile phone , the machine held in the hand. Completely replaced the earlier 手提电话 and the formal 移动电话 in everyday usage. 玩手机 wán shǒujī = "to play on one's phone" (ubiquitous activity).
手记	shǒu jì	handwritten notes; personal record
手头紧	shǒu tóu jǐn	short of money
手写	shǒu xiě	handwritten; to write by hand
手信	shǒu xìn	A small gift brought back from travel — A small gift brought back from travel: a souvenir or local specialty given to family, friends, or colleagues on return from a trip. The term is particularly common in Cantonese-speaking areas and in Hong Kong. The expectation of bringing 手信 from any notable journey is near-universal.
手足	shǒu zú	hands and feet; brothers; siblings — 手 (hand) + 足 (foot). The literal limbs of the body, used figuratively for brothers or close companions. Classical Chinese: 手足之情 (shǒuzú zhī qíng, the feeling between hands and feet) means brotherly affection , your siblings are as necessary to you as your own hands and feet.
守江山	shǒu jiāng shān	to defend the realm; to hold and preserve what was won — 守 shǒu (to guard; to protect; to hold steady , the character shows a hand over a roof, the posture of protection) + 江山. The harder and longer task: not the dramatic moment of conquest but the slow, unglamorous work of governing, maintaining order, and preventing the realm from unraveling.
守旧	shǒu jiù	conservative; set in old ways; resistant to change — 守 (shǒu, to guard, to hold onto , a hand over a roof) + 旧 (old). To guard the old order against change. In Chinese political and social discourse since the late Qing dynasty, 守旧 has been the standard term of criticism for those who resist modernization or reform.
首次	shǒu cì	the first time; for the first time — 首 shǒu (first) + 次 cì (time, occurrence). The first occasion on which something happens, common in news and formal writing. 首次访问 "a first visit," 首次公开 "made public for the first time." More formal than the spoken 第一次 dì-yī cì.
首都	shǒu dū	capital city — 首 shǒu (head, chief) + 都 dū (metropolis, city). The head-city of a country, its seat of government. 中国的首都是北京 "China's capital is Beijing." Note the reading: here 都 takes its first tone dū (city), not the toneless dōu (all).
首富	shǒu fù	the richest person
首领	shǒu lǐng	chieftain; leader; head (of a group) — 首 shǒu (head, chief) + 领 lǐng (to lead; the neck, the collar). The leader of a tribe, band, or organization. Carries a slightly traditional or martial flavor: the chief of a clan, the head of an outfit. 部落首领 "a tribal chieftain."
首先	shǒu xiān	first of all; firstly; in the first place — 首 shǒu (first) + 先 xiān (before, prior). The standard opener for an ordered list of points, often paired later with 其次 qícì (next) and 最后 zuìhòu (finally). Marks the very first item in a sequence of reasons, steps, or arguments.
首相	shǒu xiàng	prime minister (of a modern foreign government) — 首 shǒu (head; chief) + 相 xiàng (minister). The modern title for the head of government in countries such as Britain and Japan: 英国首相 "the British Prime Minister."
首要	shǒu yào	of prime importance; the foremost — 首 shǒu (first) + 要 yào (important). Marking the single most important matter, ranking above all others. 首要任务 "the primary task," 首要问题 "the chief problem." Stronger and more formal than 重要 zhòngyào (important), since it claims the top spot, not just high rank.
寿	shòu	longevity; the birthday blessing
寿命	shòu mìng	life-span; lifetime; (of a thing) durability, service life — 寿 shòu (longevity; long life) plus 命. The measured length of a life. Used both for living beings (人均寿命 "average human life-span") and, by extension, for objects (机器的寿命 "the service life of a machine").
受	shòu	to receive; to undergo
受不了	shòu bu liǎo	can't stand it; unbearable; past one's limit — 受 shòu (to endure; to receive) + 不了 liǎo (cannot complete the action of enduring). The potential complement with 不了 means the action cannot reach completion , endurance hits its limit. One of the most frequently used expressions in daily speech for frustration, exhaustion, or overwhelm.
受困	shòu kùn	to be trapped, stranded
受事	shòu shì	patient (linguistics)
扌	shǒu	hand radical — the 手 family
书本	shū běn	books; book learning (vs. practice) — 书 shū + 本 běn (root; copy; the original volume). The physical book as an object. Often used in the contrast 书本知识 (book knowledge) vs. 实践 (practice) , a tension that runs through Chinese educational discourse.
书法家	shū fǎ jiā	calligrapher — 书法 shūfǎ + 家 jiā (specialist; master of a domain). A practitioner of calligraphy at the level of mastery. 王羲之 Wáng Xīzhī (4th c.) is the canonical master; his 兰亭序 Lántíng Xù (Preface to the Orchid Pavilion) is the most celebrated piece of Chinese calligraphy.
书房	shū fáng	study, library room — 书 (shū, books) plus 房. The room set aside for reading and writing. In the scholar's house it was the heart of the home, lined with 书 and the four treasures of the study (brush, ink, paper, inkstone).
书架	shū jià	bookshelf
书简	shū jiǎn	(literary) letter, epistle
书面语	shū miàn yǔ	written language
书让他拿走了	shū ràng tā ná zǒu le	the book was taken away by him (colloquial 让 passive) — 让 ràng as passive marker: Subject + 让 + Agent + Verb + Result. Equivalent to 书被他拿走了, but more colloquial. 让 feels more spoken, lighter in register. Common across Mandarin dialects and in casual conversation. Note that 让 can also mean "let/allow" , context disambiguates.
书圣	shū shèng	Sage of Calligraphy — Sage of Calligraphy: the title given to Wang Xizhi.
书是他借的	shū shì tā jiè de	the book was borrowed by him (FOCUS: by him, not by someone else) — Agent focus: who performed the known action. The agent (他) is placed between 是 and the verb + 的. Both speakers know the book was borrowed , the question or point of emphasis is who borrowed it. This is also how 是…的 can function like a passive: the book (subject) + 是 + agent + verb + 的.
书同文	shū tóng wén	writing made uniform — The seven warring states had developed their own variant scripts. Qin imposed the small seal script (小篆 xiǎozhuàn) as the official form throughout the empire, with prime minister Li Si (李斯) overseeing the standardization.
书屋	shū wū	study; book room; a small library or bookshop — 书 shū + 屋 wū (room; small dwelling). A room given over to books , intimate, personal, unlarge. The word appears in study names of scholars and poets: 鲁迅的百草园 is a garden; his workroom was a 书屋. It is also the second half of this site's name: 角落書屋.
书写	shū xiě	to write formally; written expression
书信	shū xìn	letter; written correspondence
书桌	shū zhuō	writing desk
疏远	shū yuǎn	to grow distant from; to drift apart; estranged — 疏 shū (loose; scattered; infrequent; originally: a channel that has grown shallow) + 远 yuǎn (far). The channel of a relationship silting up, the contact thinning until the other person feels far away. Can be transitive (to push someone away deliberately) or intransitive (to drift apart gradually).
蔬菜	shū cài	vegetables (collectively) — 蔬 shū (vegetables) + 菜 cài (greens). The formal, category word for vegetables as a food group, the term you see on nutrition labels and in recipes: 多吃蔬菜 "eat more vegetables."
熟练	shú liàn	skilled, proficient, fluent — 熟 (cooked, ripe, familiar) + 练 (drilled). The same 熟 used for cooked food: a skill drilled until it is done, no longer raw. Describes a practiced, effortless command of a task.
熟能生巧	shú néng shēng qiǎo	practice makes perfect
属	shǔ	To belong to a zodiac sign , 你属什么？(nǐ shǔ shénme?) "What sign are you?" The verb for stating your zodiac animal.
属相	shǔ xiàng	Zodiac sign, literally "belonging-image — Zodiac sign, literally "belonging-image." The colloquial word for your zodiac animal, used in everyday speech.
属于	shǔ yú	to belong to; to be part of; to fall under — 属 shǔ (to be subordinate to) + 于 yú (to). States membership or ownership: 这本书属于我 "this book belongs to me," 鲸鱼属于哺乳动物 "whales belong to the mammals."
蜀	shǔ	Shu Han — the southwest, the legitimist cause — Founded in 221 by 刘备 Liu Bei, a distant relative of the Han imperial house who claimed to be restoring, not replacing, the dynasty (hence Shu Han, 蜀汉).
树	shù	tree
恕	shù	reciprocity; forgiveness
数词	shù cí	numeral
数量	shù liàng	quantity: the amount or number of something — 数 shù (number) + 量 liàng (amount). The neutral, countable "how many / how much" of a thing. 数量 answers questions of scale and stock: how many units, how large a supply.
数学	shù xu é	Mathematics — Mathematics: number-study. 数学家 (shùxuéjiā) is a mathematician; 数学课 (shùxué kè) is math class, the collocation every student uses daily.
数字	shù zì	Number, numeral, digit — Number, numeral, digit. The everyday word for a figure, whether spoken, written, or on a screen. 数字化 (shùzìhuà) extends it to "digitization," the same move English made with "digit."
数字系统	shù zì xì tǒng	the number system
数字象征	shù zì xiàng zhēng	Number Symbolism — 4 (四 sì) , inauspicious; sounds like 死 sǐ (death). Buildings skip floor 4, hospitals avoid room 4. 8 (八 bā) , extremely auspicious; sounds like 发 fā (prosper). License plates with 8s sell at premium.
帅哥	shuài gē	handsome guy; hey, handsome — 帅 (handsome) + 哥. A good-looking young man, and also a friendly way to flag down any young man whose name you don't know, the male counterpart of 美女 (měinǚ).
涮	shuàn	to swish (meat through hot liquid); to shabu
双	shuāng	pair; double; even
双十节	Shuāng shí Jié	Double Ten Festival (Taiwan's National Day)
双重	shuāng chóng	double; dual; twofold — 双 shuāng (pair; double) + 重 chóng (layer). Two layers of the same thing: 双重标准 "double standard," 双重身份 "dual identity." Here 重 is the layer-counting chóng.
霜	shuāng	frost
爽	shuǎng	refreshing; great; on form (colloquial)
水单	shuǐ dān	bank remittance slip — The bank's proof that you sent the money, also 汇款水单. The factory will ask for it the moment you say you have paid, and production often waits for it. Send it promptly; it is the fastest way to start the lead-time clock.
水果	shuǐ guǒ	fruit
水磨腔	shuǐ mò qiāng	Water-ground tone , the smooth, polished vocal style developed by Wei Liangfu that defines 昆曲 singing. The metaphor refers to the way stone is ground by water to a perfect smoothness. Characterized by slow tempos, elaborate ornamentation, and seamless melodic flow.
水墨	shuǐ mò	ink wash; water-and-ink; the classical Chinese painting medium — 水 shuǐ (water) + 墨 mò (ink , a slab of compressed carbon pigment, dissolved in water to create the medium). The classical Chinese painting medium: ink dissolved in varying concentrations of water, applied with a brush to paper or silk to create images through gradations of tone from dark to light.
水墨画	shuǐ mò huà	ink-wash painting
水牛	shuǐ niú	water buffalo
水平	shuǐ píng	level; standard; horizontal; one's degree of proficiency — 水 shuǐ (water) + 平 píng (level; flat; equal). A water surface is perfectly level , the universal measure of flatness and standards. From this physical fact, 水平 became the word for any standard of quality or proficiency.
水善利万物	shuǐ shàn lì wàn wù	water benefits all things; the Daoist image of yielding
水袖	shuǐ xiù	Water sleeves — Water sleeves: the long white silk extensions attached to the costume sleeves of major 昆曲 characters.
睡不着	shuì bu zháo	cannot fall asleep — Sleep + not + take-hold, with 着 in its zháo reading. The 3 a.m. word, and the proof that the complement is about achieving a result: you can 睡 (lie down to sleep) all night and never 睡着.
睡觉	shuì jiào	to sleep (觉 read jiào)
顺	shùn	smooth; unobstructed
顺便说一下	shùn biàn shuō yī xià	"By the way — "By the way." Marks an aside or a piece of side information that occurs to you mid-conversation. 顺便 literally means "in passing." The English equivalent fits cleanly. Often shortened in casual speech to just 顺便.
顺便问一下	shùn biàn wèn yī xià	"While I'm at it, let me ask…" Lets you slide an unrelated question into the conversation without it feeling like an interruption. Pairs naturally with 顺便说一下 (the side-remark version).
顺从	shùn cóng	submissive; to yield
顺序	shùn xù	order; sequence
瞬间	shùn jiān	an instant; the blink of an eye — 瞬 shùn (a blink) + 间 jiān (interval). The smallest interval of time, the moment it takes to blink: 一瞬间 "in a flash." The interval shrunk almost to nothing.
说不定	shuō bu dìng	maybe; hard to say; possibly — 说 shuō + 不定 (cannot be fixed/determined). Means "cannot be stated with certainty," i.e., maybe, possibly. One of the most natural hedges in spoken Mandarin. More casual and spoken than 也许 yěxǔ or 可能 kěnéng.
说出	shuō chū	to say out; to speak out; to voice (what was held inside) — 说 shuō (speak; say) + 出 (emergence complement). To bring words out from an interior state into speech: what was private, unspoken, or held back becomes expressed.
说到	shuō dào	to mention; to come to (a topic); speaking of — 说 shuō (to speak) + 到 dào (arrive at). Speech that arrives at its topic: to come around to mentioning something, to bring something up. 说到这里 (at this point, speaking of this) is a common transitional phrase in both speech and writing.
说到这个	shuō dào zhè ge	"Speaking of which" or "since you mention it — "Speaking of which" or "since you mention it." Pivots to a related topic that the previous remark suggested. Cleaner than 对了 when the connection is more thematic than associative.
说道	shuō dào	to say (literary narration marker) — 说 shuō (to say; to speak) + 道 dào (way; to say). A literary verb used in fiction and formal narrative to introduce direct speech. Written as X说道，"……" (X said, "……"). Seldom appears in casual spoken Chinese; it marks elevated or written register.
说得对	shuō de duì	"Well said" or "you're right — "Well said" or "you're right." Compliments the rightness of what was just said. Slightly more formal than 没错; acknowledges the other person's reasoning, not just the conclusion.
说服	shuō fú	to persuade; to convince — 说 shuō + 服 fú (to submit; to be convinced; to be at someone's service). To speak until someone submits, to persuade successfully. The complement 服 marks the result: the persuasion worked.
说话	shuō huà	to speak; to talk; to say something — 说 shuō (to say) + 话 huà (words). The everyday verb for speaking, formed as verb-plus-object: literally "to say words." Because 话 is already the object, you do not say 说话一件事; to speak about something you use 说 alone: 说一件事.
说几句	shuō jǐ jù	to say a few words — 说 shuō (to say) + 几 jǐ (a few) + 句 jù (measure word). To make a few brief remarks, the polite formula a speaker uses before a short toast or comment: 我想说几句 "I would like to say a few words."
说了半天才明白	shuō le bàn tiān cái míng bai	finally understood after talking for ages — After a long period of effort, explanation, or struggle , 才 finally. The effort is encoded in the first clause (说了半天 = talked for half a day, idiom for "a long time"); the 才 marks that only after all that effort did the result occur. This is 才 at its most expressive: hard-won achievement.
说慢一点	shuō màn yì diǎn	speak a little slower — Lead with 不好意思 (bù hǎoyìsi, "excuse me") to soften it. Most people slow down for one sentence and then speed back up; asking twice is fine.
说明	shuō míng	to explain; to illustrate; explanation; instructions — 说 shuō + 明 míng (clear, bright). To make something clear through speech. As a noun: 说明书 shuōmíngshū = instruction manual. As a verb: 这说明了… = "this demonstrates/shows that…"
说实话	shuō shí huà	"To tell the truth" or "honestly — "To tell the truth" or "honestly." Signals that what follows is candid, sometimes uncomfortably so. Often used before a confession, an admission, or a frank criticism. The English "honestly" works as a direct translation.
说文解字	Shuō wén Jiě zì	Explaining Simple and Analyzing Compound Characters
说着说着她哭了	shuō zhe shuō zhe tā kū le	as she kept talking, she started to cry — The model sentence for this pattern. The action (talking) repeated in the V + 着 frame signals continuation; the main clause introduces the unplanned outcome (crying). Used whenever a shift happened gradually, through sustained activity, not at a discrete moment.
丝	sī	silk thread
丝绸	sī chóu	silk
司机	sī jī	driver
司马迁	Sī mǎ Qiān	Sima Qian
私	sī	private; selfish
思考	sī kǎo	to think carefully; to reflect; to consider — 思 sī (to think; thought) + 考 kǎo (to examine; to investigate; to test). Deliberate, directed thinking. 思考 is the most common everyday word for careful, intentional mental activity , the kind of thinking you do when you have a problem in front of you and are genuinely working through it.
思路	sī lù	train of thought; line of reasoning; thinking process — 思 sī (thought) + 路 lù (road; path; route). Lit. "the road of thought." The direction and movement of how thinking unfolds , not the conclusion but the path that leads there. 思路清晰 (a clear line of reasoning) is high praise in Chinese academic and professional contexts.
思念	sīn iàn	to miss; to think of with longing; to yearn for — 思 sī (to think; to miss) + 念 niàn (to think of; to read aloud; to remember , itself containing 心 in its lower half). The compound that doubles the weight. 思念 is used specifically for people , loved ones who are far away, people who have died, a home one has left.
思无邪	sī wú xié	Confucius's single-phrase summary of the entire 诗经 — Confucius's single-phrase summary of the entire 诗经: "thoughts without depravity" or "sincere in thought." The phrase became the canonical statement of the anthology's moral purpose.
思乡	sī xiāng	homesickness; longing for one's hometown — 思 sī (to miss; to long for) + 乡 xiāng (hometown; native place; countryside). Lit. "to miss the home place." Homesickness as a deliberate, named condition , not vague discomfort but a specific directional longing, pointing back to the place of origin.
思想	sī xiǎng	thought; ideology; way of thinking — 思 sī (to think; thought) + 想 xiǎng (to think; to want; idea). Thought as a formed body , both personal and systematic. 思想 operates on two levels. At the personal level it describes someone's habitual way of thinking, their mental orientation or values: 思想开放 (open-minded , lit. "open thought").
死板	sǐ bǎn	rigid, inflexible
死路	sǐ lù	dead end; no way out
死人	sǐ rén	a dead person; a corpse
死亡	sǐ wáng	death; to die (formal)
死心	sǐ xīn	to give up; to let hope die
四川盆地	Sì chuān Pén dì	the Sichuan Basin — the geography that produced this cuisine's ingredients and intensity — The Sichuan Basin , the large inland depression enclosed by mountains on all sides. 盆 means "basin" or "bowl"; 地 means "land" or "ground." The shape of a basin (四面环山 sì miàn huán shān, "mountains on all four sides") is the defining geographic fact of the region.
四大发明	sì dà fā míng	the Four Great Inventions
四大美人	sì dà měi rén	the Four Beauties of ancient China
四大名著	sì dà míng zhù	the Four Great Classical Novels — 四 sì + 大 dà (great) + 名著 míngzhù (famous work). The four canonical novels of Chinese literature: Romance of the Three Kingdoms (三国演义), Water Margin (水浒传), Journey to the West (西游记), and Dream of the Red Chamber (红楼梦).
四方	sì fāng	the four directions; all around; everywhere — 四 sì + 方 fāng (direction; side; square). Literally the four cardinal directions, and by extension every direction, all around. 四面八方 sìmiàn bāfāng ("four faces, eight directions") intensifies it to mean from every conceivable side. A 四方形 sìfāngxíng is a square or quadrilateral, the four-sided shape.
四海	sì hǎi	the four seas; the whole country; the entire world — 四 sì + 海 hǎi (sea). The four seas thought to bound the inhabited world, hence the whole realm, everywhere under heaven. 四海之内皆兄弟 ("within the four seas all men are brothers"), from the Analects, is one of the most quoted lines on universal kinship.
四季	sì jì	the four seasons — 四 sì + 季 jì (season). Spring (春 chūn), summer (夏 xià), autumn (秋 qiū), winter (冬 dōng). 四季如春 sìjì rú chūn ("four seasons like spring") describes a mild climate where it feels like spring all year, a stock phrase of praise for places such as Kunming.
四九城	sì jiǔ chéng	The "four-nine city" — The "four-nine city": a colloquial name for old Beijing, referring to its four inner city gates and nine outer city gates. A marker of old Beijing nostalgia and native identity.
四旧	sì jiù	the Four Olds (ideas, culture, customs, habits)
四君子	sì jūn zǐ	Four Gentlemen — Four Gentlemen: the four emblematic plants of the literati painting tradition: prunus plum 梅 (perseverance), orchid 兰 (secluded virtue), bamboo 竹 (resilience), chrysanthemum 菊 (dignified retirement). Together they map the principal Confucian virtues onto natural forms.
四气五味	sì qì wǔ wèi	Four temperatures and five flavors — Four temperatures and five flavors: the two-axis system for classifying drug properties. The four temperatures: cold (寒), cool (凉), warm (温), hot (热). The five flavors: sour (酸), bitter (苦), sweet (甘), pungent (辛), salty (咸). Each flavor has specific organ affinities and physiological effects.
四人帮	Sì rén bāng	Gang of Four (arrested 1976)
四声	sì shēng	the four tones (of Mandarin) — 四 sì (four) + 声 shēng (tone). The four-way tone system of standard Mandarin: high-level, rising, dipping, and falling. The neutral tone 轻声 is counted separately.
四书	Sì shū	the Four Books of Confucianism — 四 sì + 书 shū (book). The Great Learning (大学), the Doctrine of the Mean (中庸), the Analects (论语), and the Mencius (孟子). Selected and grouped by the Song scholar Zhu Xi as the core Confucian curriculum, the Four Books became the basis of the imperial examination system for centuries.
四象	sì xiàng	The Four Symbols — The Four Symbols: the four mythological animal guardians of the cardinal directions: Azure Dragon (east, spring), Vermilion Bird (south, summer), White Tiger (west, autumn), Black Tortoise (north, winter).
寺庙	sì miào	temple; Buddhist monastery
寺庙建筑	sì miào jiàn zhú	Buddhist temple architecture — the visual language of Chinese Buddhism — The Chinese Buddhist temple complex (寺 sì or 寺庙 sìmiào) follows a distinctive axial layout: the main gate (山门 shānmén, "mountain gate," the formal Buddhist term for the entrance), then the Hall of Heavenly Kings 天王殿 (with the four directional guardians), then the main hall 大雄宝殿 (the Great Hero's Hal…
松	sōng	released; unbound — the key quality sought in practice
宋朝	sòng cháo	the Song dynasty
宋词	Sòng cí	Song lyric poetry; the classical 词 of the Song — 宋 Sòng (the Song) + 词 cí (lyric). The lyric-poem form at its height, written to set tune-patterns and meant to be sung. Paired with 唐诗 Táng shī, Tang regulated verse, as one of the high points of classical poetry.
宋江	sòng jiāng	Song Jiang — the leader, "Timely Rain" — The clerk turned outlaw chief, nicknamed 及时雨 ("Timely Rain") for his open-handed generosity, who becomes the band's leader.
送别	sòng bié	to bid farewell, see off — 送 (see off) plus 别 (to part). A literary, emotionally weighted word for the moment of parting, the subject of countless Tang poems written at the roadside or the river crossing.
送达	sòng dá	to deliver, serve (formally) — 送 (send) plus 达 (reach, arrive). A formal verb stressing successful arrival, used for delivered mail and, in law, for serving legal documents.
送给	sòng gěi	to give to (someone) — 送 (give) plus 给 (to, for the benefit of). The recipient-marking pattern: 送给 + person + thing. The thing crosses over and stays with the receiver.
送魂	sòng hún	soul guiding — escorting the dead to the afterlife — 送魂 (guiding the soul , lit. "sending the soul") is the funerary function of the shaman: after death, the soul of the deceased may be disoriented, confused about its condition, or unable to find the path to the spirit world.
送货	sòng huò	to deliver goods — 送 (send) plus 货 (goods). Door-to-door delivery: the courier who 送货 to your home, the staple verb of online shopping and takeout.
送礼	sòng lǐ	to give a gift — 送 (give) plus 礼 (gift, ritual courtesy). A separable verb-object pair for the social act of presenting a present, with all the etiquette that surrounds it in Chinese life.
送行	sòng xíng	to see someone off (on a journey) — 送 (see off) plus 行 (to travel, set out). To go to the station, dock, or airport to bid a traveler farewell as they begin a journey.
送子	sòng zǐ	child-giving (form of Guanyin)
送子观音	Sòng zǐ Guān yīn	child-giving Guanyin — the form prayed to for fertility and safe birth — 送 sòng (to send, to give) + 子 zǐ (child) + 观音. The most commonly petitioned form of Guanyin outside formal temple Buddhism. Depicted holding a child, or seated with children around her, this iconographic type is the primary focus of domestic devotion in households hoping for a child.
艘	sōu	classifier for ships
苏菜	Sū cài	Jiangsu Cuisine
苏式面	Sū shì miàn	Suzhou-Style Noodles
苏轼	Sū Shì	Su Shi — Song literati theorist
素食	sù shí	vegetarian food; plain eating
速度	sù dù	speed; velocity
宿命	sù mìng	predetermined fate; fatalism
酸	suān	Acid; sour — Acid; sour. One word covers the chemical class and the taste, plus muscle soreness (腿酸, sore legs) and the sting of envy (心里酸酸的). Chemistry students never forget which taste an acid has.
算筹	suàn chóu	Counting rods — Counting rods: the predecessor to the suanpan. Small bamboo, bone, or ivory sticks arranged in patterns on a flat surface to represent numbers in a base-10 positional system. Used in China from at least the Warring States period (475–221 BCE) through the Tang dynasty.
算命	suàn mìng	to tell fortunes; to calculate one's fate — 算 suàn (to calculate) plus 命. The phrase reveals the cultural assumption: a 命 is something that can be computed. Given the year, month, day, and hour of birth (生辰八字 shēngchén bāzì, "the eight characters of birth"), a fortune-teller can read out the trajectory.
算盘	suàn pán	The abacus, literally "calculating tray — The abacus, literally "calculating tray." The standard Chinese bead-frame instrument with 2 upper beads and 5 lower beads per rod, in use in its canonical form since at least the 16th century.
算是	suàn shì	counts as; could be considered
虽然	suī rán	although; even though — 虽 suī (although) + 然 rán (so). Opens a concession and almost always pairs with 但是 dànshì or 可是 kěshì in the second clause: 虽然 ... 但是 ... "although ..., yet ...".
虽然但是	suī rán dàn shì	although…but (concessive)
随笔	suí bǐ	casual essay; jottings as you go
随便	suí biàn	casual; whatever
随手泡	suí shǒu pào	instant-hot kettle / electric gongfu kettle
随意	suí yì	As you like, freely, casually , the softer alternative to 干杯 that allows the recipient to drink as much or as little as they wish. Using 随意 rather than 干杯 is a show of consideration, often offered to guests who are known to drink little or who have already flagged a limitation.
岁	suì	year of age; year
孙悟空	sūn wù kōng	Sun Wukong — the Monkey King, the protector — The brilliant, fearless, impatient monkey whose magic and loyalty carry the party through every peril. He sees through every demon's disguise (with his 火眼金睛, fiery golden eyes) and fights when the monk cannot.
孙中山	Sūn Zhōng shān	Sun Yat-sen, father of the republic
孙子	sūn zi	grandson
孙子兵法	Sūn zǐ Bīng fǎ	The Art of War — the foundational Chinese military text — Attributed to 孙武 Sūn Wǔ of the state of Wu (late Spring and Autumn / early Warring States). Thirteen chapters on strategy, deception, terrain, and the nature of victory.
损害	sǔn hài	to damage, impair
缩短	suō duǎn	to shorten
所爱	suǒ ài	that which is loved; the one one loves
所见	suǒ jiàn	that which is seen; what one sees
所说的话	suǒ shuō de huà	the words spoken; what was said
所谓	suǒ wèi	so-called; what is called; what people call
所以	suǒ yǐ	therefore; so; that is why — 所 (that which) + 以 (the means/reason by which). The most common consequential conjunction in modern Chinese. Follows a cause clause (often introduced by 因为) and introduces the result: 因为下雨，所以我没去 (because it rained, so I did not go).
所有	suǒ yǒu	all; every; everything one has — 所 suǒ (that which; a place; nominalizing particle) + 有 (have/exist). 所有 as a determiner: 所有的学生 (all the students). As a noun: 他失去了所有 (He lost everything he had). As a formal possession compound in legal/written Chinese: 所有权 suǒyǒuquán = ownership rights, title.
所有 · 全部	suǒ yǒu quán bù	all of, the entirety of a set — 所有(的) + noun names the complete set up front: 所有的学生都来了 (all the students came). 所有 began as "that which is possessed" (所 + 有, what one has), and the possessive flavor survives in 我所有的钱 (all the money I have).
所在	suǒ zài	where something is; the location of; what something lies in — 所 suǒ (that which; nominalizing particle) + 在 (to be located). A formal noun meaning "the place/point where something is located; the essence of what something consists in." Commonly appears in 问题所在 (the crux of the problem; where the problem lies) and 关键所在 (the key point; where the key lies).
所知	suǒ zhī	that which is known; what one knows
所做的事	suǒ zuò de shì	the things done; what was done
他回来就走	tā huí lái jiù zǒu	he leaves as soon as he comes back (no pause between) — When 就 connects two verbs or clauses, it signals immediate, seamless sequence , the second action follows the first without pause. This is the "as soon as" function: the first event triggers the second instantly. No 一 is needed here , just the bare sequence: Clause 1 + 就 + Clause 2.
他结婚了	tā jié hūn le	he got married; he is now a married man — Major life transitions , marriage, graduation, birth, death, career changes , are natural territory for sentence-final 了. The 了 frames the event as a change that affects the current situation: the relevant new fact is that he is now married. It answers an implicit question about the present.
他力	tā lì	other-power (Amitābha's)
他们	tā men	they (mixed or male group)
他们是在上海见面的	tā men shì zài Shàng hǎi jiàn miàn de	they met in Shanghai (FOCUS: in Shanghai, not elsewhere) — Both speakers know they met , the question is where. The 在上海 sits between 是 and 见面的, specifying the location of the known meeting. Common in stories, biographical accounts, and conversations about how relationships began.
他们正在开会	tā men zhèng zài kāi huì	They are in a meeting right now — One of the most useful office and workplace phrases. 开会 kāihuì = to hold/attend a meeting. The progressive here signals that interrupting is not appropriate. Variant: 他们在开会，请稍后再来 (They are in a meeting , please come back later). 正在 gives it a slight formality appropriate to professional contexts.
他人	tā rén	others; other people (formal/literary) — 他 tā (he; other) + 人 rén. The formal and written equivalent of 别人. Appears in legal texts, philosophical writing, ethical discussions, and formal prose. In classical Chinese, 他 itself meant "other" before it was narrowed to the third-person pronoun, making 他人 doubly "other."
他日	tā rì	another day; some future time
他三十岁才结婚	tā sān shí suì cái jié hūn	he didn't marry until thirty (late, in the speaker's view) — From the speaker's perspective, thirty is late to marry , perhaps later than expected or social norms dictate.
他是开车去的	tā shì kāi chē qù de	he went by car (FOCUS: by car, not by train, not walking) — Manner focus: the verb of transportation or method of doing something sits between 是 and 去的 (or the main verb + 的). The presupposition is that he went somewhere , the new information is how. 开车 (drive a car) is the manner. Other manners: 坐飞机 by plane, 骑车 by bike, 走路 on foot, 打电话 by phone.
他是去年来的	tā shì qùn ián lái de	he came last year (FOCUS: last year, not any other year) — Both speakers know he came; the question is when. 是去年 places "last year" in the focus position, bracketed by 是 and …来的. The construction answers a question or corrects a misunderstanding about the timing: "No, it wasn't three years ago, it was last year."
他说得很清楚	tā shuō de hěn qīng chu	he speaks clearly — verb + 得 + degree complement — 得 here follows a verb and introduces a complement describing the degree, manner, or result of that action. Think of it as "to the extent that…" or "in a way that…" The complement can be an adjective, another verb, or a clause. This is the most analytically complex use of 得.
他五岁就会读书了	tā wǔ suì jiù huì dú shū le	he was already reading at age five! (impressively early) — Age five is the timeline; 就 signals it is earlier than the expected baseline for reading ability. The sentence-final 了 reinforces the new-situation quality , at that point, as early as five, he had already achieved this. The combination 就…了 is extremely common for describing precocious achievements.
他乡	tā xiāng	a strange land; a place away from home
它的	tā de	its (modern spoken)
它们	tā men	they, them (non-human)
她来了两个星期了	tā lái le liǎng gè xīng qī le	she has been here for two weeks (and is still here) — The double-了 construction with a duration complement is one of its most important uses. The first 了 marks the initial arrival (completed action of coming); the second 了 marks the current relevance , she is still here now. Without the second 了, the sentence implies the visit is concluded.
她们	tā men	they (all-female group) — 她 + 们 (men, plural marker). Used only when every member of the group is known to be female. If the group is mixed-gender or the gender is unknown, the default is 他们 (tāmen) , the male-radical form is the unmarked plural.
她是今天早上走的	tā shì jīn tiān zǎo shang zǒu de	she left this morning (FOCUS: this morning, not yesterday) — More precise time focus , this morning, as opposed to last night or yesterday. The presupposition is clear: she has already left (both speakers know this). The new information is the precise time of departure. Commonly used in conversations about arrivals, departures, and events.
她字	tā zì	"the 她 character" — shorthand for the gendered-pronoun debate — A meta-term in linguistic and feminist writing. 她字之争 ("the dispute over 她") refers to the recurring debate over whether to use 她, abolish it, or replace it with a non-marked alternative.
牠	tā	it (animal, traditional)
祂	tā	He/She (capitalized, of a deity) — 礻 (the spirit/altar radical, a variant of 示) + 也. Coined later than 她, by Chinese Christian translators of the Bible, to render English "He" (capitalized of God) without committing to the human-male radical 亻. 祂 carries no gender; it carries divinity.
踏青	tà qīng	spring outing; walking in green countryside
台词	tái cí	an actor lines; stage dialogue; script lines — 台 tái (stage) + 词 cí (words). The lines a performer speaks in a play, film, or show. 背台词 bèi táicí is to learn one lines by heart.
台灯	tái dēng	desk lamp, table lamp — 台 (tái, platform, stand) + 灯 (lamp): a lamp that sits on a desk or table, the small pool of light you read and study by.
台风	tái fēng	Typhoon — Typhoon. The Chinese word and the English one echo each other, and scholars still argue over which direction the borrowing ran. Coastal summer news vocabulary.
台海	tái hǎi	The Taiwan Strait — The Taiwan Strait: the body of water between Taiwan and the Chinese mainland. Used as shorthand for the cross-strait political situation: 台海关系 (cross-strait relations), 台海局势 (Taiwan Strait situation).
台阶	tái jiē	A step, a staircase , and in face contexts, a way out — A step, a staircase , and in face contexts, a way out. 给他一个台阶下 ("give him a step to descend") means to provide a face-saving exit from an awkward position. One of the most useful face-vocabulary phrases in practice.
台湾	Tái wān	Taiwan
抬头	tái tóu	to raise the head; to look up — 抬 tái (to lift) + 头 tóu (head). To lift the head and look up, literally or in the sense of holding one's head high after hardship. Its opposite is 低头 dītóu, to lower the head.
太后	tài hòu	empress dowager; the emperor's mother — 太 tài (grand; senior; beyond; supreme) + 后 (empress). The emperor's mother or grandmother who has survived her husband's reign.
太极	tài jí	the Supreme Ultimate; Taiji (the source of yin and yang) — 太 tài (supreme) + 极 jí (the pole; the ultimate). In the Yijing tradition, the Supreme Ultimate from which 阴 yīn and 阳 yáng divide. The familiar swirl symbol is the 太极图. The slow martial art is 太极拳 tàijíquán.
太极拳	tài jí quán	Tai Chi Chuan — the soft internal martial art based on yin-yang principles — 太极 tàijí (the Supreme Ultimate , the cosmological concept) + 拳 quán (fist; boxing style). The soft, circular martial art based on the principles of 阴阳: yielding overcomes force, softness overcomes hardness, stillness overcomes motion. A paradigm of 内功 (internal cultivation).
太监	tài jiān	eunuch official
太客气了	tài kè qi le	too polite, too much fuss
太空	tài kōng	outer space — 太 tài (grand) + 空 kōng (void; empty). The "grand void," outer space. 太空人 tàikōngrén and 宇航员 yǔhángyuán both mean astronaut.
太了	tài le	too… (excessive)
太平	tài píng	great peace — the classical political ideal
太平天国	Tài píng Tiāng uó	Taiping Heavenly Kingdom
太认真了	tài rèn zhēn le	taking it too seriously; too earnest about it
太太	tài tai	Mrs.; madam; wife (polite) — The doubled 太, the senior lady of the house. Used as a title after a surname (王太太 "Mrs. Wang") and as a polite word for one's own or another's wife. The 太 marks seniority and respect.
太学	Tài xu é	Imperial Academy
太阳	tài yáng	the sun — 太 tài (supreme; ultimate; the greatest) + 阳 yáng (yang force; light). The Supreme Yang , the ultimate source of yang energy in the cosmos. In the 五行 framework, the sun is the source of Fire phase energy. 太阳能 (solar energy) uses exactly this logic.
态度	tài dù	attitude, stance
谈	tán	to talk; to chat
谈价	tán jià	price negotiation
谈判	tán pàn	To negotiate; negotiation — To negotiate; negotiation. Formal register, used for deals and diplomacy alike. Everyday haggling is 讨价还价; you 谈判 a joint venture, you 讨价还价 over a jacket.
坦白	tǎn bái	to confess; come clean
坦诚	tǎn chéng	candid; frank
汤面	tāng miàn	noodles in soup; noodle soup
汤药	tāng yào	Decocted herbal medicine — Decocted herbal medicine: herbs simmered in water to produce a strong tea. The traditional and most common form of herbal administration, now competing with granules and capsules for convenience.
唐朝	táng cháo	the Tang dynasty
唐卡	táng kā	Thangka — Thangka: a Tibetan painted or embroidered hanging scroll, used as a devotional object and teaching tool. Thangkas depict deities, mandalas, and scenes from Buddhist narratives. The production of thangkas is a living art form maintained by trained artisans in both Tibet and the exile community.
唐僧	táng sēng	Tang Sanzang — the holy, helpless monk — The pilgrim monk, also called 唐三藏, modelled on the historical 玄奘 Xuanzang. He is pure in heart, devout, and utterly committed to his sacred mission, but physically weak and often foolishly compassionate, forever being captured by demons who wish to eat his flesh to gain immortality.
唐诗	táng shī	Tang poetry
堂哥	táng gē	older male cousin (paternal)
塘	táng	pond, dyke-pool
糖醋鲤鱼	táng cù lǐ yú	Sweet-and-Sour Carp
烫	tàng	scalding
逃跑	táo pǎo	to flee; to escape; to run away — 逃 (táo, to flee) + 跑. The standard verb for running away from a threat, a captor, or an obligation. Used for fugitives, escaped animals, soldiers fleeing a battlefield, and (figuratively) people who avoid responsibility.
讨论	tǎo lùn	to discuss; to talk over — 讨 tǎo (to seek out, to ask for) + 论. The everyday word for discussing a matter: a group talking something through to reach understanding or a decision. Cooperative by default, two or more people pooling views rather than fighting over them.
讨论会	tǎo lùn huì	a discussion forum; a symposium
讨厌	tǎo yàn	to dislike; to find annoying
套房	tào fáng	suite, self-contained flat
套路	tào lù	form; sequence; a fixed pattern (also: routine, formula) — A choreographed sequence of martial techniques practiced solo, encoding combat principles. Also used colloquially for any formulaic routine or pattern: 走套路 "going through the motions" · 那只是套路 "that's just standard formula."
特别	tè bié	especially
特别是	tè bié shì	"Especially" or "particularly — "Especially" or "particularly." Singles out the most important case within a broader claim. 我喜欢中国菜, 特别是川菜 ("I like Chinese food, especially Sichuan food"). Direct, useful, common.
特意	tè yì	specially; on purpose (warm or neutral)
特长	tè cháng	special skill; particular strength; forte — 特 tè (special; particular) + 长 cháng (strength; advantage, the area where one "goes long"). 你有什么特长？ "What are your special skills?" is the standard job interview form. 特长生 = a student admitted on the basis of a special talent (music, sports, art) rather than exam scores alone.
踢	tī	to kick (football)
提高	tí gāo	to raise; to improve; to enhance — 提 tí (to lift, to raise; to bring up) + 高 (high). To raise the level of something: 提高效率 (improve efficiency); 提高水平 (raise the standard); 提高工资 (increase wages). The standard verb for improvement in formal and professional contexts.
提前	tí qián	to move up in time; to do in advance; ahead of schedule — 提 tí (to lift; to bring forward; to raise up) + 前 qián (front; before). Literally "to lift forward": to move a scheduled event earlier than planned, to do something in advance. The opposite is 推迟 tuīchí (to postpone, to push back).
提问	tí wèn	to ask a question; to raise a question — 提 tí (to raise; to bring up) + 问 wèn (to ask). To put a question forward, typically in a class or meeting. 老师提问 "the teacher asks a question," often of a student called on to answer.
题款	tí kuǎn	inscription on a painting
题目	tí mù	exam question; title; subject (of an essay) — 题 tí (heading) + 目 mù (item; entry). The item set at the head of a test, or the title given to a composition: 考试题目 "exam questions," 作文题目 "the essay prompt." It points at the specific thing to be answered or written about.
题诗	tí shī	to inscribe a poem (on a painting, wall, or scroll) — 题 tí (to inscribe) + 诗 shī (poem). To write a poem directly onto a surface, a classic gesture in Chinese painting where verse and image share one scroll.
题字	tí zì	to inscribe characters; an inscription — 题 tí (to inscribe) + 字 zì (characters). To brush words onto a painting, plaque, or wall, or the inscription itself. Often done by a respected figure as a mark of honor.
体会	tǐ huì	to grasp through experience; the understanding so gained — 体 tǐ (body) + 会 huì (to understand). To come to understand a thing by living it, and the insight that results. 深刻的体会 "a deep understanding (won from experience)."
体积	tǐ jī	volume; bulk
体力	tǐ lì	physical stamina; bodily strength; endurance — 体 tǐ (body) + 力 lì (strength). The body's stamina, the strength that sustains physical effort over time. Often paired against 脑力 nǎolì (mental effort): 体力劳动 "physical labor."
体面	tǐ miàn	decent; respectable; dignified
体贴	tǐ tiē	considerate; thoughtful; attentive to others' needs — 体 tǐ (to feel in the body) + 贴 tiē (to stick close). To feel another's situation closely and act with care: 体贴的丈夫 "a considerate husband." It is warmth shown through attention to small needs.
体系	tǐ xì	framework; structured system (of ideas or institutions) — 体 tǐ (body; structure) + 系 xì (connection). An organized framework, usually of something abstract: 理论体系 "a theoretical framework," 教育体系 "the education system." More architectural and conceptual than the everyday 系统.
体验	tǐ yàn	to experience; to live through; an experience — 体 tǐ (body) + 验 yàn (to test, to verify). To know something by passing through it in person: 体验生活 "to experience real life." As a noun, the experience itself: 难忘的体验 "an unforgettable experience."
体用	tǐ yòng	substance and function; essence and manifestation — 体 tǐ (body; substance; the underlying essence) + 用 yòng (function; use; the outward expression). A compound used in philosophy, aesthetics, Buddhist thought, and political theory to describe the relationship between a thing's nature and its operation in the world.
