小红书	xiǎo hóng shū	Little Red Book (Mao's quotations)
小鸡	xiǎo jī	chick, baby chicken — 小 (xiǎo, small) + 鸡 (chicken). The fluffy yellow chick just out of the shell, a favorite in children's songs and picture books.
小姐	xiǎo jiě	miss; young lady — 小 (small, young) plus 姐 is the classic polite title for a young, unmarried woman, like English "Miss." Register matters: in some mainland contexts a bare 小姐 can sound off, so service settings often prefer 美女 (měinǚ) or a surname plus 姐.
小令	xiǎo lìng	Short lyric form — Short lyric form: tune patterns of 58 characters or fewer. The most concentrated 词 form, requiring the greatest compression. Contrasted with 长调 chángdiào or 慢词 màncí, the extended long forms of 91 characters or more.
小名	xiǎo míng	Childhood nickname , a pet name used within the family, often deliberately humble or silly to avoid attracting bad luck to the child. 狗蛋 (Gǒudàn, "Dog Egg") or 二毛 (Èrmáo, "Second Fuzz") are classic examples.
小女	xiǎo nǚ	my humble daughter (respectful reference) — A father's way of referring to his daughter when speaking to someone of higher status. The 小 does not mean the daughter is young or small , it encodes the father's self-positioning as lesser. Compare 犬子 quǎnzǐ (my unworthy son , lit. "dog son"), the parallel self-deprecating term for sons.
小气	xiǎo qì	stingy; petty — Small qi: a constricted spirit, tight with money and with grace. The direct opposite is 大方 (dàfang, generous, poised). Milder than 小人; a friend can be teased as 小气 and remain a friend.
小声	xiǎo shēng	quiet; softly; in a low voice — 小 xiǎo (small) + 声 shēng (voice). A small voice, the opposite of 大声: 小声说 "to speak quietly," often so others do not overhear.
小时	xiǎo shí	hour (unit of time) — 小 xiǎo (small) + 时 shí (time; hour). A small slice of time: one hour. The compound reflects a traditional view of time's granularity , the hour as the smallest standard unit. Contrast 小时候 xiǎoshíhou (childhood, "when one was small"), where 小时 carries the older sense of "young age."
小时候	xiǎo shí hou	in childhood; when (one) was young — "When little," a fixed expression for one's childhood. 我小时候 = "when I was a child." Compare 那时候 (back then, at that time) and 到时候 (when the time comes). Note: 小时 (without 候) means "an hour," a different word, so the 候 matters.
小说	xiǎo shuō	novel; fiction — 小 xiǎo (small; minor) + 说 shuō (speech; theory). Literally "minor talk" , the genre of fiction was long considered a lower form of literature compared to history and philosophy. The term was coined to contrast with 大道 "the Great Way" of classical learning.
小屋	xiǎo wū	small room; cabin; cottage; little shelter — 小 xiǎo (small) + 屋 wū. A small dwelling , intimate, often cozy or rustic. In fiction and poetry, 小屋 carries warmth: a retreat, a nest, a place apart. In real estate, sometimes used lightly for a studio flat.
小心	xiǎo xīn	be careful; take care; cautious — 小 xiǎo (small) + 心 xīn (heart; mind). A small heart is one drawn in, watchful, attentive to fine detail. The literal image captures the bodily experience of caution: the heart pulling back, contracting, alert to what is delicate or dangerous.
小心眼	xiǎo xīn yǎn	petty-minded; quick to take offense — Small heart-aperture: a mind with a narrow gate, through which slights enter easily and never leave. The grudge-keeping cousin of 小气, aimed at temperament rather than wallet.
小意思	xiǎo yì si	a small token; "it's nothing"; the understated gift — The standard self-deprecating framing when presenting a gift. Used across all gift-giving contexts , bringing something to a dinner host, giving a business contact a present, sending something to a teacher.
小篆	xiǎo zhuàn	small seal script — the Qin standardized writing form — Lesser Seal Script , standardized by the Qin minister Li Si as part of the First Emperor's unification project. Characterized by even-weight strokes, systematic construction, and balanced proportions.
小组	xiǎo zǔ	small group
孝道	xiào dào	the way of filial piety
孝敬	xiào jìng	to honor with gifts/service
孝顺	xiào shùn	filial
孝心	xiào xīn	filial intention; filial love
效	xiào	effect; to imitate; efficacy
效仿	xiào fǎng	to imitate, to follow the example of
效率	xiào lǜ	efficiency
校长	xiào zhǎng	school principal; university president — 校 xiào (school) + 长 zhǎng (elder; head; chief). The head of a school at any level, from primary school principal to university president. The pattern [institution] + 长 = head of that institution is extraordinarily productive: 院长 (dean), 部长 (minister), 市长 (mayor), 省长 (governor), 班长 (class monitor).
笑话	xiào hua	a joke; to laugh at (someone) — 笑 xiào (laugh) + 话 huà (speech; words). Laugh-words: a joke, a funny story. As a verb it flips to "to laugh at, to make a fool of." 讲笑话 = to tell a joke; 闹笑话 = to make an embarrassing blunder that others laugh at.
笑脸	xiào liǎn	smiling face; a smile worn for others — 笑 (laugh, smile) + 脸 (face). A face shaped into a smile, especially the welcoming one you put on for guests or customers. The opposite expression is 板着脸 (bǎnzhe liǎn), to hold the face stiff and stern.
笑容	xiào róng	a smile; a smiling expression — 笑 xiào (to smile; to laugh) + 容 róng (facial expression). The smile as a look worn on the face, treated as a noun: something one has, shows, or loses. Where 笑 is the act of smiling, 笑容 is the expression itself, the way the face holds a smile.
笑死我了	xiào sǐ wǒ le	"I'm dying laughing"; hilarious
笑着说	xiào zhe shuō	said with a smile; said, smiling — The canonical attendant circumstance: 笑着 (smiling) + 说 (to say). The 着 links the two actions , the smiling frames and accompanies the speaking. This construction is the default way to describe facial expression or gesture accompanying speech in Chinese narrative.
歇脚	xiē jiǎo	to rest one's feet (during a journey); to take a break en route — 歇 xiē (to rest) + 脚. The classic verb for stopping during a long walk or trip , at a tea-house, an inn, a roadside pavilion. Carries the texture of pre-modern travel; still used for any pause on a long journey.
协议	xié yì	agreement
协助	xié zhù	to assist (cooperatively)
谐	xié	harmonious; to accord
谐音	xié yīn	Near-homophone, sound-alike , the phonetic mechanism underlying most Chinese number symbolism. 谐音 relationships exist wherever two syllables sound similar enough that one can evoke the other.
鞋	xié	shoes
写法	xiě fǎ	method of writing a character; stroke order
写实	xiě shí	realism; to depict reality faithfully
写下来	xiě xià lái	write it down — For technical terms and every figure. Hand over your notebook or phone. Pinyin input is fine; most people will type the characters for you.
写信	xiě xìn	to write a letter
写意	xiě yì	impressionist; expressive — capturing spirit over likeness
写照	xiě zhào	a portrayal; a true reflection of reality
写真	xiě zhēn	portrait photograph; lifelike depiction — 写 xiě (to write; to depict) + 真. To depict the real. Originally a classical term for a lifelike portrait painting; in modern usage, a posed portrait photo (especially of a model or celebrity). In Japanese, the same word 写真 shashin simply means "photograph."
写字	xiě zì	to write characters; to write by hand — 写 xiě (to write) + 字 zì (character). The fundamental literacy act: hand meeting surface, character taking form. Specifically implies handwriting rather than typing or printing. In Chinese civilization, handwriting is never merely functional.
写作	xiě zuò	to write; to compose — literary register
谢谢	xiè xie	thank you
心得	xīn dé	insight from experience; what one gained
心动	xīn dòng	heart moved; tempted
心房	xīn fáng	atrium of the heart
心即理	xīn jí lǐ	the mind is principle — Wang Yangming's direct challenge to Zhu Xi. Principle 理 is not located in external things requiring investigation; it is not separate from the moral mind. The mind is itself the site of 理.
心经	Xīn jīng	the Heart Sutra · 260 characters · Xuanzang's 649 CE translation — The shortest and most chanted Buddhist scripture in East Asia. The condensed essence of the Prajñāpāramitā literature: emptiness applied to every Buddhist category in turn, then the closing mantra. Memorized by every Chan novice; recited daily across China, Japan, Korea, and Vietnam.
心里	xīn lǐ	in one's heart; in one's mind; emotionally — 心 (heart, mind) + 里 (interior). Functions as a locative: something is in the heart, occurs in the heart. Covers both cognitive interior (I understand in my mind) and emotional interior (I feel in my heart). More intimate and less clinical than 内心 nèixīn (inner heart , a literary register).
心里话	xīn lǐ huà	sincere words; what one truly means — 心里 (inside the heart) + 话 (speech, words). The words that come from the interior rather than from performance. Sharing 心里话 is an act of trust and intimacy , it signals that the speaker is not managing their presentation but revealing what is actually held inside.
心里有数	xīn lǐ yǒu shù	to have a clear grasp; to know what's what — 心里 (inside the heart) + 有 (to have) + 数 (numbers, a count). To hold the numbers privately in one's interior , to have a clear reckoning of a situation that one carries internally.
心理	xīn lǐ	psychology; mental state; mindset — 心 xīn (heart) + 理 lǐ (logic; principle; grain of wood). "The logic of the heart." Used both as the general term for one's psychological state and as the root of 心理学 (psychology as a discipline).
心灵	xīn líng	soul; inner spirit
心情	xīn qíng	mood; state of mind — 心 xīn (heart) + 情 qíng (emotion; situation). The weather of the inner world. Where 情 alone is broad, covering emotion, circumstance, and romantic feeling, 心情 focuses it: the specific emotional atmosphere a person inhabits right now. It fluctuates by the hour.
心思	xīn sī	thoughts; mental energy; what the mind is occupied with — 心 xīn (heart) + 思 sī (to think; thought). What the heart is currently thinking about, or whether it has the mental space to think about something at all.
心无挂碍	xīn wú guà ài	a mind free of obstruction — From passage 7. 挂碍 guà ài means hindrance or obstruction in the literal sense of something snagging or hooking. 心无挂碍 describes the mind that, having seen emptiness, has nothing left to snag on.
心学	xīn xu é	School of Mind; Wang Yangming's Neo-Confucian tradition — The Neo-Confucian tradition associated with Wang Yangming. Holds that 理 is not separate from the mind; the moral faculty 良知 is the ground of both knowledge and action.
心眼	xīn yǎn ( r )	wits; smarts; (also) intent, motive of the heart — 心 xīn (heart) + 眼. The eye of the heart , the inner faculty of judgment and quick-wittedness. 心眼多 xīnyǎn duō "to have many wits" (clever, calculating, or scheming, depending on tone). 心眼好 xīnyǎn hǎo "to have a good heart-eye" (kind-hearted, well-intentioned).
心意	xīn yì	intention; goodwill; the feeling behind a gesture — 心 xīn (heart) + 意 yì (intention; meaning). The feeling or thought behind an action: the sincerity and warmth that a gift, gesture, or visit carries. Often used when downplaying the material value of what you have brought: 这只是一点心意 ("this is just a small token of my regard").
心脏	xīn zàng	the heart (anatomical organ) — 心 xīn + 脏 zàng (internal organ, the biological sense; same character used in 器官, organs of the body). In modern Chinese, 心 alone almost never refers to the biological organ; that role belongs entirely to 心脏. The standalone 心 is always the conceptual, emotional, and cognitive heart.
辛	xīn	pungent; laborious
辛亥革命	Xīn hài Gé mìng	the 1911 Revolution that ended the Qing
辛苦	xīn kǔ	hard, toilsome; thanks for your trouble — 辛 (xīn), pungent or laborious, pairs with 苦 (kǔ), bitter, to name effort that wears you down. As a set phrase it doubles as warm thanks: telling someone 辛苦了 acknowledges the toil they just put in for you.
辛苦了	xīn kǔ le	thank you for your trouble — Acknowledges effort someone made for you: picking you up at the station, preparing samples, staying late. Factory staff hear it constantly from managers and appreciate it from a buyer. Answer with 不辛苦 or 应该的 (yīnggāi de, "it's what I should do").
欣赏	xīn shǎng	to appreciate; to enjoy
新华字典	Xīn huá Zì diǎn	the Xinhua Dictionary — 新华 Xīnhuá (New China, the name of the official news agency and many state institutions) + 字典. The most widely-sold book in Chinese history and the standard reference for mainland Chinese students since its first edition in 1953. Simple, portable, and comprehensive for modern literacy needs.
新婚	xīn hūn	newly married; newlywed — 新 (new) + 婚 (hūn, marriage). Newly married , in the fresh state of the new relationship. 新婚快乐 (congratulations on the marriage , Happy Newlywed); 新婚夫妇 (newlywed couple); 新郎 xīnláng (groom , new-man) and 新娘 xīnniáng (bride , new-woman) are the standard terms for a wedding couple.
新疆	Xīn jiāng	Xinjiang
新旧交替	xīn jiù jiāo tì	the new replacing the old; transition between old and new — 新 (new) + 旧 (old) + 交替 (jiāotì, to alternate; to replace one another in succession). The pairing of 新 and 旧 as a compound unit , 新旧 , appears constantly in Chinese: 新旧对比 (contrast between old and new), 新旧交融 (old and new blending together), 新旧矛盾 (conflict between old and new).
新年	xīn nián	New Year — 新 (new) + 年 (year). The new year , the most basic New Year compound, usable for both the Gregorian New Year (January 1) and the Chinese Lunar New Year. 新年快乐 (Happy New Year) and 新年好 (New Year greetings) are the standard phrases.
新年快乐	xīn nián kuài lè	Happy New Year , literally "new year happy — Happy New Year , literally "new year happy." The standard greeting for the solar New Year (January 1). For Lunar New Year, 新春快乐 (xīnchūn kuàilè) or 恭喜发财 (gōngxǐ fācái, "congratulations and may you prosper") are more appropriate.
新儒家	xīn rú jiā	New Confucianism
新文化运动	Xīn Wén huà Yùn dòng	New Culture Movement
新闻	xīn wén	news
新鲜	xīn xiān	fresh; novel; new and interesting — 新 (new) + 鲜 (xiān, fresh , especially of food; vivid; rare). Fresh in the sensory sense: 新鲜蔬菜 (fresh vegetables), 新鲜空气 (fresh air). Also used for novel experiences: 对我来说这是个新鲜事物 (this is something new to me). 鲜 alone means fresh-vivid; 新鲜 combines freshness with newness.
信封	xìn fēng	envelope — 信 (xìn, letter) plus 封 (the sealing/wrapper): the paper sleeve that closes around a letter. The most common everyday word built on 封.
信号	xìn hào	signal; indicator — 信 xìn (trustworthy communication; word) + 号 hào (sign; designation; mark; sound used as a signal). A signal is a word that has been stripped of language and reduced to a reliable mark: it carries only its presence or absence, its strength or weakness, rather than semantic content.
信件	xìn jiàn	letters; correspondence (as a category) — 信 xìn (letter; trust) + 件 jiàn (a piece). A collective, somewhat formal term for letters and written correspondence. A single letter is 一封信 (using 封 fēng); 信件 names the whole category of correspondence, the kind of word a post office or office form would use.
信任	xìn rèn	trust; to trust; to have confidence in — 信 xìn (trustworthiness; to trust) + 任 rèn (to appoint; to entrust; to allow , 任 carries the sense of delegating responsibility to someone). To trust is to appoint a person as worthy of reliance. 信任危机 xìnrèn wéijī = a crisis of trust (institutional or personal). 建立信任 jiànlì xìnrèn = to build trust.
信息	xìn xī	information; news; data — 信 xìn (trustworthy word; communication) + 息 xī (breath; rest; news , 息 contains 自 zì, nose, over 心 xīn, heart: breath as the sign of life; by extension, news and tidings). A word that travels and carries meaning , information as a form of 信. 信息时代 xìnxī shídài = the information age.
信用	xìn yòng	creditworthiness; credit; trustworthiness in financial conduct — 信 xìn (trustworthiness) + 用 yòng (use; to use; practical application). Trustworthiness made actionable , the record of a person or institution reliably standing by its commitments. 信用卡 xìnyòng kǎ = credit card. 信用评级 xìnyòng píngjí = credit rating.
信愿行	xìn yuàn xíng	faith, vow, practice (three essentials)
星期	xīng qī	week; day of the week — 星 (star) + 期 (period): the star period, from the seven-luminary week. Days attach numbers: 星期三 is Wednesday. Asking the day is 今天星期几? , one of the few questions where 几 needs no measure word.
星宿	xīng xiù	the lunar mansions
星星	xīng xing	Star, in the affectionate reduplicated form used in speech and songs. Single 星 does the compounding work: 星空 (starry sky), 星系 (galaxy, "star system"), 星座 (constellation, and the zodiac sign small talk runs on).
星座	xīng zuò	constellation; zodiac sign — 星 + 座 (seat): the seats of the stars. In young urban small talk it means the Western zodiac sign: 你是什么星座的? has largely displaced asking one's Chinese zodiac animal among the under-forty, an ironic import given the antiquity of native Chinese astral lore.
行当	háng dang	role category; performance line
行动	xíng dòng	to act; action; movement — 行 xíng + 动 dòng (to move). The general word for action , both as a verb (to take action) and as a noun (an action, an operation). Used in everything from personal life (我们应该立刻行动 "we should act at once") to police and military operations (军事行动 "military operation").
行情	háng qíng	market quotation; the going rate; market conditions — 行 háng + 情 qíng (situation; circumstances). The state of the trade. The going prices, the market mood, the supply-and-demand conditions in a particular industry. 股市行情 = stock market quotes; 看行情 = "to watch the market"; 行情看涨 = "the market is bullish."
行善	xíng shàn	to do good deeds; to practice virtue — 行 xíng (to practice; to carry out) + 善 shàn (goodness). Goodness made active , not merely possessed as a quality but enacted as conduct. Appears equally in Confucian moral discourse and Buddhist merit accumulation.
行书	xíng shū	running script
行为	xíng wéi	behavior; conduct (formal/clinical noun) — 行 xíng + 为 wéi (to do; to act). What one does, framed as observable behavior. The clinical/formal noun used in psychology, law, and ethics , 行为艺术 "performance art," 犯罪行为 "criminal behavior," 不文明行为 "uncivilized behavior."
行业	háng yè	industry; trade; profession (as a category) — 行 háng + 业 yè (occupation; enterprise). The trade as a category. Used for entire industries: 教育行业 the education sector; 服务行业 the service industry; 各行各业 "every trade, every profession" (the standard phrase for "all walks of life").
行云	xíng yún	drifting clouds
行住坐卧	xíng zhù zuò wò	walking, standing, sitting, lying down — enlightenment in every posture — The four bodily postures covering all of waking life. Chan's radical claim: enlightenment is not a special state achieved in meditation and then lost in ordinary life , it is the natural condition of mind, available in every action.
行走江湖	xíng zǒu jiāng hú	to travel the martial world
形成	xíng chéng	to form; to take shape; to come into being — 形 xíng + 成 chéng (to become; to complete). To shape-into-being. The all-purpose verb for something forming through a process: 形成习惯 form a habit; 形成共识 reach (form) a consensus; 逐渐形成 gradually take shape. Used constantly in writing about how systems, opinions, and conditions arise.
形容	xíng róng	to describe; to characterize in words — 形 xíng + 容 róng (appearance; countenance). To give a thing its look in words. The verb for describing, especially feelings and impressions that resist plain statement. 难以形容 = hard to describe; 无法形容的美 = indescribable beauty.
形式	xíng shì	form; format: the outward shape, as opposed to content — 形 xíng + 式 shì (pattern; format). The form or format of something, set against its 内容 nèiróng (content). 各种形式 = various forms; 形式上 = in form only, nominally. 形式主义 = formalism, prizing appearance over substance. Do not confuse with 形势 (the situation): see the 辨析 section above.
形势	xíng shì	the situation; the lay of forces; circumstances — 形 xíng + 势 shì (momentum; force). The configuration of forces at a given moment, weightier than 情形. Used for politics, war, economy, and any contest of momentum: 国际形势 the international situation; 形势严峻 the situation is grim; 看清形势 read the situation clearly. Do not confuse with 形式 (form vs. content).
形体	xíng tǐ	physical form; body; bodily figure — 形 xíng + 体 tǐ (body). The body as form: the physical figure of a person, often in contexts of posture, dance, and bearing. 形体训练 = physical/figure training; 形体优美 = a graceful figure. This is the bodily branch of 形, where shape means the shape of a living body.
形象	xíng xiàng	image; figure; vivid
形制	xíng zhì	garment structure; the form category of a historical garment
形状	xíng zhuàng	shape: the outward form of a thing — 形 xíng + 状 zhuàng (state; appearance). The everyday word for the shape of a physical object: what outline it has, what it looks like. 奇怪的形状 = a strange shape; 各种形状 = all kinds of shapes. This is the first 形-word a learner meets.
兴	xìng	Evocative image — Evocative image: the poetic mode in which a natural scene opens a poem and resonates emotionally with the human subject to follow without being a direct comparison. The most distinctively Chinese of the three classical poetic modes.
兴趣	xìng qù	interest, hobby — 兴 (aroused spirit, the xìng reading) + 趣 (inclination, delight). 对…感兴趣 ("to feel interest toward") is the essential pattern: 我对中国历史很感兴趣. 兴趣班 are the after-school enrichment classes filling Chinese childhood weekends.
杏仁	xìng rén	apricot kernel; almond — 杏 xìng (apricot) + 仁 (kernel). The seed inside the apricot pit , the everyday Chinese word also used for almonds in modern usage (e.g. 杏仁奶 "almond milk"). In Chinese medicine, 苦杏仁 is a classical herb used to "moisten the lung and stop coughing."
姓	xìng	surname; clan name
姓名	xìng míng	full name — surname + given name — 姓 xìng (surname) + 名 míng (given name). The formal term for a full name. Note: Chinese names are surname-first, given-name-second, the reverse of Western convention. 你叫什么名字？= "What is your name?" (informal, just the name). 请问您的姓名？= "May I ask your full name?" (formal).
幸福	xìng fú	happiness; well-being; blessed fortune — 幸 xìng (fortunate; lucky; to survive) + 福 fú (blessing). The compound for subjective happiness and well-being , what you feel when life is going right. 幸福感 = sense of well-being. 幸福生活 = a happy life. One of the most searched Chinese words globally.
幸会	xìng huì	a pleasure to meet you — Formal "fortunate meeting," said with the handshake on first contact. Pairs naturally with 久仰 (jiǔyǎng, "I've long admired you") when you know the person by reputation. Plain 很高兴认识您 is never wrong.
性别	xìng bié	sex; gender — 性 xìng (sex) + 别 bié (distinction, category). The category of male or female: 性别平等 "gender equality," the 性别 field on an application form. The standard, neutral word in both everyday and formal contexts for the sex or gender of a person.
性恶	xìng è	human nature is bad — innately insufficient for morality without cultivation — Xunzi's foundational claim: 人之性恶，其善者伪也, "Human nature is bad; what is good in humans is their deliberately cultivated achievement." 性 xìng: what one is born with. 恶 è: bad, harmful, insufficient. 伪 wèi: artificial, deliberately made (not fake). Goodness is real but must be built, not grown.
性格	xìng gé	character; personality; disposition — 性 xìng (nature) + 格 gé (standard, frame). The settled set of traits that make up who a person is: 性格开朗 "an open, cheerful character," 性格内向 "an introverted personality." The standard word for someone's overall character, often the answer to "what kind of person are they."
性能	xìng néng	performance; capabilities; properties (of a machine) — 性 xìng (property) + 能 néng (capability). The functional properties of a machine, device, or material: 性能稳定 "stable performance," 高性能 "high-performance." How well a thing does what it is built to do, the practical measure of its inherent quality.
性情	xìng qíng	temperament; disposition; nature and feeling — 性 xìng (nature) + 情 qíng (feeling). Temperament considered through the lens of mood and emotional reaction: 性情温和 "of a gentle temperament," 性情大变 "a sharp change in temperament." Warmer and more feeling-laden than the neutral 性格, closer to the heart's weather than its frame.
性质	xìng zhì	nature; character of a thing
兄	xiōng	elder brother (literary)
兄弟	xiōng dì	elder and younger sibling — fraternal respect — 悌 tì , fraternal respect , is the virtue governing sibling relations. The elder brother owes kindness and example; the younger owes deference and respect.
匈奴	Xiōng nú	the Xiongnu; northern nomadic confederation
胸有乾坤	xiōng yǒu qián kūn	"heaven and earth in the breast" — a person of sweeping strategic vision and depth — 胸 xiōng (chest; breast; the seat of mind and intention in classical Chinese thought) + 有 (to have; to hold) + 乾坤. A grand classical expression of praise for someone who holds the entire world in mind: a strategist, a statesman, a general, a writer who sees at the largest possible scale.
熊猫	xióng māo	giant panda
修改	xiū gǎi	to revise, to amend, to edit — 修 (mend, trim) + 改 (alter). To go back over a text, plan, or law and revise it. The standard word for editing a document or amending a draft.
修炼	xiū liàn	to cultivate; to train (spiritual connotation)
修身	xiū shēn	self-cultivation (Confucian)
修行	xiū xíng	to cultivate; spiritual practice
修养	xiū yǎng	cultivation; self-cultivation
修长城	xiū cháng chéng	to build/repair the Great Wall; (slang) to play mahjong — 修 (xiū) means to build, repair, or construct. 修长城 in its literal sense describes the labour of wall-building. In colloquial slang, 修长城 is a playful way to say "playing mahjong": the rows of mahjong tiles arranged on the table look like sections of wall.
羞耻心	xiū chǐ xīn	sense of shame
羞恶之心	xiū wù zhī xīn	the heart of shame and dislike — sprout of 义 righteousness — 羞 xiū: shame (about one's own moral failures). 恶 wù: dislike, aversion (to moral failure in others). Together: the capacity to feel shame about one's own wrong actions and moral distaste for wrongdoing in general.
秀才	xiù cai	the lowest examination degree; a local scholar — The degree produced by the local examination (童试), the entry point of the examination ladder. A 秀才 had cleared the first hurdle and earned a recognized scholarly identity, but stood far from power.
虚	xū	empty; void
虚实	xū shí	deficient and substantial
虚伪	xū wěi	hypocritical; fake
虚心	xū xīn	open-minded; receptive; humble in one's willingness to learn — 虚 xū (empty; hollow; receptive , the vessel with space in it) + 心 xīn (heart; the seat of thought and feeling in Chinese understanding). An empty heart , a mind that has not pre-filled itself with conclusions and so can genuinely receive what it encounters.
虚心学习	xū xīn xu é xí	to study with an open mind; to learn receptively — A fixed phrase in educational and developmental discourse. The combination of 虚心 (open-minded) + 学习 (to study; to learn) makes the stance toward learning explicit: approach the subject without assuming you already know, without defending existing positions, with the mind empty enough to receive new…
需求	xū qiú	demand; need
需要	xū yào	to need; to require (necessity, not merely desire) — 需 xū (to need; to require, a more formal or objective necessity) + 要. 需要 focuses on objective need rather than personal desire. 我需要帮助 (I need help , genuinely require it) vs. 我要帮助 (I want help , requesting it).
许可	xǔ kě	permission (formal noun)
续	xù	to continue; to extend
宣传	xuān chuán	to publicize; to promote; publicity; propaganda — 宣 xuān (to proclaim) + 传 chuán (to spread). To put out a message deliberately so it reaches the public: 宣传海报 "a promotional poster," 宣传活动 "a publicity campaign." Carries a sense of organized, purposeful spreading, and in political contexts the meaning of propaganda.
宣读	xuān dú	to read out formally (a statement, verdict, decree) — 宣 xuān (to announce; to proclaim) + 读. To read aloud in a formal setting , a court verdict, a wedding vow, an official document at a ceremony. The 宣 marks the public, declarative force.
宣礼塔	xuān lǐ tǎ	minaret
宣纸	xuān zhǐ	Xuan paper, made in Jingxian, Anhui province — Xuan paper, made in Jingxian, Anhui province. The standard for Chinese calligraphy and painting. Made from wingceltis bark and rice straw. Comes in raw (生宣, highly absorbent) and sized (熟宣, controlled absorbency) varieties. Archival: properly stored examples survive over a thousand years.
玄奘	Xuán zàng	the monk who walked to India — Tang Buddhism's greatest figure — The monk 玄奘 Xuánzàng (602–664 CE) traveled overland to India in 629 CE , without imperial authorization, defying a travel ban , spent sixteen years there studying at Nalanda and other Buddhist centers, and returned to Chang'an in 645 CE carrying 657 Sanskrit texts.
悬河	xuán hé	Suspended river , a river whose bed has been raised by sediment deposition above the level of the surrounding plain, contained only by embankments. The lower Yellow River is the world's most extreme example, running up to 10 meters above the adjacent farmland.
悬崖	xuán yá	cliff; precipice
旋律	xuán lǜ	melody
选	xuǎn	to choose; to elect; to select
选票	xuǎn piào	ballot: a ballot paper; a vote (as a counted unit) — 选 xuǎn (to choose; to elect) + 票 piào (slip; ballot). The physical ballot paper you mark and cast, and by extension a vote as a unit to be counted. The tally of votes is 票数 piàoshù, and to win is to gather the most 选票. Here 票 is the slip that carries a single person's choice.
选择	xuǎn zé	to choose; choice
穴位	xu é wèi	acupoint — Acupoint , a specific location on a channel where qi gathers close to the surface and is most accessible to needling or moxa stimulation. Also called 腧穴 shùxué in classical texts. The body has 361 standard points on the 14 main channels.
学坏	xu é huài	to pick up bad habits, to be led astray — 学 (to learn) + 坏 (bad). To learn the wrong things, the classic parental worry that good company makes good children and bad company corrupts them. 坏 here works as a result complement: the learning turns out badly.
学会	xu é huì	to master, to learn (successfully) — Study + acquire-the-skill. 学了三年 states duration; 学会了 states victory. The complement 会 restricts itself to learnable skills, which is a nice grammatical definition of what a skill is.
学了三年	xu é le sān nián	studied for three years (bounded period) — 了 with a duration complement signals an action sustained for a period of time and treated as a completed, bounded episode. This construction is essential for talking about study, work, residence, and sustained experience. The duration frames the action as a finished chunk of time.
学生	xu é sheng	student — 学 xué (to study; to learn) + 生 shēng (one who is still "green"; a junior person). The student as someone still growing, not yet ripe. One of the most common nouns in Chinese , every school context relies on it.
学识	xu é shi	scholarly learning; erudition
学问	xu é wèn	learning; knowledge; scholarship — 学 xué (to study) + 问 wèn (to ask). Knowledge built by studying and asking, the deep, earned kind. 很有学问 "highly learned." The word itself teaches that real knowledge grows from questions, not answers alone.
学习	xu é xí	to study; to learn (ongoing process) — 学 xué (to learn) + 习 xí (to practice; to review; to get accustomed to). The compound captures the Confucian dual process: 学 (receiving new knowledge) + 习 (practicing and consolidating it). Not just acquisition but repeated reinforcement. The most common word for "to study" in general.
学校	xu é xiào	school
学业	xu é yè	studies; schoolwork; academic work — 学 xué (to learn) + 业 yè (course of work). One's studies taken as a whole: 完成学业 "to complete one's studies," 学业成绩 "academic results." The general term for the body of work a student carries.
学院	xu é yuàn	college, institute
学者	xu é zhě	scholar; academic; researcher — 学 (to study) + 者. The standard modern word for a scholar or academic researcher. This compound has been in continuous use since at least the Han dynasty. A 学者 is someone whose work consists of systematic study , the word appears in book reviews, conference programs, and academic citations alike.
雪白	xu ě bái	snow-white; pure, dazzling white — 雪 xuě (snow) + 白 bái (white). The brightest, purest white, with snow as the standard of comparison, exactly parallel to English "snow-white." This is a productive pattern in Chinese: a vivid object plus a color word yields an intensified shade.
雪耻	xu ě chǐ	to wipe out a humiliation
雪花	xu ě huā	snowflake — 雪 xuě (snow) + 花 huā (flower). A snowflake, the snow seen as a falling blossom. The flower metaphor is old and pervasive in Chinese: snow falling is 雪花飘 (snow-flowers drifting), and the six-petaled structure of a real snow crystal was noticed early, with classical texts remarking that snow flowers h…
雪人	xu ěr én	snowman
雪域	xu ě yù	Snow Land — Snow Land: a poetic name for Tibet, used in both Chinese and Tibetan contexts. Refers to the snowcapped mountains and high plateau character of the Tibetan landscape and appears in poetry, song, and political rhetoric from multiple perspectives.
血	xu è	blood
血脉	xu è mài	bloodline
旬	xún	ten-day period
询问	xún wèn	to inquire; to ask about; to seek information — 询 xún (to inquire) + 问 wèn (to ask). A formal verb for asking in order to gather information, common in writing and service contexts: 询问情况 "to inquire about the situation," 向某人询问 "to make inquiries of someone."
荀子	Xún zǐ	Xunzi
训练	xùn liàn	to train; training — 训 (to instruct, to discipline) + 练 (to drill). Organized, directed training: athletes, soldiers, dogs, new staff. Heavier and more institutional than 练习.
迅速	xùn sù	swift, rapid
驯鹿	xùn lù	reindeer
压力	yā lì	pressure; stress (physical or mental) — 压 yā (to press down) + 力 lì (force). The force pressing from outside. In physics it is pressure on a surface; in daily life it is the weight of stress: 工作压力 "work stress," 压力很大 "under a lot of pressure."
压岁钱	yā suì qián	lucky money for children at New Year — New Year's money, given in red envelopes; literally "money that suppresses the year-beast." The year-beast (岁 suì) was a mythological monster; the money and noise were protective against it.
押解	yā jiè	to escort (a prisoner) under guard — 押 yā (to detain; to hold) + 解 jiè. The clearest home of the second reading jiè. To escort a prisoner or valuables under guard from one place to another: 押解犯人 = escort a prisoner; the related 解送 jièsòng = send under escort.
鸦片	yā piàn	opium
鸦片战争	Yā piàn Zhàn zhēng	the Opium Wars (1839–42, 1856–60)
鸭	yā	duck
鸭蛋	yā dàn	duck egg; a zero (slang) — 鸭 (yā, duck) + 蛋: a duck egg, larger and richer than a hen's. Among students it means a score of zero, since the round 0 on a test paper looks like an egg sitting there.
崖山之战	Yá shān zhī Zhàn	Battle of Yamen, final Song defeat
揠苗助长	yà miáo zhù zhǎng	pulling up seedlings to help them grow
烟	yān	smoke; cigarette
烟火	yān huǒ	fireworks; smoke and fire; the warmth of lived life — 烟 yān (smoke) + 火 huǒ (fire). Literally: fireworks. Poetically: 人间烟火 rénjiān yānhuǒ , "the smoke and fire of the human world" , the warmth and vitality of ordinary life.
焉	yān	classical final particle
严肃	yán sù	solemn, grave
严重	yán zhòng	serious — Say plainly how serious it is; understatement is heard as "not urgent." 影响很大 (the impact is large) and 不能接受 (bù néng jiēshòu, cannot be accepted) are firm without being rude.
言语	yán yǔ	speech; the words someone uses — 言 yán (speech; words) + 语 yǔ (language; speech). The near-synonym of 语言, but with the elements reversed and the emphasis shifted: where 语言 is language as system, 言语 is the specific words someone uses , their speech acts, their chosen phrasing, what came out of their mouth in a particular moment.
岩韵	yán yùn	rock rhyme — the mineral terroir of Wuyi
炎热	yán rè	blazing hot (summer)
研究方向	yán jiū fāng xiàng	research direction; field of specialization — The standard academic term for one's research focus. Appears on every Chinese CV, every graduate-school admission form, and every department web page. 你的研究方向是什么? — "What's your research direction?" — is the second question Chinese academics ask each other after the name.
研究所	yán jiū suǒ	research institute; graduate research division
阎王	Yán wáng	King Yama — judge of the underworld — The ruler of the underworld and its ten courts of judgment, borrowed from Buddhist cosmology and fully integrated into Chinese folk religion by the Tang dynasty. Every soul that died passed through Yama's courts for a comprehensive moral accounting.
颜色	yán sè	color (general term) — 颜 yán (color; face , the face radical) + 色 sè. The standard compound for "color." 什么颜色？"What color?" Also used figuratively: 给你点颜色看看 gěi nǐ diǎn yánsè kànkan = "I'll show you what I'm capable of" (lit. "let you see some color" , a threat or challenge to someone who has been underestimating you).
颜色象征	yán sè xiàng zhēng	Color Symbolism — 红 hóng (red) , the color of luck, celebration, life force. Worn at weddings, hung at new year, placed in red envelopes (红包 hóngbāo). 白 bái (white) , the color of mourning; worn at funerals, avoided at celebrations.
颜真卿	Yán Zhēn qīng	Yan Zhenqing — Tang 楷书 master
眼光	yǎng uāng	vision; taste; judgment; the discerning eye — 眼 yǎn + 光 guāng (light). Literally eye-light. The kind of seeing a person has , their taste in art, their judgment in business, their ability to spot a good investment or a good partner. 有眼光 yǒu yǎnguāng "to have a good eye"; 没眼光 "to have poor judgment"; 长远的眼光 "long-term vision."
眼花	yǎn huā	to have blurred vision; to be dazzled — 眼 yǎn (eye) + 花 huā (blurred). Eyes swimming or dazzled, unable to focus, from too much to look at or from dizziness: 看得我眼花 "it left my eyes swimming." 头晕眼花 is "dizzy and bleary."
眼界	yǎn jiè	horizon (of experience); breadth of one's outlook — 眼 yǎn + 界 jiè (boundary; realm). The boundary of what one's eye has taken in , how widely one has seen, traveled, read, lived. 开阔眼界 kāikuò yǎnjiè "to broaden one's horizons" is one of the standard reasons given in Chinese for travel, study abroad, and reading widely.
眼睛	yǎn jing	eye(s); the eyes as organ — 眼 yǎn + 睛 jīng (eyeball; pupil). The standard everyday word for "eyes" in spoken Chinese. Single 眼 alone almost never serves this role; for "her eyes are beautiful" you say 她的眼睛很美 not 她的眼很美. The 睛 is light and unstressed in pronunciation.
眼泪	yǎn lèi	tears — 眼 yǎn + 泪 lèi (tear). Standard word for tears. 流眼泪 liú yǎnlèi "to shed tears"; 擦眼泪 cā yǎnlèi "to wipe away tears." The classical/literary equivalent is just 泪 alone or 泪水 lèishuǐ.
眼力	yǎn lì	perceptiveness; discerning power; visual acuity — 眼 yǎn + 力 lì (strength). The strength of one's seeing , both literally (sharpness of vision) and figuratively (judgment, ability to spot quality or fakery). The skilled antique-dealer is praised for 眼力 in detecting forgeries.
眼皮	yǎn pí	eyelid — 眼 yǎn + 皮 pí (skin). Eyelid. The colloquial idiom 眼皮底下 yǎnpí dǐxià "right under one's eyelids" means "right in front of one, in plain sight" , usually used to describe something missed or stolen despite being right there.
眼神	yǎn shén	the look in one's eyes; gaze; expression — 眼 yǎn + 神 shén (spirit; expression). Not the eyes themselves but the spirit visible in them , the look one gives, the expression of feeling that the eyes carry. A high-frequency word in fiction and dialogue: 他的眼神很温柔 "the look in his eyes was very gentle"; 用眼神告诉我 "tell me with your eyes."
演	yǎn	to act; to perform; to develop
演唱	yǎn chàng	to perform (a song); vocal performance — 演 yǎn (to perform) + 唱 chàng (to sing). To sing before an audience as a performance. 演唱会 yǎnchànghuì is a concert, the standard word for a pop or vocal live show.
验货	yàn huò	goods inspection — Inspection before shipment, by your own staff or a 第三方 (dì-sān fāng, third party). 抽检 (chōujiǎn) is sampling inspection, usually by AQL level; 全检 (quánjiǎn) is 100% inspection. 来料检验 checks incoming materials.
羊	yáng	sheep, goat
羊肉	yáng ròu	mutton, lamb — 羊 (yáng, sheep/goat) + 肉 (meat). The meat of choice in the northwest, central to hotpot and skewers.
阳光	yáng guāng	sunlight; sunshine; (of a person) sunny, positive, and open — 阳 yáng (yang; sun) + 光 guāng (light; radiance). Literal sunlight, and by extension a personality quality: someone who is 阳光 is warm, open, optimistic , they bring light into the room. The metaphor is exact: they embody yang qualities in their social presence.
阳历	yáng lì	the solar (Gregorian) calendar — 阳 yáng (the sun; the yang principle) + 历 lì (calendar). The sun-based calendar, in modern usage the Gregorian calendar used for official and international dates. Also called 公历 gōnglì (the "common," that is internationally standard, calendar).
阳明洞	Yáng míng dòng	Yangming Cave; the retreat for which he was named
阳气	yáng qì	yang energy: warm, active vital force — 阳 yáng (active; bright) + 气 qì (vital energy). The warm, rising, animating energy of the body in traditional medicine, set against 阴气 yīnqì. A person low on 阳气 is thought to feel cold and sluggish.
阳台	yáng tái	balcony; terrace — 阳 yáng (sun) + 台 tái (platform; stage). The sun-platform: the balcony or open terrace of a home, valued precisely because it catches the light and is where laundry is hung to dry.
阳性	yáng xìng	positive (medical test); masculine (grammatical gender) — 阳 yáng (the active; the positive pole) + 性 xìng (nature; gender). In medicine, a positive test result; in grammar, the masculine gender. Its pair is 阴性 yīnxìng (negative; feminine).
阳虚	yáng xū	yang deficiency — the yang warmth is insufficient; cold dominates — Classic 阳虚 symptoms: aversion to cold (怕冷 pà lěng), cold hands and feet, pale complexion, fatigue and low energy, frequent clear urination, low libido, loose stools.
阳宅	yáng zhái	yang dwelling = home for the living
洋	yáng	open ocean
洋务运动	Yáng wù Yùn dòng	Self-Strengthening Movement
养老	yǎng lǎo	elder care
养生	yǎng shēng	health cultivation; nourishing life
氧气	yǎng qì	Oxygen (as breathable gas) — Oxygen (as breathable gas): the nourishing 氧 plus 气. 缺氧 (quēyǎng) is oxygen deprivation, used literally at altitude and figuratively for stuffy rooms and dull meetings.
样板戏	yàng bǎn xì	model operas (sole permitted art form)
样品	yàng pǐn	sample; specimen (of a product) — 样 yàng (sample) + 品 pǐn (article, product). One specimen held up to represent a whole batch: a free sample, a trade-show display, a test piece sent to a buyer.
样品间	yàng pǐn jiān	sample room
样子	yàng zi	appearance; look; manner; the way it seems — 样 yàng (appearance) + 子 zi (noun-suffix). The look or manner of a person or thing. The idiom 看样子 kàn yàngzi means "from the looks of it." For the toneless suffix 子, see the 子 entry.
谣言	yáo yán	rumor; false report
摇头	yáo tóu	to shake the head
遥远	yáo yuǎn	remote; very far away; vast distance — 遥 yáo (far off; distant; also a walk-movement compound) + 远 yuǎn (far). Both components carry the meaning of distance, making 遥远 the intensified form: not just far, but vertiginously far. Used for cosmic distances, ancient history, or inaccessible places. Primarily literary and written.
药	yào	Medicine — Medicine. 吃药 is taking it, 开药 is prescribing it, 中药 is the herbal kind, and 药店 the pharmacy. The grass radical 艹 on top remembers that medicine began as herbs.
药食同源	yào shí tóng yuán	food and medicine share an origin
要 · 打算 · 准备	yào dǎ suàn zhǔn bèi	intention and plan: what someone is going to do — 要 before a verb marks intention or imminence: 我要回家 (I'm going home / I want to go home; context decides). 打算 dǎsuàn (to plan) and 准备 zhǔnbèi (to prepare, to be about to) make the plan explicit: 我打算明年去中国 (I plan to go to China next year).
要紧	yào jǐn	important; serious; mattering — 要 (yào, to want, to need) + 紧 (tight). What is held tight is what matters. Most common in the negative 不要紧 (bùyàojǐn), meaning never mind, it doesn't matter, often said to reassure.
要紧关头	yào jǐn guān tóu	critical moment; crucial juncture — 要紧 (crucial) + 关头 (guāntóu, the pivotal point). The hinge of a situation, where the outcome is decided and there is no room to hesitate.
要是	yào shi	if; supposing (colloquial conditional) — 要 (want/will) + 是 shì (be/this). A colloquial conditional meaning "if; supposing that." Slightly warmer and more personally invested than the neutral 如果 rúguǒ. Implies the speaker has some stake in the condition. Typically paired with 就 jiù in the consequence clause.
钥匙	yào shi	key
爷爷	yé ye	grandpa (colloquial)
也 , 追加	yě , zhuī jiā	也: one more added to an existing set — 也 always implies that the predicate has already been established for another entity. The sentence using 也 adds one more item. This requires a prior context (explicit or implicit) in which the predicate was already true of someone or something else. 也 cannot start a discourse out of nowhere.
也好	yě hǎo	that works too; either way is fine
也就是说	yě jiù shì shuō	"In other words" or "what I mean is — "In other words" or "what I mean is." The restatement marker. Used to reframe something you just said in clearer or simpler terms. Indispensable when you sense the listener has not quite caught your point.
也是	yě shì	that is also true; that's fair
也许	yě xǔ	maybe; perhaps
也许吧	yě xǔ ba	"Maybe so — "Maybe so." Noncommittal acknowledgment that signals you are not convinced but are not interested in arguing the point. The 吧 keeps it soft. Functionally close to English "I guess" or "could be."
野蛮	yě mán	barbaric; uncivilized
野台戏	yě tái xì	outdoor stage performances — theatre for an audience of spirits — 野 yě (outdoor; wild) + 台 tái (stage) + 戏 xì (theatre; opera). Temporary wooden stages erected in public spaces for Ghost Month performances , 歌仔戏 (Taiwanese opera), puppet theatre (布袋戏 bùdài xì), and traditional opera forms.
叶	yè	leaf
叶子	yè zi	leaf
夜	yè	night
一把汗	yī bǎ hàn	a palm's worth of sweat — anxious, breaking out in a sweat — 把 can measure abstract quantities that could theoretically fill a cupped hand , a handful of something. 一把汗 (a palm of sweat) is an idiom for being anxious or nervous to the point of sweating. 一把泪 (a handful of tears) appears in literary contexts for weeping.
一把年纪	yī bǎ nián jì	getting on in age; a certain age (often with mild reproach) — 一把年纪 literally "a handful of years" , used to describe someone who is getting on in age. Often carries a mildly reproachful or surprised tone: someone that age should know better, or it's surprising they're doing something. Common in colloquial speech and semi-formal contexts.
一把椅子	yī bǎ yǐ zi	a chair (one handle-shaped piece of furniture) — 把 as a measure word applies to objects that have a handle or can be grasped, or that you can take in hand , chairs (by the back), tools, weapons, fans, keys, locks, umbrellas, guitars. The connection to the core meaning of 把 (to grip) is transparent.
一般	yī bān	general; average; so-so
一杯水	yī bēi shuǐ	a cup / glass of water — 杯 bēi — 杯 bēi (cup, glass) counts liquids held in cups or glasses. The container becomes the classifier. This is the most transparent type: the classifier is the vessel. A large class of classifiers work this way , 碗, 盘, 瓶, 桶, 壶 , each named for the physical container holding the substance.
一笔钱	yī bǐ qián	a sum of money — 一 yī (one) + 笔 bǐ (measure word) + 钱 qián (money). A definite, lump amount of money, the kind written down as a single entry: 一大笔钱 "a large sum," 这笔钱 "this sum of money."
一笔生意	yī bǐ shēng yì	a (single) business deal — 一 yī (one) + 笔 bǐ (measure word) + 生意 shēngyì (business). One transaction or deal, counted as a single recorded piece of business: 做一笔生意 "to do a deal," 一笔大生意 "a big deal."
一部电影	yí bù diàn yǐng	a film; one movie — 一 yī (one) + 部 bù (measure word) + 电影 diànyǐng (film). The standard way to count a film, treating it as a single complete work. The same 部 counts a 小说 (novel) or a 电视剧 (TV series).
一部手机	yí bù shǒu jī	a mobile phone; one cellphone — 一 yī (one) + 部 bù (measure word) + 手机 shǒujī (phone). 部 counts a phone as a self-contained device. You will also hear 一个手机 in casual speech, but 一部手机 is the proper classifier.
一部小说	yí bù xiǎo shuō	a novel; one work of fiction — 一 yī (one) + 部 bù (measure word) + 小说 xiǎoshuō (novel). 部 counts a full-length novel as one finished work. For a short story you would more likely use 篇 piān, the classifier for shorter writings.
一次性筷子	yī cì xìng kuài zi	Disposable chopsticks — Disposable chopsticks: the individually wrapped bamboo or wood pairs given at most restaurants. Billions are used annually; their environmental cost is a topic of ongoing public debate.
一带一路	yī dài yī lù	Belt and Road Initiative
一到就	yī dào jiù	as soon as [someone/something] arrives — Productive frame for arrival-triggered actions. 他一到就开始工作 (He started working as soon as he arrived). Pairs naturally with 回来 huí lái (return) and 到家 dào jiā (get home): 一回来就 / 一到家就.
一点	yī diǎn	a little; a bit (post-verbal, neutral or positive) — 一 yī (one) + 点 diǎn (dot; bit). Appears after adjectives and verbs, typically in commands, requests, or comparisons. Neutral to positive in coloring. 便宜一点 (a little cheaper , as a request), 快一点 (a little faster), 多吃一点 (eat a little more). In comparisons: 比昨天冷一点 (a little colder than yesterday).
一点儿	yì diǎn r	a little; a bit (neutral, often comparative) — After an adjective, 一点儿 makes a neutral comparison, "a little more X": 大一点儿 (a bit bigger), 快一点儿 (a bit faster), 便宜一点儿 (a little cheaper). After a verb, it means "a little / some": 喝一点儿水 (drink a little water), 说一点儿 (say a bit).
一点儿 · 部分	yī diǎn r bù fen	a little (mass) · a part (of a whole) — 一点儿 measures out a small amount of an uncountable substance: 一点儿水 (a little water), 会一点儿中文 (speaks a little Chinese). It pairs with mass nouns where 几 pairs with countables. 部分 bùfen slices a defined whole: 一部分学生 (a portion of the students), 大部分时间 (most of the time). The trap of 一点儿 vs.
一定要	yí dìng yào	must; be sure to; definitely will — The emphatic resolve-and-urging frame. For yourself it is determination: 我一定要赢 ("I'm determined to win"). For others it is earnest pressing: 你一定要照顾好自己 ("you really must take good care of yourself").
一队	yí duì	a column / band of (measure word) — 队 as a measure word counts people or animals moving in a formed line: a column of soldiers, a string of camels, a file of schoolchildren.
一份工作	yí fèn gōng zuò	a job (one position) — When 工作 means a discrete job or position, it is counted with the measure word 份 fèn, the same word used for a portion or a document. 一份工作 ("one job"), 两份工作 ("two jobs," as in working two jobs). When 工作 means work as an activity or workload, it takes no measure word: 工作很多 ("there's a lot of work").
一封信	yì fēng xìn	one letter — 封 also works as the measure word for letters and other sealed correspondence: each sealed packet counts as one 封. 一封信 is one letter; 一封电子邮件 (yì fēng diànzǐ yóujiàn), one email.
一个	yí gè	one (with general measure word) — 一 + 个 (general measure word). 一 before tone 4 (个 gè) → yí. The most frequent combination of 一 in everyday speech: 一个人 yí gè rén (one person), 一个问题 yí gè wèntí (one question), 一个地方 yí gè dìfang (one place). 个 gè is the default measure word when no specific one applies.
一共	yí gòng	altogether; in total
一国两制	yī guó liǎng zhì	"One country, two systems" , the constitutional principle under which Hong Kong was reunified with China in 1997. Proposed by Deng Xiaoping, it promised Hong Kong fifty years of maintaining its existing legal, economic, and social systems under Chinese sovereignty. The principle expires in 2047.
一件礼物	yī jiàn lǐ wù	a gift: one present — 一 yī (one) + 件 jiàn (measure word) + 礼物 lǐwù (gift; present). A present counts as a discrete object, so it takes 件. The same classifier extends to other countable belongings and pieces of luggage: 一件行李 (one piece of luggage), 一件作品 (one work of art).
一件事	yī jiàn shì	a matter: one affair; one thing to deal with — 一 yī (one) + 件 jiàn (measure word) + 事 shì (matter; affair). 件 is the standard classifier for an affair, an errand, or a piece of business, anything that can be treated as a single, separable concern. You do not say 一个事 in careful speech; the matter takes 件.
一件衣服	yī jiàn yī fu	an item of clothing: one garment — 一 yī (one) + 件 jiàn (measure word) + 衣服 yīfu (clothing). 件 counts upper-body and one-piece garments: shirts, coats, dresses, sweaters. Lower-body garments take 条 tiáo instead (一条裤子 a pair of trousers, 一条裙子 a skirt), so the measure word itself signals which part of the body is meant.
一节课	yì jié kè	one class period; one lesson — 一 yī (one) + 节 jié (measure word) + 课 kè (class). 节 here is the counter for class periods, each a fixed segment of the school day. 上一节课 "to attend a class period," 两节课 "two periods."
一句话	yī jù huà	one remark; a single sentence; in a word — 一 yī (one) + 句 jù (measure word) + 话 huà (speech). A single spoken remark, counted with 句. Used adverbially, 一句话 means "in a word, to put it briefly."
一看就懂	yī kàn jiù dǒng	understand at a glance; self-explanatory — The prototype 一…就 sentence. Used to describe something immediately clear, a design that needs no instructions, or a person who grasps something with no effort. 这个图表一看就懂 (This chart is self-explanatory).
一块儿	yí kuài r	together (colloquial)
一路平安	yí lù píng ān	"safe journey": the standard farewell to a traveler — 一 yī (one, whole) + 路 lù (road) + 平安 píng'ān (peace, safety). "May the whole road be peaceful." The phrase you say at the airport, the train station, or as the car pulls away. Slightly more formal than 一路顺风 yílù shùnfēng ("may the wind be with you").
一面	yī miàn	one side; one surface; (measure) one flat object — 一 yī (one) + 面 miàn (measure word). 面 counts flat, surface-like objects: 一面镜子 (a mirror), 一面墙 (a wall), 一面旗 (a flag). As a noun 一面 also means "one side" of an argument or "one aspect."
一生	yī shēng	one's whole life
一生一世	yì shēng yí shì	all one's life; a whole lifetime; forever — 一生 yìshēng (one life) + 一世 yíshì (one lifetime). A doubled phrase for the entirety of a person's days, common in vows of love and loyalty: 我会爱你一生一世 "I will love you for the rest of my life." Warmer and more lyrical than the plain 一辈子.
一首歌	yī shǒu gē	one song; a song — 一 yī (one) + 首 shǒu (measure word) + 歌 gē (song). 首 is the classifier for songs and poems: 一首歌 a song, 一首诗 a poem, 一首词 a lyric. Each piece is counted as one self-contained "head." 唱一首歌 "to sing a song."
一首诗	yī shǒu shī	one poem; a poem — 一 yī (one) + 首 shǒu (measure word) + 诗 shī (poem). The same classifier counts poems. 写一首诗 "to write a poem," 背一首诗 "to recite a poem from memory." A classical anthology is described as so many 首 of verse.
一同	yì tóng	together (formal)
一头牛	yī tóu niú	one cow; a head of cattle — 一 yī (one) + 头 tóu (measure word) + 牛 niú (cow). Here 头 keeps its full tone as the classifier for large livestock: cattle, pigs, sheep, elephants. You count beasts by the head.
一下子	yí xià zi	all at once; suddenly — A related but distinct adverb: 一下子 means "all at once, in one go, suddenly," describing rapid or abrupt change rather than softening a verb: 天一下子黑了 ("the sky went dark all at once"). Do not confuse it with the verb-softening 一下: 看一下 ("have a look") versus 一下子看完了 ("finished reading it in one go").
一向	yī xiàng	all along; consistently; up to now — 一 yī (one; throughout) + 向 xiàng (facing back along). A habit or state held unbroken up to the present: 他一向如此 "he has always been like this." Marks long-standing consistency, not a one-off.
一些	yī xiē	some (partitive)
一些 · 有的 · 有些	yī xiē yǒu de yǒu xiē	some: indefinite vs. some-of-a-set — 一些 introduces an indefinite amount: 我买了一些水果 (I bought some fruit). 有的 and 有些 pick out a subset of a known or generic whole, and they live at the front of the sentence: 有的人喜欢，有的人不喜欢 (some people like it, some don't). The classic restriction: 有的 phrases resist the object position.
一样	yí yàng	the same; alike; equally — Marks two things as the same. The frame is A 跟 B 一样 (跟 gēn or 和 hé link the two). Add an adjective to compare a specific quality as equal: A 跟 B 一样 + Adj. Negate as A 跟 B 不一样 ("different") or A 跟 B 不一样 + Adj. 一样 alone can also modify a noun: 一样的衣服 ("the same clothes").
一直	yī zhí	continuously, all along
一致	yī zhì	unanimous; consistent; in agreement — 一 + 致 zhì (to reach; to arrive at). All arriving at one point. 意见一致 (unanimous opinion), 行动一致 (acting in concert), 保持一致 (maintain consistency). Higher register than 一样 yīyàng , used in formal and official contexts. 众口一致 (everyone speaking with one mouth = unanimous) is a near-chengyu variant.
一走了之	yī zǒu liǎo zhī	to simply leave; to walk away from a problem — 一 + 走了之 , "just walk off and be done with it." A set phrase for leaving without dealing with the situation. Pejorative: the person who 一走了之 is abandoning responsibility. Used critically: 你不能就这样一走了之 (you can't just walk away like that).
伊	yī	she (literary, classical, regional) — The classical and Wu-dialect "she." In the 1917 debate, some intellectuals advocated reviving 伊 instead of coining 她, since 伊 already had a feminine reading in some classical poetry and avoided the male-default asymmetry.
伊斯兰教	Yī sī lán jiào	Islam — practiced by the 色目人 administrators and merchants of the Yuan
衣钵	yī bō	robe and bowl (transmission)
衣服	yī fu	clothes
医生	yī shēng	doctor; physician — 医 yī (medicine; to heal) + 生 shēng (practitioner). The standard word for doctor in mainland China. Note the 生 suffix , it originally meant "one who practices/studies" (compare 学生, 先生). 看医生 = "to see a doctor."
依然	yī rán	still (literary, often emotional)
移动	yí dòng	to shift; move
移民	yí mín	immigrant; to migrate
遗传	yí chuán	heredity; to pass on (genetically); hereditary — 遗 yí (to leave behind) + 传 chuán (to hand down). The biological passing of traits from parent to offspring: 遗传病 "hereditary disease," 遗传基因 "the genes one inherits." The body's own version of handing something down across generations.
疑问	yí wèn	doubt; query; an unresolved question — 疑 yí (to doubt) + 问 wèn (to ask). A question that arises from uncertainty: 毫无疑问 háo wú yíwèn "without the slightest doubt." Also the grammatical term, as in 疑问句 yíwènjù "interrogative sentence."
已读不回	yǐ dú bù huí	read but no reply
已故	yǐ gù	the late; deceased
以便	yǐ biàn	in order to facilitate; so as to make convenient; so that — 以 (in order to) + 便 (convenient; to facilitate). Introduces a purpose clause describing how the preceding action creates favorable conditions for what follows. Register: formal written Chinese.
以茶代酒	yǐ chá dài jiǔ	Substitute tea for spirits — Substitute tea for spirits: the accepted formula for toasting without drinking alcohol. The phrase is spoken while raising a cup of tea, acknowledging the substitution explicitly.
以防	yǐ fáng	as a precaution against; in order to guard against — 以 (in order to) + 防 (to guard against; to defend). Often extended to 以防万一 yǐfáng wànyī , "in order to guard against the one-in-ten-thousand chance," i.e., as a just-in-case precaution. One of the most common 以 compounds in everyday written Chinese.
以后	yǐ hòu	after; afterward; in the future; from now on — 以 (positional marker) + 后 (behind; after). The paired counterpart to 以前. Stands alone for "in the future" or follows a time reference for "after X." Compare: 之后 zhīhòu (slightly more classical feel; often interchangeable) and 后来 hòulái (what happened next in a narrative , retrospective only).
以免	yǐ miǎn	in order to avoid; so as to prevent; lest — 以 (in order to) + 免 (to avoid; to exempt from). Introduces a clause describing what the preceding action is intended to prevent. Appears in instructions, warnings, and formal recommendations.
以前	yǐ qián	before; previously; in the past; formerly — 以 yǐ (by means of; from; at) + 前 qián (front; before). "From the front / from before": the time that stands in front of the present moment. The most versatile and common temporal use of 前. Used after a specific time phrase (三年以前, three years ago) or alone (以前, in the past, formerly).
以前 · 现在 · 以后	yǐ qián xiàn zài yǐ hòu	before · now · after: the relative frame — 以前 and 以后 attach to any reference point: 三年以前 (three years ago), 吃饭以后 (after eating), or stand alone to mean "formerly" and "from now on". 的时候 de shíhou (when …) frames simultaneity: 我小的时候 (when I was little).
以上	yǐ shàng	above (a number); the above-mentioned; and more — 以 yǐ (from; by means of; at) + 上 shàng (above; higher). Used after a number or category to mean "and above" or "the following." Three uses: quantitative (三十以上 = thirty or more), sequential (如以上所述 = as stated above), and categorical (十八岁以上 = eighteen and older).
以上 · 以下	yǐ shàng yǐ xià	above / the above-mentioned · below / the following · more than / less than — 以上 yǐshàng (以 + 上 above): covers both spatial above and "more than a quantity" (十八岁以上 , 18 and above) and "what has been stated above" in written discourse (以上是本次报告的主要内容 , the above is the main content of this report). 以下 yǐxià mirrors it exactly in the opposite direction.
以为	yǐ wéi	to (mistakenly) think; to assume incorrectly — 以 (taking) + 为 (to be; to regard as). In classical Chinese the construction meant "to regard X as Y." In modern speech, 以为 has narrowed to almost always imply that the assumption turned out to be wrong , making it a useful signal of correction or disappointment.
以我为中心	yǐ wǒ wéi zhōng xīn	self-centered; to place oneself at the center
以下	yǐ xià	below (a number); the following; under — 以 yǐ (from; from this point) + 下 xià (below; the following). Three uses mirror exactly those of its counterpart 以上: quantitative (十八岁以下 = under eighteen), sequential (以下是通知 = the following is an announcement), and organizational. The pair 以上/以下 frames all formal Chinese enumeration.
以中文版为准	yǐ Zhōng wén bǎn wéi zhǔn	the Chinese version governs — A bilingual contract needs a clause saying which version prevails if they conflict (如有冲突 rú yǒu chōngtū). Chinese courts and arbitrators work in Chinese, so the Chinese text is the one that will be read. Have it translated back to you independently rather than trusting that the two versions match.
矣	yǐ	classical sentence-final: change of state
义和团	Yì hé tuán	Boxer movement
义举	yì jǔ	a righteous act; a just cause; a deed done for principle — 义 (righteous) + 举 (act; move; deed; to raise up). A deed that rises to the level of a principled act , something done not for personal gain but for a cause recognized as right. Often used of charitable acts, acts of solidarity, or historical deeds that defended the weak or resisted injustice.
义气	yì qi	loyalty among friends; the code of doing right by your people — 义 (righteousness; what is right) + 气 (spirit; energy; disposition). The disposition to do right by the people bound to you , friends, brothers, colleagues, sworn companions. 义气 operates in a different register from 正义: where 正义 is institutional and universal, 义气 is personal and relational.
义士	yì shì	a righteous person; a man of principle; one who sacrifices for a just cause — 义 (righteousness) + 士 (a person of standing; an educated or principled person). Someone whose defining quality is acting on principle rather than self-interest, particularly at personal cost. 义士 is a term of high historical admiration.
义务	yì wù	duty; obligation; compulsory — 义 (what is right) + 务 (affair; task; work). The affair of doing what is right , the tasks that fall to you not because you chose them but because they are the right ones for someone in your position. 义务 spans the full range from formal legal obligation to moral duty.
亿	yì	One hundred million, the great unit above 万 — One hundred million, the great unit above 万. China's population is 十四亿 (shísì yì, 1.4 billion), a figure every learner should be able to parse and produce.
艺术	yì shù	art
议论	yì lùn	to comment on; to talk about (often behind the scenes) — 议 yì (to deliberate, to propose) + 论. To comment on or talk about something, frequently with an undertone of gossip or unsolicited opinion. 议论纷纷 yìlùn fēnfēn describes a crowd buzzing with opinions. Also the name of a school essay genre: 议论文 yìlùnwén, the argumentative essay.
亦	yì	also (classical)
异性	yì xìng	the opposite sex
易	yì	change; easy; the name of the text itself — The single character at the heart of the entire system. The commentary tradition insists it holds three meanings at once: 变易 biànyì (the fact of change, everything transforms), 不易 bùyì (the unchanging principle behind change, the laws of transformation are constant), and 简易 jiǎnyì (the simplicity be…
易安体	yì ān tǐ	The "Yi-an style" — The "Yi-an style": the distinctive lyric mode of Li Qingzhao (whose courtesy name was Yi-an 易安). Characterized by colloquial precision, physical specificity, and a technically innovative use of reduplicated binomes to convey emotional states.
易经	Yì jīng	Book of Changes (a Five Classic)
驿站	yì zhàn	postal relay station
意见	yì jiàn	opinion; view; objection — 意 yì (notion) + 见 jiàn (to see; view). What the mind sees on a matter, that is, a considered opinion: 提意见 "to offer a view." It can lean negative, where 有意见 means to have a complaint or grievance.
意境	yì jìng	the poetic realm — atmosphere, mood, and implied space — 意 yì (meaning; intention; feeling) + 境 jìng (realm; situation; space). The characteristic Chinese aesthetic concept of the space created beyond the words themselves: what the poem evokes rather than describes. Good poetry has 意境; pedestrian verse merely reports facts.
意识	yì shí	consciousness; awareness
意思到了	yì si dào le	"the intention has arrived" — the gesture is enough; the thought counts — Used to accept a gift graciously without demanding more, or to reassure a giver that their token gesture was sufficient and appreciated. Conveys: "I see your heart in this; the object is secondary." Also used reflexively by givers: 意思到了就好 , "as long as the intention is communicated, that's enough."
意思意思	yì si yì si	a token gesture; to make a symbolic offering; "just a little something" — Reduplication softens and miniaturizes the meaning , a universal Chinese pattern. 意思意思 as a verb phrase means "to make a token gesture, to acknowledge the relationship symbolically." It signals effort without demanding reciprocal debt.
意图	yì tú	intention; motive
意外	yì wài	unexpected; an accident; a mishap — 意 yì (notion; expectation) + 外 wài (outside). Outside what the heart anticipated, hence unexpected: 意外的消息 "surprising news." As a noun it names an accident or mishap that fell beyond anyone's reckoning.
意象	yì xiàng	Image, imagistic motif — Image, imagistic motif. Tang criticism valued the evocative image (意象) over explicit statement , show, don't tell, carried to its highest refinement.
意义	yì yì	significance; deeper meaning; meaningfulness — 意 (intention) + 义 (righteousness; principle). More serious and weighty than 意思. 意义 refers to significance in the deeper sense , the kind that requires reflection. Used for historical significance, life meaning, moral weight. 人生的意义 (the meaning of life) uses 意义, never 意思.
意在笔先	yì zài bǐ xiān	The intention precedes the brush — The intention precedes the brush: the principle that a painter must have fully conceived the work before beginning to paint, because ink on xuan paper cannot be corrected. Applies equally to calligraphy.
因此	yīn cǐ	"Therefore" or "for this reason — "Therefore" or "for this reason." More formal than 所以, common in written and considered spoken contexts. Often used to introduce a conclusion drawn from preceding points: 因此, 我们决定… ("therefore, we decided…").
因而	yīn ér	thus; as a result; consequently — 因 yīn (because of) + 而 ér (and so). A written connective that draws a result from a stated cause, close to 因此 but smoother in flowing prose: 准备充分，因而很顺利 "well prepared, and so it went smoothly."
因果	yīng uǒ	cause and effect; (Buddhism) karma — 因 yīn (cause) + 果 guǒ (fruit; result). The cause-and-effect pair as a single concept, from the everyday 因果关系 "causal relationship" to the Buddhist law of karma, where every act ripens into its fruit.
因素	yīn sù	factor; element (contributing to a result) — 因 yīn (cause) + 素 sù (element). One of the contributing causes that feed into an outcome, used of the many partial causes behind a complex result: 经济因素 "economic factors," 不安全因素 "safety hazards."
因为	yīn wèi	because; due to — 因 yīn (cause; reason; because of) + 为 wèi (for; on account of). The most important conjunction in the language for expressing cause. Pairs with 所以 suǒyǐ (therefore) to form the standard causal construction: 因为 X，所以 Y (because X, therefore Y).
阴间	yīn jiān	the underworld; the realm of the dead
阴历	yīn lì	the lunar calendar; the traditional Chinese calendar — 阴 yīn (moon; lunar) + 历 lì (calendar). The traditional calendar reckoned by the phases of the moon, as opposed to the 阳历 (yánglì, the sun-based Gregorian calendar). Strictly the Chinese traditional calendar is 阴阳合历 (a combined lunisolar calendar), but in everyday speech 阴历 is what people call it.
阴凉	yīn liáng	shady and cool; a shaded cool spot — 阴 yīn (shade) + 凉 liáng (cool). Shaded and pleasantly cool, the welcome shade of a tree on a hot day. Here the shadow is entirely positive, the relief the shaded slope offers in summer.
阴谋	yīn móu	conspiracy; sinister plot; scheme operating in darkness — 阴 yīn (dark; covert; hidden) + 谋 móu (to plot; to scheme). A plan that operates in shadow, unseen , the hidden face that 阴 takes when it manifests as human deception. The contrast with open, legitimate action (which would be 阳) is built into the word.
阴气	yīn qì	yin energy; cool, receptive qi
阴天	yīn tiān	an overcast, cloudy day — 阴 yīn (shaded; clouded) + 天 tiān (sky; day; weather). A day when clouds cover the sun, the most concrete everyday use of 阴. This is the original geographic shade made meteorological: the whole sky becomes the shaded slope.
阴虚	yīn xū	yin deficiency — the yin coolant is insufficient; yang heat dominates — 虚 xū = deficient; empty; insufficient. Classic 阴虚 symptoms: heat sensations in the palms and soles of feet (五心烦热), night sweats (盗汗 dào hàn), dry mouth and throat, redness of the cheeks in the afternoon, irritability, insomnia.
阴阳调和	yīn yáng tiáo hé	harmony of yin and yang; the ideal state of health and well-being — 调 tiáo (to tune; to harmonize; to adjust) + 和 hé (harmony; peace). The goal of all Chinese medical practice: not the maximization of either force, but their active, dynamic harmony. 调和 is active , it is a verb about process, not a noun describing a state.
阴阳平衡	yīn yáng píng héng	balance of yin and yang; the state of dynamic equilibrium — The core goal in 阴阳 thinking is not the elimination of one force or the dominance of the other , it is 平衡 pínghéng: dynamic balance between them. This is not static equilibrium (like a scale perfectly level) but living balance (like a tightrope walker, constantly adjusting).
阴阳五行	yīn yáng wǔ xíng	yin-yang and the Five Phases — the paired cosmological framework — The two systems developed in parallel during the Warring States period and were synthesized into a unified framework by the Han dynasty, particularly in the 黄帝内经 (Yellow Emperor's Classic, compiled ca. 200 BCE–200 CE).
阴影	yīn yǐng	shadow; a shadow over the heart; a lingering trauma — 阴 yīn (shade) + 影 yǐng (shadow; image). A literal shadow, the dark shape cast where light is blocked, and also a medical shadow on an X-ray (肺部阴影, a shadow on the lung).
阴宅	yīn zhái	yin dwelling = tomb
音调	yīn diào	pitch
音乐会	yīn yu è huì	concert — Music + 会 (gathering), usually implying the classical hall; a pop star's arena show is a 演唱会 (yǎnchànghuì, "performed-singing gathering"). Tickets for either are 票, and 抢票 ("snatching tickets") describes the purchase experience accurately.
音乐家	yīn yu è jiā	musician (mastery level); composer — 音乐 yīnyuè (music) + 家 jiā. Implies serious, dedicated musical mastery: a composer, a concert performer, a conductor rather than a casual musician. Distinguished from 歌手 gēshǒu (singer/pop artist) by the 家 suffix, which implies depth, discipline, and classical standing.
音量	yīn liàng	volume; sound level
音信	yīn xìn	news; word; tidings (of a person) — 音 yīn (sound; word) + 信 xìn (message; letter). News or word of someone far away. Most common in the negative: 没有音信 "no word at all," and the idiom 杳无音信, vanished without a trace.
音韵	yīn yùn	Guanyin rhyme — Tieguanyin's signature quality
银	yín	silver
银河	yín hé	the Milky Way — 银 (yín, silver) + 河 (river). A silver river across the night sky. Chinese named the galaxy as a river of stars long before it was a galaxy, and the metaphor stuck.
银行	yín háng	bank — 银 yín (silver) + 行 háng. The silver-row , originally a row of money-changers along a market street, then by extension the modern banking institution. The single most-tested 行-háng word: students who default to xíng on every 行 will read this wrong forever.
引进	yǐn jìn	to introduce; to bring in (technology, talent, methods) — 引 yǐn (to draw; to lead) + 进 jìn (to take in). To bring something in from outside, typically from abroad: 引进技术 "introduce technology," 引进人才 "bring in talent."
饮	yǐn	to drink (literary)
饮茶	yǐn chá	yum cha; going for dim sum; drinking tea
饮料	yǐn liào	beverage
饮食	yǐn shí	diet; food and drink
饮食文化	yǐn shí wén huà	food culture; culinary civilization — 饮食 yǐnshí (food and drink, diet) + 文化 (culture). Chinese food culture is one of the most discussed topics in 文化 discourse , not just cuisine but the entire social system of eating: banquet etiquette, regional identity, seasonal eating, the pairing of food with medicine and philosophy.
隐居	yǐn jū	to live as a hermit
印泥	yìn ní	Seal paste — Seal paste: the vermilion medium used to ink seals. Made from cinnabar 朱砂 (mercuric sulfide), sesame oil, and mugwort fiber. The highest-quality seal paste is made in Zhangzhou, Fujian.
印章	yìn zhāng	name seal; personal stamp
窨花	xùn huā	scenting — the process of infusing tea with flower fragrance
应当	yīng dāng	ought to; should; be obliged to — 应 yīng (should) + 当 dāng (be fitting). A modal of obligation, near-identical in meaning to 应该 yīnggāi but slightly more formal, so it shows up often in rules, contracts, and writing.
应付	yìng fu	to cope with; to handle; to deal with — 应 yìng (to meet) + 付 fù (to hand over, to deal out). To handle a task or person, often a difficult one. Can carry a tinge of just getting through it: 应付考试 (manage to get through the exam), 应付了事 (do something perfunctorily just to be done with it).
应该是	yīng gāi shì	should be; probably is
应聘	yìng pìn	to apply for a job
应用	yìng yòng	to apply; application
英明	yīng míng	brilliant; enlightened (of a leader) — 英 yīng (outstanding; heroic; talent that flowers) + 明 míng (luminous). The compound used to praise rulers, commanders, and decision-makers of exceptional vision. 英明的领导 "an enlightened leader."
英武	yīng wǔ	valiant and impressive; heroic in bearing — 英 yīng (heroic, outstanding) + 武 wǔ (martial). Of a person, valiant and impressive in look and manner, the brave, spirited bearing of a hero or a fine soldier: 英武不凡 "valiant and out of the ordinary." A literary, admiring word, often used of a young officer or commander.
英雄	yīng xióng	hero
婴儿	yīng ér	infant, baby
迎	yíng	to welcome, meet
迎接	yíng jiē	to welcome, to go out to meet — 迎 (yíng, to go toward) + 接 (to receive). You walk out to meet an arrival and bring them in. Often warm or ceremonial: greeting guests, soldiers, the new year.
影	yǐng	shadow; reflection
影子	yǐng zi	shadow
硬件	yìng jiàn	hardware
拥有	yōng yǒu	to own; to cherish; to hold close — 拥 yōng (to embrace; to hold close; to crowd together) + 有. More emotionally warm than 所有 or 具有. Suggests not just legal or abstract possession but holding something dear. Used for things of personal value: relationships, opportunities, qualities of character, cherished experiences.
永乐帝	Yǒng lè Dì	third Ming emperor, builder of Beijing
永明延寿	Yǒng míng Yán shòu	Yongming Yanshou (904–975 CE) — Song dynasty Chan-Pure Land synthesis — The sixth patriarch of Pure Land and a major Chan master simultaneously, Yanshou embodied the Chinese Buddhist principle that the two traditions are not opposed but complementary.
俑	yǒng	tomb figure; funerary effigy
勇敢	yǒng gǎn	brave; courageous
用法	yòng fǎ	usage; how something is used — 用 yòng (to use) + 法 fǎ (way). The way a thing is properly used, especially a word or a tool: 这个词的用法 "the usage of this word." Built on the same "way of doing" sense as 方法.
用功	yòng ōng	to study hard; to work diligently; to apply oneself — 用 yòng (to use; to deploy) + 功 gōng (effort; merit; achievement). To deploy effort , the literal meaning maps exactly onto the English "to apply oneself." Used particularly of students and academic work, but extends to any disciplined sustained effort.
用力	yòng lì	to exert force; to use one's strength; hard (of physical effort) — 用 yòng (to use) + 力 lì (force). To put physical force into an action: 用力推 "push hard," 用力拉 "pull with all your strength." Describes how an action is done, with muscle behind it.
用途	yòng tú	use; purpose; application; intended function — 用 yòng (use; function) + 途 tú (route; destination; intended course). The destination of deployment: what something is for, where its use is headed. 用途 answers the question "what is this for?" and tends to describe a range of applications rather than a single act of using.
用心	yòng xīn	attentively; with care
优点	yōu diǎn	strong point; merit; advantage — 优 yōu (excellent) + 点 diǎn (point). A good point, a strength. Its exact opposite is 缺点 quēdiǎn, a shortcoming. Together they are the standard pair for weighing a person, plan, or product.
优美	yōu měi	graceful; elegant
优质产品	yōu zhì chǎn pǐn	high-quality product; premium product — 优质 yōuzhì (excellent quality , 优 excellent + 质 substance/quality) is the premium tier modifier. 打造优质产品 (to build premium products) is a common mission statement. Contrasts with 劣质产品 (lièzhì chǎnpǐn, inferior-quality products) , counterfeit or substandard goods.
忧	yōu	worry; anxiety
悠闲	yōu xián	leisurely, at ease
尤其	yóu qí	especially; in particular; above all — 尤 yóu (outstanding; especially) + 其 qí (that; those). "Especially that" , used to single out one item from a broader set as particularly true or notable. Appears before verbs, adjectives, or noun phrases. Roughly equivalent to "particularly" or "above all."
由于	yóu yú	due to; owing to; because of — 由 yóu (from) + 于 yú (at; in). A formal, written near-synonym of 因为, marking the cause at the front of a sentence: 由于天气原因，航班取消了 "owing to the weather, the flight was cancelled." It pairs with 因此 rather than 所以.
犹如	yóu rú	just as; like
邮件	yóu jiàn	mail; email; a postal item — 邮 yóu (post; mail) + 件 jiàn (a piece). Originally any item sent through the post. In everyday modern use it most often means email: 电子邮件 diànzǐ yóujiàn (electronic mail) is regularly shortened to 邮件. To send one is 发邮件 fā yóujiàn.
邮票	yóu piào	postage stamp: a stamp for mailing letters — 邮 yóu (post; mail) + 票 piào (slip). A postage stamp: a tiny printed slip stuck to an envelope that stands for prepaid postage. It is 票 in miniature, a small piece of paper carrying a fixed value, and a favorite object of collectors (集邮 jíyóu, stamp collecting).
油画	yóu huà	oil painting — 油 yóu (oil) + 画 huà (painting). The Western art of painting in oils, named for its medium. Its native counterpart is 国画 guóhuà, named for its nation rather than its material.
游船	yóu chuán	pleasure boat, cruise boat
友	yǒu	friend; friendly
友好	yǒu hǎo	friendly; amicable
友情	yǒu qíng	friendship; the feeling between friends — 友 yǒu (friend) + 情 qíng (feeling). The bond of friendship. Close to 友谊 yǒuyì in meaning, but 友情 leans on the felt warmth, while 友谊 leans on the formal relationship.
有道理	yǒu dào lǐ	to make sense; to be reasonable; that's a fair point — 有 yǒu (to have; there is) + 道理 dàolǐ (reason; logic). "To have reason," to possess the quality of making sense. One of the most common spoken responses to an argument you find convincing, used the way "fair point," "that makes sense," and "good point" function in English.
有的	yǒu de	some (of a set); certain ones — 有 + 的 (nominalizing particle). Functions as an indefinite partitive , "some of [a set already mentioned or implied]." The pattern 有的…有的… (some…others…) distributes a set into contrasting subgroups and is one of the most natural ways to express "some people…some people…" in Chinese.
有点	yǒu diǎn	a bit; slightly (pre-verbal, negative or complaint coloring) — 有 + 点 diǎn (a dot; a small amount). Always appears before verbs and adjectives as a pre-modifier. The key nuance: 有点 carries a slightly negative or complaint-inflected tone. You use it for problems, inconveniences, or things that fall short.
有点儿	yǒu diǎn r	a bit; somewhat (often "a bit too…") — Placed before an adjective (or a feeling-verb) to say something is "a bit / somewhat" that way, usually with a tinge of complaint or an unwanted state. 有点儿 + 累 / 冷 / 贵 / 难 = a bit tired / cold / expensive / hard. It describes a current condition the speaker finds slightly undesirable.
