走出	zǒu chū	to walk out of; to emerge from — 走 zǒu (walk; leave) + 出 (exit complement). The most neutral directional compound: walking across a threshold from inside to outside. Used for physical exits and metaphorical ones , 走出困境 (walk out of a difficult situation), 走出阴影 (emerge from the shadow of something).
走道	zǒu dào	corridor; aisle; walkway — 走 zǒu (walk; go) + 道 (way; path). The path you walk: a corridor between rooms, an aisle on a plane or train, a narrow walkway between structures. Practical and architectural: the space designated for movement through an enclosed or constrained area.
走关系	zǒu guān xi	to use personal connections to get something done — 走 zǒu = to go through; to travel via. 走关系 means to go through the channel of personal connections to accomplish something that might otherwise be difficult or slow.
走后门	zǒu hòu mén	Literally "going through the back door" — Literally "going through the back door": using gifts and personal relationships to obtain favors, preferential treatment, or access that would not be available through formal channels. The phrase acknowledges the reality of this practice without fully endorsing it.
走江湖	zǒu jiāng hú	to travel the roads making a living; to hustle; to work itinerantly — Traditional use: traveling performers, fortune-tellers, medicine sellers, acrobats , people who made their living moving from town to town, outside the settled economy.
走廊	zǒu láng	corridor; hallway
走路	zǒu lù	to walk; walking — 走 zǒu (to walk/go) + 路 lù (road; path). To walk , the action of foot-travel. 走路去 = to walk there (contrast: 开车去 to drive, 坐地铁去 to take the subway). Also used metaphorically: 走上了一条不归路 "walked a road of no return" · 走对了路 "took the right path (in life)." 请走路 = "please use the footpath" (on signs).
走向	zǒu xiàng	to move toward; trend; the run or layout of something — 走 zǒu (to go) + 向 xiàng (toward). As a verb, to head toward an outcome: 走向成功 "to head toward success." As a noun, the trend or physical run of a thing, as in the 走向 of a mountain range or a market.
走运	zǒu yùn	to be lucky
走资派	zǒu zī pài	capitalist roader (target of persecution)
租房	zū fáng	to rent a place
租界	zū jiè	Concession , the zones of extraterritorial foreign governance established in Shanghai and other treaty ports. The foreign concessions in Shanghai operated from 1843 to 1943, when wartime politics prompted their formal retrocession to China.
租借	zū jiè	to lease, to rent on loan — 租 (to rent) + 借 (to borrow). A bound compound for taking something for temporary use, often with a fee: leasing land, chartering equipment, the historical "leased territories" (租借地) of treaty-port China.
足迹	zú jī	footprint; trace; tracks — 足 (foot) + 迹 (trace, mark). The imprint a foot leaves , both physical footprints and the metaphorical traces left by a life or a civilization. 留下足迹 (to leave one's footprints); 遍布全国的足迹 (footprints spread across the whole country).
足球	zú qiú	football; soccer — 足 (foot) + 球 (ball). The foot-ball: soccer in China and Taiwan. The Chinese Football Association (中国足球协会) governs 足球 nationally.
族谱	zú pǔ	clan genealogy — the lineage's living memory — 族谱 (also 家谱 jiāpǔ or 宗谱 zōngpǔ) is the written genealogical record of a lineage. It records birth, death, marriage, official appointments, and notable achievements of every member, generation by generation.
祖父	zǔ fù	paternal grandfather (formal) — 祖 zǔ (ancestor, forebear) + 父 fù (father). The father's father, in formal register. Spoken Mandarin uses 爷爷 yéye for the same man, the way 爸爸 stands in for 父亲.
祖宗	zǔ zōng	ancestors (collective, formal)
钻	zuān	to bore, to drill
最初	zuì chū	at first; the very beginning
最好	zuì hǎo	best; had best, it would be best to
最后	zuì hòu	finally; in the end; last — 最 zuì (most; superlative) + 后 (behind; last). The furthest back position in a sequence: the final item, the last moment, the ultimate outcome. Functions as both an adverb before a verb (最后他同意了 , in the end he agreed) and as an adjective modifying a noun (最后一页 , the last page).
最近	zuì jìn	recently; lately; nearest
最喜欢	zuì xǐ huan	to like the most; favourite — With 最 zuì (most), 喜欢 expresses a favourite. The attributive pattern 最喜欢的 + Noun gives "favourite X": 我最喜欢的电影 (my favourite film). This is the standard way to say "favourite," since Chinese has no single adjective for it.
最终	zuì zhōng	ultimately; finally
最重要的是	zuì zhòng yào de shì	"Most importantly — "Most importantly." Marks the top-priority item in a list or argument. 最重要的是健康 ("the most important thing is health"). Useful for ranking points by significance.
尊称	zūn chēng	honorific address
尊严	zūn yán	dignity
尊重	zūn zhòng	to respect
昨天	zuó tiān	yesterday — 昨 zuó (yesterday; past) + 天 tiān (day). The 昨 character is rarely used outside this compound and 昨晚 zuówǎn (last night) in modern Chinese. Note the tonal transition: 昨 zuó is tone 2, 天 tiān is tone 1, two rising pitches in sequence, which requires attention in spoken practice.
昨天 · 今天 · 明天	zuó tiān jīn tiān míng tiān	the day series, and its symmetric siblings — The day series runs 前天 (day before yesterday) · 昨天 · 今天 · 明天 · 后天 (day after tomorrow). Years mirror it exactly: 前年 · 去年 · 今年 · 明年 · 后年. Weeks and months use 上/下 (above/below) instead: 上个星期 (last week), 下个月 (next month).
左边	zuǒ biān	the left side; on the left — 左 (left) + 边 (side; edge). The most neutral, everyday word for "the left side." Used in directions, descriptions of position, and navigation. 在左边 (on the left), 左边的门 (the door on the left). Paired with 右边 yòubiān (the right side) for balanced descriptions of both sides of something.
左传	Zuǒ Zhuàn	the Zuo Commentary — the primary narrative source for the Spring and Autumn period — Traditionally attributed to Zuo Qiuming (左丘明 Zuǒ Qiūmíng) and covering 722–468 BCE, the 左传 is the great narrative expansion of the Chūnqiū chronicle.
左道旁门	zuǒ dào páng mén	heterodox sects and methods; unorthodox ways — 左道 (the left way, the deviant path) + 旁门 (a side gate, a door off to the side). 道 is the orthodox central path; 左道 is the path that veers left of it. 旁门 extends the spatial metaphor , not the main gate, but a side entrance used by those who cannot or will not use the front.
左手	zuǒ shǒu	the left hand — 左 (left) + 手 (hand). Straightforward compound for the left hand. 左撇子 zuǒpiězi is the colloquial word for a left-handed person (撇 piě is the name of the left-falling stroke in calligraphy; 子 nominalizes).
左右	zuǒ yòu	both sides; approximately; to control
佐	zuǒ	to assist; an aide; subordinate official — The cognate character: 人 (person radical) + 左 (left, the assisting hand). 辅佐 fǔzuǒ means "to assist a ruler or superior" , the two characters are near-synonymous, both meaning to support from a flanking position.
作弊	zuò bì	to cheat (on an exam) — 作 (do) + 弊 (fraud, malpractice). Exam cheating is a criminal offense in China for national exams, policed with metal detectors and signal jammers, which measures how much the tests are worth. The honest sibling 打小抄 (crib notes) covers the classroom misdemeanor.
作风	zuò fēng	work style; conduct
作家	zuò jiā	writer; author (literary) — 作 zuò (to create; to make; to compose) + 家 jiā. The term implies literary seriousness and sustained creative output, not just anyone who writes. The 家 suffix distinguishes this from 记者 jìzhě (journalist) or 编辑 biānjí (editor), which are professional roles without the mastercraft implication.
作品	zuò pǐn	a work (of art, literature, music) — 作 zuò (to compose) + 品 pǐn (article; item). A finished creative work: 文学作品 "a literary work," 这是他最好的作品 "this is his finest work."
作为	zuò wéi	to act as; in the capacity of; conduct; achievement — 作 zuò (to do; to act) + 为 wéi (to be; to serve as). Two near-synonyms combined for emphasis on role or capacity. As a preposition phrase: 作为一个老师 (in the capacity of a teacher / as a teacher). As a noun: 他很有作为 (he has accomplished a great deal; he is a person of achievement).
作业	zuò yè	homework; assignment; (work) operation — 作 zuò (to do; to make) + 业 yè (coursework). The schoolwork a student is assigned: 做作业 "to do homework," 家庭作业 "home assignment." In technical contexts it also means a work operation, as in 水下作业 "underwater operations."
作业被老师批评了	zuò yè bèi lǎo shī pī píng le	the homework was criticized by the teacher — Here the agent (老师, teacher) is explicitly named , the full 被 structure. The subject (homework) is criticized; criticism is typically unwanted. Note the result element 了 marking completion. Compare: 老师批评了作业 (teacher criticized the homework , SVO, neutral) vs.
作用	zuò yòng	function; effect; role; action upon — 作 zuò (to do; to make; to act) + 用 yòng (function; use). The most abstract 用 compound: not the act of using, but the role or effect that something exerts. 作用 describes what an entity does in a system , its causal contribution.
作者	zuò zhě	author; writer; the one who made it — 作 zuò (to compose) + 者 zhě (the one who). The maker of a work, most often a writer but usable for any creator. Compare 作家 zuòjiā, which specifically means a professional writer or author by trade.
坐	zuò	to sit
坐禅	zuò chán	sitting meditation — the formal practice of Chan — 坐 zuò (sit) + 禅 chán (dhyāna). Extended periods of seated meditation , in Japan called zazen 坐禅. Chan monasteries typically structure the day around multiple periods of 坐禅, sometimes lasting hours in intensive retreats (禅七 chán qī, seven-day intensive).
坐船	zuò chuán	to travel by boat
座右铭	zuò yòu míng	personal motto
做菜	zuò cài	to cook; to prepare dishes — 做 zuò (to make) + 菜 cài (dish). To cook, in the sense of making the dishes for a meal. 他很会做菜 "he is a good cook." Its near-neighbor 做饭 zuò fàn frames the same act around the staple rice.
做到	zuò dào	to accomplish; to achieve; to manage to do — 做 zuò (to do; to make) + 到 dào (arrive at the result). Doing that reaches its endpoint: the task is completed, the goal achieved. Carries a sense of managed difficulty , you worked until arrival.
做饭	zuò fàn	to cook (a meal)
做梦	zuò mèng	to dream; (figuratively) to fantasize
做七	zuò qī	to observe the seven-day mourning rites — 做 zuò (to perform) + 七 qī (seven, a seven-day period). The funeral custom of holding rites for the dead every seventh day for seven cycles, forty-nine days in all. Each 七 marks a stage in the soul's passage; the seventh and final period (七七 or 满七) closes the formal mourning.
做青	zuò qīng	working the green — the oxidation control step
做人	zuò rén	to conduct oneself; to be the kind of person one should be — 做 zuò (to do; to act as; to make) + 人 rén. The verb that captures the Confucian view that being human is an achievement, not a given. 做人 is learned: how to navigate relationships, honor obligations, maintain dignity, and treat others with genuine consideration.
做生意	zuò shēng yi	to do business; to trade
做事	zuò shì	to handle matters; to work; to conduct oneself in action
做完	zuò wán	to finish doing
做作	zuò zuo	affected, pretentious
